# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

Site Monitor — full-stack SSL/TLS certificate monitoring system. Spring Boot 4.1 / Java 25 backend serves a React 18 (Vite 5) SPA; PostgreSQL is the system of record. Single deployable: the React `dist/` is served as static content from the Spring Boot jar in prod. Single VERSION file (`./VERSION`) is authoritative for both backend (`pom.xml` uses `${revision}`; the Docker build passes `-Drevision=$(cat VERSION)`, local builds produce `0.0.0-local`) and frontend (`vite.config.js` reads it at build time).

The UI is bilingual TR/EN. **The default language is English** (product decision 2026-10-02): a user with no stored choice sees EN, and a chosen language is kept. The default is read in three places that must agree: `storedLang()` in `i18n/index.jsx`, `dateLocale()` in `i18n/dateLocale.js` (en-GB), and the `X-Lang` header in `api/client.js` (gates: `defaultLanguage.test.jsx`, `client-lang-header.test.js`). The backend `Msg.t` fallback stays `tr` for requests without a header (mails, jobs). Most identifiers, log messages, and admin-panel strings are Turkish — keep i18n keys in sync (see `i18n-parity.test.jsx`).

## Common commands

Java + Maven paths are user-specific: `JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Zulu\zulu-25`, Maven at `D:\portablePrograms\apache-maven-3.9.9\bin\mvn.cmd`. Backend health endpoint is `/health` (Actuator remapped, base-path is `/`).

### Backend (from `backend/`)
```
mvn spring-boot:run           # dev — runs on :8080
mvn -B clean verify           # what CI runs (Surefire + Jacoco)
mvn test                      # tests only; report in target/site/jacoco/index.html
mvn -Dtest=SchedulerServiceTest test           # single class
mvn -Dtest=SchedulerServiceTest#methodName test # single method
mvn package -DskipTests       # produces target/*.jar (consumed by start-local.ps1)
```

### Frontend (from `frontend/`)
```
npm install
npm run dev                   # Vite on :5173, proxies /api → :8080
npm run build                 # outputs dist/, embeds VERSION via define
npm run test                  # vitest single-run (what CI runs)
npm run test:watch
npm run test:coverage         # coverage/index.html
npx vitest run src/test/Nav.test.jsx   # single file
```

### Local full-stack (Windows)
- `start-local.ps1` — kills running `java.exe`, reads `.env`, maps env vars to `-D` system properties, launches the freshly built jar, polls `/health` for UP. Backend log goes to `backend\app.log`; errors to `backend\app-err.log`.
- `START.bat` / `start-backend.bat` / `start-frontend.bat` — simpler convenience launchers.

### Smoke + perf
```
pwsh ./scripts/smoke.ps1 -BaseUrl http://localhost:8080
k6 run -e BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080 ./perf/k6-smoke.js
bash scripts/helm-template-check.sh                                   # rendered chart: shutdown budget, multi-pod guards (CI helm-lint job)
SMOKE_IMAGE=<image> bash scripts/multipod-smoke.sh                    # Docker: pod-a + pod-b + throwaway PostgreSQL — one leader, shared session, SIGTERM handover ≤ 40 s (and back), durable-state VERIFY 0 mismatches + inherited rows after each flip, no duplicates
PGHOST=… PGDATABASE=… PGUSER=… PGPASSWORD=… bash scripts/multipod-smoke.sh dup-check   # read-only duplicate / lost-notification detector (scripts/multipod-dup-check.sql)
NS=… REL=… TARGETS="host:port …" [TLS_TARGETS="host:443 …"] bash scripts/multipod-egress-check.sh <node…>   # prerequisite P1 egress (TCP + direct TLS handshake + issuer) + P2 clock, one pinned pod per node
```

### Docker / image
```
docker-compose up -d                          # local stack (app on :8080)
./scripts/build-image.ps1                     # branch-aware tags (master→VERSION+latest, develop→develop-<sha>+develop, release/*→VERSION-rc+staging)
./scripts/build-image.ps1 -Registry ghcr.io/your-org -Push
```

## Architecture — what you need to know across files

### Single-process deployment, multi-replica safe
Frontend ships **inside** the backend jar (`spring.web.resources.static-locations=file:./frontend/dist/,classpath:/static/`). In Kubernetes the same image runs N replicas, so anything stateful must survive pod death and not double-fire. Multi-pod release 1 (2026-10-21, user request: "2 pod şimdi, N sonra — çift kontrol / alarm / posta / push / rapor yok, kayıp bildirim yok") made two pods safe; prod stays at `replicaCount: 1` until a separate 1 → 2 upgrade (see "Deployment specifics"). The contract:

- **Who runs what (2026-10-21).** The **sweep leader** (lease holder) runs every monitor-type sweep, ACCESSIBILITY, confirmation / recovery rechecks, DNS / domain change detection, the manual-eval inbox drain and the DB-down watchdog alerts. **Any pod** that wins the `scheduler_lock` row and / or the cluster round runs the cert sweeps (hourly, stale, full — deliberately NOT leader-gated), cron reports / reminders / digests, nightly cleanup, the push outbox, the storm lifecycle, escalation steps, the watchdog round and the sweepers (`mail-retry-orphans`, `resolution-notify-sweep`). Since multi-pod Sürüm 2a (2026-10-23) also on any pod, under its own lock: the disabled-types alarm reconcile (`disabled-types-reconcile`), the one-time monitoring-group team fill (`monitoring-group-team-backfill`) and the manual retention run (`nightly-cleanup`, the nightly job's lock); since multi-pod Sürüm 3-1 (2026-10-23) the TLS profile job (lock `tls-profile` + two-phase round `round:tls-profile`, period `site.monitor.tls-profile.tick-ms`; it is no longer leader-gated). The **request pod** serves UI / API, diagnostics and "Check now" (its alarm evaluation is forwarded to the leader). Extra pods add HA and UI / API capacity, not monitoring throughput (since Sürüm 3-1 they also take the long side jobs and the org-wide metric families off the leader; the monitoring checks, rechecks and the inbox drain stay on the leader until Sürüm 3-3): scale the leader vertically, the HPA stays off. Runbook: `docs/RUNBOOK.md` "Çok pod".
  - **Side jobs off the leader (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 3-1, plan 3e).** While another live pod exists (`schedule/PodLiveness`: the R2b heartbeat rule — a `system_heartbeat` row with an `instance_id` ≠ this pod, no shutdown marker, last beat within `ExtendedHealthService.POD_LIVE_MINUTES` (3, now `public`); one grouped query cached 30 s (`PodLiveness.CACHE_MS`); unreadable = unknown = "no other pod" — never defer / yield on unknown; a 20.166.0 pod (NULL `instance_id`) is not counted; another instance id with THIS pod's name (`ClusterHealthStore.podOf` — the previous process after an unclean restart, OOM / SIGKILL without a shutdown marker) is not counted either, FX-SIDE #3) and `site.monitor.scheduler.leader-yield.enabled` is on (BOOL, group `scheduler` — Settings → Genel → Zamanlayıcı, GLOBAL_ONLY, live, default true, env `SCHEDULER_LEADER_YIELD_ENABLED`; `SideJob.LEADER_YIELD_KEY`), the sweep leader (`sweepLeaderHint()`) DEFERS cron side jobs by `site.monitor.scheduler.leader-yield.cron-defer-ms` (20000, boot-time, clamped to `SideJobPlacement.MAX_CRON_DEFER_MS` 25000 with a WARN — 25 s + the cron round claim retries stay inside `CRON_ROUND_SLACK_MS` 60 s) and YIELDS the round-based heavy jobs (`cert-stale` 5 min, `tls-profile` tick, `executive-summary-catchup` 55 min) when ANOTHER pod COMPLETED that round within 2 periods (`SchedulerService.leaderYieldsRound`, one PK read of the completion stamp `round:<name>:done` — `ClusterScheduleService.DONE_SUFFIX` / `doneKey` / `markDone`, written by `finishSideRound` / `noteSideRoundCompleted` (and `ExecutiveSummaryScheduling.catchUpTick` after its `completeRound`) only when the round body returned normally: `claimed_by` = that pod, `claimed_at` = completion time (`next_due_at` = the same instant, NOT a due date; no round ever claims it), newest wins (`UPDATE … WHERE claimed_at IS NULL OR claimed_at <= now`, else `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), `round:` prefix so `ClusterScheduleService.prune` keeps it (20.167.0 pods too); a write failure is swallowed (no evidence → the leader claims). A bare claim is NOT evidence — a follower whose body fails every period no longer keeps the leader yielding (review FX-SIDE #4); this pod's own previous process (same pod name, `SchedulerService.samePod`) is never evidence; no evidence — 20.167.0 followers (they never stamp), stalled / failing followers, first rounds after a deploy — → the leader claims as before: fallback ≤ 2 periods after the last completion). The deferred attempt skips when the trigger is already taken (`SchedulerService.cronRoundTaken(round, cron, zone)` — read-only: the `round:<name>` row's `next_due_at` lies in the window `cronRoundDue` would call "not due"; or the round was claimed after the trigger fired, 30 s tolerance (`roundClaimedSince`); or the job's own check: tech-error reminder `roundTaken`, data-quality `snapshotWritten`, cert inventory / executive monthly `cronRoundTaken` on the live cron), otherwise it runs the unchanged body (lock + round + item claims) ≤ 20 s late — never a duplicate, never a lost trigger. The one-time ping roll-up rebuild: the leader skips at most `PING_REBUILD_LEADER_MAX_SKIPS` (2) consecutive hours while another live pod exists, then runs. Long jobs run on the per-pod side-job runner (`schedule/SideJobRunner`; long lane `side-job-N` = `SideJobRunner.effectiveThreads(configured, schedulingPoolSize)` = max(`site.monitor.scheduler.side-jobs.threads` (default 8, env `SCHEDULER_SIDE_JOBS_THREADS`), `MIN_THREADS` 8, `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size`), ≤ `MAX_THREADS` 32, boot-time, 0 = inline (no lanes at all) — a long job waits for a thread only when ≥ 8 other long jobs run on the pod, the case in which 20.167.0's shared 8-thread pool was full too; short lane `side-job-short-N` (`SHORT_LANE_THREADS` 5, one per short cron side job) runs the leader's DEFERRED short cron attempts (`defer(name, delay, heavy = false, …)`) so they never queue behind long jobs, FX-SIDE #1; the test constructor `SideJobRunner(int, LongSupplier)` takes the long-lane size exactly (no floor); non-daemon threads; coalesced per job name — a second trigger while one is queued / running is skipped + counted; fixed-delay jobs (`SideJob.fixedDelay(name, delayMs, body)`, last record component `fixedDelayMs`: `cert-stale`, `tls-profile`, `ping-rollup-rebuild` — its annotation reads `PING_ROLLUP_REBUILD_TICK_MS`) start at the earliest `fixedDelay` after this pod's LAST FINISH (`SideJobRunner.submitAfterGap` holds them on the long lane; the `@Scheduled` invocation returns at once, so the cadence would otherwise be "tick + delay"; a tick while the job waits / runs is dropped silently, not counted as skipped; clock moved back → waits at most one delay; the Küme card shows a held job as `queued`, a neutral state) — single-pod cadence = 20.167.0's "finish + delay"; closed at `ContextClosedEvent` — queued / deferred items never start; `SchedulerService.onShutdown` calls `stopSideJobs()` BEFORE `lockService.releaseAllOwn()`: cancel deferred, wait `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(5000)` (both lanes inside that one wait — the short lane gets the remainder, never a second full cap), `shutdownNow()` (interrupt), then `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(1000)` so an interrupted job releases its own `LockTicket` first): hourly cert sweep (incl. the manual-cert step), stale cert sweep, nightly cleanup, ping rebuild, domain-expiry refresh, weekly availability + catch-up, TLS profile, cert inventory report, executive summary + catch-up, data-quality snapshot. Short cron jobs (CA pin refresh, Friday weekly-report reminder, issue digest, incident action reminder, tech-error reminder) stay on the scheduling pool and are only deferred (the leader's deferred attempt runs on the short lane). The incident action reminder claims a per-trigger cron round `round:incident-action-reminder` (permanent, = its lock name) inside its lock (`SchedulerService.claimItemProtectedCronRound(name, cron, zone)` = `cronRoundDue(…, claimProtected = true)`: item-claimed, so a round read error still runs it) and its SideJob carries `cronRound`, so the leader's deferred attempt is skipped by the default pre-check — before, during a muted system maintenance the follower and then the leader's deferred attempt each wrote a `SKIPPED: sistem bakımı` row per owner (FX-SIDE #2); the manual `run(LocalDate)` claims no round; the tech-error reminder already claims its day round inside its lock. Known edge (accepted): a claim-protected cron round (`weekly-availability`, `ca-pin-refresh`, the incident reminder) whose FOLLOWER claim hits a transient DB error runs anyway without moving the round row, so the leader's deferred attempt can run the body again — at most a second `SKIPPED` trace, never a second mail (item claims). Light lock jobs (storm, escalation steps, system maintenance, quiet digest, sweepers, push, watchdog round, failed-login scan, disabled-types reconcile), the 14 monitoring sweeps and the inbox drain are unchanged. Single pod (no other live pod) or `leader-yield.enabled=false` = 20.167.0 placement (the runner still runs the long bodies, so they no longer hold the 8-thread scheduling pool). Job names = round names (permanent): `cert-hourly`, `cert-stale`, `ca-pin-refresh`, `domain-expiry-refresh`, `nightly-cleanup`, `ping-rollup-rebuild`, `weekly-report-reminder`, `issue-report-digest`, `weekly-availability`, `weekly-availability-catchup`, `tls-profile`, `cert-inventory-report`, `executive-summary`, `executive-summary-catchup`, `incident-action-reminder`, `tech-error-reminder`, `data-quality-snapshot`. **A new long `@Scheduled` job** goes through `SchedulerService.runSideJob(SideJob…)`; a service outside `SchedulerService` calls `if (!scheduler.offloadSideJob(job)) body.run()` (`offloadSideJob` returns false when it did not take the job — no runner, inline runner, a short job that is not deferred; a Mockito scheduler also returns false, so mocked tests and a hand-built `SchedulerService` run the body inline = today). TLS profile job: `scheduledTick` → `leaderYieldsRound("tls-profile", tickMs)` → side runner (fixed delay `tick-ms`) → `runWithSchedulerLock("tls-profile")` → `claimRound("tls-profile", tickMs)` → tick → `finishSideRound(name, completed)` (an interrupted / failed / shutdown-stopped tick is not completed: due again after its run lease); the tick waits for its probes in 200 ms slices (`TlsProfileJobService.STOP_POLL_MS`) and stops as soon as `SchedulerService.sideJobsStopping()` (= `stopping()`: ContextClosedEvent / `acquiringStopped()`) is set: unfinished probes are cancelled (interrupted), finished ones saved, no grade reconcile / drop notice (`TickResult.stopped`, last component, 4-arg constructor kept) — the runner is normally idle by `onShutdown` and the shared destroy budget stays with the notification drains (FX-SIDE #5); the drop notifier runs under the same lock on whichever pod ran the tick (DB-claimed since R2a); probing from a follower node needs the same egress as the cert sweeps (P1). Rolling deploy (20.167.0 + 3-1): a 3-1 leader sees a 20.167.0 follower's heartbeat (it has an `instance_id`) and defers; the old follower fires at T and wins the lock / round, the leader's deferred attempt finds the round taken (exception: old pods never claim `round:incident-action-reminder` — the leader re-runs the body; item claims stop a second mail, a muted day can get one extra `SKIPPED` row per owner, as with 20.167.0's late tick); a 20.167.0 pod never writes a completion stamp, so a 3-1 leader never yields `cert-stale` / the executive catch-up to it (both race as in 20.167.0); old pods never claim `round:tls-profile` → a 3-1 leader runs TLS; a 20.167.0 leader + 3-1 follower → up to one extra TLS tick per period during the rollout (the due set is `tls_profiles.probed_at` and the lock serialises — no endpoint probed twice in a row). Rollback: the `round:tls-profile` row stays unused. System Health → Küme card "Yan işler" (this pod). Gates: `PodLivenessTest`, `SideJobRunnerTest` (incl. `statusContractForSystemHealth`), `SideJobPlacementTest`, `SideJobPlacementMultiPodTest` (every cron job × 20 rounds: leader defers, follower claims, exactly one run; no follower / silent follower / yield off / a 20.167.0-like neighbour without the R3-1 beans; cert-stale, TLS, executive catch-up yield + fallback within 2 periods; cert inventory / executive monthly / data quality), `TlsProfileJobServiceTest.anyPodRoundAndLeaderYield`, `SchedulerMultiPodCoordinationTest.hourlySweepOnSideRunner_coalesced_otherPodSkips|shutdown_stopsSideJobsBeforeReleasingOwnLocks`, `ShutdownBudgetGateTest.sideJobsStopBeforeOwnLocksAreReleased`, `TechErrorReminderSideJobTest`, `IncidentActionReminderSideJobTest` (incl. `claimsTheCronRoundInsideTheLock`), `SideJobRunnerTest.deferredShortCronAttemptStartsOnTimeWhileThreeLongJobsRun|productionThreadFloor|submitAfterGap_holdsUntilFinishPlusGap|deferredHeavyAttemptRunsOnTheLongLane|stopAndWaitCoversBothLanes`, `SideJobPlacementMultiPodTest.tlsProfile_singlePodCadenceIsFinishPlusTick|staleSweep_singlePodCadenceIsFinishPlusInterval|staleSweep_followerFailsEveryPeriod_leaderClaimsWithinTwoPeriods|uncleanRestart_previousProcessOfSamePodIsNeitherLiveNorEvidence|incidentActionReminder_mutedMaintenance_oneRowPerOwnerPerDay|techErrorReminder_mutedMaintenance_oneRowPerTeamPerDay`, `PodLivenessTest.samePodNameOtherProcessNotCounted`, `TlsProfileJobServiceTest.tickReturnsWithinOneSecondOfShutdownFlag|scheduledTickIsAFixedDelaySideJob`.

- **Sessions (fixed 2026-10-09)**: one switch, `site.monitor.session.store` (env `SPRING_SESSION_STORE_TYPE`): `jdbc` in the `prod` profile → Spring Session JDBC (`spring_session` / `spring_session_attributes`, cookie `SESSION`); `memory` (default, local dev + tests; blank/`none` too) → Tomcat session, `JSESSIONID`. `config/SessionStoreAutoConfigurationFilter` (an `AutoConfigurationImportFilter` in `META-INF/spring.factories`) drops Boot's `org.springframework.boot.session.*` auto-configs unless the switch is `jdbc` — Boot 4 moved them into `spring-boot-session-jdbc` and has no on/off property, and `spring.session.store-type` no longer exists (that is why prod silently ran in-memory from the 3.3.6 → 4.1.0 upgrade on 2026-06-17 until this fix). `service/session/SessionStoreService` reports the REAL store (bean present?) to System Health (`session_store`) and does the startup marker cleanup: memory clears every non-TERMINATED `active_session_id` (unchanged), jdbc clears only markers with no live `spring_session` row. Session attribute VALUES must be JDK types (`java.lang` / `java.util` / `java.time` / `java.math`, type arguments too; no app class, record, enum, `Object` or raw collection): old and new pods deserialize the same JDBC session mid-rollout (multi-pod Sürüm 2b, MP-50). Gates: `SessionStoreRestartIntegrationTest` (login → restart on the same DB → same cookie still 200), `SessionStoreServiceJpaTest`, `SessionStoreAutoConfigurationFilterTest`, `SessionAttributeTypesGateTest` (javac type-attributed scan of every `HttpSession` / Spring Session `setAttribute`, `WebUtils.setSessionAttribute`, session-scoped `RequestAttributes.setAttribute`; self-test with a sample source).
- **Scheduler distributed lock (renewed since 2026-10-21, R1-A, MP-02)**: `scheduler_lock` rows are taken through `service/schedule/ClusterLockService` (owner = `SchedulerService.instanceId()`; tests pass their own owner + clock). Acquire keeps the old SQL (delete expired, then INSERT; the PK decides; `locked_until` stays the UTC `yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm:ss` text so old and new pods agree during a rollout). A daemon renewer (`cluster-lock-renewer`, every 30 s) runs `UPDATE … SET locked_until = now + TTL WHERE name = ? AND locked_by = me`; 0 rows → the hold is LOST (one WARN) and `held(name)` is false; a renewal DB error keeps the hold only until the last written `locked_until`. Hard cap: renewal stops after 3 h (`nightly-cleanup`, `ping-rollup-rebuild`: 4 h), so a hung job frees the lock one TTL later. Renewed TTL = min(requested, 3 min) (`SchedulerService.renewedTtl`; sweep locks stay 2 min; the watchdog uses `callWithSchedulerLock(name, Duration, task)` with 2 min). Table missing (`BadSqlGrammarException`) → legacy "acquired, not renewed"; any other DB error → not acquired (D2). `tryAcquireSchedulerLock` / `releaseSchedulerLock` / `callWithSchedulerLock` / `runWithSchedulerLock` delegate to it (field `lockService`, `@Autowired(required = false)`; null = the old SQL, byte-identical). Long jobs release the HOLD they took (`LockTicket`), never "by name" — a stale run's `finally` never deletes a newer run's lock (admin force-release + new run). **The cert sweep refuses to save or process results once its lock is lost** (`runCheckForDomains` → `lockStillHeld` before `saveResult`). `forceReleaseLock` also forgets this pod's hold. Own job locks are released in `onShutdown` (after the scheduler stopped, `releaseAllOwn`). The stale cert sweep (`checkStaleInventory`, every pod every 5 min, NOT leader-gated) is gated by the two-phase round `round:cert-stale`; the hourly sweep takes `cert-check` FIRST (retry every 5 s ≤ 60 s) and only then claims `round:cert-hourly` — a lock held by another pod's stale sweep no longer consumes the hour. Startup catch-up runs under `round:startup-catchup` (15 min). Admin can still force-release `cert-check` via `DELETE /api/admin/system/scheduler-lock`. System Health `scheduler.held_locks` lists this pod's holds. Gates: `ClusterLockServiceH2Test`, `ClusterLockPostgresTest`, `SchedulerMultiPodCoordinationTest`.
- **Cluster schedule + sweep leader (2026-10-09; fail-closed / early release / wedge yield 2026-10-21)**: `service/schedule/ClusterScheduleService` keeps per-monitor due times (`monitor_check_schedule`, key `<type>:<id>`, claim = CAS UPDATE on `next_due_at`), round claims (`round:<name>`) and the sweep leader lease (`lease:sweep-leader`, `site.monitor.scheduler.sweep-leader-ttl-ms` 180 s). Every scheduled sweep that feeds `MonitoringOutageService` starts with `noteSweepTick()` and then `if (!isSweepLeader()) return;` — a new monitoring sweep must add both (alarm decisions run on ONE pod; its in-memory confirmation state is reset on every leadership change, see below). Manual "Check now" never goes through the schedule. **Lease renewal is independent of the sweep threads (2026-10-09):** `startSweepLeaderRenewer` (at boot, only with the cluster store) renews the lease on its own daemon thread every `ttl/3` (`renewSweepLeadership`), so 54 `@Scheduled` jobs filling the 8-thread pool for longer than the TTL no longer hand leadership over; a follower's renewer takes an expired lease. It also installs `MonitoringOutageService.setScheduledResultsFence(this::isSweepLeader)`: SCHEDULED results (and `handleDnsSweep`, `requeueDnsChanges`) from a pod that lost the lease are dropped (manual checks pass; **a failing fence is CLOSED** since 2026-10-21 — a pod that cannot confirm its leadership never writes alarm state). Single pod: identical behaviour.
  - **Leadership change event (2026-10-21, P0):** `SchedulerService` publishes `service/schedule/SweepLeadershipChangedEvent(leader, owner, atMs, reason)` through its `eventPublisher` exactly once per transition — `GAINED` / `LOST` from `refreshSweepLeader` (sweep threads and the renewer), `RELEASED` from `releaseSweepLeadership` only when this pod held the lease; steady renewals, followers and the missing-table local fallback publish nothing; a transient lease error publishes `LOST` only when the leader's confirmed window lapses, and a wedge yield publishes `LOST`. It is dispatched synchronously while the leadership monitor is held (ordered per pod; listeners must be quick, a listener exception is caught + WARN and never changes the decision). A refresh racing shutdown never re-takes a released lease. Gate: `SweepLeadershipEventTest`.
  - **Fail closed (R1-A, MP-11):** only a MISSING table (`BadSqlGrammarException`) keeps the legacy local fallback (lease → every pod leader; `clusterDue` → `localDue`). A transient DB error: the holder stays leader only until `lastConfirmedUntil − 30 s` (`LEASE_SAFETY_MARGIN_MS`), then stops and publishes `LOST`; a follower never self-promotes; a monitor due key decides locally only inside that confirmed leader window, otherwise false; a fixed-period `round:` key is retried after 250 ms and 1 s, then the round is SKIPPED with a WARN and counted (System Health `scheduler.round_claim_failures` / `last_round_claim_failure`) — never "first sight → due" (that sent a second weekly report). **Cron rounds (review 2026-10-22, CORE-2):** a cron round's claim error no longer loses a weekly / daily trigger. Jobs whose work is item-claimed run anyway with a WARN (`cronRoundDue(…, claimProtected = true)`: `weekly-availability` — team × week claim `uk_wal_team_week`; `ca-pin-refresh` — rotates only expiring pins). All other cron rounds (`cert-hourly`, `nightly-cleanup`, `domain-expiry-refresh`, `weekly-report-reminder`, `issue-report-digest`, `domain-critical-sweep`) retry the claim within the 60 s cron slack (`cronRoundRetryDelaysMs` 0.25 / 1 / 4 / 10 / 15 s) and, if it still fails, are skipped with a trace: ERROR log + System Health `scheduler.skipped_rounds` (last 20, per pod) + `round_claim_failures`. `skipped_rounds` stays per-pod memory on purpose: the skip happens BECAUSE the database is failing, so the ERROR line (shipped from every pod) is the durable cross-pod trace. **The round-claim retry stops at shutdown (round 3, R4):** after each retry sleep the loop checks `stopping()` (`shutdownStarted` or `ClusterLockService.acquiringStopped()`) and gives up, and `cronRoundDue` checks it once more before returning "run" (also on the claim-protected "run anyway" path) — a dying pod never starts the Friday reminder / digest / cleanup after the ~30 s retry. A round claimed right before the shutdown gate is NOT run on that pod: weekly availability is rescued by its catch-up, every other cron job loses that one trigger (logged at INFO). **Weekly availability catch-up (F5):** `scheduledWeeklyAvailabilityCatchUp` (cron `site.monitor.weekly-availability.catchup-cron`, default `0 25 * * * *` Istanbul) runs only on the weekly trigger's Istanbul day, from 20 min after the trigger to midnight, under the same `weekly-availability` lock (skips while the main run holds it) and its own round `round:weekly-availability-catchup` (50 min); it calls `WeeklyAvailabilityReportService.sendMissingWeeklyReports()`, which mails only teams with NO `weekly_availability_log` row for the week (SENT / FAILED / SENDING rows are left alone; the team × week claim keeps two racing runs to one mail). `lastConfirmedSweepLeader()` = the last SUCCESSFUL lease read (DB errors never change it); `sweepLeaseObservation()` = when the lease was last read, by whom held and until when. The observation is ONE pair (lease `until`, the moment that value was read): written by the leader's own successful renewal and by the follower's renewer read of the row — never by a follower's ordinary `isSweepLeader()` refresh (CORE-1: that moved only the timestamp, so a healthy lease looked stale to the watchdog fallback and a follower announced "DB down" next to the live leader).
  - **Two-phase rounds (R1-A, MP-15):** `claimRound(name, periodMs)` advances the row to `now + runLease` (`runLeaseFor`: daily rounds ≤ 30 min, others ≤ 10 min, never longer than the period); `completeRound(name)` CASes it to the grid due only if the body returned normally and (leader-gated rounds) the pod is still leader; an aborted round is due again after runLease — renewal of the round's job lock keeps a live owner from being doubled. Used by uptime, page-crawl, http-ssl-domain, keyword-ssl-domain and cert-stale. Public API for R2/R3: `claimRound`, `claimCronRound(name, cron, zone)` (single phase, = `cronRoundDue`), `completeRound`; `ClusterScheduleService.claimRun` / `complete`.
  - **Early release, no new work after `ContextClosedEvent` (R1-A, MP-08):** `onContextClosed` (`@EventListener` + `@Order(HIGHEST_PRECEDENCE)` — the FIRST listener, so `NotifyExecutorDrain`'s ≤ 20 s wait never delays the handover; only this context's event) stops the startup check and the renewer and releases the lease (`RELEASED`); `@PreDestroy onShutdown` stays the idempotent fallback and releases own job locks. From that moment the pod takes NO new work: `ClusterLockService.stopAcquiring()`, `acquireLock` / `claimRound` / `roundDue` / `cronRoundDue` / `isSweepLeader` all refuse (Spring keeps firing `@Scheduled` ticks for ≈ 40 s during shutdown); a running job finishes its current step and its lock keeps being renewed until `onShutdown`. Handover: the follower takes over on its next lease check (≤ 5 s recheck + next 30 s sweep tick ≈ 35 s).
  - **Wedged leader (R1-A, MP-24):** every one of the 14 monitoring sweep entry methods calls `noteSweepTick()` as its FIRST statement (before the "paused" and leader gates, so a paused type is not a wedge). The renewer: a leader with no tick for 10 min (`WEDGE_YIELD_MS`, counted from max(last tick, leadership start)) releases the lease (`LOST`) and does not re-acquire for 2 × TTL nor until ticks resume; 30 min with no tick after such a yield (`WEDGE_LIVENESS_MS`) → `AvailabilityChangeEvent(LivenessState.BROKEN)` once (kubelet restarts the pod), `CORRECT` when ticks resume. A pod that never ticked (no scheduler) is never treated as wedged. System Health `sweep_leader.last_sweep_tick` / `wedge_yielded_at`.
  - **Bootstrap (R1-A, MP-20):** admin bootstrap, public-id backfill, session markers, history backfill, permission seeds (3), incident options and the default threshold run in `runBootstrapSeeding()` under the `SMSCHEMA` advisory lock (fresh `SchemaPatchRunner.runExclusive`; JVM monitor on H2), each step in its own try/catch (`bootstrapStep`); deployment history, schema registry, orphan warning, stale-lock cleanup, renewer start and outage restore are guarded steps too and always run.
  - **Hostname (R1-A, MP-21):** `resolveHostname` = machine name → `HOSTNAME` → `POD_NAME` → `host-<random>` (never the constant `node`; `localhost` / `node` are ignored; `%` / `_` removed) — boot cleanup can no longer delete live pods' locks and lease.
  - **Leadership change = restart (R1-B, MP-12):** `MonitoringOutageService.onSweepLeadershipChanged` (`@EventListener` on `SweepLeadershipChangedEvent`, every reason) calls `resetVolatileState`: recovery counters + last-counted stamps, `downObserved`, `scheduledRoundSeen`, `suppressionActive`, `outageReconciled` are cleared; running confirmation chains drop out at their next step (`confirmEpoch` + per-key `chainOwner` id — a late step of an old chain never releases a new chain's `inFlight` key) and recovery chains through the cleared `recoveryGeneration` (no thread is killed). A scheduled chain asks the fence again right before its final step (`finishConfirmed` before `processConfirmedOutage`, `runRecoveryAttempt` before `closeAlarm`) — a pod that lost the lease writes no decision; a recovery chain started by a MANUAL check is exempt. Never reintroduce per-pod alarm memory that survives a leadership change. Gate: `LeadershipChangeResetTest` (real lease flip A → B → A: the first healthy result after regaining does not close).
  - **Manual "Check now" on a follower (R1-B, MP-14):** every `evaluate*Now` (9 types incl. `evaluateDomainAlarmsNow`) starts with `forwardManualToLeader(type, id, raw)`: on a follower (cluster store present, `!isSweepLeader()` AND the current sweep leader advertises that it drains the inbox) the raw result goes to `monitor_eval_inbox` (`service/monitoring/ManualEvalInbox`, JSON, `manual=true`) and is NOT evaluated locally (the follower's memory is empty — the sibling rule was bypassed and the leader's recovery counter never reset); if the insert fails it is only logged. The leader's `drainManualEvalInbox` (`@Scheduled` 5 s, `site.monitor.manual-eval.drain-ms`) claims rows with a conditional UPDATE (PostgreSQL: one `UPDATE … WHERE id IN (… FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED) RETURNING`, H2: CAS per row; 2 min lease), deletes each row CONDITIONALLY (`take`: owner + lease value) right before evaluating (exactly once — a re-claimed row's old owner cannot process it; a pod dying between take and evaluation loses that one observation), drops rows older than 10 min and rows of missing / paused monitors, and calls the same `evaluate*Now` (self-invocation → synchronous; it re-checks leadership and re-forwards if the lease moved). **Drainer advertisement (review 2026-10-22, CORE-3 / B1) — capability, not freshness:** the leader writes the row `lease:eval-inbox-drainer` (`monitor_check_schedule`, permanent name `SchedulerService.EVAL_INBOX_DRAINER`) UNCONDITIONALLY in its own name (`ManualEvalInbox.advertiseDrainer` — UPDATE, else INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING; it never waits for a dead previous leader's row) at the start of every drain tick and between batches of a long drain; a pod that lost leadership releases its own row on its next tick. A follower forwards only when the advertisement's owner EQUALS the sweep-leader lease owner (`lease:sweep-leader`) — a busy shared scheduler pool that delays drain ticks no longer turns forwarding off. No row / another owner (a 20.164.0 leader never writes it; a new leader before its first tick, ≤ 5 s) / unreadable → LOCAL evaluation exactly as 20.164.0 (manual rule: reconcile only). The row's `until` (now + 10 min, `EVAL_INBOX_DRAINER_TTL_MS`) is only a safety net: a leader that ran no drain tick for 10 min makes followers evaluate locally. No forwarding window remains for an old leader (owner mismatch at once). Semantics are today's manual rule (reconcile only, never opens an alarm). The leader, a single pod and a setup without the cluster store evaluate locally exactly as before. A new monitor type's `evaluate*Now` must start with the same call and get a case in `dispatchManualEval` + a `ManualEvalInbox.TYPE_*` constant (names permanent). Gates: `ManualEvalForwardingTest`, `ManualEvalRoundTripTest` (JSON round trip of all 9 raw results gives identical sweep items), `ManualEvalInboxTest`, `AlarmStatePostgresTest` (postgres-it, SKIP LOCKED), `ManualEvalForwardingTest.oldLeaderWithoutHeartbeat_followerEvaluatesLocally|busyPool_noDrainFor40s_sameLeader_stillForwarded|drainerSilentBeyondSafetyNet_followerEvaluatesLocally|deadLeader_newLeaderForwardedAfterFirstTick|exLeaderRetiresHeartbeat_newLeaderTakesOver`.
  - Gates: `SchedulerClusterScheduleTest` (renewer / fence cases), `MonitoringOutageServiceTest.scheduledResultsFence_*`, `SchedulerMultiPodCoordinationTest` (lock renewal vs stale sweep, lock lost mid-run, holder dies, shutdown releases own locks, leader / follower transient errors, round-claim failure, monitor due window, interrupted round, hourly lock-first, stale round, catch-up round, ContextClosedEvent early release + no new work, wedge yield + liveness, saturation never yields), `SchedulerMultiPodCoordinationTest.cronRoundClaimError_claimProtectedJobRunsAnyway|cronRoundClaimTransientError_retriedWithinSlack|cronRoundClaimPersistentError_skippedWithTrace|weeklyAvailabilityCatchUp_window_andOncePerHour|shutdownDuringRoundClaimRetry_noNewJob`, `WeeklyAvailabilityClaimTest.catchUpSendsOnlyToTeamsWithoutARow`, `ClusterScheduleRoundRunH2Test`, `ClusterSchedulePostgresTest.claimRunAndComplete_onPostgres`, `BootstrapConcurrencyTest`, `HostnameFallbackTest`, `ShutdownBudgetGateTest.leaseIsReleasedBeforeTheNotifyDrain`.
- **Durable evaluation state — dual-write, behaviour unchanged (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 3-1, plan 3a).** The sweep leader mirrors `MonitoringOutageService` evaluation memory into four raw-DDL tables (`service/evalstate/EvalStateSql`, created by `EvalStateSchemaPatch` at ready; names PERMANENT): `monitor_eval_state` (per `type:domain`: recovery counter, last counted, active recovery chain generation / start; a reset is written as 0 / null, row kept), `monitor_last_status` (last result per monitor item, key `m:<monitor_id>` or `d:<domain>|<detail>`, UP rows and id-less items too: up, network-class down, `error_class` NETWORK / DNS / CONFIG / OTHER — never the error text —, ownership ids, source S / M), `monitor_eval_type_state` (per alert type: suppression flag + since, last scheduled round, round interval; plus the `'*'` generation / writer heartbeat row), `monitor_confirmations` (running confirmation chains + the descriptor 3-2 needs to resume one: `monitor_type` = `RecheckRefs.monitorType(alertType)`, `monitor_id`, `ctx_json` ≤ 16 KB; state RUNNING / FINISHING / ABANDONED; never log `ctx_json` / `attempts_json`). Every row carries `writer`, `gen`, `seq`, `updated_ms`, `updated_at`. **In 3-1 no decision reads these tables (invariant I-1):** with the recorder absent, NOOP, failing or the tables missing every alarm / notification decision is identical; the per-round suppression window is not persisted (3-2 would seed it from `monitor_last_status`).
  - **Recorder (`EvalStateRecorder`, one per pod, implements `EvalStateSink`).** Mutation points only ENQUEUE (no DB on sweep / request threads, never throws, never waits): coalesced per key (highest `seq`; the seq is taken before the value is read from `EvalStateSource` — for an item with a `monitor_id` the last status too: `downObservation` → memory DOWN = that DOWN row (domain, detail, ownership, class, time from memory), else UP, so a late stale DOWN hook never overwrites a newer UP), a value equal to the last written tuple is dropped, last status is written on change (domain included — a rename is a state change) or every `site.monitor.eval-state.last-status-refresh-ms` (120000); a DOWN observation MOS prunes without a new result (`pruneObservations` / `downSibling`: 26 h TTL or no longer monitored) deletes its row (`EvalStateSink.observationPruned`, `SQL_LAST_STATUS_DELETE` guarded "newer (gen, seq) wins") — or, when memory holds a DOWN entry again, writes that DOWN row, cap `MAX_PENDING` 50 000 (+ dirty keys that VERIFY skips). One daemon writer `eval-state-writer` flushes every `site.monitor.eval-state.flush-ms` (1000) or at once on `sweepEnd`: one JDBC batch per table, autocommit, never inside a caller transaction. Only the sweep leader writes (enqueue gate `SchedulerService.sweepLeaderHint()`), under a DB FENCING TOKEN: a generation CAS'd on the `'*'` row (never a clock; a missing `'*'` row is seeded with the largest `gen` of the four tables — `EvalStateSql.maxExistingGeneration`, 0 on an empty database —, so a new generation is above every existing row), taken right after GAINED; with the cluster store the CAS is bound to the sweep-leader LEASE in the same statement (`SQL_GEN_CAS_LEASE`: `… AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM monitor_check_schedule WHERE monitor_key = 'lease:sweep-leader' AND claimed_by = me)`, owner = `ClusterScheduleService.owner()` through an `ObjectProvider`; no bean → unbound CAS — a pod that lost the lease can never take a generation, so a stale former leader cannot fence the real one; a 0-row CAS with an unchanged `gen` returns at once) and hint + leadership epoch + mode (+ not closing / fenced) are re-checked before every CAS try; a heartbeat UPDATE (`WHERE gen = mine AND writer = me`) at every flush and every 60 s idle — 0 rows = a newer leader took a generation: this pod drops pending and writes nothing (`Status.fenced`) until it leads again (GAINED) or, while its hint stays true, the `'*'` row's holder has been silent > 180 s (`HOLDER_SILENT_MS` = 3 heartbeats, checked every 60 s; row missing counts as silent) — then it re-acquires through the lease-bound CAS (self-heal, INFO `… çitten çıkıp yeni kuşak alacak`, no ping-pong); every upsert is guarded "newer (gen, seq) wins" (PostgreSQL `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE … WHERE`, H2 / unknown dialect conditional UPDATE → `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`); a chain row is deleted only with its own `key_hash + chain_id + writer` and `gen ≤ mine`. EVERY leadership event (GAINED too) clears pending and the generation, like MOS resets its memory (listener order: MOS `@Order(LEADERSHIP_LISTENER_ORDER = LOWEST_PRECEDENCE − 1000)` resets first, the recorder `@Order(LOWEST_PRECEDENCE)` clears after; the only other listener, `SweepLeadershipCounter`, is metrics only); every new generation marks older chains ABANDONED; the first one after GAINED counts the INHERITED rows once (only the rows of the generation its CAS replaced — `previous_generation` = gen − 1, `EvalStateSql.previousGeneration`, older generations' stale rows are never counted: recovery counters > 0, DOWN siblings within the 26 h TTL, active suppressions, abandoned chains — INFO line + System Health; the evidence of what 3-2 would carry over a handover). Failures: `writeFailures` + one WARN per minute (`Kalıcı değerlendirme durumu yazılamadı — karar etkilenmez (bellek kaynak)`), backoff 1 → 30 s, ops stay pending (coalesced, capped); tables missing (first seconds of the first boot) → pending KEPT, existence rechecked every 60 s. Every recorder / verifier statement runs on its own `JdbcTemplate` (same DataSource, `EvalStateSql.withQueryTimeout`) with a 15 s query timeout (`EvalStateSql.QUERY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) — a lock wait never stalls the single writer for the pool's 30 min socket timeout (the shared template is untouched). Daily prune on the writer thread (first 10 min after the generation; last status `observed_ms` older than 7 d; recovery rows not updated for 7 d that are zero and chain-less OR of an older generation, whatever the count; chains ABANDONED or older than 1 h; type rows of an older generation not updated for 7 d — never the `'*'` row). Shutdown: `ContextClosedEvent` sets a flag only — NO final flush (losing the last second is harmless in 3-1, nothing reads it; 3-2 must flush BEFORE `onContextClosed` releases the lease); `@PreDestroy` joins the writer for `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(1000)`.
  - **Mode** `site.monitor.eval-state.mode` = `off | write | verify` (`EvalStateMode`, ENUM, group `monitoring` — Settings → Genel → İzleme Alarmları, GLOBAL_ONLY, live through the 10 s settings refresh, default `verify`, env `EVAL_STATE_MODE`): `off` = kill switch for the DB load (no enqueue, no writer work; the gap is remembered — keys not re-enqueued since are skipped by VERIFY — and the GENERATION IS DROPPED, also when nothing was pending: back on, a new generation is taken, VERIFY starts it with a resync round, pre-gap rows are never compared; a `MAX_SEEN` map overflow does the same), `write` = dual-write only, `verify` = write + VERIFY; an unrecognized value (`EvalStateMode.unrecognized`) logs ONE WARN per distinct value (`Kalıcı değerlendirme durumu kipi tanınmadı …`) and runs as `verify`. Boot-time keys (no catalogue entry): `site.monitor.eval-state.flush-ms` (1000), `…verify-ms` (300000), `…last-status-refresh-ms` (120000).
  - **MOS hooks.** Every mutation of MOS evaluation memory is followed (never preceded) by an enqueue-only hook (`note*` helpers; every exception swallowed and counted in `evalStateHookFailures` — visible as `EvalStateSink.Status.hookFailures` (+ the recorder's own enqueue exceptions)): recovery counter / last counted / active-chain generation (only when something really changed), every item's last observation (slim copy, id-less items too; a confirmation recheck that comes back UP is an UP observation; a DOWN observation pruned without a new result — `pruneObservations` / `downSibling`, after a successful `remove` — is `observationPruned`), type flags (every scheduled round — the recorder re-stamps it at most every 60 s —, suppression flip, round interval change > 10 %), confirmation chain start / progress / finishing / end, and a flush hint at the end of every sweep call (`handleSweepResults` / `handleDnsSweep` / `requeueDnsChanges` delegate to private bodies, the hint runs in `finally`). The sink is bound once at `ContextRefreshedEvent` through an `ObjectProvider` (no injection cycle with `SchedulerService`; `setEvalStateSink` for hand-built tests). `isOutageClass` delegates to `classifyError` (also the recorder's `error_class`); the sweep's suppression rule is `suppressDecision` (also VERIFY's window seed). Read-only VERIFY API: `verifySnapshot()` (immutable `EvalSnapshot`), `peekWindowSample` (writes no mark / tick), `replayEvalState()`, `downObservation(type, monitorId)` (memory DOWN entry or `DownValues.ABSENT`; `EvalStateSource` default null = caller's values), `stillMonitoredOrNull` (predicate exception → null, no predicate → true), `hookFailures()`. `EvalStateSink.Status` = 16 components (… `lastVerify`, `boolean fenced`, `long hookFailures`; the old 14-arg constructor kept = false / 0; `EvalStateContractsTest.fixStateContracts`).
  - **VERIFY (`EvalStateVerifier`, the evidence for 3-2).** On the writer thread every `verify-ms` while this pod leads with a generation and mode = `verify`: `s0 = seq` → `verifySnapshot()` → `s1` → await the flush of `s1` (≤ 5 s) → this generation's rows (`WHERE gen = ? AND writer = ?`) → compare; keys enqueued after `s0`, dirty, or not re-enqueued since an OFF gap are skipped. `EvalStateVerification.Context.stillCurrent()` (recorder: leadership epoch unchanged, same generation, not fenced) is asked after the flush wait and after the comparisons: a leadership event, a dropped generation or a fence during the round → `unknown` reason `leadership_changed` (`EvalVerifyReport.REASON_LEADERSHIP_CHANGED`), and a pod that is no longer the generation holder never publishes its report (a context that throws counts as not current). Every pass / fail kind compared 0 while keys were skipped → `unknown` reason `nothing_compared` (`REASON_NOTHING_COMPARED`; kinds keep the skipped counts, first keys empty; never green). A failing "still monitored?" lookup (`MonitoringOutageService.stillMonitoredOrNull` → null) skips the key instead of counting a mismatch (the decision path's fail-open `stillMonitored` is unchanged). Kinds (`EvalVerifyReport.KINDS`): `recovery`, `sibling`, `suppression`, `round_seen`, `confirmation` (pass / fail, `PASS_FAIL_KINDS`), `descriptor` (RUNNING rows 3-2 could not resume) and `window_seed` (would a window seeded from `monitor_last_status` change the suppression decision; `max_delta`) — MEASURED (`MEASURED_KINDS`), never red. The first round of a generation and a round after a gap > 3 × `verify-ms` are RESYNC rounds (`unknown`, reason `resync`, `EvalStateVerifier.REASON_RESYNC`: memory re-enqueued through `replayEvalState`, nothing compared). Unreadable tables (`read_failed`), flush lag (`flush_lag`), no generation (`no_generation`), snapshot failure (`snapshot_failed`), verifier exception (`verifier_error`, `EvalStateRecorder.REASON_VERIFIER_ERROR`) = `unknown`, never "0 mismatches". The report (also unknown) is published as cluster observation **`eval-state-verify`** (`ClusterHealthStore.OBS_EVAL_STATE_VERIFY`, permanent; SNAKE_CASE JSON of `EvalVerifyReport`, hashed keys only, always valid and ≤ `ClusterHealthStore.PAYLOAD_MAX` 4000 — if it ever did not fit, the first keys and then the kinds are dropped with reason `payload_too_large`); mismatch > 0 → one WARN per round (`Kalıcı değerlendirme doğrulaması: N uyuşmazlık — … karar etkilenmez, bellek kaynak`). The Küme card translates every reason (TR + EN `hmp.esReason.*`; gate `clusterRuntimeModel.test.js` "doğrulama nedenleri sunucu sabitleriyle eşleşir" reads the Java `REASON_*` constants + the `payload_too_large` literal; equality both ways — `EVAL_UNKNOWN_REASONS` lists exactly the server reasons, incl. `leadership_changed|nothing_compared`).
  - **3-2 go criterion (proposal, RUNBOOK "Çok pod"):** ≥ 7 days of prod VERIFY on 2 pods with `recovery`, `sibling`, `suppression`, `round_seen`, `confirmation` mismatches = 0 (unknown rounds excluded), write failures < 0.1 % of flushes, `descriptor` unresolvable = 0 outside deleted monitors, `window_seed` decision divergence < 1 % of rounds — and at least one real leadership change observed with non-zero inherited counts. Left for 3-2: load the rows at GAINED when the `'*'` row shows continuity (`writer` = previous lease owner, `updated_ms` fresh), flush before the lease release, resume or keep dropping RUNNING chains (`RecheckRegistry` over the `recheck*` methods), decide the window seed / D-6 replacement from the VERIFY evidence; an OFF gap / map overflow now drops the generation, so pre-gap rows are never in the current one; 3-2 loads ONLY rows with `gen = '*'.gen` — never `gen < myGen` (a lost leader's in-flight flush can still land rows of its OLD generation after the new leader's CAS; the `(gen, seq)` guard keeps them off newer rows, not out of the table); with the lease-bound CAS, `'*'.writer` is always a pod that held the lease when it took the generation; `window_seed` evidence is inflated by design (manual `M` rows, recheck-UP rows, other generations' recent rows), and pruned DOWN observations no longer leave rows (a seed from `monitor_last_status` matches memory).
  - **Rules.** A NEW MOS state field needs a hook (after the mutation), an `EvalStateSource` value / `EvalStateSink` method and a VERIFY kind (`EvalVerifyReport.KINDS` + TR / EN `hmp.esKind.*`). A new monitor alert type needs a `RecheckRefs.monitorType` mapping (gate `RecheckRefsCoverageTest` — every `EscalationService.MONITORING_ALERT_TYPES` member; mirrors `SchedulerService.isStillMonitored`'s prefixes; values = `ManualEvalInbox.TYPE_*` + `uptime`, permanent — the "add everywhere `HTTP_SLOW` appears" rule). `SchedulerService.sweepLeaderHint()` (`shutdownStarted` → false; no cluster store → true; else `sweepLeaderHeld && !sweepLeaderStopped`; the missing-lease-table legacy fallback reads false) and `sweepLeaderSinceMs()` (last GAINED, -1 = never) are DB-FREE HINTS for the recorder, the side-job placement and the exporter — an alarm decision still goes through `isSweepLeader()` + the scheduled-results fence.
  - **Rolling deploy / rollback.** A 20.167.0 leader + 3-1 followers: nobody writes (followers fail the hint). A 3-1 leader + old followers: the leader writes and verifies. Leadership 3-1 → old → 3-1: the second 3-1 leader takes a NEW generation; the first one's rows are counted as inherited, its chains ABANDONED; the `'*'` row's `updated_ms` stopped while the old pod led (exactly what 3-2's continuity check reads). Rollback: the tables stay unused (no FK, no trigger; old code reads only `rdap-source` from `cluster_observations`); the settings key stays, ignored. Retention: `RetentionCatalog` `monitor-eval-state`, `monitor-last-status`, `monitor-eval-type-state`, `monitor-confirmations` (EXTERNAL, OPERATIONAL — self-pruned by the recorder; `RetentionCoverageTest.RAW_DDL_TABLES`). Gates: `EvalStateSqlH2Test`, `EvalStatePostgresTest` (postgres-it: real `ON CONFLICT … WHERE`, 2 writers × 1000 keys, 8-thread generation CAS, fillfactor), `EvalStateSchemaPatchTest`, `EvalStateRecorderTest` (incl. `enqueueDoesNoJdbcOnCallerThread|activeExactlyWhileWritingAsGenerationHolder`), `RecheckRefsCoverageTest`, `EvalStateContractsTest`, `SweepLeaderHintTest`, `MonitoringOutageDualWriteTest` (incl. `realRecorderTuplesOnH2`), `MonitoringOutageDualWriteDecisionTest` (15 representative scenarios incl. the prune hooks `PRUNE_HOOKS_SIBLING_AND_PAUSED` × no / recording / H2-mirror / throwing recorder, real recorder on H2, real recorder whose JDBC always fails → identical interaction transcripts of every MOS dependency), `EvalStateVerifierTest` (incl. `realRecorder_zeroMismatches|worstCasePayloadFits`), `EvalStateMultiPodTest`, `EvalStateWiringTest`, `LeadershipChangeResetTest.regainedLeader_withThrowingRecorder_sameSemantics|reset_withRecorderAttached_noHooksSourceEmpty`; review fixes (FX-STATE): `EvalStateSqlH2Test.leaseBoundCas_onlyTheLeaseHolderTakesAGeneration|reseedStartsAboveExistingGenerations|inheritedCountsOnlyThePreviousGeneration|pruneOldGenerationsRegardlessOfCount|lastStatusDeleteIsGuarded`, `EvalStateRecorderTest.staleFormerLeaderLateAcquire_cannotFenceLeaseHolder|fencedLeaderRecoversWhenHolderSilent|modeOffOnThenReset_noStaleRowCompared|modeOffWithoutStateStillDropsGeneration|staleOldGenerationCounterNotInheritedAndPruned|productionRecorderUsesQueryTimeout|everyStatementCarriesTheQueryTimeout|observationFollowsMemoryAfterSeq|prunedObservationDeletesRow|domainChangeIsAStateChange|statusCarriesFencedAndHookFailures|unknownModeWarnsOnce`, `EvalStateVerifierTest.leadershipLostDuringVerifyRound|fencedDuringVerifyRound|pausedThenResumedDownMonitor|downMonitorHostRenamedWithinRefresh|modeToggleThenReset_verifyStaysClean|stillMonitoredLookupFails_skippedNotMismatch|nothingCompared_isUnknown|verifierReadsUseQueryTimeout`, `EvalStateMultiPodTest.staleFormerLeaderCannotFenceLeaseHolder|leadershipListenerOrder`, `EvalStateWiringTest.leadershipListenersDispatchMosFirst|leaseKeyMatchesScheduler`, `EvalStateSchemaPatchTest.indexRaceLost_noWarnWhenIndexExists`, `MonitoringOutageDualWriteTest.pruneObservations_enqueuesPrunedObservation|downSiblingRemoval_enqueuesPrunedObservation|downObservation_readsMemory|stillMonitoredOrNull_triState|hookFailuresExposedThroughSource`, postgres-it `EvalStatePostgresTest.leaseBoundCas_notTheHolderGetsNothing|lockWaitIsCutByQueryTimeout`. Rolling deploy of the fixes: pre-fix 3-1 builds existed only on the integration branch / staging — a pre-fix pod's unbound CAS could still fence a fixed leader, which heals once the pre-fix holder has been silent 180 s.
- **Shared state is claimed before the side effect (2026-10-21, R1-B / C1 / C2 / E).** A sender acting on a shared row claims it with a conditional UPDATE (or insert-first under a unique index) BEFORE the mail / push / webhook, never `save`s a whole entity read before the claim, and every claim has a final state for a dead pod (stale SENDING → UNKNOWN without resend, or re-run only when idempotent). Where it applies (details in the named bullets): durable DNS / domain `change_state` ("Escalation & notifications"); cert network-outage state in the DB ("The check pipeline → Bulk network outage detection"); single monitoring-suppression rows ("Other services → `MonitoringOutageService`"); storm close claim + lock renewal and NOC re-claim (storm lifetime bullet); push lease per batch + event-less unique index ("Push outbox is claimed"); cert alarm open lock + re-alert claims, the `resolution_notify` marker + `ResolutionNotifySweeper`, SMTP 421 orphans ("Escalation & notifications"); failed-login scan gate ("Other services → `AuditService`"); system-maintenance claims ("Auth → System maintenance → Catch-up"); weekly report transitions and the weekly availability mail ("Other services → Weekly reports"). Multi-pod Sürüm 2a (2026-10-22/23): R2a-D2 — domain-expiry reminders (`SENDING` row first, `ux_der_mon_exp_thr` — "Other services → Domain registration monitoring"); silent alarm closes of disabled types and of import deactivation (`SilentAlarmCloser`, conditional UPDATE — "Escalation & notifications → Auto-close"); quiet-digest claims (stale `SENDING:` → `FAILED` + trace — "Quiet hours"); page usage (`ON CONFLICT` upsert — "Other services → Page usage …"). R2a-D1 — tech-error sends over a live queue (`claimSendUnlessQueued`) and the two-phase reminder claim (`inflight_at`) ("Channel technical error reports"); the executive summary's "sending started" marker before the first chunk, stale SENDING settled from marker + traces, conditional finish ("Monthly executive summary → Delivery"); the TLS drop notice per team ("TLS configuration grade → Drop notification"). Multi-pod Sürüm 2b: R2b-F — every `EscalationService` alarm close (silent, inventory, manual / automatic after the CAS) writes only the close columns conditionally (`SilentAlarmCloser`), and every other write of an existing alarm (ack, post-send bookkeeping, re-key, team move) writes only its own columns (`AlertEventFieldWriter`) — "Escalation & notifications → Auto-close"; R2b-C2 — the deploy notice is claimed in `schema_patch_markers` before it is published ("Sürüm & Dağıtım geçmişi").
- **Cross-pod cache invalidation (2026-10-22, multi-pod R2a-A, MP-30).** Every cache eviction used to be local: the other pod served stale data for the TTL (cert dashboard 300 s, card extras 60 s, on-call 30 s — the leader alarmed the PREVIOUS on-call person —, team access grants / 7/24 operator teams 30 s — a revoked grant still edited on the other pod —, maintenance windows 30 s — cert check rows got a wrong `maintenance` stamp —, the passive / deleted-account gate 5 s). `service/cache/CacheBus` + table `cache_bus(name PK, generation BIGINT, updated_at VARCHAR(30))` (raw DDL `CacheBus.DDL`, created by `CacheBusSchemaPatch` at ready (existence = an unqualified read of the table, same schema resolution as the bus; a lost `CREATE` race between two booting pods is tolerated without WARN; no `SMSCHEMA` lock on purpose — it would block boot up to 60 s while the other pod patches); NOT a `monitor_check_schedule` key — a 20.165.x pod's `ClusterScheduleService.prune` deletes unknown keys). One row per name plus `_global`, bumped with every publish (the one number the client carries). Names are permanent (`CacheBusNames`; Spring caches use their cache name).
  - **Publish (`bump(names…)`, never throws):** inside a transaction → at `afterCompletion` (NOT on rollback; `afterCompletion`, not `afterCommit`, so a bump requested from another synchronization's `afterCommit` — `TechErrorReportService.analyticsChanged` — is still delivered); on a request thread → collected per request; right before the response body is written (`CacheBusResponseAdvice`) the batch is handed to the bus thread and the request waits at most 250 ms (`REQUEST_FLUSH_WAIT_MS`) for the new global generation — the response carries `X-Cache-Gen` when it arrived, otherwise no header (the client then behaves as before the bus; the publish still happens). The request thread never touches the database. A batch pending > 2 s (`REQUEST_HANDOFF_MS`, long requests — also without a later bump: the bus thread checks open batches every 500 ms) and leftovers of body-less responses go to the async queue; a completed request closes its batch, so a cache cleared later on that thread (a filter after the chain) is queued too. Background threads → async queue drained by the bus's own thread after 50 ms coalescing (`evictAllCaches`' 6 caches = one update), failures retried with backoff 1 → 30 s, at most 12 times, then dropped with a rate-limited WARN (TTL takes over). Every publish locks the `_global` row first (one lock order — no deadlock), on PostgreSQL with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '500ms'` (a dead idle-in-transaction session holding that row delays publishes ≤ 0.5 s, then they back off); a missing name row (deleted by hand) rolls the publish back and re-inserts it. A missing table is re-checked at most every 30 s (`TABLE_RECHECK_MS`; the schema patch signals `tableReady()`).
  - **Apply:** daemon thread `cache-bus` (NOT the shared scheduler pool), `site.monitor.cache-bus.poll-ms` (2000): `SELECT name, generation, updated_at FROM cache_bus` (3 s statement timeout); every name whose generation CHANGED (`!=` — a deleted / recreated row is seen too) runs its registered LOCAL evictor, which never publishes again and must be CHEAP (clear a field / mark stale — no database read; `MaintenanceService` marks stale and the next `isUnderMaintenance` reloads once); an evictor that throws leaves its name AND the global generation un-applied (retried next poll). The first successful poll is a baseline that evicts only names updated since the bus was created − 30 s. The writing pod records its own consecutive generations, so it does not evict itself twice (no second warm). Evictors run before the applied generation is published. Snapshot builds that race an eviction are not stored (generation guard: inactive-recipient guard, 7/24 operators, quiet hours, system maintenance, team grants, tech-error analytics, the `user-gate` caches; generation in the `TtlMemo` key: on-call, weak-algo). An eviction MARKS a snapshot stale instead of dropping it, so a rebuild that hits a DB error keeps the previous data (never "no 7/24 operators / no quiet hours / no passive filter" on every pod during a DB hiccup). Spring caches (`BusAwareCaffeineCache`): every value is stamped with the generation its load STARTED at; a value older than the cache's generation is a miss and is reloaded once — a GET arriving after the forced poll that joins a `sync = true` load started before the writer's commit does not get the old list. A remote eviction only bumps the generation (O(1)) and hands the physical removal of older-stamped entries to a short background task, so the bus thread never waits on a Caffeine bin lock held by a recomputing entry. `domain-team-names` is `sync = true`; other non-sync caches store at put time (their puts after a racing evict can still be stale for the TTL — `teamNames`, monitoring stats; accepted).
  - **Read your own writes:** `api/client.js` keeps the highest `X-Cache-Gen` it saw for 30 s (`CACHE_GEN_TTL_MS`) and sends `X-Cache-Min-Gen` on GET / HEAD (`requestInit` merges the caller's own `headers` over the defaults, so they no longer drop `X-Lang` / `Content-Type` / `X-Cache-Min-Gen`); `CacheBusRequestFilter` (only `/api/**` reads with a session and a valid number) asks a lagging pod's bus THREAD for ONE forced poll (≥ 100 ms apart per pod; the request thread never polls itself — no thread yet / shutting down → no wait) and waits at most `site.monitor.cache-bus.min-gen-wait-ms` (500) before serving — "Check now" / save on pod A, list reload on pod B shows the fresh data. It runs before DispatcherServlet, so `AuthInterceptor`'s grant / 7/24 / maintenance syncs see the evicted snapshot. CORS allows `X-Cache-Min-Gen` and exposes `X-Cache-Gen` (`WebConfig`).
  - **What is on the bus:** every Spring cache (`BusAwareCacheManager`, a `CaffeineCacheManager` subclass from `CacheConfig`: `evict` / `evictIfPresent` / `clear` / `invalidate` publish; the 8 `AdminController` `@CacheEvict`s, `InventoryInsightController`, `evictAllCaches`, `ThresholdPreviewService.afterThresholdChange`, `TlsProfileJobService.evictLatestList`, `card-extras`, `today-monitors` need no call-site change; caches are still `CaffeineCache` for the metrics); a remote eviction of `cert-latest|cert-stats|cert-warnings|renewal-advice|card-extras` also publishes `CachesEvictedEvent` on that pod (warmer; pods' poll phases differ, so the two full scans are not simultaneous). Registered snapshots (local evictor + bump where they invalidated before): `oncall` (`OnCallService`), `quiet-hours` (`TeamQuietHoursService.invalidate`), `inactive-recipients` (`InactiveRecipientGuard.evict`), `noc-operators` (`NocOperatorService.invalidate`), `team-grants` (`TeamAccessGrantService.invalidate`; remote = `invalidateLocal`), `maintenance-windows` (`MaintenanceService.refresh` publishes, the 30 s `scheduledRefresh` does not; remote = `markStale`, the next read reloads once), `system-maintenance`, `weak-algo`, `te-analytics` (remote = `invalidateLocal`), `user-gate` (`UserService.setCacheBus` / `publishUserGate`, bumped in `forgetUserCaches`, `onActiveChanged` and `evictActiveSessionCache`; remote = clears the passive / deleted-account gate (`inactiveCache`, 5 s) and the single-session supersede cache (`activeSessionCache`), so `AuthInterceptor` on the other pod cuts a deactivated / deleted account, a forced logout or a role change within ~2 s instead of the TTL; every eviction goes through `UserService.evictGateEntry` — `gateGeneration` + 1 and the entry removed at once and, inside a transaction, once more after commit — and a DB read that started before an eviction is not stored, so a racing read can never bring back the old passive flag / session id; gate `UserGateGenerationGuardTest`). Membership changes that alter the 7/24 / grant snapshots (team member added / removed) still rely on the TTL (unchanged, same on one pod). Not on the bus (TTL-only, accepted): the `CertNetworkOutageService` memo (10 s), the TTL memos of the overview / status page / NOC console / data quality …
  - **Known limit (N pods, R3):** every cert-cache eviction (also one per row in a bulk import / inventory-add burst, and the TLS-profile reconcile every 10 min) makes EVERY pod's warmer recompute `getAllLatest` / `getStats` / `card-extras` (a full `latest_checks` scan each) — the warmer's 2 s debounce and the 50 ms bus coalescing collapse a burst per pod, but the cost is N × per burst. Fine at 2 pods; before N pods consider warming only on the pod that serves the Overview traffic, a jittered / longer remote-warm debounce, or skipping the remote warm when the evicting pod already warmed. Unchanged by Sürüm 3-1 (it moved the long side jobs and the exporter's org-wide families off the leader, not the warmer); the sweep leader also still runs every monitoring check, recheck and the inbox drain until Sürüm 3-3 (distributed checks behind a flag).
  - **Adding a cache:** a Spring cache is automatic. A hand-made memo / snapshot that other pods read calls `cacheBus.register(NAME, localEvict)` in an `@Autowired(required = false)` setter and `cacheBus.bump(NAME)` next to its existing local clear; the local evictor must NEVER bump (ping-pong), must be cheap (no DB read), should bump a generation so a build racing it is not stored, and should MARK the snapshot stale (keep the previous data for a failed rebuild) rather than drop it. Append the name at the END of `CacheBusNames.ALL` (permanent).
  - **Compatibility:** table missing (`BadSqlGrammarException`, first seconds of the first boot) → no bus, TTL only; `site.monitor.cache-bus.enabled=false` → off (all three keys are boot-time `@Value`s, not in `AppSettingsCatalog`). A 20.165.1 pod neither publishes nor polls and ignores `X-Cache-Min-Gen` — mixed operation is TTL-only for writes served by the old pod. Single pod: own publishes never evict itself, the header never waits — same behaviour. Rollback: the table stays, unused (`RetentionCoverageTest` lists `cache_bus` as raw DDL, exempt: one row per name). Shutdown: `ContextClosedEvent` only sets a flag (no new polls / waits; the bus thread keeps sending queued publishes); `@PreDestroy` stops the thread and sends what is still queued ONCE on a helper thread joined for `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(2000)` — skipped when the table is missing or the last database call failed (`ShutdownBudgetGateTest.cacheBusFinalFlushUsesTheSharedDeadline`). `CacheBus.status()` = per-pod counters (`running`, `applied_generation`, `last_poll_ok_at`, `poll_failures`, `bump_failures`, `dropped_names`, `table_missing`; shown in System Health → Zamanlayıcı ve yürütücüler → Küme card for the ANSWERING pod as `data.cache_bus`, multi-pod Sürüm 2b).
  - Gates: `CacheBusH2Test` (two buses on one H2: bump on A → evictor on B once, no re-bump, no self-evict; rollback → no bump; bump from another `afterCommit`; failed poll / failed bump retried; deleted row; missing table silent; min-gen sync apply with and without the thread, bounded wait; request batch + destruction callback; long-request handoff; concurrent bumps; baseline; shutdown flush), `CacheBusH2Test.slowDatabase_requestDoesNotStall|failingEvictor_isRetriedNextPoll|tableMissing_rechecksRarely_thenRecovers|bumpAfterDispatcherCompleted_isQueued|longRequest_agedBatchHandedOff|shutdown_boundedFinalFlush`, `CacheBusRegistrationTest` (each of the 10 snapshots incl. `userGate`: write on A → B fresh after one poll, stale without it; `evictDuringBuild_isNotStored|weakAlgo_evictDuringBuild_isNotStored|remoteEvictThenDbError_keepsPreviousSnapshot`), `UserGateGenerationGuardTest`, `BusAwareCacheManagerTest` (incl. real `@CacheEvict` proxy over `CacheConfig` in two contexts; `joinerOfLoadStartedBeforeEvict_getsFreshValue|remoteClear_doesNotBlockOnRecomputingEntry|stampedValues_staleIsMiss_nullSupported`), `CertificateServiceCacheEvictionTest.domainTeamNamesIsSync`, `CacheBusWebTest` (header round trip: write on A, read on B evicts BEFORE serving; filter conditions), `CacheBusSchemaPatchTest` (incl. lost create race), `CacheBusPostgresTest` (postgres-it: 8 pods × 25 concurrent bumps, no deadlock / duplicate key; a session holding `_global` → the publish fails within lock_timeout), `CertificateServiceCacheEvictionTest.everyEvictedCacheIsPublishedOnTheBus|remoteEvictOfOverviewCachesWarms`, `ShutdownBudgetGateTest.cacheBusFinalFlushUsesTheSharedDeadline`, `WebConfigBeansTest` (CORS headers), `clientCacheGen.test.js` (incl. `requestInit` header merge).
- **Security limits are cluster-wide (2026-10-23, multi-pod R2a-B, MP-31 / MP-32 / MP-33).** A limit that protects against abuse may not be per pod (N pods = N× the limit). Login per-IP limit: `service/lockout/RateLimitCounterService` — table `rate_limit_counters(limit_key VARCHAR(120) PK, window_start, attempts, blocked_until, expires_at, updated_at)` (entity `RateLimitCounter` + `RateLimitCounterSchemaPatch`, `idx_rlc_expires`; key `<scope>:<value>`, scope `login-ip` (permanent), values longer than the column → `<scope>:#<sha256 prefix>`; fixed 60 s window, `site.monitor.login.max-attempts` failures → `block-seconds` block; the same algorithm as the old per-pod maps), PostgreSQL `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (limit_key) DO UPDATE … RETURNING`, H2 / unknown dialect conditional UPDATE → `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` → UPDATE; 5 + 5 failures on two pods → the 11th request gets 429 on either; a real session on any pod deletes the row (AUTH-9 semantics kept). Every 429 body, `wait_seconds` and the `RATE_LIMITED` audit are unchanged. DB error → this pod's memory fallback (20.165.1 behaviour, never a failed login; WARN `Oran sınırı sayacı yazılamadı — bellek yedeği`); hourly purge of rows past `expires_at` on every pod (`site.monitor.rate-limit.cleanup-interval-ms` 3600000, `…cleanup-initial-delay-ms` 600000, boot-time only; `RetentionCatalog` `rate-limit-counters`, EXTERNAL). k6 secret reveal: successful reveals are counted from `audit_log` (`SCRIPTED_SECRET_REVEAL` per `actor_id`, one query, `service/lockout/PersistedRowLimits`) FIRST (read-only), the per-pod deque SECOND; a cluster refusal is not recorded in the deque. Public `/api/login-help` (3 / h) and `/api/client-error-report` (5 / h): accepted rows of `login_issue_reports` (`source` + `ip_address` + last hour, `idx_lir_ip_reported` in `DeferredIndexBuilder.CATALOG`, portable) are counted inside the per-IP deque lock; a refused request is not recorded in the deque, but the refusal is cached per IP until the first slot frees (oldest accepted row + 1 h + 1 s, `clusterRefusedUntil`), so an IP at the cap is refused without a DB round trip. Count unreadable → the per-pod deque alone (never weaker than 20.165.1). Approval link (`TechErrorApprovalLinkController`): limiter key and the decision audit IP come from `ClientIpResolver` (they were `getRemoteAddr()` = the ingress, one shared bucket for every PO); the limit itself stays per pod on purpose (load limit on a 256-bit token). The weekly-report link controller already audits through `AuditService.resolveIp` (resolver) and has no limiter. Rolling deploy: a 20.165.1 pod keeps its own maps (worst case 10 per old pod + 10 cluster-wide on new pods; a success on one kind does not clear the other's counter); old pods' accepted reveals / reports write the same rows the new pods count. Gates: `RateLimitCounterServiceTest` (two pods on one H2, race, DB down), `RateLimitCounterPostgresTest`, `RateLimitCounterSchemaPatchTest`, `PersistedRowLimitsTest`, `AuthControllerTest.ipLimit_sharedAcrossPods|ipLimit_realSessionClearsSharedCounter`, `ScriptedSecretRevealControllerTest.cluster*`, `LoginHelpControllerTest.cluster*` / `ClientErrorControllerTest.cluster*` (incl. `clusterRefusal_cachedUntilFirstSlotFrees`), `TechErrorApprovalLinkControllerTest.limiterKeyedByClientIp|decisionAuditCarriesClientIp`.
- **`app_users` rows are never written from a detached entity (2026-10-23, multi-pod R2a-B, MP-51).** `AppUser` is `@DynamicUpdate` (a MANAGED save writes only changed columns — login vs a concurrent deactivation keeps `active=false`). `@DynamicUpdate` does NOT protect `save()` of an entity read outside the transaction: merge marks every column that changed concurrently as dirty and writes the stale value back (a passive account came back active). Such paths write targeted columns: `UserService.savePushOptOut|savePushQuietHours|savePushPreferences|savePushSnooze` (`AppUserRepository#updatePush*`), `TourStateService.apply` (`updateTourState`), the issue report "save e-mail to profile" (`updateEmailIfBlank` — only while still blank), `checkLockout`'s expired-lock clear (`clearExpiredLockout`, conditional on the value read), and the admin create / update profile fields (`UserService.applyProfileFieldsAndSave` — own transaction, fresh row). A new write path either reads and saves the user INSIDE one `@Transactional` method or adds a targeted `@Modifying` query — gate `AppUserDetachedSaveGateTest` (source scan: every `AppUserRepository.save(x)` sits in a `@Transactional` method AND `x` was read in that method before the call — `find*` / `getReferenceById` / `new AppUser()`, an `Optional` of such a read, a `for` over a `find*` result or a `find*(…).map/ifPresent(x -> …)` lambda; a parameter = violation (review LIM-1: the 20.165.1 `@Transactional savePushOptOut(AppUser user) { save(user); }` shape is a negative fixture); allow-list `LdapProvisioningService#upsert` with its reason). Rolling deploy: old pods still merge detached entities until the rollout ends (no schema change on `app_users`). Behaviour gate: `AppUserConcurrentWriteTest` (H2, `NOT_SUPPORTED`: each path vs a deactivation committed in between, plus a control test proving the detached merge reverts it).
- **Shutdown budget + Helm (2026-10-21, R1-D).** A pod must finish its shutdown before the kubelet's SIGKILL (the lease is released first, but in-flight work is cut and job locks wait for their TTL). `application.properties`: `spring.lifecycle.timeout-per-shutdown-phase=${SHUTDOWN_PHASE_TIMEOUT:20s}` (was 30 s), `spring.task.scheduling.shutdown.await-termination=true` + `…await-termination-period=${SCHEDULING_AWAIT_TERMINATION:20s}`. With await-termination the `@Scheduled` pool is NOT shut down early at `ContextClosedEvent` (Spring "late shutdown", verified in spring-context 7.0.8): running jobs finish instead of being interrupted by `shutdownNow`, ticks keep firing until the pool bean is destroyed (hence the "no new work" gate above). Helm: `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` (120, was hard-coded 60) and `preStopSleepSeconds` (10, `sh -c "sleep N"`, 0 = no hook; `helm template` fails when ≥ grace or < 0). Budget: preStop 10 + `NotifyExecutorDrain` 20 (`WebConfig.NOTIFY_AWAIT_SECONDS`) + web phase 20 + executor phase 20 + scheduler await 20 + every other destroy-time wait together ≤ 10 (`config/ShutdownDeadline`, see "Things that bite"; since multi-pod Sürüm 3-1 incl. the side-job runner — ≤ 5 s for running side jobs (both lanes inside that one wait; the TLS profile tick stops at ContextClosedEvent, so normally nothing is running) + ≤ 1 s unwind after the interrupt, stopped in `onShutdown` BEFORE `releaseAllOwn` — and the evaluation-state writer join ≤ 1 s, all from the shared deadline) = 100 < 120; if you raise one, raise the grace. Gates: `ShutdownBudgetGateTest` (reads application.properties, values.yaml, the deployment template and the script's `SPRING_BUDGET_S`; listener order), `scripts/helm-template-check.sh`.
- **System Health shows the cluster (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b, R2b-C1, MP-34 / MP-35 / MP-37a / MP-37d, N1–N5).** Scan status lives in `scan_runs` (one row per kind `hourly` / `full` / `stale`: run id, instance, last run, duration, totals, last failure — written in the `finally` of `runCheckForDomains`, not on the shutdown / lock-lost early returns; conditional — an older run never overwrites a newer row; a failure-only write keeps the last good totals) through `service/health/ClusterHealthStore` (every access `NOT_SUPPORTED` — `getSystemHealth` is a read-only tx; a missing table on PostgreSQL would poison it). `SchedulerService.clusterScanStatus()` (record `ScanStatus`) feeds `getStatus`, `getSystemHealth` and Config Health: `running` = live `cert-check` row (`locked_until` checked) or this pod's flag, `running_on` = lock owner, `lock.held` checks `locked_until` (+ `expired`, `locked_by_pod`, `held_by_me`), `scan_alarm` = no live lock and cluster last run ≥ 2 h; unreadable table → this pod's memory, `source: "local"` (`scheduler.status_source`) — the scan badge is a muted "Bilinmiyor", the scheduler chip says "Bu pod", the scheduler KPI is `unknown`: unknown cluster status is never green (the 2 h alarm stays critical); Config Health "scheduler" reports "never" only when the cluster has no run. The scan_runs write runs in its own try inside an inner `try … finally`, so a failed write (WARN) never skips the flag reset or the lock release, also on an `Error`. The "Şimdi çalıştır" poll ends on the cluster values (`healthModel.scanPollStep(state, data, prevScan)`: live lock seen then gone, or cluster `scan.last_run` changed — compared only between `source: 'cluster'` answers; when the click answer came from a 20.166.0 pod the first cluster answer is the baseline and never ends the poll; unknown lock → no decision). The weekly availability card's `running` = a live, unexpired lock row (shared `lockRowLive`). Heartbeat rows carry `system_heartbeat.instance_id` + a `shutdown_marker` (`@PreDestroy`, written on a daemon thread awaited at most `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(2 s)` — 0 when the shared destroy budget is gone, never `join(0)`); the timeline counts DISTINCT minutes (two pods → 1/min), `pods[]` per pod (received / expected over its own life), status `pods` = live / silent (no marker and no beat ≥ 3 min, no replacement — warn band reason `podSilent`) / restarted (silent, but the same pod name has a newer live process — neutral) / replaced (the silence is followed by a live pod with a NEW name never seen before it, one new pod covers one silent pod — eviction / node loss, neutral, `replaced_by`; a hung pod or a container restart of an existing pod never qualifies) / stopped (marker — neutral), 24 h window, ≤ 20, plus `pods_live` / `pods_silent`; the Pods card is green only with ≥ 1 live pod (otherwise muted + `hmp.podsNoneLive`, e.g. a rollout from 20.166.0). RDAP source status is published per pod on the heartbeat tick into `cluster_observations` (`rdap-source`, only after a NEW lookup, newest wins) and read back cluster-wide with `observed_by` / `observed_at` (`scope: local` when unreadable). Both raw-DDL tables and the heartbeat columns come from `service/health/ClusterHealthSchemaPatch` (a lost `CREATE` race is re-checked); `RetentionCoverageTest` lists `scan_runs` / `cluster_observations` in `RAW_DDL_TABLES` + `EXEMPT` (one row per kind / observation name). `served_by` / `served_by_pod` on `/api/admin/system`, `/metrics`, `/http-metrics`; UI `health/HealthParts.PodChip` ("Bu pod: <ad>" / "Küme") on every card — a new System Health card must say which (per pod: JVM memory and charts, executor, DB pool, HTTP summary; "Küme": scheduler, lock, scan, heartbeat, pods, RDAP; SMTP / push / weekly / network / cleanup are read from the database without a tag); the Build card's "Instance ID" is "Yanıtlayan pod". The KPI tiles carry the same scope tag, a caption under them names the answering pod (and the `/http-metrics` pod when different), the memory sparkline is dropped when `/metrics` came from another pod, and per-pod band reasons (memory, executor, pool, DB slow, HTTP) end with "(pod: <ad>)" (`reason.pod`, `hmp.reasonOnPod`).
