Küme card (first card of "Zamanlayıcı ve yürütücüler", `SchedulerSection.ClusterCard`, `healthModel.clusterModel`): lease owner = cluster (`sweep_leader.owner_pod`, "Lider yok" when the lease is free); "Lider mi", last sweep tick, wedge yield, held locks (states translated, `hmp.lockState.*`; a `LOST` hold raises the card to warn), round-claim failures, skipped rounds and the cache bus (`CacheBus.status()` → `data.cache_bus`; `cacheBusStale` — server `timestamp` − `last_poll_ok_at` > 30 s, or failures without a successful poll → warn "yoklamalar başarısız"; no poll yet → unknown) = the ANSWERING pod. `lastMonitorCheckAt` is cleared (monitor keys only) on leadership LOST / RELEASED and every pod prunes its own map daily (`pruneOwnMonitorCheckState`, `site.monitor.scheduler.monitor-state-prune-cron` `0 50 3 * * *` Istanbul, boot-time key, no lock). The nightly cleanup retries a busy `nightly-cleanup` lock (an admin "run now") every 30 s for ≤ 15 min — gives up at once when this night's `round:nightly-cleanup` is already claimed, at shutdown (1 s sleep slices) and at the deadline (WARN "bu gece atlandı") — and releases its HOLD (`LockTicket`), not by name. The ~33 "held elsewhere / round ran on another pod, skipping" scheduler lines go through `logSkip` / `logSkipInfo` (once per message + first arg per hour at their level, then TRACE). Touch targets: `ui/ErrorDetails` toggle + copy, health `RefreshButton`, `CardCta`, "Şimdi çalıştır", "Kilidi zorla bırak" ≥ 40 px on touch. Rolling deploy: a 20.166.0 pod writes neither table and heartbeats with a NULL `instance_id` (counted in the strip, not in the pod list); an old pod answering the new UI sends no `served_by` (chips say "Bu pod" without a name), no `scan.source` and no `cache_bus` — nothing breaks; rollback leaves the tables / columns, ignored. Gates: `SystemHealthClusterMultiPodTest`, `ClusterHealthStoreTest`, `ClusterHealthPostgresTest`, `HeartbeatMultiPodTest`, `RdapSourceClusterTest`, `ConfigHealthServiceTest.schedulerCheckUsesClusterStatus`, `SystemControllerTest.metricsCarryServedBy|healthCarriesCacheBusStatus`, `healthModel.test.js` (scanPollStep, clusterModel, podSilent), `SystemHealth.test.jsx` ("çok pod"), `HeartbeatHistoryModal.test.jsx` ("pod başına"), `e2e/system-health-cluster.spec.js` (390 / 768 / 1280). **Multi-pod Sürüm 3-1 (2026-10-23):** `GET /api/admin/system` adds `data.eval_state` (only when the recorder bean or `ClusterHealthStore` exists — `controller/ClusterRuntimeHealth`): `this_pod` = THIS pod's `EvalStateSink.status()` (mode, `active` = writing as the generation holder, writer / writer_pod, generation, pending, dropped, flushes, write_failures, last_flush_at / last_failure_at / last_failure (exception class only), `failing` = this pod WRITES (`active`) and its last failure is newer than the last flush — a former leader's old failure is not "failing" (it never retries; review M2, the client also requires `active !== false`), `fenced` (sent ONLY while this pod is the sweep leader — `ClusterRuntimeHealth.recorder(status, leaderHint)` with `SchedulerService.sweepLeaderHint()`, because the recorder's own flag survives a LOST event until GAINED / the holder is silent), `hook_failures` (cumulative since start: MOS hook + recorder enqueue exceptions), table_missing, `inherited` {previous_generation, recovery_counters, down_siblings, active_suppressions, abandoned_chains}, last_verify; null = no recorder / NOOP; `this_pod_error` when the status call threw) and `verify` = the cluster's latest `eval-state-verify` observation (observed_by / _pod / _at, `stale` = older than 3 × max(60 s, `site.monitor.eval-state.verify-ms`), status / reason / generation, `kinds{kind: {compared, mismatches, skipped, max_delta, measured}}`, `pass_fail_mismatches` (null for an unknown round), first 5 hashed keys; SNAKE_CASE and camelCase payloads read; no row → null; unreadable row / payload / no store → `verify_error`), and `data.side_jobs` (only with a `SideJobRunner` bean) = THIS pod's runner status (threads, accepting, leader_yield_enabled, leader_hint, other_live_pod — null = not in the liveness cache right now (it is filled only for ~1 min after a leader placement decision), `other_live_pod_seen` {value, at} = the last non-null value THIS pod's health endpoint saw (memory only; `ClusterRuntimeHealth.OtherPodSeen`, review L4), `threads` = the LONG-lane size (the 5-thread short lane is not reported), jobs[] in name order with ISO times, running / queued / failed / deferred_total / yielded_total / skipped_total; `{error}` when unreadable). Küme card (`health/ClusterRuntimeParts.jsx`, model `health/clusterRuntimeModel.js`): section "Kalıcı değerlendirme durumu" with a "Küme" chip (verify badge: only a FRESH ok round is green — unknown / unreadable / outdated / not yet / off are muted; verifying pod, generation, per-kind counts with the measurement kinds marked, hashed first keys, every unknown reason translated (`hmp.esReason.flush_lag|read_failed|no_generation|snapshot_failed|resync|verifier_error|payload_too_large|leadership_changed|nothing_compared`, never a raw code; `EVAL_UNKNOWN_REASONS` equals the server `REASON_*` constants both ways), "decisions always from memory" note) between the leader and the per-pod sections; in the "Yanıtlayan pod" section ("Bu pod: <ad>" chip) the rows "Değerlendirme kaydı" (mode, writing / not writing, pending, write failures, dropped, last write, "currently failing" warning (only on a WRITING recorder), a warning when `fenced` is true ("this pod is the leader but another pod took the write generation — not writing; takes over by itself once that holder has been silent for 3 minutes"), a muted `hook_failures` line (cumulative since start; the mirror may be incomplete, decisions unaffected), tables missing, inherited counts) and "Yan işler" (running / queued, deferred / yielded since start, leader-yield on / off, other live pod yes / no — on a follower "only the sweep leader checks", on a leader with an empty cache "at the last check (N min ago)" / "not checked yet", omitted while leader yield is off (`clusterRuntimeModel.otherPodState`), active jobs with state, jobs whose last run failed / was cut). A fresh verify mismatch or a failing recorder raises the card to warn. Old server without the fields → rows hidden; no `sweep_leader` (single-pod branch) → the R3-1 rows still render. Gates: `ClusterRuntimeHealthTest`, `SystemControllerTest.healthCarriesEvalStateAndSideJobs`, `ClusterRuntimeHealthTest.failingOnlyWhileActive|sideJobsRemembersLastSeenOtherPod|fencedOnlyWhileLeader`, `clusterRuntimeModel.test.js` / `SystemHealthClusterR31.test.jsx` (former leader's old failure not a warning, other-pod states, fenced warning + hook failures), `clusterRuntimeModel.test.js` (incl. "doğrulama nedenleri sunucu sabitleriyle eşleşir"), `SystemHealthClusterR31.test.jsx`, `e2e/system-health-cluster.spec.js` (390 / 768 / 1280: R3-1 rows, unknown verify grey, failing recorder warn, no clipping / overflow, touch targets).
- **Rolling update without a blank page (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b, R2b-E, MP-47, user decision: "old files + auto reload", no sticky sessions).** Old and new pods serve together during a rollout; an old pod's `index.html` asks for its own hashed `/assets/*` and the request may land on a new pod (and a tab opened before the deploy loads lazy chunks later). **Image:** `Dockerfile` global `ARG PREVIOUS_IMAGE=` (empty = today's build) → stage `previous-release` (`FROM ${PREVIOUS_IMAGE:-previous-release-none}`) → `asset-carry` runs `scripts/carry-previous-assets.mjs`: the OWN assets of the last `CARRY_RELEASES` releases (stage ARG, default 5, max 10 — prod is deployed by hand and skips releases). Each image writes `/app/frontend/asset-history.json` (outside the served dist: itself + the releases it carried, newest first) and `carry-report.json`; the next build reads ONLY the previous image and carries the files of its history's first K entries that are present in it — K releases back from one image, older ones drop out, over 5000 files / 200 MB the OLDEST releases are dropped first (never a growing pile). A backend-only release carries nothing but keeps the history (the frontend did not change). A 20.166.0 image has no list → its whole `dist/assets` is one entry (label = its `/app/VERSION`). Names this build has, invalid names, symlinks and dirs are skipped. Runtime: carried assets first, then the new dist, lists in `/app/frontend/{own,carried}-assets.txt`. `release.yml` / `docker-build.yml` / `scripts/build-image.ps1|sh` pass the previous release / the environment's current image digest-pinned (`scripts/resolve-previous-image.sh`; `-PreviousImage <ref>` / `-NoPrevious` / `PREVIOUS_IMAGE=<ref|none>` override; missing → empty → nothing carried). **Client:** `frontend/public/boot-guard.js` (classic, unhashed, `no-store`, loaded in `index.html` BEFORE the bundle — CSP has no `'unsafe-inline'`, so never inline it) reloads the page once when a same-origin script / stylesheet / modulepreload fails before the first `load`; no right left → a visible notice instead of a blank page (entry script only; TR / EN from `site-monitor-lang`, default EN, 44 px "Reload"). One shared rule with `utils/chunkReloadGuard.js` (sessionStorage `eb-chunk-reload`, shape `{v:2, at, builds:{id:n}}`; the old `{version, count}` fields are read AND kept when rewritten — a rolled-back ≤ 20.166.0 bundle reads only them): one automatic reload per build (id = entry script path) and none within 60 s of any automatic reload (`RELOAD_COOLDOWN_MS`) — boot guard, ErrorBoundary (incl. `Unable to preload CSS`, no error-report mail for it) and the boot EN-dictionary load together reload at most once; the rule is duplicated in the boot script (no bundle when it runs) — change both, `bootGuard.test.js` runs one truth table through both. A runtime TR→EN switch failure never reloads. `LanguageLoadNotice` resets its counter on unmount: under React StrictMode (dev) it mounts in the same commit as `ToastProvider`, whose (simulated) unmount closes the boot-fallback toast. Missing assets keep the plain-text 404 contract (`SpaNotFoundAdvice`). Coverage: carry covers an OLD index whose assets land on a NEW pod, for the last 5 releases (more than 5 releases skipped → that direction too falls back to the boot guard). The other direction — a NEW index whose files land on an OLD pod — can never be carried: the boot guard reloads once, then shows the notice; with 1 new + 2 old pods most new-index loads during the rollout need that one reload. The FIRST rollout of this release mixes with 20.166.0 pods that have no `boot-guard.js` at all (one blank page possible, F5 fixes it). A rollback to 20.166.0 reads the kept legacy fields (alternating old / new claims: ≤ 3 reloads in 10) — never a loop. Gates: `carry-previous-assets.test.mjs`, ci.yml `image-assets` (`scripts/check-carry-output.mjs`), `image-boot-check.sh` step 7, `bootGuard.test.js`, `chunkReloadGuard.test.jsx` (legacy fields kept), `i18n-lazy.test.jsx` (incl. the StrictMode boot toast), `brand-default.test.jsx` (index.html: exactly the boot guard + the module entry, no inline script), `SpaNotFoundAdviceTest.bootGuardAndCarriedAssetCachePolicy`, `e2e/rolling-update-assets.spec.js` (390 / 1280).
- **JPA**: `spring.jpa.open-in-view=false` — entities must be DTO-mapped inside the service transaction; lazy associations after return will throw. Hibernate `ddl-auto=update` is the schema-evolution mechanism (no Flyway/Liquibase).

### Auth — custom interceptor, not Spring Security filter chain
`AuthInterceptor` (registered in `WebConfig`) guards `/api/**`. Login goes through `AuthController`, sessions are HttpSession-based with a remember-me cookie validated by `RememberMeService` (token table: `remember_me_tokens`). Three orthogonal lock states on `AppUser`:
- `lockoutUntil` — temporary progressive lockout (durations: 30s→120s→600s→1800s by default). **Since 2026-09-26 there is no automatic permanent lock** (product decision: an anonymous attacker could permanently lock any account): beyond the last level every further violation repeats the last duration (30 min). Attempts made while locked (`LOGIN_FAILED` with outcome `BLOCKED`) don't count, and the counter uses the canonical username.
- **Unknown usernames (2026-10-09):** a non-existent name climbs the SAME ladder as an active account (`service/lockout/LockoutLadder` is the single source for failures-needed and durations), stored in `login_unknown_lockouts` (all pods agree), so status, body and `wait_seconds` are identical at every level; no `ACCOUNT_LOCKED` audit and no AppUser row for it. A failed local login always burns one BCrypt. Gate: `AuthControllerUnknownLockoutParityTest`. The per-IP login limit (10 failures / 60 s → 30 s block, 429 `wait_seconds`) is DB-backed too since 2026-10-23 (`rate_limit_counters`, see "Security limits are cluster-wide").
- `permanentLock` — legacy state from before 2026-09-26 (and any admin-set lock); admin-only release. Nothing sets it automatically any more.
- `mustChangePassword` — while set, only `/api/me`, `/api/me/change-password`, `/api/login`, `/api/logout` are reachable. Don't add bypass paths.
- `active=false` (passive, user decision 2026-10-02) — a passive account can NEVER sign in, by any path. Local password, LDAP/AD bind (checked on the EXISTING row before `provisionFromAd`, which never runs, so the user is not reactivated) and remember-me all return `ACCOUNT_INACTIVE` (login 403, otherwise 401; JSON `code`/`error_code`). The "passive" message is shown only after the credentials are verified; a wrong password keeps the generic 401 (no enumeration). Audit `LOGIN_FAILED` outcome BLOCKED does not feed the lockout. `AuthInterceptor` runs the passive check BEFORE `isSessionSuperseded` (5 s per-user cache, `site.monitor.session.inactive-cache-ms`; a deactivation / delete clears it on the other pods within one cache-bus poll, `user-gate` — "Cross-pod cache invalidation"). On a hit it strips the session and leaves a TOMBSTONE (`accountInactive` attribute) instead of `invalidate()`, so every tab, every in-flight request and every pod (JDBC store) sees the same signal — don't "fix" this into `invalidate()`. It also deletes the user's remember-me tokens and audits `SESSION_ENDED_INACTIVE`. Deactivation (`UserService.updateUser`, single + bulk) writes the `TERMINATED:` sentinel and deletes the tokens at once. The client (`utils/accountInactive.js`, `AccountInactiveDialog`) shows a non-dismissable 10 s countdown, then goes to `/?session=inactive`. No passive check per endpoint — the central gate is the design. Notifications: `InactiveRecipientGuard` (60 s snapshot, `site.monitor.notify.inactive-cache-ms`) drops contacts linked to passive users (email + webhook) on every contact path. `EmailNotificationService.doSend` drops passive-only addresses; if none remain it logs `SKIPPED: pasif kullanıcı`. Team member lists show passive members last, with a destructive "Pasif" badge; pickers stay active-only.
- **Deleted user = gone (2026-10-22, AUTH-1/16).** `AuthInterceptor` checks `UserService.isAccountGone(username)` right after the passive gate (same cache entry as the passive flag, `site.monitor.session.inactive-cache-ms`; an existence query runs only when the active-flag projection is empty; a READ failure never logs anyone out: the passive gate runs the SAME read first (`accountState`), so a DB error throws there and the request fails with 5xx / `DB_UNAVAILABLE` — no session invalidated, no token deleted; `isAccountGone`'s own catch (= not gone) is only a safety net for direct callers): no user row → session invalidated, that name's remember-me tokens deleted, cookie cleared, 401 `Session superseded`. A remember-me cookie whose user row is missing deletes that name's tokens (tokens are keyed by USERNAME — a re-provisioned name must not inherit an old cookie). `AdminController.deleteUser` deletes the tokens and calls `UserService.forgetUserCaches`. Gates `AuthInterceptorTest.liveSession_accountStateUnreadable_requestFailsNobodyLoggedOut`, `UserServiceAccessFixTest.gone_readFailure_inactiveGateThrows_goneDirectCallFalse`.
- **LDAP never takes a LOCAL row (2026-10-22, AUTH-3).** Routing is by the TYPED name; if the AD canonical name (`sAMAccountName`) maps to an existing row whose `authSource` is not `LDAP` (LOCAL or null = local), `tryLdapLogin` returns `FAILED` (generic 401 + lockout ladder, WARN) — after the username-prefix check, before passive / maintenance / provisioning. `LdapProvisioningService.upsert` throws for a non-LDAP existing row in EVERY mode (defence).
- **Role change = re-login (2026-10-22, AUTH-4, default decision).** A REAL `system_role` change of an ACTIVE user writes the `TERMINATED:` sentinel (like deactivation) → 401, re-login with the new role. Team-membership changes stay on the `/me` scope refresh (no forced logout). The admin auto-reset (`/users/{id}/auto-reset-password`) also terminates the session and deletes remember-me tokens (AUTH-10). The same applies to role changes that come from AD (A2, final review 2026-10-22): `LdapProvisioningService.upsert` writes the sentinel when the `system_role` really changes in `ADMIN_RESYNC` ("AD'den yeniden eşitle") or `MANAGER` mode (manager provisioning, group → role mapping incl. an ADMIN demotion) for an ACTIVE account and evicts the pod's supersede cache (`UserService.evictActiveSessionCacheFor`); the `LOGIN` path writes nothing (the person is signing in with the new role right now). Remember-me tokens are kept (silent re-login with the new role). Gates `LdapRoleMappingProvisioningTest.adminResync_roleChange_endsLiveSession|noRoleChange_loginPath_andPassive_keepSession`.
- **Scoped user writes (2026-10-22, AUTH-5 / UIA-1 / AUTH-6 / AUTH-11).** `applyUserUpdate` checks only ADDED and REMOVED teams against the writer's manage scope (the editor sends the whole set; a TEAM_ADMIN's unchanged extra team passes); raising a user to TEAM_ADMIN, or adding a team to a TEAM_ADMIN target, needs EVERY team of the target after the change (+ teams it leads) in the writer's `manageTeamIds` (403). `POST/DELETE /teams/{id}/members` run `requireCanAdministerTarget` (ADMIN / AUDIT accounts: global admin only). Only a global admin writes `phone` (`applyProfileFields(…, isGlobalAdmin)` on update; create unchanged).
- **Passwords with edge whitespace are rejected on create / change (2026-10-22, AUTH-7 variant)** — 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` field `password` (`UserService.requireNoEdgeWhitespace`; the change dialog shows it under the new-password field); login keeps stripping (deferred: should login stop stripping?). The password rule is 6–64 characters on both sides (`site.monitor.password.min-length|max-length`).
- **Login hardening (2026-10-22, AUTH-8 / AUTH-9).** JSON-null `username` / `password` → generic 401 (never 500). The per-IP counters are reset only after the 409 "session elsewhere" branch, i.e. when a session is really created.
- **Small access rules (2026-10-22):** "Bildirimlerim" (`/api/issue-reports/mine`) lists only session-created sources (USER_REPORT, CLIENT_ERROR) — sign-in page (LOGIN) reports are not "mine" (AUTH-13, default decision; `LoginIssueService.SESSION_SOURCES`); self audit / device history lower-case with `Locale.ROOT` (AUTH-15); `/api/admin/users/search` for a scoped admin (not TEAM_ADMIN, not global viewer) searches the whole view scope, `teamId` honoured when in scope, an out-of-scope `teamId` → empty page.
- **Code login (OTP) + login methods (2026-10-02/03, user request).** `service/otp/*` handles 6-digit single-use codes sent by push or e-mail (default 45 s each, 30–300 s, configurable per channel).
  - **Storage:** the DB holds only `HMAC(SITE_MONITOR_SECRET_KEY sub-key, challengeId:code)` in `login_otp_challenges`. State changes are conditional UPDATEs: 3 attempts by default, single use.
  - **Limits:** counted in a 15-minute window from the DB. The IP limit returns 429. The per-user request limit, the resend cooldown and the failed-verification suspension are SILENT.
  - **No enumeration:** the request endpoint returns the SAME 200 body for eligible, ineligible and unknown users. An ineligible request creates a DECOY row (`user_id=null`, `SUPPRESSED_*`) whose verification behaves exactly like a wrong code. Ineligible means passive, locked, global admin while `allow-global-admins` is off (default), `mustChangePassword` (2026-10-03: use the temporary password), or no target. Delivery is async (`LoginOtpDeliveryService`). Never add a short-circuit (`LoginOtpServiceFlowTest`).
  - **Contact verification (2026-10-03, user request):**
    - **Switches:** `site.monitor.login.otp.push.require-phone` / `.email.require-email`, default ON, GLOBAL_ONLY.
    - **Request:** while a switch is on, the request also carries `phone` / `email`. The code is sent only if the value matches the username's account.
    - **Matching (`OtpContactMatcher`):**
      - phone: digits only; drop `0090`, `90` (when ≥ 10 digits remain) and the trunk `0`; ≥ 10 digits; compare the last 10; constant-time. `AppUser.phone` = AD `mobile`.
      - e-mail: trim + case-insensitive.
    - **Same answer every time:** a mismatch, no usable registered value, or an unknown user returns the SAME 200 plus a decoy (`SUPPRESSED_CONTACT_MISMATCH` / `NO_PHONE` / `NO_TARGET` / `UNKNOWN_USER`).
    - **Blank but required:** 400 `PHONE_REQUIRED` / `EMAIL_REQUIRED` + `field`. Writes no row and uses no IP quota.
    - **Anti-guessing:** `max-contact-mismatches` (1–20, default 5) per username per 15 min → silent `CONTACT_LOCK`. Requests made while locked are not counted. Password login is unaffected.
    - **Timing:** the mismatch count query runs on EVERY request while the switch is on — no short-circuit. Switch OFF ≡ the old behaviour (no query, no `contact` audit key).
    - **Privacy:** the ENTERED value is never stored, logged or audited; audit gets only `contact`=MATCHED/MISMATCH/MISSING/LOCKED/NOT_CHECKED.
    - **Admin view:** adds `coverage` {active_users, with_phone, with_email} from one aggregate query. The UI warns below 80 %.
    - **Gates:** `LoginOtpContactVerificationTest`, `OtpContactMatcherTest`, `LoginOtpCodeNeverLoggedTest.enteredContactNeverLeaks`.
  - **Login statistics + editable OTP push text (2026-10-03, user request):**
    - **Audit `method`:** every LOGIN, and every password-path LOGIN_FAILED, carries detail `method` (LOCAL / LDAP / REMEMBER_ME / OTP_PUSH / OTP_EMAIL; the legacy value `PASSWORD` means LOCAL). Event types, outcomes, failure_reason and lockout counting are unchanged.
    - **Classification:** single source is `service/loginstats/LoginEventClassifier`. An unknown actor gets no channel. Method-less legacy rows are classified by the account source and marked `estimated`.
    - **Service:** `LoginStatsService` runs one row query (cap 250k → `truncated`) plus one grouped count for the previous period. Results are cached 30 s per period; `fresh` recomputes at most every 5 s.
    - **Endpoints:** `/api/admin/login-methods/stats[/users[/{username}]]` are global-admin only. The user detail goes through IdentityMask.
    - **UI:** tab `lm_tab=stats`, URL prefix `lm_`. The user directory shows channel badges.
    - **Push text keys:** `site.monitor.login.otp.push.title|message-tr|en`, GLOBAL_ONLY, rules in `OtpPushTemplate`:
      - message: `{kod}` exactly once, plus optional `{sure}` / `{saat}`;
      - title: ≤ 60 characters, no placeholders;
      - worst-case length after `PushText.pushSafe` must fit the push limit;
      - an invalid stored template falls back to the default at delivery.
    - **Push test:** `push-test` sends only to the session admin, 3/min, audit `LOGIN_METHODS_PUSH_TEST`.
    - **Client mirror:** `utils/pushSafeText.js` mirrors `PushText` (gate `pushSafeText.test.js`).
    - **Push charset:** `UserPushService.sendDirect` runs `PushText.pushSafe` like the alarm path. The channel is ISO-8859-9, so an unfiltered "—" showed as "?" after the code (`LoginOtpPushSafeTextTest`).
  - **Session:** a successful verification goes through `AuthController.establishSession`, the SAME path as password login (409/forceLogin, remember-me, maintenance, passive, lock, LOGIN method=OTP_*).
  - **The code is never written anywhere:** not to logs, DB columns, `notification_logs`, push delivery rows or audit (`LoginOtpCodeNeverLoggedTest`). `RequestLoggingFilter` skips `/api/login/otp/` bodies. Push goes straight to the gateway (identifier = username, no outbox row). E-mail goes through the funnel with `force=true`. Shutdown (2026-10-21, multi-pod R1 / MP-07): `LoginOtpDeliveryService` no longer `shutdownNow()`s silently — it stops accepting work, waits up to 10 s for queued and running deliveries, then every task that could not start is finished `FAILED: SHUTDOWN` + `LOGIN_OTP_DELIVERY_FAILED` audit (queued tasks are a named `DeliveryTask` whose `toString` carries no code). Gate: `LoginOtpDeliveryShutdownTest`.
  - **Lockout:** OTP failures are not `LOGIN_FAILED` and never feed the password lockout.
  - **LDAP toggle:** with `site.monitor.login.ldap-enabled=false`, every non-local name and a wrong local password get the same 401 `LDAP_LOGIN_DISABLED`. The bootstrap `admin` always reaches local verification.
  - **Settings:** keys live in group `login-methods`, GLOBAL_ONLY, at Settings → Güvenlik → Giriş Yöntemleri. Endpoints: `GET /api/public/login-methods` and `/api/login/otp/{request,verify}` (PUBLIC).
  - **Adding a channel or event:** update `AuditEventCatalog` + `audit.ev.*` + `auditFormat` SPECIAL.
- **Username prefix rule (2026-10-21, user request: "yalnız N ile başlayan kullanıcıya login izni; MP'li ya da başka harfli olana izin vermeyelim; harfleri ayarlardan yöneteyim").**
  - **Rule:** single source `service/loginpolicy/UsernamePrefixRule` (pure truth table): rule off or no usable prefix → allow; blank name → allow; exact exempt name or the bootstrap admin (`site.monitor.username`) → allow; otherwise the name (trimmed, `Locale.ROOT` upper case, Turkish İ / ı folded to I) must start with an allowed prefix — a `DOMAIN\user` / `user@domain` name must pass BOTH as typed and with the domain stripped. Global admins are NOT exempt (the rule is about which account to use). `UsernamePrefixPolicy` reads the live settings (memo keyed by the raw values, no DB per request; a read failure is fail-open).
  - **Keys** (group `login-methods`, GLOBAL_ONLY, part of `LoginMethodsService.KEYS`): `site.monitor.login.username-prefix.enabled` (default false ≡ byte-identical), `.allowed` (CSV of `[A-Za-z0-9]{1,10}`, ≤ 20), `.exempt` (CSV, ≤ 50 names of ≤ 100 chars). `AppSettingsService.save` validates the format per key and, when a save touches these keys, the cross-field rule: on with an empty list → 400 field `username_prefix_allowed`; the saving admin's own account must pass → 400 `LOCKOUT_SELF` (`FieldValidationException(code, field, message)`). The page sends these keys only when they changed.
  - **Login (review 2026-10-21: passive / maintenance pattern):** decided only AFTER the credentials are verified — a wrong password or an unknown name gets the normal generic 401 + lockout / unknown-name ladder, byte-identical to the rule being off, so exempt names and the bootstrap admin name can't be read from the answer or its timing. Valid credentials + a refused TYPED name or stored local / AD `sAMAccountName` → 403 `USERNAME_PREFIX_NOT_ALLOWED` + `allowed_prefixes`, checked before `provisionFromAd` (it never runs), no session. Audit `LOGIN_FAILED` BLOCKED, reason `USERNAME_PREFIX`, detail `method` LOCAL / LDAP — not counted for the lockout ladder, the IP rate limit or failed-login stamps. Code login: a non-matching name is ineligible → decoy `SUPPRESSED_USERNAME_PREFIX` (same 200, no code); a real challenge requested before the rule was turned on → 403 after the correct code. Remember-me: tokens deleted + 401.
  - **Live sessions:** `AuthInterceptor` order passive → **username prefix** → maintenance → superseded; TOMBSTONE attribute `usernamePrefixDenied` (never `invalidate()`), remember-me tokens deleted, `activeSessionId` cleared, audit `SESSION_ENDED_USERNAME_PREFIX`, 401 `USERNAME_PREFIX_NOT_ALLOWED`. A present tombstone ALWAYS answers that 401, whatever this pod's (≤ 10 s stale) settings say — a pod that still sees the rule off must not turn it into `/?session=expired`; the re-login replaces the session. Stale-pod window: for ≤ 10 s after the rule is turned on, a pod with the old settings can still accept a login; the next request on an updated pod cuts it. Client: `utils/usernamePrefix.js` → `AccountInactiveDialog variant="prefix"` (10 s, non-dismissable) → `/?session=prefix` → login notice; the signal flag is reset on a successful login and on the in-tab session reset (`App.handleLogin` / `resetSessionData`), so a refused remember-me at load never swallows a later cut.
  - **E-mail approval links (review 2026-10-21):** an account the rule keeps out can't decide through a link either. Tech-error PO approval: `TechErrorApprovalService.approvers()` leaves such accounts out (no request mail / token / push, not counted), `status()` answers reason `username_prefix` (after the passive check, before `isApprover`; own page text; token NOT consumed; security event `TECH_ERROR_APPROVAL_LINK_DENIED`), `linkDecisionAllowed` / `decideViaToken` refuse it too. Weekly report: `WeeklyReportService.approverPrefixRefused` — every addressee of the latest `SUBMIT_PO` mail can't act (≥ 1 whose ACTIVE owners are all refused, the rest passive-only) → reason `username_prefix`, `ApproverUsernamePrefixException` (an `ApproverInactiveException`), `WEEKLY_REPORT_LINK_DENIED`; an address with an allowed owner or no owner (team box) keeps the link open; rule off → no query. In-memory rule check only.
  - **UI:** Settings → Güvenlik → Giriş Yöntemleri → "Kullanıcı adı ön eki" card (`loginmethods/UsernamePrefixCard`, `TokenChipsInput`, `usernamePrefixModel`): switch, chip inputs (invalid entry under the field, entries over the limit stay in the box with `lm.prefix.err.tooMany*`, pending text blocks the save), live preview `GET /api/admin/login-methods/username-prefix/preview?allowed=&exempt=` (global admin; active users that would be refused + first 50 names, `self_refused`; a failed read says "couldn't be read", never 0), confirm when tightening (with the refused count). `GET /api/public/login-methods` adds `username_prefix.allowed` only while the rule applies (never the exempt list) → hint under the username on the login form and the code flow.
  - **Gates:** `UsernamePrefixRuleTest`, `UsernamePrefixPolicyTest`, `AuthControllerUsernamePrefixTest`, `AuthInterceptorUsernamePrefixTest`, `LoginOtpUsernamePrefixTest`, `AppSettingsUsernamePrefixTest`, `LoginMethodsUsernamePrefixTest`, `TechErrorApprovalServiceTest.usernamePrefixRefusedApprover`, `TechErrorApprovalLinkControllerTest.deniedReasons`, `WeeklyReportApproverInactiveTest.prefix*`, `WeeklyReportControllerTest.approveLink_usernamePrefix_refusedAndAudited`, `usernamePrefixModel.test.js`, `LoginMethodsUsernamePrefix.test.jsx`, `LoginUsernamePrefix.test.jsx`, `AppUsernamePrefix.test.jsx`, `clientUsernamePrefix.test.jsx`, `e2e/login-username-prefix.spec.js` (390/768/1280). Runbook: "MP hesaplarıyla giriş kapatma".
- **System-wide bulk deactivation (2026-10-02)** — `UserBulkDeactivationController` (`/api/admin/users/bulk-deactivate[/preview|/{opId}/undo]`, `/bulk-operations`), GLOBAL admin only. Criteria are AND-combined (scope all/teams, N days without login incl. never-logged-in accounts created ≥ N days ago, source, role). Server-side it ALWAYS excludes every `systemRole=ADMIN` account (global + scoped), the operator and already-passive users. Apply recomputes the set; a mismatch with `expected_count` returns 409 `BULK_LIST_CHANGED`. It is capped at 5000 and runs in chunks of 200, each user through `UserService.updateUser(active=false)` so the passive gate kicks them. The operation is stored in `user_bulk_operations` (BOUNDED, never deleted). Undo reactivates only members still carrying THIS op's TERMINATED marker and not in a later un-undone op; the claim is atomic, so a second undo gets 409. Weekly-report approval links are refused (`WEEKLY_REPORT_LINK_DENIED`) only when EVERY addressee of the latest `SUBMIT_PO` mail is passive; otherwise the check fails open.
- **System maintenance mode (2026-10-02, user decision)** — Settings → Platform → Sistem Bakımı, GLOBAL admin only (backend 403 for a scoped admin).
  - **Phase:** `system_maintenance_windows`. The phase is DERIVED from time, never stored (`SystemMaintenanceService.phaseOf`: PLANNED → ANNOUNCED (`announce_hours`) → WARNING (`warn_minutes`) → ACTIVE → ENDED / CANCELLED). It is cached per pod for ≤ 5 s (`site.monitor.system-maintenance.cache-ms`) and fails open.
  - **Who gets in:** while ACTIVE only GLOBAL admins (ADMIN with no `viewTeamIds`) may log in or stay. `AuthInterceptor` order is passive → username prefix → **maintenance** → `isSessionSuperseded`. The cut reuses the passive TOMBSTONE pattern (attribute `systemMaintenance`, remember-me tokens deleted, `activeSessionId` cleared, audit `SESSION_ENDED_MAINTENANCE`) and returns 401 `MAINTENANCE`.
  - **Login:** local and LDAP check maintenance after the credential check and before provisioning, and return 403 `MAINTENANCE`. The audit is `LOGIN_FAILED` BLOCKED, which does not count toward the IP limit or failed-login stamps.
  - **Client:** `/api/session/ping` and `/api/me` carry an additive `maintenance` block plus `server_now`. The client computes phases on SERVER time, and the per-second tick lives only in `SystemMaintenanceLayer`, never in App. A 401 `MAINTENANCE` never redirects: it opens the non-dismissable countdown, then `/?session=maintenance`.
  - **Public status:** `GET /api/public/system-maintenance` (no-store, no ids/counters). Its path must not contain "status" (`StatusPageControllerTest.notPublic`).
  - **Mute toggle:** "Bildirimler bakım boyunca sussun" defaults OFF, and OFF ≡ byte-identical behaviour. When ON, mail/webhook/push/NOC are suppressed for every trigger except MANUAL resend. The trace is `notification_logs` trigger `SYSTEM_MAINTENANCE` + `SKIPPED: sistem bakımı`, push `SKIPPED_SYSTEM_MAINTENANCE`, plus a `system_maintenance_suppressions` row. No storms form while muted. Timed escalation steps and the quiet digest are held until catch-up.
  - **Catch-up:** `SystemMaintenanceJobService` runs under `scheduler_lock` key `system-maintenance`. It sets `last_re_alert_at=NULL` on open, unacked alarms whose INITIAL/ESCALATION was suppressed, so the next sweep sends one INITIAL. Alarms closed during maintenance get nothing. Claims are never left half-done (2026-10-21, multi-pod R1 / MP-18): an exception after `claimCatchUp` / `claimAnnounceMail` / `claimCorrectionMail` / `claimEndMail` releases the claim with a conditional UPDATE (`releaseCatchUp` / `releaseAnnounceMail` / `releaseCorrectionMail` / `releaseEndMail` — mails only while no chunk went out, i.e. the recipient read or mail build failed) and the next round retries; a catch-up claim older than 10 min with `caught_up_count` still NULL (the owner died) is re-run (`reclaimCatchUp`; catch-up is idempotent); a window whose catch-up is not settled is NOT marked `jobs_done` and its end audit waits. Bounded: after `MAX_JOB_ATTEMPTS` (10) failures per pod the claim is kept (ERROR; mail jobs write `FAILED: e-posta hazırlanamadı — 10 denemede vazgeçildi` into `announce_mail_status` / `end_mail_status`) and a stale catch-up is given up so the window can finish. An announce / end-mail claim no longer stays half-done (review F7, round 3 N2): right before the first BCC chunk the job writes `SENDING: gönderim başladı — sonuç henüz yazılmadı` (`markAnnounceMailSending` / `markEndMailSending`, conditional on its own claim time and a NULL / `RETRY:` status); if that write fails or the claim changed hands, nothing is sent (the claim is released, which also clears our own `SENDING:`). After the send the status write is tried 3 times with backoff (`recordWithRetry`); if it still fails → ERROR + exception, the `SENDING:` status stays. An unsettled end mail keeps the window out of `jobs_done`. A stale takeover (claim older than 10 min, `reclaimEndMail` / `reclaimAnnounceMail`) re-sends ONLY a claim whose status is still NULL (claimed, nothing sent — once, as `RETRY: önceki gönderen pod yarıda durdu …`; the end mail only within 6 h of the window end (`END_MAIL_RETRY_WINDOW`), the announcement only before the start); a `SENDING:` claim is never re-sent and is given up as `FAILED: sonuç bilinmiyor — gönderim başladı ama sonucu yazılamadı; yeniden gönderilmedi` (`STATUS_SEND_UNCONFIRMED`); a second interruption, a too-late end mail or an announcement after the start gives up as `FAILED: sonuç bilinmiyor — gönderen pod yarıda durdu; yeniden gönderilmedi` (`STATUS_RESULT_UNKNOWN`, `giveUpEndMail` / `giveUpAnnounceMail`, ERROR + `SYSTEM_MAINTENANCE_MAIL` audit). Gates: `SystemMaintenanceCatchUpRetryTest` (incl. `endMailRecordRetriedAfterSuccessfulSend|endMailSentButUnrecorded_takeoverNeverResends|markSendingFails_noSendAndReleased|announceSentButUnrecorded_takeoverNeverResends`), `SystemMaintenanceQueriesTest.endMailSendingMarker`.
  - **Counters and mails:** counters and job stamps are `updatable=false` and written by native claims only (never via an entity save). Announcement and correction mails are opt-in, sent BCC in chunks of 100, and exclude passive users. Weekly-report approve links show a maintenance page.
  - **Gates:** `AuthInterceptorMaintenanceTest`, `AuthControllerMaintenanceTest`, `SystemMaintenanceNotificationTest`, `SystemMaintenanceJobServiceTest`, `SystemMaintenanceQueriesTest`, `AppSystemMaintenance.test.jsx`.
  - **Ended notice + end mail (2026-10-02, user request):**
    - **State:** for `site.monitor.system-maintenance.ended-notice-minutes` (60; 0 = off) after a NON-cancelled window ends, `clientBlock` and `publicStatus` return an additive `state:"ended"`. The public variant carries no id or revision. An announced, warning or active window always wins. `live()` looks back over that period in its single cached query.
    - **UI:** a dismissable success strip (`maint-ended`, dismiss kind `ended` per id + revision; global admins see it too), the login card with `data-state="completed"`, and a status-page note.
    - **End mail:** controlled per window by `email_on_end` (default TRUE, null = TRUE). `SystemMaintenanceJobService` sends `Kind.ENDED` exactly once (`claimEndMail` on `end_mail_at`; `end_mail_status` and `end_mail_count` are `updatable=false`; audit `SYSTEM_MAINTENANCE_MAIL` kind ENDED) to the same recipients as the announcement, in BCC chunks of 100.
    - **Immediate windows** never send announce or correction mails; their recipients get only the end mail.
    - **Subject:** written `[Site Monitor] …`, because `EmailResponsiveContractTest` rejects the one-word brand in mails.

Spring Security is **not** on the classpath as a filter chain — only `spring-security-crypto` for BCrypt. Don't pull in `spring-boot-starter-security`; it conflicts with the manual interceptor.

### Single active session per user
A user holds at most one live session. `UserService` keeps `AppUser.activeSessionId` (newest login wins) plus `lastSeenAt`; the SPA pings `/api/session/ping` (~15s) to keep it fresh. On every `/api/**` request `AuthInterceptor.isSessionSuperseded(user, currentSessionId)` compares the registered id — if it differs, the interceptor invalidates the session, clears the remember-me cookie, and returns **401** (the client then redirects to `/?session=expired`). Login returns **409** when another live session exists; the frontend re-posts with `forceLogin=true` after a confirmation modal. Admin force-logout (`POST /api/admin/system/terminate-session`) sets `activeSessionId` to a `TERMINATED:<uuid>` sentinel that can never match a real session. Two traps: sessions persist in the Spring Session JDBC store but this registry is a single column, so stale ids are cleared at startup (`SessionStoreService.clearStaleActiveSessionMarkers`: jdbc clears only ids with no live `spring_session` row, memory clears all; TERMINATED sentinels always survive); and `activeSessionId = null` means *grandfathered*, not *kicked* — `isSessionSuperseded` returns false for null. So the app never writes NULL any more (2026-10-22, AUTH-2): logout, the maintenance cut and the username-prefix cut write a `TERMINATED:<uuid>` sentinel — NULL let `adoptSessionIfNone` re-adopt an older superseded / admin-killed session on its first ping. NULL exists only on legacy rows. The sentinel is not live (`hasLiveSession`), survives the startup cleanup, and the next login overwrites it.
- **Logout always ends the client session (2026-10-22, UIM-1).** Idle auto-logout and manual "Çıkış yap" call `/logout` through `utils/pendingLogout.tryServerLogout` (≤ 3 s, never rejects) and then ALWAYS run the client cleanup (`App.endSessionLocally`: timers, session data, remember-me key, personal storage, identity). Before, a rejected `api.logout()` (wake from sleep, Wi-Fi / VPN drop, rollout) aborted the cleanup and the app stayed signed in with all data while the server session lived on. When the server did not confirm, `sm.logout.pending` (localStorage, a token) is written BEFORE personal storage is cleared and a toast says so (`app.logoutPendingNotice`). The flag is settled (a) at boot BEFORE `/me` — if it still fails, `/me` is not asked and the Login form shows (the cookie must not bring the session back on F5); (b) on the Login screen on `online` and every 30 s (≤ 20 tries, `startPendingLogoutRetry`); (c) inside `api.login` / `api.loginOtp.verify` before the request; on a successful login the flag is cleared right there (and again in `handleLogin`). **No late `/logout` response can expire the new session's cookies (D1, 2026-10-22):** every `/logout` attempt (`tryServerLogout`) is abortable and hard-aborted after `LOGOUT_ABORT_MS` (10 s; an aborted request's response is never applied, and its late "success" is not a confirmation) and is announced cross-tab (`sm.logout.inflight.<id>` = abort deadline). Login (`settleLogoutsBeforeLogin`) first writes `sm.login.hold.<id>` (30 s TTL, removed after the request), lets this tab's in-flight logouts finish within `LOGIN_LOGOUT_WAIT_MS` (5 s, the pending-flag attempt included), ABORTS whatever is left, then waits for other tabs' announced ones (bounded by their deadlines); the retry loop and the boot settle (`respectLoginHold`) start no new attempt while a hold is live (announce first, then check the hold — the login side does the reverse, so one always sees the other). Nothing pending → no delay. Gate `pendingLogoutLoginRace.test.js`. A late success of a timed-out request clears only its own token.
- **Cross-tab identity (2026-10-22, UIM-2).** While signed in, App listens to `storage` on `sm.storage.owner`: another user's name → `assignLocation('/?session=expired')` (the reload shows the real identity — the ping returns none); removed (logout in another tab) → `sm.logout.pending` set → local logout here too, otherwise one immediate `/session/ping` (the 401 path redirects). Same user → nothing.
- **Logout stops a running "Şimdi Kontrol Et" (UIM-26):** `resetSessionData` sets `checkCancelRef` and bumps `checkRunSeqRef`; the run's queue, row writes and `finally` only act while their run id is current.
- **Session signals reset on login (2026-10-22, UIA-H2).** `pages/Login.completeLogin` (password, force, code) and `App.handleLogin` reset the page-lifetime passive-account and maintenance signals (`resetAccountInactiveSignal`, `resetMaintenanceSignal`), like the username-prefix signal.

### Opaque user ids (2026-10-08, user decision: "bir kullanıcı başka bir kullanıcının id'sini okuyamasın")
- **Rule:** a GLOBAL admin sees and may send numeric `app_users.id` (byte-identical to before). Everyone else (AUDIT and scoped admins included) sees the opaque `app_users.public_id` (UUID, assigned once, never changes) and may send only that; a numeric user id from them resolves to "not found".
- **Output — one gate:** `config/UserRefResponseAdvice` converts every Jackson response for a non-global viewer into a fresh tree (`UserRefWire`). Values under user-ref KEYS (`user_id`, `manager_id`, `leader_id`, `actor_id`, `author_id`, `editing_user_id`, `contacted_user_id`, … plus camelCase, incl. inside the JSON-text fields `changes`/`detail`/`details`/`snapshot`, and `resource_id` when `resource_type=USER`) become the UUID; an unknown user becomes null. An ambiguous `id` key is NOT rewritten by name: `AppUser.id` carries `@JsonSerialize(UserRef.LongSerializer)` and map rows use `UserRef.of(id)` (user directory, team members, bulk result rows). Outside the gate (storage, logs, global admin) both write a plain number.
- **Input:** every endpoint that takes a user id resolves it with `UserPublicIds.resolve(svc, raw, session)` (path ids are `String`). Controllers get the service as an optional field (`@Autowired(required=false)`) so slice/unit tests without the bean keep the old numeric parsing. Hand-written outputs bypass the gate: the audit CSV/JSON export converts itself (`ExportView`).
- **Adding a field/endpoint:** name a user-id field so its snake_case is in `UserRefWire.KEYS` (or `@JsonIgnore` it), and use `UserRef.of` for a user under an `id` key. The frontend never `Number()`s a user id (`utils/userRef.js`: `userRefValue`, `sameUser`).
- **Gates:** `UserRefCoverageGateTest` (model fields), `UserRefWireTest`, `UserPublicIdsTest`, `UserRefResponseAdviceTest`, `opaqueUserIds.test.jsx`.

### Permissions
Per-resource permission model layered on top of `systemRole` (`USER` / `AUDIT` / `ADMIN`) and org role (`PO`/`TECH`/`MANAGER`/`CLEVEL`). Single source of truth: `PermissionCatalog.ALL` — every resource key + allowed actions (`view`/`edit`/`execute`). The Permission Matrix UI renders from this; bootstrap seed is driven from it. **If you add a new resource_key in code, add it to `PermissionCatalog` in the same change** — otherwise the matrix UI won't show it and grants won't bootstrap.

**UI flags for global-only and manage-scope gates (2026-10-22, Theme G).** `/api/me/permissions` `scope.global_admin` (= `SessionScope.isGlobalAdmin`, K1) and `/api/me` + the login response `manage_team_ids` (= `SessionScope.manageTeamIds`; OMITTED for a global admin, `[]` for USER / AUDIT, K2) and `view_team_ids` (= the session's `viewTeamIds`, the `SessionScope.canView` teams; OMITTED for a global viewer — global admin / AUDIT; `[]` for a session without a scope; FE-1). The UI's "global admin only" and "can I manage this team" gates read these (see "Frontend shape" → Global-only actions), never the role string. Gates `AuthControllerTest.myPermissions_carriesScope|me_carriesManageTeamIds|me_carriesViewTeamIds`.

**7/24 monitoring team (2026-10-04, user decision).**
- **Setting:** Settings → 7/24 → "7/24 izleme ekibi takımları" (`noc_settings.operator_team_ids`). `PUT /api/admin/noc/operator-teams` is GLOBAL admin only (audit `NOC_TEAMS_UPDATE`); scoped admin / AUDIT see it read-only.
- **Who:** active members of those teams are 7/24 operators WITHOUT a role change. `NocOperatorService` keeps a 30 s snapshot; `AuthInterceptor.syncNocOperator` writes the session attributes `nocOperator` / `nocTeamIds` on every request, so revocation needs no re-login (other pods within one cache-bus poll after the team list is saved, `noc-operators`).
- **Reads:** operators read all monitoring (alarms, incidents, maintenance, status, storms, the nine monitor types, inventory/certs, overview, notes, search) through `SessionScope.canViewMonitoring` / `seesAllMonitoring` / `monitoringViewTeamIds`, plus the dynamic grants in `PermissionCatalog.NOC_OPERATOR_GRANTS` (since 2026-10-13 also `oncall.read` and `data_quality.view`, user request: "7/24 ekibi kişisel panoda tüm sistemleri görsün" — those two endpoints already read org-wide for operators; the grant keeps the widgets even if the matrix turns them off for USER; `system_health.read` stays excluded).
- **Live scope for the UI (2026-10-13):** `GET /api/me/permissions` carries an additive `scope` = `{monitoring_all (seesAllMonitoring), noc_operator, noc_console (= `NocCallLogService.seesAllAlerts`, the console's own gate — NOT the matrix snapshot: a scoped admin gets `noc_calls.write` from the ADMIN row but the console refuses them; fallback without the service: global viewer ∨ operator)}`, refreshed with the permission snapshot every 60 s (`usePermissions().scope`, null on an old server) — joining / leaving a 7/24 team shows without a re-login. Kişisel Panom uses it for the console widget's visibility and shows a scope chip ("Tüm kurum (7/24 ekibi)" / "Tüm kurum" / "Takımlarınız"). Gates: `AuthControllerTest.myPermissions_carriesScope`, `PermissionCatalogTest.nocOperatorGrantsArePinned`, `PermissionsProvider.test.jsx`, `MyDashNocWidgets.test.jsx`. The 7/24 tab (`NocPage`) also uses the live `scope.noc_console` / `scope.noc_operator` (boolean → it decides; absent → login-time `/me`), and every coverage deep link (`nocCoverageTarget`, the coverage banner, who-notified, Team members → call list) carries `n_view: 'coverage'` — an operator's default view is the console (2026-10-22, UIM-29 / UIM-30).
- **Writes:** operators gain only `noc_calls.write` and comments on any alarm they can read.
- **Never widen writes or admin:** `viewTeamIds` is untouched. `canManage`, `canOperateTeam`, `canWriteInventory`, ack / resolve, incident write, the audit log and admin areas NEVER read the flag. A new READ path uses the monitoring helpers; a WRITE path never does. Memo keys use `seesAllMonitoring`.
- **Console:** `GET /api/noc/console` (memoised 15 s per window, batch-loaded, max 1000 rows). `GET /api/noc/console/alerts/{id}/call-sheet` is the ONLY endpoint that exposes call-list phone numbers (operators / writers).
- **Alert history:** `noc=sent` = an OPEN `noc_deliveries` row with SENT* / QUEUED_RETRY*.
- **Legacy:** AUDIT + `noc_calls.write` operators keep working.
- **Bug-fix rules (2026-10-22):** the NOC storm-resolved mail's member rows carry the team name of their OPEN delivery (`NocCallListService.teamNames`, one query, L-N2); a standalone alarm (`AlertOwnership.routesLikeInventory` false) takes no inventory SY / UG team on the console (`ug_team_id` null; unstamped → no team), like the call sheet's `owningTeam` (L-N3); call-list errors carry no numeric user id and go through `Msg.t` (person's display name when known, L-N4); a passive (deactivated) on-call person comes with `active:false` and NO phone on the call sheet (`NocController.oncallPerson`, L-N1) — the call sheet (`NocCallDialog`) and the alarm detail (`AlertOnCall`) show "Pasif hesap — aranmaz" (`noc.passiveNoCall`) and no `tel:` link.
- **Gates:** `NocOperatorServiceTest`, `SessionScopeTest`, `InventoryOrgVisibilityAdminTest` (operator variants), `AuditControllerTest` / `SystemControllerTest` noc cases, `AuthInterceptorNocOperatorTest`, `NocConsole.test.jsx`.

**Team access sharing (2026-10-12, user request: "A takımı her neyi görüyor, siliyor, editliyor ise yetki verdiği kişi de aynı o ekipteki gibi").**
- **Data:** `team_access_grants` (`TeamAccessGrant`, `@DynamicUpdate`, UNIQUE owner_team_id × `grantee_key` "T:<team>" | "U:<user>"; `access_level` VIEW < EXECUTE < EDIT < DELETE (`service/access/TeamAccessLevel`, cumulative); `expires_at` UTC = end of the chosen Istanbul day; note ≤ 500; ≤ 200 per owner team). A TEAM grant applies to the grantee team's ACTIVE members (primary or extra membership); a USER grant to that active user. Passive / deleted owner team, passive grantee team or user and expired grants never apply. Team delete / user delete remove their grants.
- **Session, not role:** `TeamAccessGrantService` keeps a 30 s snapshot (`site.monitor.team-access.cache-ms`, read failure keeps the previous one); `AuthInterceptor.syncTeamGrants` (after the NOC sync, every request) writes the cumulative lists `grantViewTeamIds ⊇ grantExecTeamIds ⊇ grantEditTeamIds ⊇ grantDeleteTeamIds` only when they change. Revocation needs no re-login: this pod at once, other pods within one cache-bus poll (~2 s, `team-grants`, see "Cross-pod cache invalidation"); a member leaving the grantee team ≤ 30 s (TTL). `viewTeamIds` / `manageTeamIds` / `systemRole` are never touched.
- **What each level opens (SessionScope helpers — use them, never re-derive):** VIEW → every monitoring READ of the team (`canViewWithGrants` / `viewTeamIdsWithGrants`, and through them `canViewMonitoring` / `monitoringViewTeamIds`): certificates (+ card extras, shared-cert window), inventory, the nine monitor types, alarms, incidents (read, incl. those the owner team created), notes, storms, status page, overview, activity, TLS grade, weak-algorithm / crypto inventory, hygiene, manual certs, maintenance windows (read), k6 team templates, channel technical error reports (release 9: channels, reports, PDF, history, template read-only). EXECUTE → also "Check now", diagnose, TLS rescan, health refresh, live registration lookup (`canExecuteTeam`), alarm ack / resolve / comments. EDIT → also edit monitors and inventory, group rename, renewal confirmation (`canOperateTeam`, `canWriteInventory`, `inventoryWriteTest`, `grantedEdit`), and the CONTENT of channel technical error reports (open a report, values, images, apply template — never send / approve / channel / template). DELETE → also delete / restore, old manual-cert versions, bulk inventory delete (`canDeleteTeamResource`).
- **Never shared:** moving a record to another team (`resolveTeamChange` needs `canOperateTeamOwn` on BOTH teams — an EDIT grantee can neither pull the owner's monitor into their team nor push their own into the owner team; a refusal caused by a grant is a 403 with a message, never a silent "saved"; users with no grants keep the old silent rule byte-identically); re-notify, the recipient preview and bulk re-notify (`requireAlertNotifyScope`; rows carry `notify_scope:false`); people lists — on-call rotation / current on-call and the 7/24 member + call list (`canViewMonitoringOwn`), NOC call records, and on an alarm read ONLY through a grant (`AdminController.alertReadOnlyViaGrant`) the detail's `oncall` is empty and `/notifications`, `/push-deliveries`, `/storm-push` hide recipient names / addresses / push usernames (address COUNT only), notification groups (the monitor form's group picker shows "saved group — can't be read here" locked, value kept), escalation contacts; team management (`canManage`), notifications (a grantee is never a recipient), incident WRITE (`inOwnIncidentScope`; rows read only through a grant / 7/24 carry `can_edit:false`), certificate notes write, audit log, admin areas; channel technical error reports: channel create / edit / delete, template save, executive recipients, approval request, SEND, report delete, the Settings overview — and their people data (executive addresses, send recipients, approver names) (release 9). A new WRITE path picks the matching helper; a new READ path uses `canViewWithGrants` / `viewTeamIdsWithGrants` (memo keys too); a people/management read uses `canViewMonitoringOwn` / `canView`.
- **Row flags:** monitor list rows `can_check` / `can_edit` / `can_delete` (the "Check now" trigger response has no flags — `utils/monitorDetailSync.withRowFlags` keeps them on the open detail; card actions render for check-only rows with no Edit/Duplicate). Rows readable ONLY through a grant carry `shared_access:true` + `can_check` (inventory list / by-domain, `/certificates/list`, uptime overview; absent for everyone else) — the UI does not treat them as "another team's read-only record" (Alerts / Changes tabs stay) but takes actions from the flags. Alarm rows read only through a grant carry `act_block:"shared_view"` ("view only" note instead of the standalone-owner note). Dashboard cards (cached `/certificates`, no per-viewer flags) use `/me` `access_grants [{team_id, team_name, level}]` — ONLY teams the user cannot already `canView` (a manager's own / managed team never appears there) — via `rowGrantAccess` (App `certGrantAccess`, `checkableCerts`; CertificateModal `grantAccess`). EDIT / DELETE teams are added to the add-form team pickers (`grantWriteTeamsFromMe` → App `writeTeams`; for admin roles merged into the `/admin/teams` list by `mergeWriteTeams` — same array back when nothing is added — in the monitor pages, InventoryManager and ManualCertsPage), never to navigation or management; the inventory form offers no team move on a grant-only row.
- **API:** `GET /api/admin/team-access[?team]` (`teams.sharing/view`; global viewer all, otherwise teams the viewer manages; rows carry `can_edit`, `owner_active`, `grantee_active`), `POST` / `PUT {id}` (only the fields sent: level, `expires_on`, note) / `DELETE {id}` (`teams.sharing/edit` + `canManage(owner)`; a row the caller could not list → 404, listed but not manageable (AUDIT) → 403; 409 `GRANT_EXISTS` / `GRANT_LIMIT`; field errors `VALIDATION_FAILED` + `field`), `GET /api/me/shared-with-me`. Audit `TEAM_ACCESS_GRANT_CREATE|UPDATE|DELETE`. `grantee_user_id` is an opaque user ref.
- **UI:** Admin → Organisation → "Erişim paylaşımı" (`g_tab=sharing`, `components/admin/access/*`): shared-with-me card, KPI tiles (counted after search / team / level filters; type and state tiles exclude each other; "Total" clears only them), search + owner / level filters (`g_aq`, `g_team`, `g_atype`, `g_alevel`, `g_astate`), table ≥ 860 px container else cards, labelled actions menu (edit / remove with confirm), `TeamAccessGrantDialog` (level choice cards, DELETE level asks again, edit sends only changed fields, server field errors under the field incl. level). A 403 on the list (no `teams.sharing/view`) is the "you manage no team" empty state, not an error. Team row menu "Erişimi paylaş" jumps there with `g_team`. Pickers use `SearchableSelect wrapLabels` so long AD names wrap; level / state badges are `rounded-md overflow-visible`.
- **Gates:** `TeamAccessGrantServiceTest`, `SessionScopeGrantTest`, `TeamAccessControllerTest`, `AuthInterceptorTeamGrantTest`, `AlertStandaloneOwnerScopeTest.teamAccessGrant*`, `MonitoringControllerTest.teamAccessGrant_*`, `CertificateControllerTest.healthConfirm_grantNeedsEdit`, `IncidentControllerTest.sharedIncidentIsReadOnly`, `teamAccessModel.test.js` (reads the Java level order; `rowGrantAccess`, `withRowFlags`), `TeamAccessManager.test.jsx`, `MonitorCardActions.test.jsx`, `e2e/team-access.spec.js` (390/768/1280).
- **Review round 4 (2026-10-12) — rules added:**
  - **Notification routing is never shared:** on an update by a grant-only writer, `MonitoringController.guardSharedNotificationFields` drops `notifyEmail`, `notifyWebhook`, `notificationGroupId`, `nocNotify`, `nocGroupIds`, `alertLevel` from the body when equal to the stored value (null e-mail/webhook = on, null 7/24 = off, null / WARNING / invalid level = WARNING, group id number or numeric text, 7/24 groups as a set) and returns 403 when they differ; own-scope writers are unchanged. The inventory form keeps group / 7/24 (`AdminController.requireSameInventoryRouting`). UI: rows carry `shared_access:true` → `NotifyChannels locked`, `NocNotifyField disabled`, team picker disabled (`formTeamSelect`; own rows drop grant-only teams from the picker); `InventoryFormModal` locks team / group / 7/24 itself when `record.shared_access`. `NotificationGroupSelect` locks only on a 403/404 answer; a network / 5xx failure shows "couldn't be loaded", stays editable.
  - **k6 (scripted) monitors follow the same rule as the other eight types (2026-10-16, user report: "edit / execute / delete adımlarında sentetik izlemede sorun var"; decision: full editing, script included).** The 2026-10-12 own-scope exception (`requireOwnScopeForScripted`) is gone: EDIT creates / edits / pauses / duplicates k6 monitors of the owner team and loads an old script VERSION (a normal update), script and env included; change-history restore needs DELETE like every type (`canDeleteTeamResource`); notification routing and team moves stay unshared exactly as for the other types (`guardSharedNotificationFields`, also on create with `new ScriptedMonitor()`; `resolveTeamChange`). List rows use `withCheckFlag` like the others; the scripted page gets `writeTeams` and its form uses `routingLockOf` / `withRoutingDefaults` / `formTeamSelect` (new record there is `{}` → `modalKey`). The risk is stated where the access is given: `TeamAccessGrantDialog` shows `access.k6Warn` for EDIT / DELETE (scripts run with the team's encrypted env values). Live check: a grantee USER at VIEW / EXECUTE / EDIT / DELETE across all nine types — API + UI "Düzenle → Kaydet" / "Sil" (all 200 at their level, 403 below). Review rules (same day): the k6 diagnose response (`url`, `candidates`, its 400 messages, leg `error` / `output_tail`) masks the monitor's SECRET env values (`ScriptedCheckerService.secretValues` + `SecretMask.maskValues`); a masked `url` sent back (the dialog pre-fills it) is mapped to the raw candidate before probing; `checks_json` is masked at run time AND at read time (list `maskScriptedSecrets`, check-history page — rows written before the change) — the run output already was; `restoreChange` on a k6 row applies the update rules (name unique in the team → 400, open alarms follow a renamed monitor — the alarm key is the NAME — a restored pause closes them, and a restore that reactivates clears `disabledReason` / `disabledAt` like the update); on a locked form a loaded draft never brings back routing fields or the team, the lock survives a create whose form stays open (`routing_locked` + `can_delete: false` put on `modal`, then the list row's flags via `freshestRow` after the reload — the role fallback counted a grant team as "own" and drew a 403 Delete), and a saved script of ANOTHER monitor resets its secret rows to `value_set: false`. Owners with live, manageable EDIT / DELETE grants see a notice on the sharing page until they dismiss it (`k6EditGrants` — every such grant, no date cut: grants made on the old release before the deploy saw the old text, and the server's zone-less UTC timestamps parse as local time; dismissal per user in `sm.access.k6Notice`). Old script versions are NEVER decrypted (the versions tab shows env NAMES only, so it has no reveal). Rule = `ScriptedSecretRevealController.canReveal` (single source, also the additive row flag `can_reveal` on `/scripted` list rows and create / update responses, `MonitoringController.withRevealFlag` + `monitoring.read/view`): `isGlobalAdmin` OR (`isScopedAdmin` ∧ `canManage(team)` — the own `manageTeamIds`; a teamless monitor = global admin only). NEVER through a team access grant (EDIT / DELETE included), TEAM_ADMIN / USER members, AUDIT or 7/24 operators — never use the grant-widened helpers here. Responses: `monitoring.read/view` missing → 403; monitor missing or not viewable (`canViewMonitoring`) → 404; viewable but not allowed → 403 `FORBIDDEN` (SecurityException → `ACCESS_DENIED` security audit); unknown / non-secret / blank variable or a name > 200 chars → 404 `NOT_FOUND`; decrypt failure (key changed, broken env JSON) → 409 `SECRET_UNREADABLE` ("re-enter and save"); per user 10 / minute and 30 / hour, cluster-wide (2026-10-23, MP-32: the cluster count of successful reveals in `audit_log` (`SCRIPTED_SECRET_REVEAL` per actor) runs FIRST and read-only, the per-pod sliding deque SECOND — the deque is the only gate when the count is unreadable; a cluster refusal is not recorded in the deque) → 429 `RATE_LIMITED` + `Retry-After` + `retry_after_seconds`. Success audits `SCRIPTED_SECRET_REVEAL` (category SECURITY; detail JSON monitor_id, monitor, team_id, variable NAME — never the value). The value never reaches logs (`RequestLoggingFilter` already omits every `/api/monitoring/scripted` body, path variants included), audit, error bodies, `notification_logs` or `monitor_change_log`. UI: `scripted/ScriptedSecretReveal.jsx` (`useSecretReveal`, `SecretRevealButton` in the env row — `data-slot="env-secret-reveal-toggle"`, `aria-pressed`, name "Gizli değeri göster: <name>" — and `SecretRevealPanel` under it: read-only monospace `env-secret-value`, Kopyala, Gizle `env-secret-hide`, error inline in a danger AlertBanner). The button shows only when the edit form is for a SAVED monitor whose row says `can_reveal` and the variable is secret + `value_set` in the row's CURRENT env (`revealNames` — duplicate / new form never); the value lives only in that component's state: never in the form, draft, local storage or saved views; hidden after `REVEAL_TTL_MS` 30 s, on window blur / `visibilitychange` hidden / `pagehide`, when the row's name changes, on unmount (form close, tab change, logout); a late response after hiding is dropped (sequence). `can_reveal` is in `monitorDetailSync` ROW_FLAGS. Gates: `ScriptedSecretRevealControllerTest` (every role, grants, 404 / 409 / 429, audit without the value, no-store, log capture with TRACE), `ScriptedSecretValuesTest.revealSecret*`, `MonitoringControllerTest.scripted_list_canRevealFlag`, `RequestLoggingFilterTest.trace_scriptedSecretReveal_responseNeverLogged`, `ScriptedSecretReveal.test.jsx`, `e2e/scripted-secret-reveal.spec.js` (390 / 768 touch, 1280).
  - **Notes:** a grant-only viewer needs an operable owner team to write a note; the note is stamped with the owner team (`MonitorNotesController`). DNS change-history restore needs the DELETE gate (`canRestore`).
  - **Bulk selection** never includes `shared_access` rows (inventory `canSelectRow`, certificate table `bulkSelectable`) — bulk activate / deactivate / contacts / tier are team management and the server skips them; single-row actions stay.
  - **Fresh grant levels:** `GET /api/me/access-grants` (same shape and filter as `/me`) is part of the dashboard load wave (`grants` source) — a new / removed share updates card actions without re-login.
  - **People never shown through a grant:** NOC call records are masked on grant-only alarms (`maskGrantOnlyNocCalls`); notification rows show only a status class (`statusClass`), no message / sender / push response. `notified_contacts` (escalation contacts' name / e-mail / role) is nulled for grant-only readers on the alarm list, detail, acknowledge and resolve responses and on `/certificates/history/{domain}/alerts` (which also needs `alerts.read/view` now); push-delivery rows keep the system marker `-` (`username:"-"`, K4) and person rows `username:null` — the client counts every `username: null` row as one hidden recipient (`alertHistoryModel.pushPeopleCount`, ONE "N gizli alıcı" badge) and never counts a `-` row as a person. The alarm detail's `oncall` follows the `/api/oncall/current` gate (`oncall.read` + `canViewMonitoringOwn` on each owner team; L-H4) (2026-10-22, ALERTS-17 / ALERTS-18). The acknowledge / resolve responses mask a COPY (`AdminController.maskGrantOnlyPeople` returns a `BeanUtils` copy, like the push-delivery rows): the instance `resolve` returns is handed to the async resolution mail, which may `save` it — masking it in place wrote `notified_contacts = NULL` for everybody (A1). `/certificates/history/{domain}/alerts` masks per ALARM OWNER (`AlertOwnership.ownerTeamIds` with the inventory SY / UG; ownerless → only 7/24 / global viewers) — the domain's inventory team no longer sees another team's standalone alarm contacts on the same host (D2). Gates `AlertStandaloneOwnerScopeTest.grantOnlyResolve_masksCopyNotTheServiceInstance`, `CertificateControllerTest.getDomainAlerts_masksContactsPerAlarmOwner`.
  - **Limits:** expiry ≤ 2099-12-31; the per-owner cap counts LIVE (non-expired) grants (`countLiveByOwnerTeamId`); a unique-constraint race → `GRANT_EXISTS`; a no-op PUT saves and audits nothing. The snapshot cache uses a generation counter: a build that raced a write is not stored; `invalidate()` marks the snapshot stale instead of dropping it (a failed rebuild keeps the previous view).
  - **DELETE level** lets every grantee (for a team grant: all its members, any role) delete — the dialog and hints say so.
  - **Gates:** `SharedNotificationGuardTest`, `TeamAccessGrantRepositoryTest` (H2, `NOT_SUPPORTED`), `TeamAccessGrantServiceTest.buildRacingInvalidateIsNotStored|expiryCapAndUniqueRace`, `TeamAccessControllerTest.myAccessGrants`, `sharedAccessSurfaces.test.jsx`, `NotifyChannels.test.jsx` (locked), `NotificationGroupSelect.test.jsx` (denied vs failed), `InventoryFormModal.test.jsx` (shared lock), `CertificatesTable.test.jsx` (shared not bulk-selectable).

The UI locks on `isRoutingLockedRow` = `routing_locked || shared_access` (`hooks/useMonitorTeamPick.js`; `routingLockOf(modal, teamId, pickTeams)` in the nine monitor forms, `InventoryFormModal`, manual-cert `TrackingFields`). `monitorDetailSync`: the trigger response keeps both flags, a LIST row is authoritative (absent = false, `freshestRow` / `reloadAndSyncDetail` drop them).

The forms reset to `ROUTING_DEFAULTS` / `INVENTORY_ROUTING_DEFAULTS` (`withRoutingDefaults`, same object back when already default) and lock the fields as soon as such a team is picked or duplicated into.
  - **Tier is routing** (it picks the certificate alarm level and the escalation contacts): a grant-only writer can't change it (`requireSameInventoryRouting.tierChanged`); a grant-only inventory edit without `ug_team_id` keeps the stored UG team (null used to clear it).
  - **Restore:** `restoreChange` by a grant-only writer skips the routing fields (response `skipped_routing`, UI toast `chg.restoreRouting`), returns 403 when ONLY routing differs (k6 too since 2026-10-16).
  - **Team moves are never shared:** inventory "Devret" is hidden and bulk selection excludes `routing_locked` rows; the monitor bulk bar skips them for team changes (toast `bulk.teamSkippedShared`; bulk 7/24 is already skipped server-side as FORBIDDEN).

The grant dialog closes and reloads on a 404 (`access.err.gone`); HTTP status comes from `errorInfoOf(res)`, never `res.status`.

A duplicate from a Dashboard card locks when the source row is `routing_locked` and the team is unchanged; the inventory table's inline tier editor is off on `routing_locked` rows. The DNS update resolves a team change only for standalone rows (as before round 5); a derived Port row whose body team equals its effective (inventory) team is an alignment, not a move (round 7).

- **Bug-fix rules (2026-10-22):**
  - **Monitor notes follow the server gate (UIM-13 / K5):** `/monitoring/notes` rows carry `can_modify` (= `monitoring.crud/edit` ∧ the edit / delete gate `requireModify`: author while still able to operate the note's team, global admin, or `canManage(note team)`; teamless legacy note = author or global admin) and the payload `can_edit_guide` (the guide-save / add-note gate). The nine monitor pages and `DnsDetailModal` always pass `canManage={canManageRow(row)}` to `MonitorNotes`; per note, Edit / Delete render only when `can_modify !== false` (absent → the row gate); a note the viewer may not change shows a lock with `mnote.cannotModify`.
  - **Restore applies the update path's alarm rules for EVERY monitor type (ALERTS-8 / ADMIN-2, `applyRestoreAlarmRules`):** restoring a paused (`active=false`) snapshot closes this monitor's open alarms silently, a DNS domain / ping host change closes the OLD key's alarms ("Sistem (domain değişti)" / "Sistem (host değişti)"), a DNS snapshot without the expected-value lock closes DNS_UNEXPECTED. k6 keeps its own rename / pause branch.
### The check pipeline
`SchedulerService.@Scheduled(cron=…)` (default `0 0 * * * *`) acquires the distributed lock and fans out to `CertificateCheckerService`, plus parallel sweeps for `PortCheckerService`, `DnsCheckerService`, `UptimeHttpCheckerService`. Concurrency uses a dedicated `certCheckExecutor` `ThreadPoolTaskExecutor` (bean in `WebConfig`, sized by `EXECUTOR_CORE_SIZE` / `EXECUTOR_MAX_SIZE` / `EXECUTOR_QUEUE_CAPACITY`).

- `CertificateCheckerService` retries **only NETWORK-class** failures once (`CHECK_RETRY=true`). SSL/DNS/CERT errors are never retried — they're real findings.
- TLS handshake fingerprint: `TLS_MODE=browser` forces TLS 1.2 + ALPN `[h2,http/1.1]` so WAFs (Akamai/F5/Imperva) don't RST. Switch to `default` only to debug.
- Outbound proxy: `HTTP_PROXY_HOST`/`PORT` + `NO_PROXY` (suffix match) — applies to OCSP/CRL fetches too.
- `ChainValidationService` does chain + OCSP (primary) + CRL (fallback) via BouncyCastle. CRL responses are Caffeine-cached (`CRL_CACHE_TTL_HOURS`).
- **Trust authType (CHECKS-1, 2026-10-22):** `TrustEvaluator.evaluate` validates with SunJSSE authType `UNKNOWN` (digitalSignature or no KeyUsage — TLS 1.3's value), then `RSA` (keyEncipherment-only RSA) / `ECDH_ECDSA` (keyAgreement-only EC); trusted when one passes, the FIRST failure's reason is kept (`TrustEvaluator.checkServerTrustedAnyAuthType` — also for every pinned / extra trust manager a caller checks by hand: manual uploads, the diagnose probe's pinned-CA fallback `RawHttpProbe.trust`). Never pass `getPublicKey().getAlgorithm()` as authType: the JDK end-entity check (runs whenever the validated chain has > 1 cert) threw "Unknown authType: EC" — every ECDSA certificate and every RSA certificate without keyEncipherment was UNTRUSTED on the hourly check, manual uploads and diagnose. After the 20.165.1 deploy EC rows turn TRUSTED on their next check, open `UNTRUSTED_CA` alarms (only with `site.monitor.trust.alert-untrusted`) close by reconciliation with a normal resolution mail, TLS grades RISE (no notification), auto-pin re-pins once. Gates `TrustEvaluatorAuthTypeTest` (real two-certificate EC / RSA chains), `RawHttpProbeTrustTest`.
- **Unresolvable host = DNS (CHECKS-2):** `CertificateCheckerService.check` maps `SsrfGuard.UnresolvableHostException` to `error_class=DNS`, stage `dns`, "Domain resolution failed" (alarm level WARNING). It is NOT counted by the bulk network-outage ratio (final review 2026-10-22): the result carries `unresolvable_host: true` (`CertificateCheckerService.UNRESOLVABLE_HOST`) and `SchedulerService.networkErrorCount` skips it — a decommissioned / mistyped host is a real finding, and a small run of them opened a fake outage (admin mail, the run's alarms suppressed). Same outage semantics as 20.165.0 (those were `BLOCKED`, not counted); a connect-time `UnknownHostException` still counts. Known limit (unchanged from 20.165.0): a corporate resolver outage is not detected as a bulk outage through this path. Gate `SchedulerNetworkErrorCountTest`.; only real policy blocks stay `BLOCKED`. Gate `CertificateCheckerReviewTest` (real SsrfGuard).
- **`http_status` on the checked port (CHECKS-10):** a cert checked on a port ≠ 443 takes `http_status` from a HEAD to `https://domain:port/` (same safe redirect rules); HSTS stays the host-wide 443 HEAD. The HEAD is sent only when the port speaks HTTP: the handshake negotiated ALPN `h2` / `http/1.1`, or the port is in `CertificateCheckerService.KNOWN_HTTPS_PORTS` (4443, 5443, 7443, 8000, 8008, 8080, 8081, 8443, 8843, 8888, 9443, 10443) — `httpLikely(port, alpn)`; LDAPS / SMTPS / IMAPS / AMQPS rows keep `http_status` null (unknown) instead of an hourly invalid request and a 4–15 s wait. Gates `CertificateCheckerReviewTest.nonDefaultPort_httpStatusFromThatPort|nonHttpPort_noHeadProbe|httpLikelyRule`.
- **Port HTTP with IP version v4 / v6 (CHECKS-5):** `PortCheckerService.doHttpPinned` connects to the vetted address and writes the GET by hand (`httpOn`, shared with the proxy path): SNI = host, `Host` = `host[:port]` (IPv6 literal in brackets), total budget `httpTotalMs` via its own watchdog. `HttpURLConnection` silently drops a `Host` header, so never set it there again. Gate `PortCheckerPinnedHttpTest`.
- **TLS profile probe tries every address (CHECKS-9):** `TlsProfileProbeService.directConnector` = `NetworkResolver.connectFirstReachable(addrs, …)`, the reached address is tried first by later probes.
- **Revocation reason (2026-10-08, user request: "show the address fields even when empty, remove misleading warnings").**
  - **Backend:** `checkRevocationDetailed` returns status + reason (`RevocationReason`: OCSP, CRL, NO_ENDPOINTS, UNSUPPORTED_SCHEME, UNREACHABLE, NO_ISSUER, NOT_CHECKED, PENDING) + attempts JSON. These are stored in `latest_checks` / `certificate_checks` `revocation_reason|revocation_detail`. The status verdict and the network calls are unchanged.
  - **Health row:** NO_ENDPOINTS is NA (not an issue, no "check access" advice). The OCSP/CRL evidence keys are ALWAYS present; null renders "Sertifikada tanımlı değil".
  - **Legacy rows:** with no reason, a read certificate (fingerprint present) with both addresses blank is treated as NO_ENDPOINTS (`RevocationReason.effective` = `utils/revocationInfo.js`).
  - **Adding a reason or attempt code:** add TR+EN `hlth.revReason.*` / `hlth.revFail.*` and keep the JS list in sync (`revocationInfo.test.js` reads the Java constants).
- **Bulk network outage detection** (cert sweep): if `NETWORK_ERROR_THRESHOLD` (default 0.50) of a run fails with network errors and at least `NETWORK_MIN_ERRORS` (3) failed, individual cert alarms for that run are suppressed and a single `NetworkOutageEvent` + admin email is raised instead. This is intentional — don't "fix" it by always alerting.
  - **The DB row is the state machine (2026-10-21, multi-pod R1-C1, MP-01).** `service/outage/CertNetworkOutageService.onRun(networkErrors, total, rate, suspected, minErrors, threshold)` (called from `SchedulerService.runCheckForDomains`, returns "process alarms?"). Detection = `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE NOT EXISTS (open CERT row)`; only the pod that INSERTED sends the admin alert. Resolution = per open row `UPDATE … SET status='RESOLVED' … WHERE id=? AND status='ONGOING'`; only the pod whose UPDATE changed the row sends the resolved mail (with several legacy open rows only the newest gets the mail, the rest `SKIPPED_DUPLICATE`). While an outage is open no run processes alarms (they only quietly close verified-healthy alarms). **Small runs (review S-2):** a non-suspected run with fewer checks than `min-errors` resolves the outage only when its network-error rate is below the threshold AND either it checked the whole active network inventory (`certificate_inventory` active, `cert_source` ≠ MANUAL — an inventory smaller than `min-errors`) or the open outage was last CONFIRMED more than `MAX_OUTAGE_AGE_MS` (24 h) ago (round 3, N5: column `last_confirmed_at`, set by the detection INSERT and by every suspected run (`SQL_UPDATE_STATS`), entity `insertable/updatable=false`, added by `NetworkOutageSchemaPatch`; `sinceLastConfirmedMs` = MAX(COALESCE(last_confirmed_at, detected_at)) — a multi-day outage re-confirmed every hour by the full sweep is never closed by 5-min stale sweeps over a few healthy domains, no daily close / re-detect / mail cycle; the age close still fires when no run can confirm, i.e. an inventory smaller than `min-errors`; WARN + trace `SKIPPED: kesinti küçük turla kapatıldı — …`); the resolved mail is still sent. Any other small run still cannot resolve.
  - **Mail state on the row:** `alert_mail_status|claimed_ms|attempts`, `resolved_mail_status|claimed_ms|attempts` (entity columns `insertable/updatable=false` — only the service writes them; `service/outage/NetworkOutageSchemaPatch` adds them idempotently). Claim = conditional UPDATE on PENDING / RETRY only; result SENT / mail-service final status / RETRY (`FAILED` after 24 attempts). Any pod's next cert run retries pending mails. A SENDING claim older than 10 min (`CLAIM_STALE_MS`) is NOT re-sent (the pod may have died after SMTP): every run's `expireStaleSending` turns it into `UNKNOWN` + a trace.
