Resolving the outage (`SQL_RESOLVE`) and the one-time dedupe map the alert mail PENDING / RETRY → `SUPERSEDED` (the resolved mail carries the timeline), stale SENDING → `UNKNOWN`, a fresh SENDING is left to the live sender. `QUEUED_RETRY…` is a final mail decision even when its `notification_logs` trace could not be written (round 3, N6 — the 421 chain is still running, a resend would deliver a second copy): a failed trace goes to the per-pod `pendingTraces` (≤ 200, oldest dropped with a WARN, flushed at the start of every `onRun` up to the first failure, keeps its decision time) — only the trace is retried, never the mail. **Every admin outage-mail decision is one `notification_logs` row:** trigger `NETWORK_OUTAGE`, alertEventId 0, role `SYSTEM_ADMIN`, recipient = the admin address or `-`, subject = `CertNetworkOutageService.ALERT_SUBJECT|RESOLVED_SUBJECT` (must equal `EmailNotificationService.sendSystemAdminNetwork*`'s subject — the 421 write-back matches subject + recipient); SENT, SKIPPED (no address / mail off / passive), FAILED, the 24-attempt give-up, SUPERSEDED, UNKNOWN and a small-run close are all logged. NULL = row written before this release = treated as sent (old outages are never re-mailed).
  - **One open row per source:** `NetworkOutageOngoingDedupe` (background, own `scheduler_lock` `noe-ongoing-dedupe`, marker `2026-10-21-noe-one-ongoing-v1` written only after full success and re-read inside the lock, ≤ 24 failed attempts per pod, re-runs if the marker exists but the index is gone) keeps the newest ONGOING row per `COALESCE(source,'CERT')` and closes the rest, then on PostgreSQL creates `ux_noe_one_ongoing ON network_outage_events((COALESCE(source,'CERT'))) WHERE status='ONGOING'` (SchemaDdlGuard, 20 s lock wait). It also covers the monitoring-suppression rows of `MonitoringOutageService` (MP-16). An unreadable DB dialect is never "not PostgreSQL → done". H2 has no partial index (the app-level insert-if-absent + the `cert-check` lock cover it).
  - **Readers** — Dashboard banner `/api/system/network-status`, System Health network card (`ExtendedHealthService.getNetworkStatus` takes one `SchedulerService.networkOutageSnapshot()`), the `checkSingleDomainAsync` (inventory-add) alarm gate — read the DB through a ≤ 10 s per-pod memo (`CertNetworkOutageService.snapshot()`, dropped on the pod's own writes, generation-guarded); every pod answers the same. `detected_at` / `resolved_at` / last-run figures come from the newest CERT row (open row first). `restoreOutageStateFromDb` only logs. A stuck outage is closed by SQL (RUNBOOK "Çok pod" → "Takılı ağ kesintisi kaydı").
  - **Rollout:** an old pod's duplicate insert fails on PostgreSQL once the index exists (caught, WARN) but it may still mail from memory during the surge window. Rollback: old code ignores the mail columns (they stay). Gates: `CertNetworkOutageMultiPodTest`, `SchedulerNetworkOutageMultiPodTest`, `NetworkOutageOngoingDedupeTest`, `NetworkOutageSchemaPatchTest`, `NetworkOutageIndexPostgresTest`, `CertNetworkOutageMultiPodTest.staleSendingBecomesUnknown_notResent|resolveMapsStaleSendingToUnknown|queuedRetryIsFinal|queuedRetryWithoutLogRow_isFinal_traceRetried|pendingTracesAreCapped|twoDayOutage_staleSweepsNeverResolve|lastConfirmedIsRefreshedBySuspectedRuns|failingSmallRunNeverResolvesByAge|everyAdminMailDecisionIsLogged|skippedDecisionsAreLogged|fullSweepOfSmallInventoryResolves|maxAgeResolvesStuckOutage`, `CertNetworkOutageMailLogTest` (subjects equal the mail service's), `NetworkOutageOngoingDedupeTest.closedDuplicateMailStatusIsFinal`.
- **Per-domain proxy override (`use_proxy`)**: `CertificateInventory.useProxy` (column `use_proxy`, default false) flips a single domain to go through `HTTP_PROXY_HOST/PORT`. `SchedulerService.runCheckForDomains` builds a `domain→forceProxy` map and calls `checkAsync(domain, port, forceProxy)`. Decision: `forceProxy && proxyEnabled() && !shouldBypassProxy(domain)`. Direct outbound stays the default — added so production can route a couple of WAF-quirky domains through the corporate proxy without dragging the rest along.

### Inventory metadata that drives behavior
`CertificateInventory` carries a wide operational schema beyond the cert URL. The fields the rest of the system actually branches on:
- **`tier` (1-4)** — 1: Customer-Facing Prod, 2: Internal Prod, 3: UAT/Pre-Prod, 4: Dev/Sandbox. Drives sort order, criticality badges, and escalation contact selection.
- **`useProxy`** — see above.
- **`teamId` / `ugTeamId`** — primary (SY) and secondary (UG = Uygulama Geliştirici) team ownership for escalation routing.
- **`active`** — `false` = paused (monitoring stops, record kept). **Deletion is PERMANENT (2026-10-07, user decision: "silme işlemi her şekilde kalıcı olsun")** — there is no bin, no restore, no `inventory.purge` permission and no auto-purge setting any more. Every inventory delete (single, bulk, network or manual row) goes through `PermanentDeletionService.deleteInventory`: owner-scoped silent alarm close, then in one transaction the derived Port/DNS monitors + their checks, uptime checks, certificate checks, `latest_checks`, notes + revisions, weak-algorithm exception, certificate diagnostic runs, manual versions and the row (flushed, so the name is free at once). Kept on purpose: closed `alert_events`, audit, `monitor_change_log` (DELETE row, "kalıcı silme"), notification/activity logs, `pinned_cas`. Standalone Port/DNS delete is permanent too (alarms closed, checks deleted, no DNS revival); inventory-derived Port/DNS rows still only pause. `deleted_at` columns stay (raw SQL reads them) but nothing sets them. A deleted name never blocks re-adding (create, rename, import, manual key, suggested key, by-domain all ignore old bin rows). The one-time `DeletedRecordsPurge` (marker `2026-10-07-permanent-delete-bin-purge`, audit `SYSTEM_DELETED_RECORDS_PURGE` with the name lists) purged every legacy bin row at startup. UI: deletes remove the card at once and hide it for 10 min via `utils/recentlyDeleted.js` (the cache bus normally clears the other pods within ~2 s and the deleting tab's next GET carries `X-Cache-Min-Gen`; the mark stays for an old pod during a rollout, a missing bus table or a DB hiccup); confirmations say permanent and name the records. Never reintroduce a soft delete or a delete without a confirmation. Deleting an inventory-DERIVED Port / DNS row (= pause) closes that monitor's open alarms silently (owner ctx, "Sistem (izleme duraklatıldı)") like the update path (2026-10-22, ALERTS-7) — the paused row is never swept and its ghost alarm blocked other teams' alarms on the same key.
- Boolean operational toggles: `externalVendor`, `openshift`, `sslPinning`, `internalCert`, `jksKeystore`, `wafEnabled`, `evCertificate`, `actionRequired`, `transferredToSy`, etc. These are admin-facing flags that show up in InventoryManager and on reports — they don't gate the check pipeline.

When you add a new boolean toggle: append to the `INVENTORY_FLAGS` array in `frontend/src/utils/inventoryFlags.js` (the single source — `inventory-flags-sync.test.jsx` locks its order against the Java side), add the EMPTY default and save-payload entry in `frontend/src/components/inventory/InventoryFormModal.jsx` (the form lives there, not in `InventoryManager.jsx` — it is shared with the dashboard card's Edit/Duplicate buttons), add the diff-builder entry in `AdminController.buildInventoryDiff`, and add the setter call in `updateInventory` — missing the setter is the most common bug (the field "saves" but disappears on reload).

**Inventory write rules (2026-10-22).** Inventory edit / bulk / SY & UG transfer stamp `updated_by` (`monitorHistory.stampUpdated`, ADMIN-4); `DOMAIN_EDIT` also diffs `groupName`, `ugTeamId`, `checkIntervalHours` (ADMIN-10); an SY team change drops a notification group of the old team and a UG equal to the new SY (ADMIN-13, the `moveAll` rule); bulk by `domains` resolves case-insensitively and counts misses as `skipped` — an all-miss request returns 200 `{processed:0, skipped:N}` (ADMIN-16); certificate notes are stamped with the domain's inventory team (ADMIN-14; the author edits / deletes while managing that team); team create name check is case-insensitive (ADMIN-12); a blank `leader_id` in `PUT /teams/{id}` clears the leader (ADMIN-11 — the team form sends `leader_id: ''` when the leader is cleared). **CSV import:** true → false `active` closes the domain's open alarms silently (`closeAlertsOnDeactivate`, like edit / bulk); the dry run never writes — groups are only looked up (`MonitoringGroupService.canonicalOrNew`, never `getOrCreate`); a cell over its VARCHAR cap (`InventoryImportService.COLUMN_CAPS`) is a row error `value_too_long` (column in `changes`, `inv.importReason.value_too_long`) instead of rolling back the whole file; a team change drops a notification group of another team and a UG equal to the new SY; updates with a session stamp `updated_by`. Gate `InventoryImportFixesTest`.

### Self-monitoring / watchdog (2026-10-11, user request)
- `service/watchdog/*`: `WatchdogService.tick` every `site.monitor.watchdog.interval-ms` (60 s) under `scheduler_lock` `watchdog` (via `SchedulerService.callWithSchedulerLock`) → `WatchdogProbes.runAll` (fixed, cheap queries: sweep-leader lease, `MonitoringOverviewService.orgTypes()` stale ratio, `MAX(latest_checks.checked_at)`, notification attempted/sent in 30 min, stuck PENDING push rows + circuit breaker, `DatabaseHealthService`) → `WatchdogTransition` (pure: `confirm-checks` consecutive results to flip, reminders every `reminder-hours`, UNKNOWN never flips, NA (check no longer applies) closes an open problem with a RECOVER, undelivered `*_FAILED` retried every 15 min) → ONE combined `WatchdogNotifier.send` per round (mail `WatchdogMail`, webhook, DIRECT push via `sendDirect` — never the outbox it monitors; system-maintenance mute → `SKIPPED: sistem bakımı`; every send logged in `notification_logs` trigger `WATCHDOG`, alertEventId 0). State in `watchdog_state` (one row per `WatchdogCheck`); DB unreachable → in-memory per pod + database-only local evaluation; when the DB is readable again a NEWER memory state replaces the stored row, so the outage's recovery is notified (2026-10-12). One evaluation per interval cluster-wide: the lock is released after each round, so a scheduled tick SKIPS (`RECENT`) when the stored states were evaluated within the last interval by any pod. A pod that skips with `RECENT` drops memory states not newer than the stored rows (no stale "problem" reused in the next outage). A SKIPPED e-mail (channel off / passive-only) is not an attempt (→ NO_CHANNEL, no 15-min retry loop); system push skips passive accounts and follows `push_lang`. Endpoints: PUBLIC `GET /api/public/health/watchdog` (status + check keys only; 503 on confirmed CRITICAL or evaluation older than max(5 min, 5 × interval)), `GET /api/admin/system/watchdog` (`system_health.read`), `POST …/evaluate` (`system_health.actions/execute`, audit `WATCHDOG_EVALUATE`; runs under the same lock — 409 `WATCHDOG_BUSY` while another round runs, 409 `WATCHDOG_DISABLED` when off and the button is hidden), `POST …/test` (global admin, 3/min, audit `WATCHDOG_TEST`). Settings group `watchdog` (`WatchdogSettings` validates ranges/addresses; recipients / push-users / webhook GLOBAL_ONLY). UI: first System Health section (`health/WatchdogSection`, `watchdogModel.watchdogReasons` feeds the overall band). Adding a check: enum in `WatchdogCheck` (labels + problem text), a probe, a `WatchdogMessages.detail` case. Gates: `WatchdogTransitionTest`, `WatchdogProbesTest`, `WatchdogServiceTest`, `WatchdogNotifierTest`, `WatchdogSettingsTest`, `WatchdogControllerTest`, `WatchdogPostgresTest`, `SystemHealth.test.jsx`, `watchdogModel.test.js`, `e2e/watchdog.spec.js`. Runbook: "Kendi kendini izleme". The "Test notification" failure toast names every channel with an i18n status word (`wd.testFailedChannels`, e.g. "e-mail: Skipped · webhook: Not delivered · push: —"), never the raw Turkish server status (2026-10-22, UIA-D9).
- **Watchdog notification states (2026-10-12):** a problem this pod opened and ANNOUNCED from memory during a DB outage gets a RECOVER when the newer stored row (another pod) is healthy (stored status exactly `OK` AND last result `OK` / `NA` — `WatchdogService.storedHealthy`; a status-less row is not a recovery, review rounds 4–5). If the stored row is not healthy YET (status OK with an unconfirmed PROBLEM / UNKNOWN last result) the announced problem waits in the per-pod `pendingRecoveries` map (one entry per check, survives `memory.clear()`): RECOVER once the row is healthy, dropped when the row turns PROBLEM (that row then owns it) or another pod announced the recovery (review round 6, `WatchdogServiceTest.pendingRecoverySurvivesPruning`) — pruning no longer swallows it (`announcedProblem` + `recoverOrphans`). System-maintenance mute stores `ALERT_MUTED` / `REMIND_MUTED`; nothing is retried while muted, and when the mute ends a still-open problem is sent ONCE. No channel → `*_NOCHANNEL` (not retried, reminder timer runs, the UI never says "sent"; keys via `watchdogModel.notifiedKey`). Gates: `WatchdogServiceTest.mutedProblemIsSentAfterMaintenance|staleMemoryPrunedOnRecentSkip|prunedProblemStillOpenIsNotRecovered`.
- **Watchdog on N pods (2026-10-21, multi-pod R1-A, MP-09):** the evaluation lock is `watchdog` with a 2 min renewed TTL (`WatchdogService.LOCK_TTL`). While the DB is unreadable (local mode — `evaluateLocalDatabase`, or an `evaluate` whose state read failed) a notification is sent only if `mayAnnounceLocally`: no scheduler (single pod / tests), or `SchedulerService.lastConfirmedSweepLeader()` (the last successful lease read said "leader" — DB errors don't change it), or the follower fallback `followerMayAnnounce` (its last successful observation showed a stale lease — remaining < TTL/2, i.e. the holder missed a renewal — AND ≥ TTL + confirm × interval have passed since). Otherwise the state advances in memory only (merged with the stored row when the DB returns; no second RECOVER). Known limit: if the leader dies and the DB goes down within the same ~90 s, every follower may announce (a duplicate is preferred over silence). Recovery of a problem this pod announced during an outage (`recoverOrphans`) is CLAIMED first (review 2026-10-22): `UPDATE watchdog_state SET notified_kind='RECOVER_SENDING', notified_at=? WHERE check_key=? AND (notified_kind IS NULL OR NOT LIKE 'RECOVER%' OR notified_at < <this pod's announcement>)`; only the pod that updates 1 row sends, then finalizes (`RECOVER` / `RECOVER_FAILED|_MUTED|_NOCHANNEL`, conditional on the claim). A claim older than 5 min (`RECOVER_CLAIM_STALE_MS`, the sender died) is taken over ONCE by any pod that reads the row (`RECOVER_RETRY`, conditional) and re-sent — at least once with a cap of two attempts (a duplicate "recovered" is preferred over a lost one); a stale `RECOVER_RETRY` becomes `RECOVER_UNKNOWN` (no more sends, WARN), and the pod whose conditional UPDATE set it writes ONE `notification_logs` row (`WatchdogNotifier.recordDecision`: trigger `WATCHDOG`, alertEventId 0, no recipient, `SKIPPED: düzelme bildirimi sonucu bilinmiyor — …`; SKIPPED so it does not count as a failed delivery in the notifications health probe). The admin screen shows no "sent" line for the interim kinds (`watchdogModel.notifiedKey` ignores unknown kinds). Gates: `WatchdogMultiPodTest` (3 pods → one ALERT + one RECOVER, follower fallback after the wait, live leader → follower never, 2 min lock TTL, `recoverIsClaimedThenFinalized|staleRecoverClaimIsRetriedOnceWithCap|followerOwnLeaseChecksDoNotFakeStaleLease`), `WatchdogPostgresTest.recoverStampOnRealSchema`, `WatchdogNotifierTest.recordDecisionWritesTraceOnly`.
- **Cluster-wide facts + clock alignment (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b, R2b-C2, MP-36 / MP-37c).** The evaluation round runs on whichever pod wins `watchdog`; a check's STATE may only be changed by database-wide facts, never by the winning pod's memory (it made `confirm-checks` flip from round to round — alarm noise or a missed alert). `push_outbox` decides on database-wide facts only: stuck PENDING ≥ 10 OR the last 30 min outcome ratio (`FAILED / (SENT + FAILED)` ≥ 50 %, at least 3 outcomes, `push_trigger <> 'TEST'`, `WatchdogProbes.SQL_PUSH_OUTCOMES` — a dead gateway turns rows FAILED within the breaker cooldown while stuck stays 0; review W-2); the circuit breaker is a fact (`circuit_open`, `evaluated_by` = the evaluating pod's name). `database` decides on `connection` / `query` / `writable` (DOWN → critical, DEGRADED → medium) plus EVERY live pod's latest `system_metric_samples` row: pool saturated (`db_pending > 0` or `db_active ≥ db_max`) or boot schema-patch failures (`schema_failed_patches > 0`, written by the sampler from `SchemaPatchRunner.last()`) → medium (facts `pool_saturated_pods`, `schema_failed_pods`, `decision_source = cluster_samples`). Without live samples (sampler off, unreadable, none yet) the evaluating pod's own pool and schema result decide as before, labelled `decision_source = evaluating_pod` (review W-3). New check `clock_skew` ("Saat uyumu" / "Clock alignment", default MEDIUM): `SystemMetricSampler` measures pod clock − DB clock once a minute (`SELECT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, midpoint of the round trip, null when the round trip > 2 s) into `system_metric_samples.clock_skew_ms` (`service/metrics/SystemMetricsSchemaPatch`; null = old pod / unmeasured); the probe reads the newest row of every pod in the last 120 min (`WatchdogProbes.livePods`: one JOIN on `MAX(sampled_at)` per instance; a pod whose newest row is a shutdown marker is stopped; live = newest sample, corrected by its own skew, within 3 min of now corrected by the evaluating pod's skew — a pod 10 min behind is still seen), the severity comes from the SPREAD between live pods (max − min of `clock_skew_ms`, > 5 s → PROBLEM medium, > 30 s → PROBLEM high — leases and locks compare POD clocks, R1's 30 s lease safety margin); the database clock offset is a fact only (`db_offset_ms` = median pod − DB; a DB 40 s off with synced pods is OK, review W-4); per-pod `deviation_ms` = skew − median (the UI shows it); one known pod → OK; no live pod with a known skew → UNKNOWN (`skew_unknown` / `no_live_samples`, counters unchanged); sampler off (`site.monitor.metrics.system.enabled=false`) → NA. Facts `pods [{pod, skew_ms, deviation_ms}]` (≤ 20, largest first), `worst_pod`, `max_abs_ms`, `unknown_pods`; detail sentence in `WatchdogMessages`. UI: `WatchdogSection` draws per-pod skew badges (`watchdog-skew-pods`, tone by threshold, names wrap) and a "Değerlendiren pod: …" line (`watchdog-evaluated-by`) on checks that carry pod-local facts (`watchdogModel.skewPods|evaluatedBy|formatSkewSeconds`). Gates: `WatchdogClusterFactsTest` (live pods incl. stopped / silent / 10 min behind / duplicate name, thresholds, unknown / NA, missing column fallback, DB-wide decision, alternating pods A,A,B,B,A,A never flap), `WatchdogClusterFactsPostgresTest`, `WatchdogClusterFactsTest.clockSkewSpreadThresholds|databaseOffsetIsInformationalSpreadDecides|deadPushGatewayIsDetectedClusterWide|schemaFailureOnAnyLivePod|samplerOffFallsBackToEvaluatingPod`, `WatchdogProbesTest.pushOutbox` (re-baselined: outcome ratio), `SystemMetricSamplerTest.clockSkewMeasuredAgainstDatabase`, `SystemMetricsSchemaPatchTest`, `WatchdogClusterFacts.test.jsx`, `e2e/watchdog.spec.js` (7 checks, skew badges unclipped at 390 / 768 / 1280).

### System metrics page (2026-10-13, user request: "sistem sağlığı üzerinden site monitör metriklerini detaylı izlemek")
- **Sampler:** `service/metrics/SystemMetricSampler` writes ONE row per pod per minute (cron second 7, no lock — every pod writes only its own `instance_id`) into `system_metric_samples`: process / system CPU, heap / non-heap, threads, Hikari active / idle / pending / max, `certCheckExecutor` active / pool / max / queue / capacity, HTTP = the sum of the minutes COMPLETED since the previous sample (`HttpMetricsService.completedMinutesAfter(lastTs, 10 + 1)` — the minute rotation runs on its own timer, so reading "the last minute" counted a minute twice or skipped one) plus `http_minutes` = the minute SPAN from the last consumed minute to the newest (a quiet minute whose bucket was never created is a 0-request minute, not a missing one; the cap asks for 10 + 1 minutes so a real truncation is told apart from exactly 10), deltas for GC, completed tasks, caller-runs, logback WARN / ERROR and Caffeine hits / misses, and `delta_minutes` = minutes since the previous sample (null on the baseline row). The first sample after a start is the baseline (deltas null) and a counter that went backwards gives null — never a fake 0; an unreadable source stays null. On a clean shutdown (`@PreDestroy`) the pod writes a final row with `shutdown_marker = true` (identity + minute only). Boot 4's `LogbackMetricsAutoConfiguration` does not activate in this app, so the sampler binds Micrometer `LogbackMetrics` once at `ApplicationReadyEvent` ONLY when no `logback.events` meter exists and unbinds it on shutdown. Micrometer registers `logback.events` as a **FunctionCounter** — look it up with `find(…).meter()`, never `.counter()` (that returned null: no counts, and a second filter on top of Boot's). A save failure never breaks the flow (one WARN per hour). Keys: `site.monitor.metrics.system.enabled` (`SYSTEM_METRICS_ENABLED`, true), `site.monitor.metrics.system.retention-days` (7, group `monitoring`, RetentionCatalog `system-metric-samples`).
- **Reads:** `SystemMetricsQueryService` aggregates in SQL — native query over a derived table that computes `SUBSTRING(sampled_at, 1, :len) AS bucket` (16 minute / 15 ten-minute / 13 hour) and groups by that COLUMN (H2 rejects `GROUP BY 1, 2`, PostgreSQL treats a re-bound parameter as a different expression), `CAST(:instance AS VARCHAR(80)) IS NULL`; new SELECT columns go at the END (`point()` reads by index: 37 `http_minutes`, 38 `delta_minutes`). The range start is aligned to a bucket boundary. Per-POD rates: counters `*_pm` = bucket sum ÷ SUM(`delta_minutes`) (never ÷ row count — the baseline row halved it and a late tick spiked it), `http_rpm` = requests ÷ SUM(`http_minutes`), rounded to THREE decimals (2 ERROR lines in an hourly bucket = 0.033/min; one decimal showed a fake 0). CLUSTER rates (client `metricsModel.podShare`, computed from the RAW totals, never the rounded server rate): Σ over pods of total × min(m, E) ÷ (m × E), E = `bucketMinutes` (a finished bucket's length, the unfinished last one's minutes passed) — summing raw per-pod rates doubled every rollout bucket, and dividing every pod by the busiest pod's minutes undercounted the others (the HTTP rotation drifts, so one pod carries 2 minutes while another carries 1); with one pod selected its own rate is shown. Shutdown-marker rows are excluded from the aggregate and from the "latest sample" read (`…AndShutdownMarkerIsNull…`). `GET /api/admin/system/metrics/overview` (`enabled`, `served_uptime_s`, pods seen in 24 h, each live pod's latest sample, last-hour totals — null, not 0, without samples) and `/series?range=1h|6h|24h|7d&instance=` (unknown range → 6h, `instance` > 80 chars → 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED`), gate `system_health.read/view`. Pod `state`: `stopped` = a shutdown marker exists (rollout / scale-down — neutral, never a warning); otherwise `live` (last sample < 3 min) or `silent` (crash, hang, cannot write — a warning however old). Two processes with the same pod name (container restart) are told apart by the id suffix, decided over the 7-day window so every screen names a pod the same way. The response carries no host / user data beyond the pod name.
- **UI:** System Health entry card → `view=metrics` (`components/admin/metrics/*`, lazy): KPI tiles from LIVE pods only (unknown = "—", neutral, never green; the queue tile takes the FULLEST pod's percentage, a sum would hide one full pod; tiles use `Tile wrapText` — label and sub-line wrap, never clip); thresholds match the main page element for element (tiles: CPU `HEALTH_THRESHOLDS.cpu`, heap `MEMORY_PCT`; charts: `HEALTH_THRESHOLDS.cpu|heap`); pod table ≥ 860 px container (headers wrap) else cards, names wrap; 15 charts per pod (safe series keys `p0…` — a pod id can't be a CSS variable name; pod colours are the non-status tokens blue / violet + mixes, never green / red / amber) or cluster totals; an empty bucket is a gap, not 0, and a value with empty neighbours is drawn as a dot (`mx-dot` — a lone point is otherwise a zero-length path); a threshold line and its legend entry show only when drawn. Only stopped pods listed (right after a rollout, before the new pod's first sample) → the pods tile is neutral; it turns red with a "samples are not being written" banner (`isNotWriting`) — never an endless "collecting" — only when the serving pod has been up longer than boot + first tick (`served_uptime_s` > `SERVING_UPTIME_GRACE_MS`, 5 min; unknown uptime → no claim) AND either no pod is listed or the newest stop is older than `FIRST_SAMPLE_GRACE_MS` (3 min). A full restart opened before the first sample therefore stays neutral. Rates (incl. the GC ms/min chart and its axis) are shown with `fmtRate` (small values keep 2–3 decimals, never rounded to 0). Sampling switched off → an "off" banner and a neutral pods tile (not "collecting", not red). A failed load says "could not be read" (pod list, charts, pods tile) — never "no pods" / "no samples"; "the last values are still shown" only when they are; a range / pod change clears the old series AND the old series failure. URL `mx_range` (default 6h not written), `mx_pod`; late responses are dropped (sequence); 60 s refresh while visible. `LogHeader`'s back button is 40 px on touch (shared with the SMTP / push log views).
- **Pod order (2026-10-17, user request: "en yeni pod en başta, canlı olan pod en önde"):** `podsSince` appends `MIN(sampled_at)` as the LAST column (index 4, marker rows counted like `MAX`) and every pod map carries `first_seen` (null on an old 4-column row). `SystemMetricsQueryService.POD_ORDER` sorts the overview pods and the `/series` pods by state (live 0 → silent 1 → stopped 2), then `first_seen` DESC (newest process first, null last), then `last_seen` DESC, then name, then id. The client mirrors it in `metricsModel.sortPods` (`PodList` + the pod picker), so an old server without `first_seen` still shows live pods first; text compares by UTF-16 code unit like Java `String.compareTo`. `seriesPods` keeps the server order (pods seen only in points go last, by name) and chart colours follow it (`p0` = the newest live pod), so colours shift when a new pod rolls out. `first_seen` is the first sample inside the 7-day name window (= retention), so two processes older than that tie and fall back to `last_seen` / name. Gates: `SystemMetricsQueryServiceTest.podOrderLiveFirstNewestFirst`, `SystemMetricSampleRepositoryTest` (first_seen), `SystemMetricSamplePostgresTest`, `metricsModel.test.js` (`sortPods`, `seriesPods`), `MetricsView.test.jsx` (list + picker order), `e2e/system-metrics.spec.js` (order).
- **Gates:** `SystemMetricSamplerTest` (HTTP span, delta minutes, shutdown marker, FunctionCounter + one turbo filter), `SystemMetricsQueryServiceTest`, `SystemMetricsControllerTest`, `SystemMetricSampleRepositoryTest` (H2), `SystemMetricSamplePostgresTest`, `HttpMetricsServiceTest.completedMinutesAfter_consumesEachMinuteOnce`, `metricsModel.test.js` (rollout bucket, rotation drift, single pod, late tick), `MetricsView.test.jsx` (incl. threshold legend), `lazyChunks.test.js`, `e2e/system-metrics.spec.js` (390/768/1280: no clipped tile text, touch targets; Turkish table headers and a lone-point dot at 1280).

### Grafana dashboard (2026-10-18, user request: "SiteMonitor'ü Grafana'da ayrı bir panoyla uçtan uca izleyeyim; her yeni sürümde panoda değişiklik gerekiyorsa JSON'u doğru güncelle, asla unutma")
- **Business metrics are NEVER on `/metrics` (2026-10-18, independent review — the first cut put monitor names, targets, teams, certificate domains / manual tracking keys and alert ids on the unauthenticated `/metrics`):** every series of `MonitoringMetricNames.ALL` (monitors, alerts, certificates / domains, notifications / storms / push, the exporter's own `sitemonitor_exporter_*`) lives in `BusinessMetricsRegistry` — a private `PrometheusMeterRegistry`, deliberately NOT a Spring `MeterRegistry` bean (Boot would add it to the composite registry, bind JVM / HTTP meters to it and back off its own Prometheus registry) — served ONLY by `MetricsExportController` at `GET /metrics/sitemonitor` (outside `/api/**`, no session) with `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Token = `site.monitor.metrics.export.token` = env `METRICS_EXPORT_TOKEN` (Helm `secret.metricsExportToken`; never in `app_settings` / the catalogue, never returned by any API — the settings page gets `token_configured` yes/no only), compared as SHA-256 digests with `MessageDigest.isEqual` (`MetricsExportToken`; header > 1024 chars refused unread). No token or < 32 chars (`MIN_LENGTH`; generate with `openssl rand -hex 32`) → the endpoint answers 404 and the exporter thread never starts (no DB load). Missing / wrong token → 401 without body + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`; 10 failures per IP per minute → 429 + `Retry-After: 60` for further BAD requests — a MATCHING token still passes from a blocked IP (behind a shared address — mesh sidecar, SNAT, ingress — strangers' bad requests must not lock Prometheus out; the compare stays constant-time); security event `METRICS_EXPORT_ACCESS_DENIED` once per IP per 10 min. Responses `no-store`; `RequestLoggingFilter` never logs `/metrics*` — skipped by the raw-URI `shouldSkip` AND listed in `BODY_NEVER_LOGGED` (the normalising check strips `;params`, double slashes and two rounds of percent-encoding, so `/metrics;x=1/sitemonitor` or `/%6Detrics/sitemonitor` never log the inventory body). Target labels keep the URL PATH (only query, fragment and userinfo are dropped): a secret embedded in a path (`/hooks/<token>`) is visible to every token holder. The token sits in the pod environment like the other secrets (k6 scripts can read pod files — `site.monitor.scripted.file-read-policy`); a Secret edited in place needs `kubectl rollout restart` (the old token works until then). `/metrics` keeps exactly what it had before the branch (JVM, HTTP, Hikari, build info, schema-patch gauges, retention / DB growth) plus the endpoint-free HTTP latency histogram (`outcome` label only). Stale guard: when the last COMPLETED refresh is older than `max(180 s, 3 × refresh-seconds)` (`MonitoringMetricsExporter.fresh()`) the endpoint serves only `MonitoringMetricNames.STALE_VISIBLE` (enabled / per-monitor / last refresh / duration — no business values, no `source_up`, so the dashboard `guard()` can't turn stale data into a fake 0), and the dashboard's full-width "Veri tazeliği" banner says why. Defaults: `site.monitor.metrics.export.enabled` true but effective only with the token, `…per-monitor` and `site.monitor.grafana.per-monitor` FALSE (team × type totals are always exported once the token exists). Gates: `MetricsExportIsolationTest` (Spring constructor takes no `MeterRegistry`; the catalogue reaches Micrometer only in the exporter; a global scrape has no `sitemonitor_monitor` / `target=` / team name), `MetricsEndpointsIntegrationTest` (real app + HTTP: `/metrics` clean, `/metrics/sitemonitor` 401 / 200 with the token, 404 without one), `MetricsExportControllerTest`, `MetricsExportTokenTest`, `MonitoringMetricsExporterTest.noTokenNoWork|staleExporterHidesBusinessValues`, `RequestLoggingFilterTest.trace_metricsPaths_skipped`. **Upgrade / rollback:** an existing Prometheus job on `/metrics` keeps working for JVM / HTTP; business data needs the new job (`/metrics/sitemonitor` + `authorization: {type: Bearer, credentials_file: …}`) and the token — without it the dashboard shows the freshness banner "veri yok". Rolling back to a build before this change puts business series back on the unauthenticated `/metrics`.
- **Metrics (`service/metrics/*`):** `MonitoringMetricNames` is the ONLY catalogue (name, scope ENTITY / AGGREGATE / CLUSTER / POD, fixed label set, help). `MonitoringMetricsExporter` refreshes on its OWN daemon thread (`refresh-seconds` 60, first run 45 s after `ApplicationReadyEvent`; never the shared 8-thread scheduler, never the request path; not started at all without the token) and writes Micrometer `MultiGauge`s into `BusinessMetricsRegistry` (never the global registry) with ONE stable value holder per series (`register(rows, overwrite=false)`: an existing series keeps its meter and only its value changes — re-registering every round made scrapes miss series; a vanished monitor's series disappears; holders are kept strongly because `DefaultGauge` holds its object through a WeakReference, which turned values into NaN after a GC); the pure maths is `MonitoringMetricsModel`. Sources: the org Monitoring Overview memo (`build(ALL, …, 24)`; additive flags `types[].unavailable|checks_unavailable|stats_unavailable`, top-level `alerts_unavailable` — written ONLY on a read failure, so the normal response is byte-identical), `AlertEventRepository.findOpenMetricRows` (narrow open-alarm projection, ownership = `AlertOwnership`: first owner `team`, second `ug_team`), `CertificateInventoryRepository.findDomainOwners`, `CertificateService.getAllLatest` (cached), `DomainCheckRepository.findLatestPerMonitor`, active storms, 24 h notification / push grouped counts (derived-table SQL, `MonitoringMetricsSqlTest` on H2), watchdog `publicStatus`, the sweep-leader lease READ-ONLY (`ClusterScheduleService.lease`, never `acquireLease`), held `scheduler_lock` rows (`name:key` collapsed to `name:*`), DB health, system-maintenance block. Target labels are scheme + host + port + path only (`MonitoringMetricsModel.safeTarget`: query and fragment are cut, userinfo dropped — the old name-list masking missed `key=`, `code=`, `sig=`, `X-Amz-Signature`, `subscription-key`); every label ≤ `LABEL_MAX` 160 chars (fits real AD team names; series still differ by `monitor_id` / `alert_id`). Families: per-monitor `sitemonitor_monitor_{state (0 up, 1 stale, 2 down, 3 unknown, 4 paused),up,response_ms,avg_response_ms_24h,last_check_timestamp_seconds,checks_24h,failed_checks_24h,open_alerts,open_alert_severity,open_alert_since_timestamp_seconds,interval_seconds}{type,team,team_id,group,monitor,monitor_id,target}`; `sitemonitor_monitors{type,team,team_id,state}` (all five states, zeros included) + `_checks_24h` / `_failed_checks_24h`; `sitemonitor_teams` (team variable source, + "Takımsız" id 0); `sitemonitor_alerts_open{family,alert_type,team,team_id,ug_team,ug_team_id,level,acknowledged}`, `_open_by_level{level}` (each alarm once), `_oldest_open_timestamp_seconds`, per-alarm `sitemonitor_alert_open_since_timestamp_seconds{alert_id,…}`; certificates `sitemonitor_certificate_{days_remaining,status (0 valid…4 expired, 5 error),tls_grade (6 A+ … 1 F),last_check_timestamp_seconds}` + `sitemonitor_certificates{status|expiry bucket|tls grade}`; `sitemonitor_domain_days_remaining`, `sitemonitor_domains_expiry`; storms, `sitemonitor_notifications_24h{channel,trigger,status class}`, push deliveries / outbox / stuck / circuit / enabled; `sitemonitor_watchdog_{status,check_status,evaluated_timestamp_seconds}`, `sitemonitor_sweep_leader` (per pod; the sum must be 1), `_lease_remaining_seconds`, `sitemonitor_scheduler_lock_held{name}`, `sitemonitor_database_status`, `sitemonitor_system_maintenance_state`; multi-pod runtime (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b, R2b-C2) `sitemonitor_cluster_lock_lost` (POD, cumulative since the pod started: locks lost during renewal — `ClusterLockService.lostCount()`, once per hold), `sitemonitor_sweep_leader_transitions{reason}` (POD, cumulative GAINED / LOST / RELEASED — `service/metrics/SweepLeadershipCounter` listens to `SweepLeadershipChangedEvent`, all three reasons always exported), `sitemonitor_push_lease_renew_failed{reason}` (POD, cumulative `error` = renewal query failed / `lost` = rows taken by another pod — `UserPushService.leaseRenewFailures()`, renewForSend + holdLease), `sitemonitor_clock_skew_seconds` (POD, pod − DB clock, only while the sampler's last measurement is < 3 min old), `sitemonitor_eval_inbox_depth` (CLUSTER, one `COUNT(*)` on `monitor_eval_inbox` — `ManualEvalInbox.depth()`, source `eval_inbox`; unreadable → series dropped); multi-pod Sürüm 3-1 (2026-10-23, all `Scope.POD`) `sitemonitor_eval_state_mode` (0 off, 1 write, 2 write + verify — this pod's recorder; absent without a recorder), `sitemonitor_eval_state_pending`, `sitemonitor_eval_state_write_failures` (cumulative since the pod started), `sitemonitor_eval_state_verify_mismatches{kind}` and `sitemonitor_eval_state_verify_timestamp_seconds` (only while this pod is the WRITING leader in verify mode — `EvalStateSink.Status.active` ∧ mode `verify`; a former leader's stale report and, after verify → write, the recorder's last report are not exported; an `unknown` round writes the timestamp but NO mismatch series — absent ≠ 0), `sitemonitor_exporter_org_families` (1 = this pod exported the organisation-wide families in its last refresh); the exporter's own `sitemonitor_exporter_{enabled,per_monitor,last_refresh_timestamp_seconds,refresh_duration_seconds,source_up{source},series{family},capped{family}}`; plus the endpoint-free HTTP histogram `sitemonitor_http_server_duration_seconds{outcome}` (`HttpLatencyMetrics`, SLO buckets, fed by `HttpMetricsInterceptor`, `/api/**` only). **Rules:** a source that fails DROPS its families (`source_up=0`), never a fake 0 / up — a source read in several steps (push, watchdog, lease, DB, maintenance) collects into a temporary family set merged only after the whole read succeeded (no half rows); every overview type writes `source_up{source="monitors:<type>"}` 1 / 0; deleted monitors are not exported; inventory-inactive derived Port/DNS rows count as `paused` in the totals only; org values are identical on every pod that exports them (dashboard `max by (labels)`), pod-specific ones are per pod; **org-family placement (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 3-1, plan 3e):** per refresh `exportOrg = !sweepLeaderHint() || !otherFreshOrgExporter()`, the second term reading the `cluster_observations` row `metrics-org` (permanent name, `ClusterHealthStore.OBS_METRICS_ORG`) written by ANOTHER instance within `2.5 × refresh-seconds` + the publisher's last refresh duration (payload `duration_ms`, capped at the stale threshold; the row carries the refresh COMPLETION time — `MonitoringMetricsExporter.ORG_OBSERVATION_FRESH_FACTOR`), that is not a shutdown tombstone (`{"stopping":true}`) and whose publisher is not KNOWN dead (the exporter's own `PodLiveness`, built only when the exporter starts — the shared side-job bean is untouched: heartbeat + shutdown marker; unknown liveness = the row is trusted); only a NON-leader writes that row, and only after a COMPLETE org export (no `source_up = 0` that round) — a leader write would overwrite the follower's ("newest wins") and keep the leader exporting; a follower with failing reads stops publishing so the leader resumes. A leader that sees a fresh foreign row exports only `Scope.POD` families + the exporter's own (no overview build, no alert / certificate / notification / lock / inbox / watchdog / maintenance reads; no CLUSTER / AGGREGATE / ENTITY series, no `source_up` rows for those sources, no `capped` rows — absent ≠ 0; the push circuit breaker is exported from pod memory without a `push` source row so `guard()` never turns another pod's unreadable push counts into 0; `sweep_leader` (POD) stays on every pod, `sweep_leader_lease_remaining_seconds` (CLUSTER) is dropped there). Single pod, unreadable row, missing cluster store, a 20.167.0 neighbour (never writes the row) → the leader exports as today. Leadership change: the new leader keeps exporting until the old one (now a follower) publishes → overlap, no gap. **Shutdown (review M1, 2026-10-23):** after `ContextClosedEvent` (`onContextClosed`, `@Order(HIGHEST_PRECEDENCE + 1)` — right after the lease release, before `NotifyExecutorDrain`; flag only, no wait, no DB on the shutdown thread) a pod never writes `metrics-org` — the old leader whose hint turned false used to publish as a "follower" and silence the survivor for 1.5–3.5 min; if the row is THIS pod's, the exporter's own thread writes a tombstone over it (1 ms newer; a live sibling's fresh row is never erased); the closing pod keeps exporting until it dies (overlap, never a gap). A POD-only leader re-checks the row every `ORG_RECHECK_MS` (10 s, one PK read) and runs a refresh at once when it is no longer the leader or the row is gone / stale / a tombstone / from a dead publisher; otherwise the 60 s cadence (single pod unchanged). Followers' exporters stuck → the row goes stale → the leader resumes within ≤ 2.5 × refresh + 10 s. Status card: `MonitoringMetricsExporter.Status.orgFamilies` (entities empty in a POD-only round; old 10-arg constructor kept = `orgFamilies true`). Gates: `MonitoringMetricsExporterTest.orgFamiliesPlacement_*|evalStateMeters|evalStateMeters_writeModeHidesOldVerify`, `MonitoringMetricsExporterMultiPodTest.followerCloses_leaderResumesWithinSeconds|closingLeaderNeverSilencesTheSurvivor` (two exporters, distinct instance ids, real `ClusterHealthStore` on H2: follower closes → leader exports within ≤ 15 s; a closing old leader never silences the survivor); no user data in labels (targets = scheme + host + port + path, labels ≤ 160 chars); gauge names never end in `_total|_count|_sum|_bucket|_info|_created` (`MonitoringMetricsScrapeTest` scrapes a real `PrometheusMeterRegistry`). Settings (group `grafana`, GLOBAL_ONLY, live): `site.monitor.metrics.export.enabled|per-monitor|max-entities (5000, 100–50000; cap per family keeps problem records first, WARN once an hour)|refresh-seconds (60, 30–600)`. Business series are served only on the token-protected `/metrics/sitemonitor`; still block `/metrics*` at the Ingress and scrape inside the cluster (RUNBOOK "Grafana panosu ve Prometheus").
- **Generator (`service/grafana/GrafanaDashboardGenerator`, pure):** options (`GrafanaDashboardOptions` ← `GrafanaDashboardSettings.current`) → Grafana 12.x classic dashboard JSON: `__inputs` `DS_PROMETHEUS`, `__requires` grafana ≥ 12.0.0 + prometheus + the panel types used, `id: null`, `schemaVersion` 41, `uid` / title / time / refresh / language from settings, `version` = `DASHBOARD_REVISION`, description "revizyon N · SiteMonitor <version>". Variables `datasource`, `team` (label_values of `sitemonitor_teams`, multi + All `.*`, optional default team), `type` (custom, ALL `MonitorTypeCatalog.ORDER` types incl. cert), `monitor` (per-monitor only), `level`, `topn`, `job` / `instance` (All WITHOUT allValue — runtime panels never match other apps). Seven sections (`SECTIONS`: overview, alerts, monitors, certificates, notifications, health, runtime; first three rows open), 100+ panels, every panel titled + described, deep links `<link base>/?tab=<type>&monitor=<id>`, `?tab=alerthistory&alert=<id>` / `&view=open&team=<id>`, `?tab=dashboard&domain=<d>&open=cert`, `?tab=domain&monitor=<id>` (no links when no base URL), annotations for deployments and critical / high alarm openings. The health section ends with the multi-pod runtime panels (multi-pod Sürüm 2b, `clusterRuntime()`: inbox depth stat (cluster `max`), "Pod saatleri arası fark" / "Clock spread between pods" stat (`max(sitemonitor_clock_skew_seconds) - min(…)`), lost locks and push lease failures in 24 h (`sum(increase(…[24h]))`), clock difference per pod (`max by (instance)`), cluster events per pod per hour (`increase(…[1h])` by instance / reason)); `DASHBOARD_REVISION` is 6 (since review L2, 2026-10-23: the "Veri tazeliği" / "Data freshness" banner counts only pods that export the org families — `time() - max((last_refresh and on(job, instance) (org_families == 1)) or (last_refresh unless on(job, instance) org_families))`, pre-3-1 pods counted through the `unless`; a POD-only leader's fresh round no longer shows "Up to date" while the business panels are empty; no value = "no pod exports the organisation metrics or /metrics/sitemonitor is not scraped"); the health section ends with the multi-pod Sürüm 3-1 panels (`evalStateAndPlacement()`, after `clusterRuntime()`): evaluation state mode, verify mismatches (pass / fail kinds only: `sum(max by (kind) (…{kind=~"recovery|sibling|suppression|round_seen|confirmation"}))` — "no data" when the round was unknown or verify is off, never 0), last verify (`time() - max(…verify_timestamp_seconds)`), pending state writes (highest pod), state write failures (24 h `increase`), pods exporting org metrics (`sum(sitemonitor_exporter_org_families)`, 0 = red: business panels empty), a per-kind mismatch time series (descriptor / window_seed are measurements) and a per-pod org-export / pending-writes time series. The "Unreadable sources" stat counts each source ONCE however many pods report it: `count(min by (source) (sitemonitor_exporter_source_up) == 0)` (it counted N× with N pods, MP-37b). Team filter on two-owner series = `X{team=~"$team"} or X{ug_team=~"$team"}` (`owned`), per-team tables count UG ownership too (`ownedPerTeam(m, labels, sum|min, …)` aggregates `team` / `team_id` away BEFORE label_replace — two SY teams sharing one UG otherwise produced duplicate label sets and an empty panel); the exporter carries only two owners per alarm (a rare third owner is not exported — documented in the model); empty-but-readable sums go through `guard()` (`… or (vector(0) and on() (max(source_up{source}) == 1))`), monitor counts through `monitorGuard()` (also "no data" while a type matching `$type` is unreadable on EVERY pod — a partial total never looks like the whole fleet); oldest-alert stats show 0 = "None" (green) when nothing is open. Deployment annotation = `count by (version, commit, environment) (build_info) unless … offset 5m` (`changes()` never fires on a constant new series — `docs/GRAFANA_SURUM_ANOTASYON.md` carries the correction); alarm-opened annotation = the alarm's OPEN epoch (the series value) `> time() - 900`, `* 1000` with `useValueForTime` — one line exactly at the opening, and a series gap (pod restart, an unreadable exporter round) can never re-announce open alarms as a false storm (an `unless … offset 1m` form did). Range panels never re-evaluate a `[$__range:$__interval]` subquery per step: the state-timeline filter carries `@ end()` (evaluated once; Prometheus ≥ 2.33) — `GrafanaDashboardGeneratorTest.noPerStepRangeSubqueries`. Type-labelled series (`{{type}}` / `{{family}}`) get per-type `displayName` overrides (`typeNames`). Build JSON only with ordered maps (`om(...)`, never `Map.of` — its iteration order is random per JVM and would break the content hash).
- **Settings page:** Settings → Platform → **Grafana panosu** (`?sec=grafana`, `components/admin/grafana/*`, model `grafanaModel.js` mirrors the server rules; the test reads the Java `SECTIONS` and controller `FIELDS`). `GrafanaDashboardController` `/api/admin/grafana`: `GET /settings` (ADMIN; scoped admin `read_only`, no team directory; values, options, live exporter status of THIS pod, saved summary, metric catalogue), `PUT /settings` (GLOBAL admin; only sent + changed fields, an unchanged stored value is not re-validated, empty change writes nothing; field errors 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` + `field`; audit `GRAFANA_SETTINGS_SAVE`), `POST /dashboard/preview` (ADMIN, unsaved values → summary, no write — `AuditCoverageTest` exemption), `GET /dashboard` (GLOBAL admin, SAVED settings, `attachment; filename=sitemonitor-grafana-dashboard-<version>.json`, `application/json`, audit `GRAFANA_DASHBOARD_EXPORT`). UI: three set-up steps (copyable scrape config, token warning), exporter live card (running / degraded / stale / waiting / disabled / no_token — unknown never green; `exporter.org_families` false = this pod exported only its own pod metrics in its last refresh → one sentence (`grafana.exp.orgElsewhere`) instead of the entity tiles, absent (old server) = old view, review L8, gate `GrafanaDashboardControllerTest.exporterOrgFamilies`; `waiting` also while the last round still ran disabled; stale = `max(180 s, 3 × refresh)` like the server), step 1 shows the token as "Tanımlı / Tanımlı değil" only (no row when an old server sends no `token_configured`), the copyable scrape config has TWO jobs (`/metrics`, and `/metrics/sitemonitor` with `authorization: {type: Bearer, credentials_file: …}` — never a token value), a warning that the token opens the whole inventory, and step 3 says a per-team dashboard (default team + UID) is a convenience, NOT access control (Grafana applies no team scoping); the JSON download's 401 goes through the session path (`api.sessionPing()` → `request()`'s 401 branch), a network error never shows browser text, form + section tiles + exporter settings with field errors under the fields (only CHANGED fields are validated, like the server — a stored default team that no longer exists never blocks another field; a save bumps the load sequence so a refresh in flight can't overwrite it), debounced sequence-guarded preview with unsaved values, metric catalogue, save bar; "Download JSON" becomes "Save and download JSON" when dirty.
- **Keep it updated — PERMANENT RULE:** every release that adds / renames / removes a `sitemonitor_*` meter, a monitor type, an alert type family or any dashboard-relevant feature updates `GrafanaDashboardGenerator` (panel / query / variable / link), bumps `DASHBOARD_REVISION`, APPENDS the line the gate prints to `src/test/resources/grafana/dashboard-content.sha256` (an append-only log: `revision=N tr=… en=… tr_no_monitor=…` — Turkish, English and per-monitor-off variants; when the content changes but the revision does not, the gate prints NO hash, only "bump the revision", so editing the line in place cannot make it green; a bump without a content change is red too) and the e2e fixture (`mvn -o test -Dtest=GrafanaDashboardFixtureTest -Dgrafana.writeFixture=true`). Gates: `GrafanaMetricCoverageGateTest` (every `sitemonitor.*` Micrometer literal is on the dashboard — EXACT names from `Summary.metrics()`, which `GrafanaDashboardGenerator.metricsIn` tokenizes as PromQL (strings, label matchers, ranges, Grafana variables, function calls, `by (…)` lists, keywords and `label_values(…, label)` are skipped), never a substring search — or in `NOT_ON_DASHBOARD` with a reason — the list only shrinks; every `MonitorTypeCatalog` type is in the `type` variable and `TYPE_TABS`, every link tab is in the frontend `VALID_TABS`), `GrafanaDashboardRevisionGateTest` (content SHA-256 vs revision), `GrafanaDashboardFixtureTest`, `GrafanaDashboardGeneratorTest` (import contract, unique ids, non-overlapping gridPos incl. collapsed rows, datasource + description + targets on every panel, defined variables, balanced PromQL, only known metrics, links, sections / per-monitor switches, settings applied; `multiPodHealthPanels|multiPodR31Panels|freshnessCountsOnlyOrgExporters`), `MonitoringMetricsModelTest`, `MonitoringMetricsExporterTest`, `MonitoringMetricsScrapeTest`, `MonitoringMetricsSqlTest`, `GrafanaDashboardSettingsTest`, `GrafanaDashboardControllerTest`, `grafanaModel.test.js`, `GrafanaDashboardSettings.test.jsx`, `e2e/settings-grafana.spec.js` (390 / 768 / 1280; real download parsed: `__inputs`, team / type variables, 30+ panels, no empty titles). Not imported into a live Grafana here — field names follow the Grafana 12 classic schema; import it once after the first deploy and report problems.

### Log format, request id, tracing (2026-10-11, user decision: "prod JSON, local plain text")
- **Format:** every logback encoder is `config/logging/SwitchableLogEncoder` (`site.monitor.log.format` = `LOG_FORMAT` text|json; prod profile defaults to json, everything else text; `LOG_JSON_FORMAT` ecs|logstash|gelf → Boot `StructuredLogEncoder`). JSON without a Spring Environment or an unknown format falls back to text with a logback WARN — logging never stops. Never reintroduce a bare `PatternLayoutEncoder` (`SwitchableLogEncoderTest.shippedConfig`).
- **Request id on every line:** `CorrelationIdFilter` puts the id in MDC `requestId` for the request (restored in finally); text patterns render `${PAT_CORR}` = `rid=<id> trace=<traceId> ` (each part only when present). Pool work keeps it via `MdcTaskDecorator` (certCheckExecutor, loginIssueMailExecutor, Boot's applicationTaskExecutor through a `ThreadPoolTaskExecutorCustomizer` — deliberately NOT a global `TaskDecorator` bean, which would also hit the scheduler and pin a request id onto dynamically scheduled jobs).
- **OpenTelemetry:** `spring-boot-micrometer-tracing-opentelemetry` + OTel bridge + OTLP exporter with the JDK sender (OkHttp/Kotlin excluded; no `micrometer-registry-otlp` — metrics stay Prometheus). Spans exist always (traceId in logs, 10 % sampling), export only when `management.opentelemetry.tracing.export.otlp.endpoint` is set (Helm `config.otel.endpoint`, full `/v1/traces` URL). No Boot HTTP client builders are used, so checks to monitored sites never carry `traceparent` — keep it that way. `scripts/image-boot-check.sh` detects ERROR lines in both text and JSON. Gates: `SwitchableLogEncoderTest`, `CorrelationIdFilterTest.mdcDuringRequestOnly`, `MdcTaskDecoratorTest`, `TracingExportConfigTest`. Runbook: "Günlükler ve izleme".
- **OpenTelemetry Java agent (2026-10-21, prod start crash: the OpenTelemetry Operator's `instrumentation.opentelemetry.io/inject-java` added the agent + `OTEL_*` env; Boot 4.1's `OpenTelemetryEnvironmentVariableEnvironmentPostProcessor` mapped `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL=grpc` into Boot's OWN exporter → `OtlpGrpcSpanExporter` → "No GrpcSenderProvider found on classpath").** `config/tracing/*`: `TracingCoexistenceEnvironmentPostProcessor` (`spring.factories`, `DeferredLogFactory`, never breaks the start) feeds the pure `TracingCoexistence.decide`. Agent detection = `OpenTelemetryAgentProbe` (bootstrap class `io.opentelemetry.javaagent.bootstrap.AgentInitializer` via `Class.forName(…, false, null)`, fallback an `otel`/`opentelemetry` `-javaagent:` path; `otel.javaagent.enabled=false` = absent). Agent + `site.monitor.tracing.defer-to-agent` (default true, `SITE_MONITOR_TRACING_DEFER_TO_AGENT`) → FIRST property source `siteMonitorTracingCoexistence`: `management.tracing.export.enabled|.otlp.enabled=false` (the exporter-specific key must be set too — it wins over the global one in `OnEnabledTracingExportCondition`, and the mapping sets it from `OTEL_TRACES_EXPORTER`; the global one also switches Boot's W3C propagation to noop), `management.logging.export.enabled|.otlp.enabled=false`, `management.opentelemetry.enabled=false` (no SDK tracer / span processor / logger provider), `management.opentelemetry.map-environment-variables=false`, marker `site.monitor.tracing.agent-active=true` → `AgentTracingConfiguration` registers `OpenTelemetry.noop()` (Boot's SDK backs off): Micrometer's `OtelTracer` opens NO span of its own (a no-op span wraps the agent's current span), so agent JDBC/Hibernate spans stay children of the agent's server span — ONE trace system (verified with the real 2.32.0 agent: no orphan parents, incoming `traceparent` continued). Without the agent (or switch off) nothing changes, except the gRPC guard: Boot's effective transport `grpc` + export active + no `io.opentelemetry.sdk.common.export.GrpcSenderProvider` (`GrpcSenderProbe`, ServiceLoader, no instantiation) → that signal's `…export.otlp.enabled=false` + ONE WARN (http/protobuf, 4318, `config.otel.endpoint`); never a silent protocol/port switch. Order vs Boot's mapping EPP (both lowest precedence; ours loads first in the fat jar) does not matter: agent mode pins `map-environment-variables=false`; otherwise the processor runs Boot's OWN mapping first when its source (`openTelemetryEnvironmentVariables`) is absent, and pins the switch only when it overrides something. The agent's client instrumentation is warned about at start (`traceparent` would reach monitored sites). Text logs: `PAT_CORR` uses `%traceCorrelation` (`TraceCorrelationConverter`, `<conversionRule>` in `logback-spring.xml`): agent MDC `trace_id` first, else `traceId`; empty / all-zero / non-id values never written. JSON keeps the MDC as is. Helm `config.otel.agent.{enabled,instrumentation,serviceName,extraEnv}` (default off ⇒ byte-identical render; `fail` with `config.otel.endpoint` or a manual `podAnnotations` inject annotation) adds the annotation + container env (the operator never overrides it): `OTEL_INSTRUMENTATION_JAVA_HTTP_CLIENT_ENABLED=false`, `OTEL_INSTRUMENTATION_HTTP_URL_CONNECTION_ENABLED=false` (the app's clients to monitored sites / third parties are java.net.http + HttpURLConnection; SMTP/LDAP/dnsjava/raw sockets are not instrumented), `OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER=none`, `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME`, `MANAGEMENT_OPENTELEMETRY_MAP_ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLES=false`, `OTEL_INSTRUMENTATION_COMMON_LOGGING_TRACE_ID_KEY=traceId` / `…SPAN_ID_KEY=spanId` (JSON field names unchanged; the non-`_KEY` names are deprecated in 2.32 and WARN), `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES`, and `OTEL_INSTRUMENTATION_SANITIZATION_URL_EXPERIMENTAL_SENSITIVE_QUERY_PARAMETERS` (review 2026-10-21: agent server spans record `url.query`; the weekly / technical-error approval links carry `?token=` valid 14 days; the setting REPLACES the agent's built-in list, so `OpenTelemetryAgentProbe.SENSITIVE_QUERY_PARAMETERS` repeats it — single source for `_helpers.tpl` `site-monitor.otelSensitiveQueryParameters` and the RUNBOOK; the app warns at start when the agent's list misses `APP_QUERY_CREDENTIALS`). A new endpoint that reads a credential from the QUERY must add its name to `APP_QUERY_CREDENTIALS` (gate `OtelAgentQueryRedactionGateTest` scans `@RequestParam` / `getParameter` and forbids credential-like PATH variables — the sanitizer does not cover paths). An explicit `podAnnotations` `inject-java: "false"` counts as not set (`site-monitor.manualOtelInject`). Agent mode also replaces Boot's `Slf4JEventListener` with `AgentTracingConfiguration.ValidSpanSlf4JEventListener` (no `traceId=000…0` in MDC when no agent span is current — e.g. an unobserved `@Scheduled` run; zeros in MDC would also stop the agent from adding its own `traceId`). Without the agent the processor runs Boot's mapping only on a COPY of the environment with a silent log (decision only) and applies it for real — once, with its warnings — only when it overrides something. With defer off the WARN says whether the app's own spans are really exported. No-release workaround for older images: env `MANAGEMENT_OPENTELEMETRY_MAP_ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLES=false` (+ the two client disables + the sensitive-query list). A new HTTP client library toward monitored sites must get its agent instrumentation disabled in the Helm block. The Spring @Scheduled instrumentation stays ON (agent default; review 2026-10-23): the agent's JDBC instrumentation needs no parent span, so without the tick span every statement of a tick would be its own root trace. Gates: `TracingCoexistenceTest` (truth table), `TracingCoexistenceContextTest` (real Boot auto-config + real mapping EPP via `OtelEnvMappingTestSupport`: crash reproduced without the fix, both EPP orders, pass-through span, noop propagation, Boot warnings once, invalid span not in MDC), `OpenTelemetryAgentProbeTest`, `GrpcSenderProbeTest`, `OtelAgentQueryRedactionGateTest`, `SwitchableLogEncoderTest.textCorrelationAgentTraceId`. Runbook: "Günlükler ve izleme".

### Request/response trace logging (off by default)
`RequestLoggingFilter` (`@Order(LOWEST_PRECEDENCE - 10)`) can log every HTTP request and response with method, URI, headers, body, status, and duration. It's behind `log.isTraceEnabled()` so the default `com.sitemonitor=DEBUG` level keeps it silent.
- Enable on demand: `logging.level.com.sitemonitor.config.RequestLoggingFilter=TRACE` (env or properties).
- Sensitive values are masked with `*******` via three regex patterns (JSON body / form body / URL query) covering ~60 field-name variants — EN + TR (`password|parola|sifre`, `*_password`, `token|*_token`, `secret|api_key|client_secret`, `private_key`, `session_id|jsessionid`, `pin|otp|mfa_*|verification_code`). Sensitive headers (`authorization`, `cookie`, `x-api-key`, etc.) are masked too.
- JSON values are masked by a LINEAR scan after the key prefix (`RequestLoggingFilter.maskJsonStringValues`, 2026-10-22, AUTH-12): escaped quotes (`\"`) no longer end the value early and an unterminated (truncated) value is masked to the end; never a group-loop regex on request bodies (Java recursion → stack overflow). `webhook_url|webhookUrl|url_secret|urlSecret` are in the name list.
- Skipped paths: `/health`, `/favicon.ico`, anything under `/assets/` or `/static/`, and common static extensions (`.js .css .map .png .svg .woff2 .ico`).
- Body log is truncated at 2000 chars.
- Bodies NEVER logged (request and response, `BODY_NEVER_LOGGED`): secret tools, login OTP, manual certs, `/api/admin/ldap`, `/api/monitoring/scripted` (env VALUES are free-named secrets; also covers the 2026-10-19 secret reveal response), `/api/admin/user-push` (2026-10-12). JSON keys whose name ENDS with password / passwd / secret / token / api_key / apikey / private_key (e.g. `bind_password`, `webhook_secret`) are masked too (`SENSITIVE_JSON_SUFFIX_FIELDS`, bounded 64-char prefix — linear).

### Escalation & notifications
`EscalationService` selects recipients from `escalation_contacts` based on alert severity (`minAlertLevel`) and org role. `EmailNotificationService` and `WebhookService` (Teams/Slack) deliver. Every attempt logs to `notification_logs` (SENT/FAILED + error). Re-alert dedupe is daily — `EscalationServiceTest` pins this behaviour; preserve it.

**`notification_logs` columns are fitted in ONE place (2026-10-12):** `NotificationLog.fitColumns` (`@PrePersist`/`@PreUpdate`) fits every VARCHAR(255) column — recipient / CC lists keep whole addresses and end with ` … +N`, other values end with `…`. A too-long value used to fail the INSERT on PostgreSQL and the caller's try/catch dropped the trace silently. Callers need no clipping of their own; an SMTP-log resend refuses a fitted recipient list (`RECIPIENTS_TRUNCATED`) and a row whose `message` is a summary / note rather than the mail body (`SmtpLogQueryService.SUMMARY_ONLY_TRIGGERS` = the tech-error reminder triggers → `SUMMARY_NOT_RESENDABLE`; review 2026-10-18 — a resend would have mailed the channel summary + push note to the team). Storm RESOLVE mails also reach the members' stamped on-call addresses (`alert_events.oncall_emails`, `StormService.planOncall`, review round 4): an on-call address joins a team's storm mail only if it received the opening of EVERY member of that dispatch; otherwise it gets a separate mail listing only the members it received (team isolation — another team's target names never leak), and each member's resolution reaches an address once across dispatches (co-owned SY + UG members). Round 5: an address that is already a recipient of the team mail of the dispatch that reports a member is seeded as "reported" (`seedTeamMail`), so it never gets a second on-call mail; an inventory row whose UG team equals its SY team is ONE dispatch (`resolveDispatches`), not two. Gates: `NotificationLogFitColumnsTest`, `SmtpLogNocRedactionTest.truncatedRecipientsAreNotResent`, `StormSilentMemberRecoveryTest.recoveryMailReachesOncallWhoGotTheAlarm|oncallSeesOnlyTheMembersItGot|partialOncallGetsSeparateMail`.

**One mail, N log rows — the 421 result reaches all of them (2026-10-22, ALERTS-16):** the quiet digest and storm mails log one row per covered alarm (same subject, recipient text, role, trigger, written together). `EmailNotificationService` write-back (`writeBackRetryStatus`, also the late correction of `STATUS_RETRY_ORPHANED` rows) and the heartbeat (`heartbeatPendingRetries`) update the matched row AND its siblings: same subject + `recipient_email` + `recipient_role` + `trigger` + status prefix, `sent_at` within ±2 min (`SAME_MAIL_WINDOW`) of the matched row — one JDBC UPDATE (no 20-row cap; LIKE prefix escaped), fallback without JDBC = the last 20 rows. The sibling UPDATE binds the FITTED status (`NotificationLog.fit(status, VARCHAR_MAX)`, the same text the matched row gets from `@PreUpdate`) — a raw JDBC bind of a long `FAILED: <SMTP text>` failed with 22001 and left the siblings queued (C1). Gate `MailRetrySameMailWriteBackTest.longFailedStatusReachesSiblingsFitted`. Mails resent elsewhere (7/24 `LOG_ROLE`, tech-error report trigger) are single-row and get no siblings. Before, N−1 rows of a delivered digest / storm mail turned "FAILED: … gönderen pod kapandı". **SMTP-log resend splits the stored recipient list (ALERTS-14):** `resendStoredHtml(to, cc, …)` splits on `,` `;` whitespace, trims, drops blanks and duplicates (case-insensitive), and sends the logged CC too (minus To addresses) — a resend is the same mail; a fitted CC list (`… +N`) is refused like a fitted To (`RECIPIENTS_TRUNCATED`). Before, every multi-recipient row failed with "Illegal address". Gates `MailRetrySameMailWriteBackTest` (H2, 25-member storm), `EmailDeliveryFixesTest`.

**Recipient routing (team vs escalation)** — two helpers, `isStandaloneMon(alertType)` + `includeManagerContacts(alertType, level)`, govern three paths (initial alert `processConfirmedOutage`, resolution `sendResolutionNotification`, manual resend `reNotify`) and MUST stay consistent across them:
- Standalone monitoring alerts (keyword/ping/http/`TYPE_DOMAIN_EXPIRY`/all `DOMAINMON_*`) resolve the team from `AlertEvent.teamId` (stamped at alarm creation) — **not** the certificate inventory. Standalone domain monitors aren't in `certificate_inventory`, so resolving their team from inventory lost the owning team (the "resend went to the müdür, not the team" bug — fixed by routing all three paths through `event.getTeamId()`).
- **Escalation contacts come ONLY from the alarm's owning team(s)** (product decision 2026-09-28) — single source `EscalationContactScope.forOwners(repo, level, syTeamId, ugTeamId)`: SY and UG each contribute only THEIR OWN contacts (union; one mail per address, webhooks deduped by URL); `teamId == null` → empty, repository never queried. There is **no global fallback**: if a team has no contact at that level the alarm goes to the team's own recipients only (team address / notification group / push) plus a WARN log. Another team's manager or contact must never be added, and teamless contacts are never recipients (cannot be created — 400). The old "fall back to global contacts" path used unscoped `findByActiveTrue…` / `findByMinAlertLevel…` queries that returned EVERY team's contacts (cross-team leak, v3.0.0 → v20.89.0); `EscalationContactScopeTest` is the source gate, `EscalationContactLeakTest` the behaviour gate. The team manager (`teams.manager_id` / LDAP) is NOT an automatic alarm recipient.
- **Acting on an alarm follows ownership (2026-10-09, user decision).** `AlertOwnership` (predicate `EscalationService.isStandaloneEvent`): a standalone alarm is owned ONLY by `AlertEvent.teamId`; inventory-routed alarms (cert, ACCESSIBILITY, unstamped derived Port/DNS) by the stamp + inventory SY/UG. Ack, resolve, re-notify, recipient preview, bulk and incident comments use it; rows carry `act_scope` so the UI hides actions. READ scope is unchanged (JPQL lists). Gate: `AlertStandaloneOwnerScopeTest`.
- **Level gate (2026-09-19, all monitor types):** `includeManagerContacts` = `!WARNING` — WARNING alarms of standalone monitors are team-only; HIGH/CRITICAL add the owning teams' escalation contacts by their `minAlertLevel`.
- **Standalone vs inventory routing:** standalone monitors (HTTP/Keyword/Ping/Page/Scripted/PageSpeed/Domain and user-added Port/DNS — `standalone` flag / team stamp in context) take their team ONLY from the event/monitor, never from the inventory. Cert alerts, ACCESSIBILITY and inventory-DERIVED Port/DNS (context deliberately unstamped — `SchedulerService.alarmTeamOf`) route like the inventory: SY + UG, each team with its own contacts. Ownerless alarms (no team after full resolution) send nothing on any channel. `TeamAdminService.moveAll` re-stamps OPEN alarm events; a team with open alarms or SY/UG-bound records cannot be deleted (409).
- **Manual "Check now" never raises an alarm** (product decision 2026-09-29, prod incident: a scoped manager's bulk "Check Now (39)" on Synthetic opened alarms and an org-wide storm push). Every trigger endpoint records the result, then evaluates with `handleSweepResults(type, items, manual=true)` — which ONLY runs `reconcileRecoveries`: no new alarm, no confirmation chain, no escalation / re-alert / storm, no suppression bookkeeping; a healthy result may close the open alarm. The next scheduled sweep opens alarms under the normal rules. `evaluateDomainAlarmsNow` is the MANUAL entry; the scheduled critical-domain check uses `evaluateDomainAlarms(m, r, false)`. A manual check also never sends a domain threshold reminder (`DomainExpiryReminderService` runs only from the daily/critical sweeps), never trips `ScriptedAnomalyGuard` (`evaluate(m, res, manual)`; the timeout streak counts scheduled runs only — `scripted_checks.manual`), and never consumes DNS change detection (manual rows are stored with `dns_records.manual = true`; the sweep baseline is `findLatestScheduledSuccessful`; domain registration likewise: manual/live rows and confirmation/recovery rechecks (`checkRecheck`) are stored with `domain_checks.manual = true` (out of band), `DomainCheckerService.checkManual`, scheduled baseline `findLatestScheduledWithData`; the Registration tab reads stored data on open and only runs a live lookup on Refresh; manual DNS rows are never change events). Gates: `ManualCheckNoAlarmTest`, `ManualCheckWiringTest`, `ManualCheckMarkerQueriesTest`. List rows carry `can_check` (= `SessionScope.canOperateTeam` on the effective team) and the UI counts/queues only those. On a follower pod the result is forwarded to the sweep leader (`monitor_eval_inbox`, see "Manual 'Check now' on a follower") and evaluated there with the same manual rule.
- **Manual re-notify content comes from the alarm (2026-10-22, ALERTS-1).** `reNotifyAsync` builds monitoring-type mails from the event: context = the alarm-time snapshot (`contextJson`; DOMAINMON = newest domain check + snapshot keys via `withSnapshot`, DNS_CHANGED = last changed record + snapshot, `detected_in_maintenance` dropped from a fresh change ctx), message = the event's own message with the current level word (`withLevelWord`) — rebuilt only from fresh data (DOMAINMON_EXPIRY / DOMAINMON_STATUS, DNS_CHANGED with a record) or when no message is stored. Days are passed only for duration types; `isDurationAlert` = cert rule for certificate types, `EXPIRY_MONITORING_TYPES` (DOMAINMON_EXPIRY, DOMAIN_EXPIRY, KEYWORD_DOMAIN_EXPIRY) for monitoring types — no "245 GÜN KALDI" on a blacklist / lock / status mail, no "N gün kaldı" hero on a non-expiry escalation step. The snapshot's `mail_disabled` is honoured (team + contact mail skipped, webhooks / push as usual). `buildMessageBody` has a case for every monitoring type (KEYWORD, PING_DOWN, PING_SLOW, DOMAINMON_TRANSFER_LOCK, DOMAINMON_BLACKLIST were falling to the certificate text) — gate `EscalationAlarmEngineFixesTest.buildMessage_neverCertificateTextForMonitoringTypes`; a new monitoring type needs a case. **Preview = send for the mail flag (C2, final review 2026-10-22):** the FIXPLAN decision stands (the alarm-time `mail_disabled` is honoured on a manual resend, like push honours the alarm-time `push_disabled`), so the preview and the count follow it: `EscalationService.reNotifyMailDisabled(id)` (monitoring / domain types only — a certificate alarm's context comes from `latest_checks` and never reads the stamp); `previewReNotify` returns no e-mail row, `reNotify`'s `recipients_queued` counts 0 e-mails, and `GET /admin/alerts/{id}/re-notify/preview` adds `email_block_reason: "SKIPPED_MONITOR_OFF"` (additive; absent = mail channel open). A push-only manual resend (every e-mail recipient removed in the dialog, K13) logs its e-mail leg as `STATUS_SKIPPED_EMAIL_DESELECTED` = `SKIPPED: e-posta alıcısı seçilmedi (elle gönderim — yalnız push / webhook)`, not "alıcı yok". Gates `EscalationAlarmEngineFixesTest.reNotify_mailDisabledSnapshot_previewEqualsSend|reNotify_mailEnabled_previewListsTeamAndSends|reNotify_certAlarm_ignoresSnapshotMailFlag|reNotify_pushOnly_logsDeselectedNotNoRecipient`. The re-notify confirm (`ReNotifyConfirmModal emailBlockReason`) shows `alh.renotifyModal.emailMonitorOff` instead of "no recipients" when the preview carries `email_block_reason: SKIPPED_MONITOR_OFF` and counts no e-mail row; absent field = today. Gate `ReNotifyEmailBlocked.test.jsx`.
- **Snapshot keys (2026-10-22, ALERTS-2):** `RESOLVED_CONTEXT_KEYS` also keeps `record_type`, `old_values`, `new_values`, `change_detail`, `detected_in_maintenance` (deferred maintenance change alarms are announced from the snapshot) and `monitor_name` (subject of manual re-notify / escalation step). `completeDeferredInitialNotification` passes the stored message (`processConfirmedOutage(…, presetMessage)`), so the deferred INITIAL carries the old → new values and the stored rich message is never overwritten.
- **Push-only re-notify (K13, UIM-8, 2026-10-22):** `reNotify(id, excludeEmails, excludeUsernames)` is refused only when no e-mail AND no push (a `UserPushService.preview` row with status `PENDING`, not excluded) AND no contact webhook remains; refusal text through `Msg.t`. Push-only returns the usual `{status: queued, recipients_queued: 0, …}` and the client toasts `alh.notifyQueuedPush`.
- **Resolution decisions leave a row (2026-10-22, L-E4 / L-Q2):** an ownerless resolution writes `SKIPPED: takım yok`, a skipped resolution mail writes `SKIPPED: e-posta kanalı kapalı` / `SKIPPED: alıcı yok` (trigger = the resolution trigger, subject = `resolutionSubject`).
- **Alarm history delivery view (2026-10-22, ALERTS-19):** `WhyOpenChips` reads `webhook_sent_count` — an alarm delivered only by the team's Teams/Slack contact webhook is not "kimseye ulaşmadı" (a `data-why-webhook` chip shows the count); `QUEUED_RETRY…` mail rows show "Kuyrukta — yeniden denenecek" (raw SMTP text only in the title); `MANUAL_RESOLVE` has its own label and the green logo (`utils/mailPreview.mailLogoVariant` treats it as a resolution — the SMTP log preview too).
- **Alert storms are TEAM-scoped** (same decision): scope key `TEAM:<id>` (per-group: `TEAM:<id>|GROUP:<g>`); the window count, threshold (COUNT, or PERCENT of the team's own active monitors — `totalActiveMonitorsForTeam`), root cause and every notification come from the owning team's events only; ownerless events never storm. Each dispatch (SY, and UG for co-owned inventory rows) shows ONLY its own count/name/root cause — there is no "Tüm monitörler" / org-wide storm; legacy `ACCOUNT`/group storms are disbanded by `lifecycleSweep`. Storm push level = the highest member level (`stormPushLevel`), never wider than the individual alarms' audience. The threshold counts DISTINCT targets (`distinctTargets` — one host's ACCESSIBILITY/PORT/DNS/HTTP alerts are one target); PERCENT has an absolute floor of 3 (`PERCENT_MIN_TARGETS`). Legacy storms stay inert for `site.monitor.storm.legacy-retire-grace-minutes` (15) after start-up, then `retireLegacy` moves still-down members into their team storm silently (above threshold) or releases them as already notified (`releaseFromStormAsNotified`) — never individual INITIALs; the recovery of recovered members goes only to their own team. Gate: `StormTeamIsolationTest`.
- **Storms have a bounded lifetime and every silent decision leaves a trace (2026-09-30, prod incident: the SY team's 3 permanently failing synthetic tests kept a team storm above the hysteresis floor forever, so `StormService.evaluate` attached EVERY new DOWN alarm of that team silently — no email, push, webhook, NOC, `notification_logs` row or push-decision row; the UI showed "0 notifications").** `alert_storms.last_member_at` is stamped at creation and on every attach; once `site.monitor.storm.quiet-minutes` (default 5, 5–1440, Settings → Alert Storm → Quiet Window) pass without a new member the storm is SEALED: `evaluate` sends individually instead of attaching and `lifecycleOne` resolves it regardless of still-down count. On resolve, still-down members announced in a storm mail (`alert_storm_members.announced_at` since 2026-10-22 — see "Announced = the member row" below; `createdAt ≤ storm.lastReAlertAt` only for legacy members without a row) are released as notified (`releaseFromStormAsNotified`), unannounced ones are unlinked (`lastReAlertAt=null` → individual INITIAL on the next sweep). The SUPPRESSED branch of `processConfirmedOutage` calls `recordStormSuppression` (log row trigger `STORM`, status `SKIPPED: fırtına #N …`, push row `SKIPPED_STORM`); the ownerless gate writes `SKIPPED: takım yok` + `SKIPPED_NO_TEAM`; `sendCombinedAlert` no longer returns early when mail is skipped and logs `SKIPPED: alıcı yok` / `SKIPPED: e-posta kanalı kapalı`; storm mails are logged per member alarm (`STORM_INITIAL` / `STORM_REALERT` / `STORM_RESOLVE`). The alert list/detail show a `StormBadge` (`storm_id`) and the timeline renders the `STORM` row as "notification handed over to the storm". `StormSuppressionBackfill` writes the trace once for historical storm members with no log rows. Never reintroduce a silent, unlogged suppression path — a notification that is not sent must say why in `notification_logs`. Gates: `StormSealingTest`, `EscalationServiceTest.processConfirmedOutage_stormSuppressed_leavesTrace`, `StormSuppressionBackfillTest`, `AlertHistoryStorm.test.jsx`. **Storm lifecycle on several pods (2026-10-21, R1-C1, MP-04):** `lifecycleSweep` takes the `storm-sweep` lock and only THEN reads the active list (a cheap pre-check still skips the lock when there are no storms). **Two-phase close (review S-1 / F1):** every closing path (`resolveStorm`, `disband`, `retireLegacy`) claims the storm with `SQL_CLOSE_CLAIM` (`WHERE id=? AND resolved=false AND close_state IS NULL`), writing `resolved = true` together with `close_state='CLOSING'`, `close_claimed_at`, `close_owner`, `close_attempts=1`, `recovery_state='PENDING'`, `resolved_at` (the closing moment) and `resolve_reason` (round 3, D1 / D2: the `ux_alert_storms_active` scope (`resolved = false`) is free from the claim, so a stuck close never blocks a new storm for the team; 20.164.0 pods read `resolved` and see a CLOSING storm as closed — they neither suppress a late member's individual resolution nor close it again). A CLOSING storm takes no members (`StormService.openForMembers`: `evaluate` sends individually, the counter bump requires `close_state IS NULL`). After the claim the members are RE-READ: a member counts as recovered if it was resolved by the closing moment (`resolvedBy`); members resolved later already got their individual resolution; each member step (release, unlink, move) has its own try/catch. The recovery mail / webhook / NOC / push is sent exactly once under its own marker: `recovery_state` PENDING → SENDING (CAS) → SENT; a send that throws → UNKNOWN + `STORM_RESOLVE` trace; nothing to recover → NONE. `close_state='DONE'` (`SQL_CLOSE_DONE`, also `resolved = true`) is written LAST.
