A member individually notified while its storm was closing (its own `last_re_alert_at` later than the closing moment, `StormService.notifiedAfter`) is detached with `releaseFromStormAsNotified(id, storm, <its own stamp>)` — never unlinked (an extra INITIAL) nor rewound to the storm mail (an early re-alert); legacy retirement uses `stampFor` with the same rule. A failed step leaves the storm CLOSING: `lifecycleSweep` finds unfinished closes by `close_state = 'CLOSING'` (`SQL_CLOSING_IDS`, ≤ 50, handled before the open storms; the cheap pre-check skips the lock only with no open storm AND no CLOSING row; `SQL_CLOSE_RECLAIM` / `SQL_CLOSE_DONE` no longer require `resolved = false`) and finishes one whose claim is older than `CLOSE_STALE_MS` (5 min), on any pod (`finishStaleClose`: re-claim by CAS on `close_claimed_at`, attempts + 1, previous owner logged; member steps repeated — at-least-once, all conditional; a stale SENDING recovery becomes UNKNOWN + trace "sonuç bilinmiyor", NEVER resent). After `CLOSE_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (5) the close is written DONE anyway (each stranded member gets a trace, trigger `STORM`, "FAILED: … kapanışı N denemede tamamlanamadı"; an unsent recovery → UNKNOWN + trace). Columns `close_state`, `close_claimed_at`, `close_owner`, `close_attempts`, `recovery_state` (entity `insertable/updatable=false`, `StormCloseSchemaPatch`; NULL = open or closed before this release); the one-phase fallback (`SQL_RESOLVE_CLAIM`, `claimResolution`) is gone — `AlertStorm` maps the `close_*` columns, ddl-auto and `StormCloseSchemaPatch` add them. The closing paths never `save` the whole entity (member count / peak via `SQL_RESOLVE_STATS`; the system-maintenance stamp in `sendStormAlert` is a conditional UPDATE). **Shutdown and lock owner (R-2 / CORE-4):** `lifecycleSweep` does nothing after `ContextClosedEvent` (`StormService.onContextClosed`) or once `ClusterLockService.acquiringStopped()`; a running sweep stops between storms. With the `ClusterLockService` bean the `storm-sweep` lock is taken from it (renewed in the background, `held()` checked between storms); without it the class uses its own SQL (`SQL_LOCK_RENEW` every `lockRenewEveryMs` = TTL/3, a failed renewal stops the round) with `lockOwner` = `StormService.INSTANCE_ID` (`SchedulerService.instanceId() + "-storm"`). Rolling deploy: an OPEN storm closed by an old pod's whole-entity path while a new pod closes it can still send a duplicate recovery mail in that window; rollback leaves the columns, ignored. **NOC re-claim (N-10):** re-trying an EXISTING `noc_deliveries` row (FAILED / stale SENDING) is a CAS `NocDeliveryRepository.reclaim(id, seenStatus, seenUpdatedAt, now)` (`COALESCE`d to '' for legacy NULLs) instead of an entity save; 0 rows → this pod does not send. Gates: `StormLifecycleMultiPodTest` (exception after the claim → stays CLOSING, a stale claim is finished by another pod with ONE recovery; exception mid member loop; two pods racing `finishStaleClose`; died while SENDING → UNKNOWN, no resend; give-up after 5; shutdown gate; `ClusterLockService` owner / `stopAcquiring` / lost hold; unique owner; CLOSING takes no members; `exceptionAfterClaim_*` resolved and scope free while CLOSING, `memberNotifiedWhileClosing_keepsItsStamp`, `preReleaseClosingRowIsFinished`, `onlyClosingStormStillSwept`), `StormCloseSchemaPatchTest`, `NocReclaimRaceTest`, `NocNotificationServiceTest.reclaimLostToOtherPod_noMail` (`StormCloseRaceTest` / `StormSealingTest` / `StormObservabilityRecordTest` / `StormServiceTest` / `StormTeamIsolationTest` fixtures stub `SQL_CLOSE_CLAIM` / `SQL_RECOVERY_CLAIM` / `SQL_CLOSE_DONE`).
- **Storm observability (2026-09-30).** `alert_storms` freezes an opening snapshot (`team_id`, `group_name`, `threshold_unit/value/effective`, `window_minutes`, `quiet_minutes`, `targets_at_open`, `peak_targets`, `trigger_event_id`) IN the atomic promotion INSERT (`insertStormIfAbsent(…, OpenSnapshot)`, 17 bind args, the first 7 keep their order — a separate UPDATE let a concurrent lifecycle `save` null them) and a `resolve_reason` (`FLOOR`/`SEALED`/`DISABLED`/`LEGACY_RETIRE`). Membership is durable in `alert_storm_members` (`AlertStormMember`: join kind TRIGGER/PEER/ATTACH/LEGACY, `announced_at`, leave kind RECOVERED/NOTIFIED/UNLINKED/MOVED/RELEASED) because `alert_events.storm_id` is cleared on close. Writes are JDBC on the alarm path and must stay O(1) round trips per event: opening = one `batchUpdate(SQL_MEMBER_INSERT)` (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, no exists-check), attach = one INSERT, announce = one UPDATE (IN chunks of 500), close = one `batchUpdate(SQL_MEMBER_LEFT)`; every recording call is try/catch — a failed write never blocks or delays a send. Since 2026-10-22 the member row is ALSO the "announced" source of the close (`announced_at`, see "Announced = the member row"), so a failed recording errs to the safe side: a failed `markAnnounced` leaves the row unannounced → the still-down member is unlinked and gets an individual INITIAL at close (a duplicate, never a lost notice); a failed `recordMembers` leaves no row → the legacy `createdAt ≤ storm.lastReAlertAt` rule decides. The in-flight lifecycle refresh uses a targeted `UPDATE … member_count, peak_targets = GREATEST(…)` instead of `stormRepo.save` (a stale entity save rewound `last_member_at`). Read side: `StormStatusService` + `StormStatusController` (`GET /api/monitoring/storm/status|history|analytics|{id}`, gate `alerts.read/view`, scope like `TodayPanelController.openAlerts`; `status` is memoized 10 s per scope key, `?fresh=1` bypasses; per-team active monitor counts come from ONE grouped query `StormService.activeMonitorsByTeam()` (60 s), triggers/live members are batch-loaded — never add a per-team or per-storm query to these polled paths) → `?tab=storms` (`StormStatusPage`, URL prefix `sf_`) and the Settings → Alert Storm live panel (`StormLivePanel`). Adding a storm column/table: patch it in `applySchemaPatches`, append new INSERT columns at the END (`StormTeamIsolationTest` reads the bind args positionally), register retention (`alert-storm-members-orphan`). Gates: `StormObservabilityRecordTest`, `StormStatusServiceTest`, `StormStatusControllerTest`, `StormObservabilityQueriesTest`, `StormStatusPage.test.jsx`, `StormLivePanel.test.jsx`.
- **"Announced" = the member row (2026-10-22, ALERTS-4 / ALERTS-5).** Storm close (`resolveClose`) and disband (`disbandClose`) share `releaseStillDown`: a still-down member is released as notified when its `alert_storm_members.announced_at` is set (`markAnnounced` writes it for the members listed in a storm mail), unlinked (individual INITIAL next sweep) when its row exists without `announced_at`; only events WITHOUT a member row (pre-2026-09-30) or an unreadable member table fall back to `createdAt ≤ storm.lastReAlertAt`. One query per close (`SQL_MEMBER_ANNOUNCED_READ`). A member that attached while the opening (or daily) storm mail was being sent is therefore notified individually, and turning storms off no longer re-sends an INITIAL for every announced member. The promotion race's losing side records `ATTACH` member rows too (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). Maintenance-muted storm mails mark nobody announced, so their members get an individual INITIAL at close. Per-group storm mode resolves the group by `ctx.monitor_id` (L-S1, `StormService.groupById`); URL / name / host lookup only for context without an id or an id that no longer exists.
- **Storm screens (2026-10-22):** `StormStatusPage` shows "Fırtına ayarları" only with `canOpenSettings` (App: `systemRole === 'ADMIN'`, K3; absent → `scope.global_admin`). `StormLivePanel`: a failed read is "okunamadı" + retry with "—" counters (never "0 / takım yok"); a failed poll after good data keeps the data with a stale strip (L-S3). Storm screens, the team-cell modal and the Today panel print alert type / level LABELS, never raw codes (UIM-36). The noise panel's "İzlemeyi aç" maps `cert` → Sertifika Panosu card (`domain` + `open=cert`), ACCESSIBILITY → Erişilebilirlik, otherwise the monitor tab; no target → no button (`noiseModel.noiseMonitorTarget`, ALERTS-20).
- **Optional: push not handed over to storms (2026-10-03; `site.monitor.storm.push-individual`, group `storm`, DEFAULT FALSE since 2026-10-04 by user decision: push is handed over to the storm as before).** When TRUE, a storm groups e-mail (plus webhook / NOC) only; each member alarm's push goes out individually, in alarm order.
  - **Joining a storm:** the `EscalationService` SUPPRESSED branch sends the OPEN push via `pushStormMember`. The STORM row says `… (push tek tek)` (`STATUS_STORM_MAIL_ONLY_SUFFIX`; the prefix stays), and no `SKIPPED_STORM` row is written.
  - **Storm-member level raise:** sends an ESCALATION push.
  - **Daily re-alert:** `StormService.enqueueStormPush` DAILY_REALERT queues one `enqueueAlert("DAILY_REALERT")` per open, unacknowledged member, ordered by createdAt then id.
  - **Storm INITIAL / RESOLVE:** the aggregated push is not sent, unless a storm notice was already SENT (`UserPushService.stormNoticeSent`, covers a mid-storm switch). Member resolves go through `enqueueResolve`, whose symmetry now finds the SENT OPEN row.
  - **Quiet hours:** team quiet hours apply via `TeamQuietHoursService.defersPush` → `SKIPPED_TEAM_QUIET`, with no digest item.
  - **Other rules:** cap / maintenance / realert-off / dedupe come from `enqueueAlert`.
  - **Flag false (default):** byte-identical to the pre-2026-10-03 behaviour (`SKIPPED_STORM` + aggregated `storm:*` push).
  - **Gates:** `StormPushIndividualTest`, `EscalationServiceTest.processConfirmedOutage_stormSuppressed_leavesTrace` (parametrised), `StormTeamIsolationTest` (fixture pins OFF), `StormSettings.test.jsx`.
- **Storm push ↔ alarm link (2026-10-04, user request).** In the default mode an alarm's push is handed over to the storm (`SKIPPED_STORM`), but the alarm history still shows which aggregated storm push covered it, when, and to whom.
  - **Recording:** `UserPushService.enqueueStormNoticeLocalized` writes the member alarms each storm push covers into `storm_push_coverage`:
    - columns: `push_key` = delivery `dedupe_key`, `team_id`; UNIQUE(push_key, alert_event_id);
    - opening / daily push: the members at that moment; resolve push: only the recovered members, the same set as the `STORM_RESOLVE` mail log.
  - **Write rules:** one `batchUpdate(StormPushCoverageService.SQL_INSERT)` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, try/catch + WARN, after the decision (sent rows or skip row), so it never changes the push.
  - **Isolation:** a TEAM:A push never covers a TEAM:B alarm. Individual push mode records nothing, except the resolve push after a mid-storm switch.
  - **Reads:** `StormPushCoverageService`, a fixed number of queries per page.
    - `GET /api/admin/alerts/{id}/storm-push` (same gate as `/push-deliveries`);
    - Push bildirimlerim (`storm_push` / `storm_alarms`);
    - push log detail (`storm_coverage` / `storm_pushes` / `storm_awaiting`);
    - storm detail `notifications.storm_pushes`.
  - **Legacy:** pushes with no recorded row are estimated from the `alert_storm_members` window (`inferred: true`); a push with any recorded row is never estimated.
  - **Pending:** `pending` means the push was handed over, the storm is active, the alarm hasn't left, and no opening/daily push covers it yet.
  - **Retention:** `storm-push-coverage-orphan`.
  - **Gates:** `StormPushCoverageRecordTest`, `StormPushCoverageQueriesTest`, `AlertStormPushEndpointTest`, `PushLogStormLinkTest`, `AlertStormPush.test.jsx`, `PushHistoryStormLinks.test.jsx`.
- **Auto-close is reconciliation, not a transition** (2026-09-29, "ghost alarm" fix): `MonitoringOutageService.handleSweepDomain` decides per sweep from "open alarm in DB + current result healthy"; every healthy result increments a consecutive counter and N consecutive healthy sweeps close the alarm (atomic, idempotent; the active recovery chain is only a fast path, stuck chains are replaced). Fleet-wide outage suppression (`noteSuppression`) and `site.monitor.<type>.alert-enabled=false` may ONLY stop new alarms / confirmation / re-alerts — never recovery (`reconcileRecoveries`). Turning a type's alerts off closes its open alarms silently in `MonitoringOutageService.closeAlarmsOfDisabledTypes` — since multi-pod Sürüm 2a (2026-10-23, MP-44) under the `disabled-types-reconcile` lock (one pod, 2 min renewed; the settings-save path retries a busy lock 6 × 10 s, boot tries once, a `source="refresh"` event — another pod's save seen by the 10 s settings poll — makes ONE attempt, so alarms opened by the leader's confirmation chains that finished after the saving pod's pass are closed too), and each alarm is closed by `SilentAlarmCloser.closeIfOpen` (one conditional UPDATE of the close columns, `WHERE resolved = false`, `AlertEventSilentCloseRepository`; the RESOLVE push only for the closer; the read entity is never saved, so a concurrent ack / manual resolve is not undone). A confirmation chain whose type was switched off while it ran opens NO alarm: `finishConfirmed` re-reads `alertEnabled` before `processConfirmedOutage` (a change type's pending row becomes `SUPPRESSED`); recovery and manual rules unchanged. The inventory import's deactivation uses `SilentAlarmCloser.closeOnInventoryDeactivate` (same `closableByInventory` rule). Since multi-pod Sürüm 2b (2026-10-23, R2b-F) every close path of `EscalationService` is conditional too: the silent closes (`resolveOpenAlertsSilently` — delete / pause / host change / orphan cleanups …, and `resolveOpenAlertsQuietlyRecovered` — the maintenance-quiet recovery) and `closeAlertsOnInventoryDelete|Deactivate` go through `SilentAlarmCloser.closeCopyIfOpen` (one conditional UPDATE; the maintenance branch uses `closeRecoveredIfOpen`, which leaves `resolved_silently` UNCHANGED); only the caller that closed the row sends the RESOLVE push (and, maintenance branch only, the 7/24 resolve) with the closed COPY — an alarm closed elsewhere gets no second push, its `resolved_by` is not rewritten and it is not counted in `alertsClosed`. The manual `resolve` and the automatic close no longer `save` the entity after their CAS (`markResolvedIfOpenNotify` already wrote the close columns): only the manual note is written, to this close's row (`SilentAlarmCloser.writeResolvedNote`, `resolved_at` + `resolved_by` match; a note that could not be written — the close columns were rewritten by another path — is cleared from the returned / mailed entity with a WARN; the automatic close uses ONE `closedAt` for CAS and memory). An ack / re-alert stamp / storm unlink written between the read and the close is kept. `SilentAlarmCloser` is a field (`@Autowired(required = false)`); without it (hand-built unit tests) the old saves run. The same release made every OTHER write of an existing alarm targeted (`service/AlertEventFieldWriter`: one `UPDATE` of exactly the columns the path changes, never the close columns or `id` / read-only columns (IllegalArgumentException), so a stale write can neither close nor REOPEN an alarm — the old `save` wrote `resolved = false` back over a close that happened during the SMTP send): `acknowledge` (ack columns, only while open; success returns an acked COPY; 0 rows = closed meanwhile → `EscalationService.AlertClosedMeanwhileException` = 409 `ALERT_ALREADY_RESOLVED` (TR / EN), no `ALERT_ACKNOWLEDGE` audit, bulk ack counts it `skipped`, a row deleted meanwhile → 404; the unused 2-arg overload is no longer `@Transactional`), the post-send / escalation / re-alert / initial-missing writes of `processResults` and `processConfirmedOutage` (claims unchanged; `saveUnlessClosedMeanwhile` keeps its fresh-read gate; the individual-send write never writes `stormId` over a concurrent peer promotion — the alarm then resolves as a storm member; the storm-suppressed write is open-only: an alarm resolved meanwhile is not attached, its in-memory link is dropped and its `alert_storm_members` row is closed RECOVERED, so the storm close never reports it twice), `reNotify` bookkeeping, the legacy team stamp in `sendResolutionNotification`, the scripted rename re-key in `MonitoringController` (`domain`, only while open), `TeamAdminService.moveAll` (one `UPDATE … team_id … WHERE team_id = :from AND resolved = false`) and `DerivedMonitorAlertRouting` (`contextJson` + changed `teamId`, only while open). Not covered: dirty-checking writes of a MANAGED `AlertEvent` mutated inside a `@Transactional` method (none found on these paths; not audited repo-wide). Rolling deploy: 20.166.0 pods keep their whole-entity saves (they can still reopen an alarm a new pod closed; the duplicate RESOLVE push stays blocked by `ux_push_event_phase_user`) until the last one is gone. Gates: `EscalationSilentCloseMultiPodTest`, `EscalationServiceTest.closeQuietly_alreadyClosed_noSecondPushNoOverwrite|closeQuietly_withCloser_conditionalClose_pushesClosedCopy|quietlyRecovered_withCloser_keepsSilencedFlag_notifiesNoc|inventoryClose_withCloser_countsOwnClosesOnly|manualResolve_withCloser_noWholeEntitySave_noteTargeted|autoResolve_withCloser_noWholeEntitySaveAfterCas`, `EscalationServiceTest.manualResolve_noteNotWritten_notReturned`, `AlertEventStaleWriteMultiPodTest` (21), `AdminControllerTest.acknowledge_alarmClosedMeanwhile_409_noAudit|bulkAcknowledge_closedMeanwhile_countedSkipped`, `TeamAdminServiceTest.moveAll_withFieldWriter_singleTargetedUpdate`, `MonitoringControllerTest.scriptedRename_withFieldWriter_rekeysTargeted`. Rolling deploy: an old pod still reconciles on every settings event without a lock and with whole-entity saves (it can rewrite `resolved_by`; the duplicate RESOLVE push stays blocked by `ux_push_event_phase_user`). Gates: `DisabledTypesReconcileMultiPodTest`, `DisabledTypesSettingsEventTest`, `DisabledTypeConfirmationTest`. A sibling monitor sharing the event key (`domain|alertType`) that is still DOWN keeps the alarm open. `DNS_CHANGED` / `DOMAINMON_CHANGED` close manually by design. Cert alarms reconcile even under outage suspicion / disabled expiry alerts (`resolveVerifiedStaleCertAlerts`). A leadership change resets this per-pod reconciliation state (restart semantics — see "Leadership change = restart"); the D-6 guard is active again until the new leader's first scheduled round of the type. Fleet-wide outage suppression is decided over a **sliding window** (2026-10-22, SOAK-1): `MonitoringOutageService` keeps per type the latest scheduled outcome per domain within `max(3 × last tick gap, site.monitor.network.suppression-window-ms (300000))` (capped at 60 min); enter = ≥ `min-errors` network-class DOWN domains AND rate ≥ `error-rate-threshold`; stay (hysteresis) while the windowed rate ≥ threshold and ≥ 1 network DOWN — one tick of the per-monitor cluster schedule no longer suppresses real outages nor flips `network_outage_events` open / resolved every tick. A confirmation chain that started before suppression and finishes while it is on does not open an alarm for a network-class failure (`finishConfirmed`). Window memory is per sweep-leader pod and cleared on leadership change (`resetVolatileState`); gate `MonitoringOutageWindowedSuppressionTest`. Known trade-off (final review 2026-10-22, not changed): entering suppression is slower than the 20.165.0 per-tick rule in a REAL egress outage — healthy marks stay in the window until the monitors are re-checked (≈ half the monitors' interval, ≤ the 5 min floor), so confirmation chains (3 × 30 s) of short-interval monitors can open alarms before the windowed rate crosses the threshold. The per-tick rule was the SOAK-1 bug (one 9-monitor tick suppressed real outages and flapped); a transition-based detector (count only up → network-down flips) would fix both and is a follow-up. On an open shared event the owner-down item with the **highest** `alert_level` drives `processConfirmedOutage` (ALERTS-11, `highestLevelItem`). Monitoring alarm lock owners use `SchedulerService.hostname()` (SOAK-3).
- **DNS_CHANGED / DOMAINMON_CHANGED are durable until decided (2026-10-21, R1-B, MP-13).** The changed row is the next sweep's baseline, so the change used to be lost forever when the in-memory 3× chain (DNS) or the end-of-loop evaluation (domain) did not finish on the pod that saved the row (handover, fence drop, shutdown). Column `change_state` on `dns_records` / `domain_checks` (`service/monitoring/ChangeStates`, VARCHAR(16), NULL = legacy / old pod → ignored): the scheduled sweep writes `PENDING` (or `SUPPRESSED` when the monitor's change alert is off, the value is within the expected set, or DNS alerts are off; domain: `changeAlert=false`); every decision is a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE change_state='PENDING'` keyed by (monitor_id, checked_at) — `ALARMED` (written INSIDE the `mon-alert` lock after re-checking the row is still pending, so two pods never alarm the same change), `TRANSIENT` (value reverted during confirmation), `SUPPRESSED`, `EXPIRED`. `SchedulerService.requeuePendingDnsChanges` (first statement of every leader DNS sweep) re-confirms PENDING rows younger than 2 h (baseline = `previous_value`; skipped while that chain is in flight on this pod; older → `EXPIRED` + one `notification_logs` row (trigger `DNS_CHANGED` / `DOMAINMON_CHANGED`, `SKIPPED: değişiklik süresi doldu …`, alertEventId 0, written only by the pod whose conditional UPDATE set EXPIRED — `MonitoringOutageService.traceChangeExpired`); inactive / deleted monitor or alert now off → `SUPPRESSED`); `runDomainChecksLocked` adds PENDING domain rows younger than 6 h to the CHANGED batch regardless of `checkDue` (`withPendingDomainChanges`). The requeue queries read only what can still be acted on plus slack (`ChangeStates.DNS_REQUEUE_LOOKBACK_MS` = 3 h, `DOMAIN_REQUEUE_LOOKBACK_MS` = 8 h; older PENDING rows are closed by a separate EXPIRY PASS with no lower time bound — `SchedulerService.expireStalePendingChanges`, at most every 15 min per pod (`ChangeStates.EXPIRY_PASS_INTERVAL_MS`), ≤ 200 rows per pass, conditional EXPIRED + one `notification_logs` row each; for `dns_records` it runs only while the partial index is VALID on PostgreSQL (`ChangeStates.dnsPendingIndexUsable`, memoised per pod; H2 always) so it never scans the big table from the start; `domain_checks` is small) with `change_state = 'PENDING'` as a SQL LITERAL (a bind parameter would keep a generic plan off the partial index) and `ORDER BY checked_at`; on PostgreSQL the partial index `idx_dnsr_pending ON dns_records(checked_at) WHERE change_state = 'PENDING'` (`DeferredIndexBuilder.CATALOG`, PG only — H2 uses the `checked_at` range) serves the DNS query; `domain_checks` is small (`checked_at` index). A `mon-alert` lock collision for a change type no longer skips silently: the final step is retried every 5 s up to 6 times (`CHANGE_LOCK_RETRY_MS` / `CHANGE_LOCK_RETRIES`) — also when the row's state cannot be READ at that moment (`changePending` → `UNKNOWN`: no decision now, never an alarm on an unreadable state) — then a `notification_logs` row (`trigger INITIAL`, `SKIPPED: alarm kilidi alınamadı …` / `SKIPPED: değişiklik durumu okunamadı …`, alertEventId 0) and the row stays PENDING for the next sweep. Manual checks never write PENDING (manual rows are out of band). Rolling deploy: an old pod ignores the column (its rows are NULL); a change saved by a new pod and handed to an old leader is evaluated once a new pod leads again (≤ 2 h / 6 h). `changePending` looks only at changed rows (`changed = TRUE`), so a manual row of the same second (NULL state) never turns a decided change back into "pending"; a NULL-state CHANGED row (written by a pre-R1 / old pod) is processed as before at the final step but is never re-queued (no alarm storm on upgrade); a missing column (`BadSqlGrammarException`, first seconds after boot) takes the legacy path. Gates: `DnsChangeDurabilityTest` (incl. `unreadableState_retriedLater_alarmsOnce|manualRowSameSecond_doesNotReopenDecision|legacyNullRows_notRequeued_finalStepAsBefore|requeueRules`), `DnsChangeDurabilityTest.expiryPass_noLowerBound_cappedAndThrottled`, `DomainChangeDurabilityTest` (incl. `suppressedAndExpired`, 30 h row), `AlarmStateSchemaPatchTest`, `AlarmStatePostgresTest`, `DeferredIndexBuilderTest` (`idx_dnsr_pending`).
- Email **content** (detail table) for `DOMAINMON_*` is rebuilt fresh from the latest `DomainCheck` via `reconstructDomainContext(domain)` — `latestCheckRepo` is cert-only and returns nothing for a domain monitor, which is why resend/resolution mails looked empty before.
- Domain expiry alarm **severity** must mirror the card status tiers (`DomainCheckerService`: `days≤crit→CRITICAL`, `crit<days≤warn→WARNING`) — set in `SchedulerService.addDomainSweepItems`. Don't reintroduce a flat `HIGH`.
- **Noise analysis suggestions are a code catalogue (2026-10-01).** `AlertNoiseService` classifies noisy targets/teams and emits `suggestions[]` with codes from `AlertNoiseSuggestion` (thresholds are the service's `static final` constants — one place); the UI renders `t('noise.sug.<CODE>.title|body', ...params)` and turns `action.kind` (`open_monitor|open_alerts|open_settings|open_maintenance`) into a button (`alerts/noiseModel.js#runSuggestionAction`). Adding a code means adding TR + EN `noise.sug.<CODE>.title` / `.body` in the same change — `AlertNoiseSuggestionI18nGateTest` reads `frontend/src/i18n/index.jsx` and fails the backend build otherwise (the frontend used-keys gate cannot see the dynamic key). The response is additive: never rename the legacy fields (`top`, `flapping`, `heat`, `series`, `by_type`, KPI totals) — the panel and its old test depend on them.

- **Quiet hours are opt-in and never silent (2026-10-01, approved proposal 15).** Team window = `teams.quiet_start/quiet_end/quiet_days/quiet_min_level` (HH:mm Europe/Istanbul, may cross midnight, days = the day the window STARTS, null = no window). Rule lives in `QuietHours` (also used for personal push quiet hours on `app_users.push_quiet_*`). `EscalationService.sendCombinedAlert` defers only INITIAL/ESCALATION/DAILY_REALERT (`TeamQuietHoursService.DEFERRABLE_TRIGGERS`) for the owning team(s) in their window: default only WARNING, `quiet_min_level=CRITICAL` also HIGH, CRITICAL never; MANUAL resend, NOC and storm mails are not deferred. Every deferral writes `notification_logs` trigger `QUIET_HOURS` + `SKIPPED: sessiz saat …` and a `quiet_digest_items` row (UNIQUE alarm×team×window); push gets `SKIPPED_TEAM_QUIET` via the `quiet_deferred` ctx flag. A resolution whose OPENING was deferred and whose digest hasn't gone is folded into the digest. `QuietDigestService` (minute job, `scheduler_lock` `quiet-digest`, claim-token = at-most-once — a claim is never left half-done (multi-pod Sürüm 2a, 2026-10-23, MP-46): an exception after the claim turns every row still carrying the token into `FAILED: özet turu yarıda kaldı — sonuç bilinmiyor, yeniden gönderilmedi (<Exception>)`, a `SENDING:` claim older than 15 min (owner died; the job runs under the lock) becomes `FAILED: … (gönderen pod durdu) …` within the next minute — the minute job's pre-check also enters the lock when such a claim exists (`countStaleClaims`, claims of the last 2 days, `idx_qdi_pending` range), even with no new item due and during a muted system maintenance (no mail then) — conditional per token (`QuietDigestItemRepository.markStatusIfToken` / `findStaleClaims`), each with one `QUIET_DIGEST` `notification_logs` row per alarm; never resent; gates `QuietDigestServiceTest.exceptionAfterClaim_failedWithTrace|staleSendingClaim_failedWithTraceOnce|scheduledRun_noDueItem_stillClosesStaleClaim`) mails one digest per team per recipient set, logs `QUIET_DIGEST` per alarm and restamps `last_re_alert_at` of still-open alarms; timed escalation steps are held while quiet/pending and `QUIET_DIGEST` is a step anchor. Settings come from a 60 s in-memory cache (no per-alarm query); other pods see a change within one cache-bus poll (`quiet-hours`). Gates: `QuietHoursEscalationTest` (no-config ≡ today on every path), `QuietDigestServiceTest`, `EscalationStepQuietHoldTest`, `UserPushQuietHoursTest`.
- **Timed escalation steps + per-family re-alert (2026-10-02, approved proposal 10) are opt-in.** `escalation_contacts.delay_minutes` (null/0 = today exactly): a delayed contact is left out of every immediate send and is notified ONCE (email + own webhook, subject `[ESKALASYON · N dk onaysız]`) by `EscalationStepService` (fixedDelay `site.monitor.escalation.step-interval-ms`, `scheduler_lock` key `escalation-steps`) when the alarm is still open AND unacknowledged after N minutes from its latest INITIAL/ESCALATION/QUIET_DIGEST anchor; dedupe = `alert_escalation_steps` UNIQUE (alarm, contact, level), claimed before sending. All routing rules still apply (owning teams only, level gate, WARNING-standalone → no contacts, ownerless → nothing); ack / storm / maintenance / fleet-outage skips write `ESCALATION_STEP` + `SKIPPED: …`. Re-alert override keys `site.monitor.realert.<family>-minutes` (0 = global interval; 15–10080). Gates: `EscalationStepRegressionTest` (no-config ≡ today incl. notification_logs), `EscalationStepServiceTest`, `ReAlertIntervalOverrideTest`.
- **Runbook note + incident link (2026-10-02, proposal 14).** If a non-blank `MonitorGuide` exists for the alarm's type+target, `sendCombinedAlert` appends a "Ne yapılmalı" block (markdown → escaped plain text, ≤ 1000 chars email / ≤ 300 webhook) — INITIAL/ESCALATION/re-alert/manual resend and the timed step only; resolution, storm, NOC and personal push are unchanged. Without a guide the output is byte-identical (`RunbookNoteNotificationTest`). Incidents carry `incident_records.alert_event_id`; the alarm detail's "Olay kaydı aç" opens the existing `IncidentFormModal` prefilled (`incidents.manage/edit`), `GET /api/incidents/by-alert/{id}` shows the link afterwards.
- **Alarm mail templates by type (2026-10-22, ALERTS-13 / ALERTS-15):** `EmailTemplateBuilder.isHttp` (`HTTP_*`): kicker / footer "HTTP/Website İzleme", rows Adres / URL / HTTP Durumu / Yanıt Süresi / Eşik / Sertifika Kalan Gün / Detay / Hata / Son Kontrol, own action steps (CA/PKI steps only for `HTTP_SSL`), resolution headlines per type; `tabFor` → `http`, so `isCert(HTTP_*)` is false (no certificate rows, no inventory enrichment — an HTTP monitor is standalone and the inventory row may belong to another team). DOMAINMON action steps are per type (BLACKLIST → DNSBL delist, TRANSFER_LOCK → re-enable `clientTransferProhibited`, STATUS → EPP codes, UNKNOWN → RDAP / WHOIS access, CHANGED → verify and close by hand); only EXPIRY / DOMAIN_EXPIRY say "renew". Resolution: "yenilendi" / "Yeni Bitiş Tarihi" only for an expiry type (`isExpiryType`) closed by a VERIFIED recovery (`verifiedRecovery`: resolvedBy blank / "Sistem (otomatik)" / "system"); `manualResolve(resolvedBy)` → "Alarm elle kapatıldı." (the "Çözen" row names the person); any other system reason → plain "Alarm otomatik olarak kapandı."; verified recoveries of cert defect / HTTP / DOMAINMON types get their own headline. Gate `EmailTemplateTypeTextsTest`; samples `alert-http-slow`, `resolved-domain-blacklist` in `EmailSamples`.
- **HTTP advanced request + `HTTP_SLOW` (2026-10-02, proposal 9).** `http_monitors` gained encrypted `custom_headers_enc` (global admin only, like Keyword/PageSpeed), `basic_auth_user/pass_enc`, `request_body`/`request_content_type` (POST only, ≤ 64 KB), `json_path`/`json_expected` (small resolver `service/http/JsonAssertion`, failure = HTTP_DOWN with "JSON doğrulaması başarısız: …") and `slow_response_enabled`/`slow_threshold_ms` → new alert type `HTTP_SLOW` (wired like `KEYWORD_SLOW`; never a storm member). With no advanced field set the old 7-argument `HttpCheckerService.check` runs and the request is byte-identical (`HttpRequestBuildTest`); headers/auth/body are dropped on cross-host redirects. A new monitor alert type must be added everywhere `KEYWORD_SLOW`/`HTTP_SLOW` appear (grep both).
- **Push outbox is claimed, not just read (2026-10-02, proposal 3; per-batch lease 2026-10-21, R1-C1, MP-05).** `UserPushService.drainOutbox` leases due PENDING rows with `claimDue` (writes a per-run unique lease stamp into `next_attempt_at`, ≥ 2 min) and reads back only `findByIdInAndNextAttemptAtOrderByIdAsc(ids, lease)`. **Before each batch** the lease is renewed with a CAS (`renewLease(ids, mine, newLease)` — still PENDING and still this round's lease) and only the renewed rows are POSTed: a round whose 50-row page outlives the lease no longer sends rows another pod has re-claimed. If the claim query fails the round sends NOTHING (the 2026-10-01 "read rows" fallback was removed — the push is delayed by one sweep, never sent twice; `writeRejected` still logs separately); a failed renewal skips that batch. Shutdown (round 3, N3): at `ContextClosedEvent` `NotifyExecutorDrain` first calls `UserPushService.beginShutdown()` (`stopping` → no new round / batch) and waits for `inFlightBatches()` (the worker's ACTIVE task only) inside the SAME 20 s drain deadline as the notify pools; the running request + its result write finish there, the round's remaining batches are not sent and their leases are released (`releaseLease`) so another pod picks them up at once; delayed backoff tasks do not run after shutdown. The destroy-time wait (`ShutdownDeadline.waitMs`) then normally finds the worker idle, so an exhausted shared budget never cuts an in-flight push before its SENT write. **In-flight lease (round 3, D3):** the batch about to be POSTed is leased for `inFlightLeaseSec()` ≥ `SENT_UNRECORDED_HOLD_SEC` (10 min; the round claim keeps its ≥ 2 min), renewed on every sweep through the same `renewLease` CAS and `leaseStamp` format — even when the outcome write AND the first hold write fail no other pod re-claims the row for 10 min; trade-off: a pod that crashes mid-POST delays that one batch by up to 10 min (a graceful shutdown finishes the request). **Sent, outcome unwritten (review F4):** when the POST succeeded but neither `saveAll` nor `updateOutcome` could write SENT, the row is remembered in `sentUnrecorded` with its lease, and its lease is pushed `SENT_UNRECORDED_HOLD_SEC` (10 min) ahead — at once, then on every sweep and drain (`flushSentUnrecorded` → `holdLease`, a CAS on this pod's stamp, run before the circuit-breaker check) until SENT is written; another pod can no longer re-claim it when the round lease expires and POST it again. If the pod dies the hold expires within 10 min and the row is resent (at-least-once on pod death, by design); an entry dropped after 24 h is logged at ERROR (`pruneSentUnrecorded`: row id, send time, "kira dolunca YENİDEN GÖNDERİLEBİLİR"; ≤ 20 lines + a total). Test seam `leaseSecondsOverride`. Gates: `UserPushOutboxLeaseTest` (two pods, real H2 repository, 300 ms gateway, 2 s lease, 40 batches → each row POSTed exactly once), `UserPushServiceTest.renewForSend_*|drain_skipsRowWhoseLeaseWasTaken|claim_queryFailure_sendsNothing`, `UserPushOutboxLeaseTest.outcomeWriteFailsOnce_noSecondPostFromAnotherPod|outcomeWriteKeepsFailing_leaseKeptAlive|inFlightLeaseCoversTheHold_whenImmediateHoldFails|droppedSentUnrecordedIsLogged|contextClosedDrain_finishesInFlightEvenWhenDestroyBudgetIsGone`, `NotifyExecutorShutdownTest.contextCloseStopsAndDrainsThePushWorker|pushDrainIsBounded`.
- **Event-less push rows are unique (2026-10-21, R1-C1, MP-17).** `ux_push_event_phase_user` does not cover NULL `alert_event_id`; team notices, direct pushes, storm pushes and the storm decision rows deduped by "exists, then insert". `DeferredIndexBuilder.CATALOG` builds `ux_push_eventless_key_user ON user_push_deliveries(dedupe_key, username, (COALESCE(team_id,0))) WHERE alert_event_id IS NULL AND push_trigger NOT IN ('TEST','OVERFLOW_SUMMARY') AND created_at >= '2026-10-21'` (PostgreSQL only, CONCURRENTLY; TEST rows may legitimately repeat in one test send, overflow summaries are lock-serialised and must be re-writable after a half-finished round; the `created_at` cut keeps possible historical duplicates out, so the build can't fail on history). All event-less inserts go through `UserPushService.insertEventless`: the `exists…` pre-check stays (quiet early exit), a unique violation = "already there" (skipped, no second queue entry); inside a caller's transaction the insert runs in its own REQUIRES_NEW transaction (a unique violation would otherwise poison the caller's PostgreSQL transaction, 25P02). Gates: `PushEventlessIndexPostgresTest`, `DeferredIndexBuilderTest` / `SchemaPatchPostgresTest` (unique catalogue entries carry the `ux_` prefix and must be `CREATE UNIQUE`; all others must not be), `DeferredIndexFallbackH2Test`, `UserPushServiceTest.eventlessDuplicate_isSkipped`.
- **Cert alarms are claimed across pods (2026-10-21, R1-C2, MP-03).** `EscalationService` has a field-injected `JdbcTemplate` (null in hand-built unit tests = byte-identical old behaviour) and a `lockOwner` (= `SchedulerService.instanceId()`). **Opening:** in `processResults`, a type with no open alarm in the batch snapshot is opened only under the `scheduler_lock` row `mon-alert:<certType>:<domain>` (120 s, same name format as `MonitoringOutageService.withLock`; cert types never collide with monitoring types) followed by a FRESH `findOpenAlert`. Lock busy (the hourly sweep, `checkSingleDomainAsync` on any pod, the manual-cert path) or a transient DB error → that type is skipped for this run; missing table → no lock (legacy); released in `finally`. **Every re-send is claimed BEFORE sending** (`claimReAlert`, `StormService.claimDailyRealert` pattern — `SQL_REALERT_CLAIM` / `_UNSTAMPED`: `UPDATE alert_events SET last_re_alert_at = :at WHERE id = ? AND resolved = FALSE AND last_re_alert_at = :seen` / `IS NULL`): cert ESCALATION, cert "interrupted INITIAL", cert DAILY_REALERT, the startup catch-up (INITIAL / DAILY_REALERT) and in `processConfirmedOutage` the ESCALATION, "initial missing" INITIAL and DAILY_REALERT. Only a 1-row claim sends; the stamp written afterwards is the claim time. An ESCALATION is claimed by `claimEscalation` (stamp AND level in one conditional UPDATE, the seen level in the WHERE — a second pod that read the first pod's stamp before its level was saved can't re-claim) BEFORE the level, message or acknowledgement change; a claim that is lost or fails on a DB error changes nothing, so the next run escalates and sends. A `sendCombinedAlert` exception releases an INITIAL / DAILY_REALERT claim (`releaseReAlert`, conditional on the claimed value) and is rethrown. After a successful `claimEscalation` EVERY following step — the entity mutation, `recipientsNow` (contact query), the save and `sendCombinedAlert` — runs inside ONE try (round 3, N1); any `RuntimeException` reverts the escalation (`revertEscalation`: level, message, ack, stamp, days / not-after and notified contacts restored, conditional on the claimed stamp) and is rethrown, so the next run escalates and sends (before, a save / contact-query failure after the claim left the new level committed and the ESCALATION never sent). The post-send save (`notified_contacts`) stays outside (the mail went out — reverting would send it twice). A revert that itself fails → WARN + `notification_logs` row trigger `ESCALATION`, `FAILED: terfi bildirimi gönderilemedi ve terfi geri alınamadı — seviye yükseltilmiş kaldı, ESCALATION gitmedi (<Exception>)` (`STATUS_ESCALATION_REVERT_FAILED`) — never silent. A pod dying between claim and send loses that one notice (at most once); the daily cadence brings the next one. The daily re-alert dedupe (`EscalationServiceTest`) is unchanged. **Monitoring "initial missing" has the 5-min grace (MP-19):** the interrupted INITIAL is sent only when `initialNotificationMissing(event, now)` (createdAt + `INITIAL_SEND_GRACE`); a younger unstamped alarm returns without sending — a storm member unlinked by `closedAfterAttach` / `unlinkFromStorm` within 5 min of its creation gets its individual INITIAL when the grace ends. Gates: `EscalationMultiPodClaimTest` (two `EscalationService` instances on one H2: sweep ∥ instant check → one alarm + one INITIAL ×20 rounds; lock held elsewhere → skipped then opened; DAILY_REALERT / ESCALATION / interrupted-INITIAL races → one send; catch-up ∥ sweep → one DAILY_REALERT; failed send releases the claim), `EscalationServiceTest.processConfirmedOutage_unstampedYoungAlarm_waitsForGrace`, `EscalationMultiPodClaimTest.monitoringEscalation_claimDbError_nextSweepSends|monitoringEscalationRace_sendsOnce|escalationClaimChecksTheSeenLevel|certEscalation_sendFailure_revertsAndNextRunSends|monitoringEscalation_saveFailsAfterClaim_revertedAndNextSweepSends|monitoringEscalation_recipientsFailAfterClaim_revertedAndNextSweepSends|certEscalation_saveFailsAfterClaim_revertedAndNextRunSends|escalationRevertFails_leavesFailedLogRow`.
- **Resolution mails are durable (2026-10-21, R1-C2, MP-07).** **Pool:** `sendResolutionNotificationAsync` and `reNotifyAsync` run on `@Async("notifyExecutor")` (`WebConfig`: core 2, max 4, queue 1000, `waitForTasksToCompleteOnShutdown` + 20 s, CallerRuns when full, WARN — never silent — when rejected after shutdown). `config/NotifyExecutorDrain` waits up to 20 s at `ContextClosedEvent` (after the lease release; all beans still alive — JPA may otherwise close before the pool's own destroy-time wait) for `notifyExecutor` + `loginIssueMailExecutor` to go idle, without shutting them down. `loginIssueMailExecutor` also waits 20 s; `certCheckExecutor` WARNs when it rejects during shutdown. **Marker:** `alert_events.resolution_notify` (`VARCHAR(16)`) + `resolution_notify_at` (`VARCHAR(30)`), read-only in the entity (`insertable/updatable=false`, `@JsonIgnore`), written only by targeted SQL. `markResolvedIfOpenNotify` sets `resolved=TRUE … resolution_notify='PENDING'` in the SAME conditional UPDATE as the resolve gate (manual `resolve` and automatic `resolveOpenAlertsForDomain`) — only when an individual resolution mail will follow (the storm-member decision is taken ONCE before the gate; no marker for storm members). Silent closes (deletion / pause / maintenance-quiet / inventory) leave NULL; missing column → `markResolvedIfOpen` (unmarked). **Claim:** the async task claims PENDING → SENDING (CAS), sends, then SENDING → DONE (conditional on its own claim time); claim lost → no send; unmarked row (NULL — old pod, legacy path) → sends as before. A claim error on a row this close marked PENDING (the entity carries the marker after the CAS) → NO send, the sweeper sends once ≥ 2 min later; a claim error on a row whose marker is unknown → sends (at-least-once). A claimed send that throws ends `FAILED` + `notification_logs` row `FAILED: çözüm bildirimi yarıda kaldı (uygulama hatası) … (<Exception>)` on BOTH paths (async task and sweeper, `deliverClaimedResolution`), never retried (an application error would repeat). Marker values: PENDING → SENDING → DONE / UNKNOWN / EXPIRED / FAILED. The claim stamp is unique (round 3, D4 — `claimToken`: ISO second + `.mmm#xxxx`, ≤ 30 chars, ordered like time to the second): when the PENDING → SENDING UPDATE throws on the client although it committed, the marker is re-read — SENDING with OUR stamp → the claim is ours and the mail is sent once (before: 10 min later UNKNOWN, mail lost). **Sweeper:** `ResolutionNotifySweeper` (`site.monitor.notify.resolution-sweep-ms` 60 s, initial 150 s; `scheduler_lock` `resolution-notify-sweep`) → `EscalationService.deliverPendingResolutions(now)`, three bounded steps (≤ 100 rows each, `resolved_at` range scans on `idx_ae_resolved_at`, 7-day lookback): SENDING older than 10 min → `UNKNOWN` + `notification_logs` row `RESOLUTION` / `FAILED: sonuç bilinmiyor — … yeniden gönderilmedi` (NO resend — the dead pod may have mailed it); PENDING older than 24 h → `EXPIRED` + `SKIPPED: çözüm bildirimi 24 saat içinde gönderilemedi …`; PENDING resolved 2 min – 24 h ago → claim, `sendResolutionNotification` (trigger `MANUAL_RESOLVE` with the resolver's name when `resolved_by` is not `system`, else `RESOLUTION`), DONE. Two sweepers / sweeper ∥ async task → one send. Gates: `ResolutionNotifyDurabilityTest` (incl. `claimErrorAfterMarking_sweeperSendsOnce|asyncSendException_endsFailedWithTrace|sweeperSendException_endsFailedWithTrace|claimCommittedButClientSawError_sendsOnce|claimTokenIsUniqueAndOrdered`), `NotifyExecutorShutdownTest`, `NotificationDurabilitySchemaPatchTest`.
- **SMTP 421 retries never stay `QUEUED_RETRY` forever (2026-10-21, R1-C2, MP-06).** The 421 retry queue lives in one pod's memory (`EmailNotificationService.mailRetryExecutor`). **Shutdown:** pending retries are tracked (`pendingRetryTasks`; a task removes itself when it starts); `@PreDestroy` waits 5 s, `shutdownNow()`, then writes `STATUS_RETRY_CANCELLED_SHUTDOWN` ("FAILED: sonuç yok — pod kapanırken 421 yeniden denemesi iptal edildi") to the log row of every task that never started; a 421 while the pool is already shut down returns that status instead of "QUEUED_RETRY". **Orphan sweeper:** `MailRetryOrphanSweeper` (`site.monitor.mail.retry-orphan-sweep-ms` 5 min, initial 3 min; `scheduler_lock` `mail-retry-orphans`) sets `QUEUED_RETRY%` rows whose `sent_at` is older than max(20 min, retry chain + 5 min) and newer than 2 days to `STATUS_RETRY_ORPHANED` ("FAILED: sonuç yok — 421 yeniden denemesinin sonucu alınamadı (gönderen pod kapandı)") with ONE conditional UPDATE (`idx_nl_sent_at`); then the tech-error queues are reconciled in the same round (`TechErrorReportService.reconcileAllQueued`, multi-pod R2a-D1). The "FAILED: sonuç yok" prefix is read by the tech-error send log as `INTERNAL_ERROR`. A late real result still corrects an orphaned row (`writeBackRetryStatus` falls back to the orphan status after `QUEUED_RETRY`). The 421 start deadline (`retryStartDeadline` = orphan age − 2 min from the chain's first attempt; `EmailNotificationService.orphanAge` is the single source the sweeper uses) applies ONLY to mail whose result is reconciled and re-sent elsewhere (round 3, N4) — `notification_logs` role `NOC` (`NocQueuedRetryReconciler`) or trigger `TECH_ERROR_REPORT` (`reconcileQueued`), `EmailNotificationService.RESENT_ELSEWHERE_ROLES|TRIGGERS`, decided from the queued row at retry time (row unreadable → send): such a late retry is not sent and writes `STATUS_RETRY_LATE` (`FAILED: sonuç yok — 421 yeniden denemesi süresinde başlayamadı …`); every other late retry (alarm, resolution, reports …) is sent as before. Liveness: `notification_logs.retry_alive_at` (`VARCHAR(30)`, read-only in the entity, `NotificationDurabilitySchemaPatch`) — at the start of every sweeper tick EVERY pod (outside the lock) stamps its own still-pending, non-reconciled retries (`heartbeatPendingRetries` → `NotificationLogRepository.touchRetryAlive`, conditional on `QUEUED_RETRY%`, ≤ 50 rows); the orphan UPDATE skips rows stamped within max(15 min, 3 × sweep interval), but never beyond 2 h after `sent_at` (`MAX_ALIVE`); a database without the column uses the old statement. **Tech-error queue:** `TechErrorReportService.reconcileQueued` finalises a queued send whose log row is still `QUEUED_RETRY…` (or missing) and whose `send_claimed_at` is older than `QUEUED_RESULT_WINDOW` (20 min): the log row gets the orphan status by a conditional UPDATE (`NotificationLogRepository.failQueuedRetryIfStill`; the loser skips), then the existing `finishQueuedWithEvent` (one SEND_RESULT event, one `QUEUE_FAILED` push). The period reminder shows such a report as "Gönderim sonucu alınamadı" (`TechErrorReminderService.stateOf`: FAILED + orphan status → `QUEUED_UNKNOWN`). **7/24 deliveries:** a NOC `noc_deliveries` row kept the first attempt's `QUEUED_RETRY…` forever (the retry result was written only to the `notification_logs` row), so a lost or finally failed NOC open counted as "sent" (`NocNotificationService.sent`, console, `noc=sent`) and was never retried. In the same sweeper round, after the log orphans, `service/noc/NocQueuedRetryReconciler` copies the FINAL status of the same attempt's log row (role `NOC`, same subject, written within 2 min of the delivery's `updated_at`; subject compared in Java) into the delivery — SENT stays SENT, FAILED becomes FAILED; a delivery older than the orphan age whose log row is missing or still queued gets `STATUS_RETRY_ORPHANED`. Writes use `NocDeliveryRepository.finishQueuedRetry`, a CAS on status + `updated_at` like `reclaim` (it does not move `updated_at`, so the log-row match window stays anchored on the attempt), so two pods never write a row twice and a row reclaimed in between is left alone; attempts are unchanged. An `STATUS_RETRY_ORPHANED` delivery is repaired to SENT when its log row later turned SENT. A FAILED open is then retried by the existing rules (alarm path at most once a day, storm tick 5 min apart with at most 3 attempts); a SENT delivery still blocks, and a resolve mail follows only a sent open. Scan: `created_at` within 7 days, at most 200 rows (`idx_noc_delivery_created`). Gates: `EmailNotificationServiceTest.shutdownCancelsPendingRetry_writesFailed|lateRealResult_correctsOrphanedRow|retryChainDuration`, `MailRetryOrphanSweeperTest`, `NocQueuedRetryReconcilerTest` (mirror SENT / FAILED, orphan, young row waits, two pods → each row once, reclaim wins, sweeper end-to-end, `orphanedDeliveryRepairedWhenLogTurnsSent`), `EmailNotificationServiceTest.retryStartingTooLateIsNotSent|retryDelayedWithinDeadlineIsSent|lateRetryOnlyDroppedForMailResentElsewhere|heartbeatTouchesOnlyPendingRowsNotResentElsewhere`, `MailRetryOrphanSweeperTest.aliveRowsAreNotOrphaned|heartbeatRunsOnEveryPodAndLegacyFallback`, `TechErrorReportServiceTest.orphanedQueueIsFinalisedOnceAcrossPods|lateRealResultWinsOverOrphan`.
- **Personal push: overflow summary, preferences, language, history, escalation-step push (2026-10-04, user-approved proposals 2–6).** Links in pushes are unreachable (the push app runs outside the company network), so never add links or one-tap actions to push texts.
  - **Overflow summary:** rows over the cap stay `RATE_LIMITED`. They are linked through `overflow_summary_id` to ONE `OVERFLOW_SUMMARY` row by `UserPushOverflowService` (lock `push-overflow`, conditional-UPDATE claim).
    - The summary is exempt from the cap.
    - Personal quiet hours or snooze defer it, unless its level gets through.
    - Opt-out, inactive account or maintenance writes a trace row instead.
  - **Critical bypass:** `critical-bypass-cap` (default off) exempts CRITICAL alarm, storm and escalation-step pushes from the cap.
  - **Preferences:** `app_users.push_min_level`, `push_families`, `push_snooze_until`, `push_snooze_critical`, `push_lang`.
    - Skips are always written as rows: `SKIPPED_USER_TYPE` / `SKIPPED_USER_LEVEL` / `SKIPPED_USER_SNOOZE`.
    - RESOLVE and `sendDirect` (login codes) are exempt.
    - With no preferences set, behaviour is identical to before.
    - There is no favourites-only filter: cert and inventory-derived alarms cannot be mapped to favourites reliably.
  - **Language:** messages are built per recipient language (`PushI18n`); Turkish output is byte-identical, and batch ids are split per language.
    - Team, storm and direct notices take `LocalizedText` through the `*Localized` methods.
    - Add English text whenever you add a push text.
    - The alarm's own reason text (`{neden}`) stays Turkish.
  - **History:** `/api/me/push-history` returns only the user's own rows plus decision rows for teams the user belongs to (never other teams).
  - **Type labels (2026-10-18):** a push row's `monitor_type` is never shown raw — `utils/pushPrefs.familyLabel` maps families to `userpush.type.<family>` and non-family types through `PUSH_TYPE_ALIASES` (`CERTIFICATE` → cert, `TECH_ERROR_REPORT` → `channel_reports`, `WEEKLY_REPORT`, `STORM`; key names never contain "error" — `errorMessageQuality`), unknown → `userpush.type.other`. Used by "Push bildirimlerim" and the admin push log `TypeBadge` (`PushLogRows.typeText`: table, cards, detail, breakdown). A new push type needs an alias + TR/EN key: `pushTypeLabel.test.jsx` reads the server types from the Java source (`MonitorTypeCatalog.ORDER`, the type argument of every `enqueueTeamNotice*` (4th) / `enqueueDirect*` (5th) call — literal or `static final String` constant —, `UserPushService.setMonitorType`) and fails when one has no own label (falls back to "Diğer bildirim").
  - **Self-service:** `/api/me/push-preferences|push-snooze|push-test`, audited. `PUSH_*` events are in the INTEGRATION category.
  - **Escalation-step push:** the contact resolves to an active user (`user_id`, otherwise an e-mail match — several active accounts with that e-mail are the same person since 2026-10-21, `ContactMatch.sameEmail`; `SKIPPED_AMBIGUOUS_USER` is no longer written, the code stays for old rows); dedupe `ESC_STEP:<contact>:<level>`; `site.monitor.escalation.step-push-enabled` (true).
  - **Schema:** `push_trigger` is VARCHAR(40); "DOMAIN_EXPIRY_REMINDER" did not fit 20 and those rows were lost silently on PostgreSQL.
  - **Dedupe keys are fitted centrally (2026-10-22, ALERTS-12 / DATA-2):** `user_push_deliveries.dedupe_key` stays VARCHAR(60); `UserPushDelivery.fitDedupeKey` returns a key ≤ 60 unchanged and turns a longer one into its first 43 characters + `#` + the first 16 hex of SHA-256(whole key) — deterministic (all pods agree), idempotent, prefix queries (`storm:<id>:`) intact. Every caller-key entry (`enqueueTeamNoticeLocalized`, `enqueueDirectLocalized`, `enqueueStormNotice`) fits it ONCE before the "already there" lookup and the write; `@PrePersist/@PreUpdate fitColumns` fits it again plus `display_name` (200), `title` (200), `monitor_name` (300), codes and `raw_response` — never `username` / `same_person_username` / `status`. Producers (TLS drop `tls-drop:<team>:<all ids>`, `WEAK_ALGO:<domain>:<day>`) pass the full key. A failed row write is "already there" ONLY for a unique violation (SQLSTATE 23505 / `DuplicateKeyException`, `UserPushService.isUniqueViolation`); anything else (22001 value too long, NOT NULL …) is a WARN naming the key, trigger and `overlongColumns()`. Before: PostgreSQL 22001 was swallowed at DEBUG as "duplicate" — TLS-drop pushes of a team with ~9+ drops and weak-algorithm notices of 40+ char domains were lost without a row. Gate `UserPushDedupeKeyFitTest` (H2, real service + repository).
  - **Push title is pushSafe on the alarm path too (L-P3):** `sendBatch` sends `PushText.pushSafe(title)` like `sendDirect`. **`"CERT"` notice type = cert family (L-P1):** `noticeFamilies("CERT")` → `cert` (TLS-drop pushes honour the personal family filter).
  - **Webhook Push settings page (K10, 2026-10-22, UIA-C3):** `GET /api/admin/user-push/settings` carries `read_only_keys` (non-global viewer: every GLOBAL_ONLY page key — URL, headers), `can_edit_types` (global admin only — TYPE scope chips) and `manage_team_ids` (non-global viewer only — the TEAM scope chips the server accepts). A scoped admin's save drops UNCHANGED GLOBAL_ONLY values (URL, header list incl. `[]` vs unset and masked secrets) before `AppSettingsService.save` — a changed one still 403s there. `UserPushSettings` disables the URL / header editor for read-only keys, the type chips without `can_edit_types` and team chips outside `manage_team_ids`, and never sends read-only keys. The save body holds ONLY changed keys (FE-7: header list / role groups only when changed; nothing changed → no request), so a scoped admin's save never reaches a GLOBAL_ONLY key even on a 20.165.0 pod that sends no `read_only_keys`; the header "Sır" checkbox is locked with the rest of a read-only header row.
  - **Same person = one push (2026-10-21, prod alarm #628: one 8-recipient delivery listed the same person twice — "… MP60146" / "… N60146" — and the gateway maps both sicils to one phone, so it buzzed twice; user decision + same-day review).** Single rule: `PushSamePersonGuard` (field-injected into `UserPushService` / `UserPushOverflowService`; absent = byte-identical old behaviour). **Who is the same person:** ACTIVE accounts whose e-mail is equal (trimmed + `toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)`) AND whose display names name the same person (`personName`: Turkish-aware fold İ/I/ı/i + accents like `foldSearchText`, `( … )` parts dropped, tokens containing a digit — the sicil — dropped, single spaces; "Emre Gökoğlu MP60146" = "EMRE GÖKOĞLU N60146"); a blank name joins by e-mail only when the address has ONE name; a shared mailbox of different people ("Ayşe Yılmaz" + "Mehmet Demir") never merges (`groupPersons`, pure; truth tables in `PushSamePersonGuardTest`); blank / null e-mail never merges; one projection query (`AppUserRepository.findSamePersonAccounts`, `[id, username, email, lastLoginAt, displayName]`), a failure merges nothing. **Paths:** every row-writing path asks once per delivery: `enqueueInternal` (OPEN / ESCALATION / RE_ALERT / RESEND / RESOLVE), `writeTeamRows` (team notices + storm notices), `enqueueDirectLocalized`, `enqueueEscalationStep`, `enqueueOnCall`, the overflow summary. `sendDirect` (login OTP, self test) and the admin `sendTest` are NOT merged. **Choice (only an account that will deliver):** (a) an account (in or outside this delivery) that already RECEIVED this exact notification — a SENT / PENDING / QUEUED row with the same dedupe key; skip rows are never evidence (`PushSamePersonGuard.delivering`) — wins; (b) otherwise only DELIVERABLE accounts of this delivery are candidates (`UserPushService.BaseStatus`: no personal skip — opt-out, level, family, snooze, quiet hours, inactive), under-the-cap before over-cap, then an account that received the same logical event (SENT, then PENDING — ESCALATION / RE_ALERT / storm daily / RESOLVE follow the OPEN account), then newest `last_login_at`, then lowest id; (c) no deliverable account → NO merge: every account writes its own honest reason. The others get `SKIPPED_SAME_PERSON` with `same_person_username` = the account the decision was made for (never the e-mail; masked for grant-only readers), not counted against the cap. A team notice whose excluded account (manager direct push, sender) has a sibling in the team writes `SKIPPED_SAME_PERSON_EXCLUDED` (`same_person_username` = the excluded account). Evidence is read through narrow projections (`findPersonEvidenceByEvent|ByKey|ByKeyPrefix` = `[username, dedupeKey, status, createdAt]`, at most one read per delivery, never per recipient). **On-call:** "already has the team push" counts only delivering rows (SENT / PENDING / QUEUED) of ANY account of the person (one event read): own account → no row; sibling → `SKIPPED_SAME_PERSON`; a team SKIP row never suppresses the on-call push (`existsRecentTeamResend` removed). **Escalation step:** `resolveContact` returns `SKIPPED_AMBIGUOUS_USER` again when the contact e-mail belongs to DIFFERENT people; accounts of one person come back in `sameEmail` and the step picks through the guard (fallback without guard: first deliverable, else none). **Overflow summary:** one summary per person, sent to the newest-login account that can receive it (active, not opted out); none can → each account gets its own summary / skip row. **Preview = send:** re-notify preview (`same_person_of`; on-call extra rows see only PENDING team rows as holders) and "Kim alır?" (`Explanation.samePersonOf`; only `RECIPIENT` accounts can be chosen). `enqueueDirectLocalized` returns `same_person` {skipped → chosen}; `TechErrorPushService.queuedIds` counts a skipped PO as reached only when its chosen account was queued. **UI wording never claims delivery:** TR "Aynı kişinin başka hesabı için karar verildi; bu hesaba ayrıca gönderilmedi" / EN "Decided for another account of the same person; this account was not sent a separate push", short `alh.push.status.*`, account note `push.samePersonOf` "Kararın verildiği hesap" / `push.samePersonExcludedOf` "Çıkarılan hesap" via `utils/pushPrefs.samePersonText` (push log + CSV column `same_person_username`, push history, alarm detail, re-notify modal, who-gets-notified, user detail). Gates: `PushSamePersonGuardTest` (personName / groupPersons / readiness / exact evidence), `UserPushSamePersonTest` (H2 + real gateway: #628, later phases, opted-out account never chosen, no deliverable account = no merge, under-cap beats over-cap, shared mailbox not merged + ambiguous step, narrow projection, preview = sent, explain, storm, on-call B, escalation step, weekly manager EXCLUDED, tech-error direct pair, OTP untouched, overflow incl. opt-out), `UserPushPreferencesTest.onCallResend_noSecondPush`, `TechErrorPushSamePersonTest`, `PushDecisionAccessTest.wireFormat_noPhoneNoUrl`, `PushSamePerson.test.jsx`.

**On-call rotation (2026-10-11, user decision: "takım seçerse nöbetçiye de").** `oncall_schedules` (one per team: enabled, DAILY|WEEKLY, `anchor` = first handover as Istanbul local `yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mm`, ordered `member_ids` ≤ 50, `notify_oncall` default OFF) + `oncall_overrides` (range → person; newest wins; pruned 180 days after end). `service/oncall/OnCallRotation` is the pure calculator (fixed UTC+3, bounded loops); `OnCallService.notifyTargets(teams)` returns the CURRENT on-call of teams with `notify_oncall` on (active accounts only, 30 s memo per team set, failure → empty); a schedule / override change reaches the other pods within one cache-bus poll (`oncall`). `EscalationService.oncallFor(sy, ug, level)` adds them ONLY for HIGH/CRITICAL: `sendCombinedAlert` (after contacts, same `seen`/`excludeEmails` dedupe; quiet-deferred teams contribute none) + `UserPushService.enqueueOnCall` (same channel/personal-pref/cap rules as the escalation-step push, skipped when the person already got the team push for that trigger — for a manual resend: a team `RESEND:` row for that person in the last 2 min — dedupe key `ONCALL:…`), `previewReNotify` (kind `ONCALL`) + the resend push preview (`UserPushService.preview(id, team, oncall)`) and the manual-resend count. The RESOLUTION mail goes to the on-call addresses that actually received the opening/escalation/re-alert (`alert_events.oncall_emails`, read-only in the entity, written only by `AlertEventRepository.setOncallEmails`) — never to whoever is on call at resolution time (2026-10-12). Overrides need a saved schedule and apply only while it is enabled (same as notifications and the Teams row). The alarm detail asks the on-call of the alarm's OWNER teams (`owner_team_ids` on enriched rows = `AlertOwnership.ownerTeamIds`; a standalone alarm never shows the inventory SY/UG on-call). Option off / no schedule / service missing → byte-identical (`EscalationOnCallTest`). Endpoints `/api/oncall/teams/{id}` (read: `oncall.read` + `canViewMonitoring`; write: `oncall.manage` + `canManage`), `/overrides`, `/api/oncall/current?teamIds=`; audit `ONCALL_*`. Shown on the Teams row ("Nöbetçi"), the team menu → `admin/oncall/OnCallScheduleModal`, the alarm detail (`alerts/AlertOnCall`, open alarms) and the 7/24 call sheet (`oncall` with phone). Team delete removes the schedule. Gates: `OnCallRotationTest`, `OnCallServiceTest`, `OnCallControllerTest`, `EscalationOnCallTest`, `oncallModel.test.js`, `OnCallScheduleModal.test.jsx`, `e2e/oncall.spec.js`. On-call addresses are stamped into `alert_events.oncall_emails` only when the mail was really handed over (`EscalationService.mailDelivered`: SENT / QUEUED_RETRY…); a FAILED / passive-skipped mail does not make the on-call a resolution recipient (2026-10-22, L-E5). **Deleted members (K11, UIA-A5, 2026-10-22):** `OnCallService.save` silently drops member ids with no user row (INFO, count only; an ENABLED schedule left with no member → 400 `members`); `OnCallController.save` skips JSON-null entries (an old client echoes a deleted member's null id); `view()` keeps them as `{user_id, missing:true, active:false, name:null}` (`user_id` null for a non-global viewer). `OnCallScheduleModal` / `oncallModel` show them as "Silinmiş hesap" (not "Pasif"), key rows by index when `user_id` is null and never send them; an enabled schedule left with only deleted members fails client validation like the server.

**Critical-domain second daily check** — `SchedulerService.runCriticalDomainChecks()` (`@Scheduled` cron `DOMAIN_CRITICAL_CHECK_CRON`, default `0 0 16 * * *` Europe/Istanbul; separate `scheduler_lock` key `domain-critical-sweep`) re-checks ONLY active domains whose last check `days_remaining ≤ DOMAIN_CRITICAL_CHECK_THRESHOLD_DAYS` (default 7). It calls the same `evaluateDomainAlarmsNow`, so a same-day renewal auto-closes the open `DOMAINMON_EXPIRY` alarm without waiting for the next morning sweep, while the daily re-alert dedupe still suppresses a second notification for a still-critical domain. Toggle with `DOMAIN_CRITICAL_CHECK_ENABLED`. Two same-day checks intentionally write two `domain_checks` rows.

### Frontend shape
- Single-page app in `App.jsx` — `tab` state (synced to `?tab=<key>` and validated against a whitelist) switches between Dashboard / All Certs / Stats / Warnings / Renewal (+ Renewal Guide) / Forecast / Inventory (`domains`) / Activity / MyActivity / AlertHistory / WeakAlgo / Uptime / Port / DNS / Weekly Reports / Incident History / Admin / Permissions / Settings (`AdminSettings`) / SystemHealth / AuditLog (`system`) / SQL Playground / Help. No React Router. Several tabs are visibility-gated: Permissions & SQL Playground (global admin), Settings (bootstrap `admin` user), AuditLog (global admin or AUDIT role).
- API client is a flat `api.*` object in `api/client.js` — `fetch` with `credentials: 'include'`. A 401 after a successful login (flagged via `sessionStorage.cm.session.active`) forces `window.location.assign('/?session=expired')`. The initial bootstrap 401 returns `null` and lets `App.jsx` render `<Login>`.
- i18n: `useT(key)` hook + provider in `i18n/index.jsx`; dictionaries live in `i18n/tr.js` (static, in the entry chunk) and `i18n/en.js` (lazy chunk, 2026-10-02 bundle split). `index.jsx` re-exports `TR` but NOT `EN` (importing EN statically would pull it back into the entry — tests import `../i18n/en.js` directly). EN at startup: `main.jsx` preloads it and `LangProvider` shows `LanguageBootSplash` until it arrives; runtime TR→EN switches only after `loadLanguage('en')` resolves (busy state on the switch); a failed load keeps TR, toasts `lang.loadFailed` and forces `X-Lang: tr` for the session. Add new keys to BOTH files. The parity test (`i18n-parity.test.jsx`) enforces both languages have every key, no empty values, matching placeholder counts.
- **Themes (2026-10-05, user decision).** Eight themes: Light and Dark (base) plus Blueprint, Parchment, Alloy, Obsidian, Slag and Crucible.
  - **Single source:** `theme/themes.js` and `ThemeCatalog.java`, kept in the same order by `ThemeCatalogSyncTest` and `theme-catalog-sync.test.js`.
  - **DOM:** `<html data-theme="<id>" data-scheme="light|dark">` + `color-scheme`, switched via `useTheme()` in `i18n/theme.jsx`.
  - **CSS keys on the SCHEME:** the Tailwind `dark:` variant and every dark rule in `App.css` / `globals.css` use `[data-scheme="dark"]`. Light tokens are `:root, [data-scheme="light"]`. Extra themes ONLY override tokens in `styles/themes.css`, imported after `App.css`.
  - **Rules:** never write a theme-specific component rule; never use `dark:` inside a nested `data-theme` preview; in JS ask `useTheme().isDark` / `scheme`, never `theme === 'dark'`. Hardcoded Tailwind palette colours (`bg-blue-50` …) only follow light/dark.
  - **Contrast gate:** `themeContrast.test.js` requires text ≥ 4.5:1, ring ≥ 3:1 and distinct status hues for every theme.
  - **Adding a theme:** update `themes.js`, `ThemeCatalog`, a `themes.css` block, `themeLabels.js`, and add `theme.name.*` / `theme.desc.*` in TR and EN.
  - **Admin:** Settings → Görünüm → Temalar (`?sec=themes`, `ThemeSettingsController`, `/api/admin/themes`, audit `THEME_SETTINGS_SAVE`).
    - Keys `site.monitor.theme.enabled` (CSV) and `site.monitor.theme.default` (`system` or an enabled id) are GLOBAL_ONLY; a scoped admin sees the page read-only.
    - Validation is also enforced in `AppSettingsService`: known ids, at least one enabled, default must be enabled, `system` needs light + dark.
    - `/api/branding` returns `themes: {enabled, default}`.
  - **Per-user choice:** localStorage `site-monitor-theme`, synced through user preferences (`LOCAL_PREF_KEYS` / `UserPreferencesService.THEME_LOCAL_KEY`; unknown values are ignored).
    - At login the server wins and `useThemePrefSync` applies it without a reload.
    - A disabled or unknown choice falls back to the admin default, but the stored choice is kept.
  - **Preview:** tab-only via `startPreview`, with `ThemePreviewBar`.
- **Scoped admin ("müdür", AD ADMIN with `viewTeamIds` set) and Settings (2026-09-10 product decision).** The Settings tab is gated by `systemRole === 'ADMIN'` (global *or* scoped); `AdminSettings` takes `globalAdmin` and renders a "global administrators only" notice instead of the four secret-bearing sections (SMTP, LDAP, Secret Decryptor, Database) when it is false. Backend mirrors this exactly: those four controllers keep `SessionScope.requireNotScopedAdmin`; General/Branding/LoginAnomaly/Storm/UserPush accept the scoped admin, and `AppSettingsService.save` rejects `AppSettingsCatalog.GLOBAL_ONLY` keys (SSRF allow-*, DNS resolvers, CA bundle/TOFU, k6 binary/API, push URL/headers, base-url, CORS, log levels) with 403 for a scoped admin no matter which controller called it; `getCatalogForClient` marks them `global_only`/`read_only` so the UI disables them. `SettingsScopedAdminGateTest` pins the controller split and the key list — add a new secret-bearing setting to `GLOBAL_ONLY`, never rely on the UI lock alone. `PUT /api/admin/general/settings` rejects keys of the groups that have their own page (`GeneralSettingsController.OWN_PAGE_GROUPS` = the frontend `SKIP_GROUPS`: branding, retention, userpush, storm, login-anomaly, login-methods, appearance, executive-summary, tech-error-report, grafana) with 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` + field (2026-10-22, ADMIN-15) — those groups validate on their own endpoints.
- **Global-only actions follow the GLOBAL admin, never the role string (2026-10-22, bug hunt Theme G).** A button / section whose endpoint requires `SessionScope.isGlobalAdmin` / `Actor.isAdmin()` reads `globalAdmin` (the prop App passes — K3: `WeeklyReportsPage`, `SystemHealth`, `CertificateModal`, `AdminSettings`, `AdminPanel`; `StormStatusPage` gets `canOpenSettings`) or, when absent, `usePermissions().scope?.global_admin === true` (K1, `components/admin/useGlobalAdmin.js`); unknown = NOT global (hidden). `systemRole === 'ADMIN'` alone includes the scoped admin ("müdür"). Covered: System Health scheduler "Run now", force-release lock, heartbeat refresh, session terminate (table, session detail, directory `rowActions`), SMTP resend, push requeue (also need `system_health.actions`), weekly-availability send-log CTA, release-history edit (`release_history.edit` ∧ not scoped admin); Settings `GLOBAL_ONLY_SECTIONS` + `weeklyavail`, `certinvreport` (notice text `settings.globalOnlyBodyOrg` for non-secret sections), Domain diagnostics proxy-CA capture; Teams add / delete / AD audit; user Directory (AD) tab; ADMIN / AUDIT rows: no Edit / temporary password / Delete for a non-global viewer (member cards open the read-only detail); user-editor role picker USER / TEAM_ADMIN for a non-global viewer (`roleOptionsFor(…, { globalAdmin })`); Keyword / PageSpeed custom headers and the Domain form "Sorun Tanıla" (UIM-15). Weekly reports: `isAdmin = systemRole === 'ADMIN' && globalAdmin` drives the team filter / picker, transfer, delete-any, revise, lock take-over and reminders; a scoped admin behaves like a member of their own team (server: list / years / create force `actor.teamId()`, a foreign `team_id` is 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` field `team_id`, K8 — the create screen shows that text under the Team step (`[data-field="team_id"]` + `wr-create-team-error`, role=alert), never only a toast; a non-global user's create request sends no `team_id`). Gates: `SystemHealth.test.jsx` (Theme G blocks), `SmtpLogView.test.jsx`, `PushLogView.test.jsx`, `AdminSettings.test.jsx`, `DomainDiagnostics.test.jsx`, `TeamManager.test.jsx`, `UserManager.test.jsx`, `UserDetailPanel.test.jsx`, `userDirectoryModel.test.js`, `WeeklyReportsPage.bugfix8b.test.jsx`.
- **Password rule = server rule (2026-10-22, UIA-H1).** `PasswordChangeModal` mirrors `site.monitor.password.min-length` 6 / `max-length` 64 (`PWD_MIN_LENGTH` / `PWD_MAX_LENGTH`, gate `passwordLengthRule.test.js` reads application.properties); no `maxLength` attribute on the password inputs (the browser cut pasted passphrases silently to 10 characters); the server's "too short / too long (min|max N chars)" text is shown with its own N (`serverLengthMessage`); a server field error `field: "password"` (edge whitespace) shows under the new-password field.
- **Server messages follow the UI language (2026-09-10).** `api/client.js` sends `X-Lang: tr|en` (read from `LANG_STORAGE_KEY`) on every request; backend `util/Msg.t(tr, en)` resolves it (X-Lang → Accept-Language → `tr`; no request context → `tr`). Any `"message"`/`IllegalArgumentException` text a settings page shows as a toast must go through `Msg.t` — never a bare Turkish literal (that was QA ISSUE-001: English UI, Turkish toast). Audit details and log lines stay Turkish on purpose; `MsgTest` pins the resolution order.
- **UI library: shadcn/ui is the project default (product decision, 2026-09-25).** Every new or changed UI element is built from the components at https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components, which live in `frontend/src/components/shadcn/` (added with `npx shadcn add`, styled with Tailwind v4). Do not write new elements with the legacy App.css classes (`.btn`, `.input`, `.modal-box`, `.badge-*`), and don't restyle a legacy element to *look like* shadcn. Convert the legacy elements you touch. If shadcn has no direct match, compose shadcn components; only as a last resort write Tailwind in the shadcn style. Read `frontend/docs/SHADCN.md` before any UI work. It covers the component catalogue, which component to use for which need, the project's adaptations and the testing pitfalls. The adaptations are: React 18 needs `forwardRef` on components used as an `asChild` child; the shadcn CLI emits a broken `cn` import; App.css is unlayered, so it beats Tailwind. Tests should query by role/name or `data-slot`, not by class. The project uses the *Radix* variants (read `ui.shadcn.com/docs/components/radix/<name>`; the docs site now defaults to Base UI pages, and `shadcn init` must never be re-run). The `shadcnOnly.test.js` gate counts raw HTML controls and hand-rolled widgets per file against a baseline that may only shrink (`npm run shadcn:baseline`).
- **Mobile web is a requirement (product decision, 2026-09-26).** Every screen must be fully usable on phones (360–414 px) and tablets (768 px). Build mobile-first with Tailwind breakpoints (`sm`/`md`/`lg`), with no fixed pixel widths on content, wrapping toolbars, tables that scroll or turn into cards, modals that fit the screen, and touch targets of at least 40 px. Verify at 390×844 and 768×1024 with `e2e/responsive.spec.js`; jsdom proves nothing about layout. Full rules: `frontend/docs/RESPONSIVE.md`.
- **Cards and detail windows never get a coloured left stripe (product decision, 2026-09-26).** No `before:` bar, no `border-l-*` status colour, no inset left shadow. Status is shown by the status `Badge` and `data-status`.
- Icons: `lucide-react` only — never emoji.
- **404, page meta, favicon (2026-10-08, user request).**
  - **Routing:** `config/SpaNotFoundAdvice` (ordered before `GlobalExceptionHandler`) answers `NoResourceFoundException`: an unknown page (GET/HEAD, accepts HTML) gets `index.html` with HTTP 404, an unknown `/api/**` a JSON 404 (`code:"NOT_FOUND"`), a missing asset (`/assets/`, `/brand/`, `/fonts/`, known extensions) a plain-text 404 — never HTML, always `no-store` (a missing asset used to inherit the 1-year immutable cache header).
  - **Frontend:** `utils/appRoutes.js` is the single source of `VALID_TABS` (`isKnownAppPath`, `tabFromSearch`, `NOT_FOUND_TAB`). `main.jsx` lazy-loads `pages/NotFoundPage` for any path other than `/` / `index.html` BEFORE the auth bootstrap (public, branded, all themes). An unknown `?tab=` shows `notfound/NotFoundPanel` in-app; a tab the role cannot open shows "Erişim yok" (used to be blank); a stale saved landing tab still falls back to the dashboard.
  - **Meta:** `hooks/usePageMeta` + `utils/pageMeta.js` are the ONLY writers of `document.title` ("<Sayfa> · <brand>", white-label `tab_title`/`app_name` as suffix) and the description meta, for every tab and the login / session / passive / maintenance / password-change / 404 / no-access states. A new tab needs `meta.tab.<id>.title|description` in TR + EN (`pageMeta-gate.test.js`: title ≤ 60, description 50–160, unique, translated; also compares `responsive.spec` tabs with `VALID_TABS`).
  - **Favicon:** `branding/assets/favicon.svg` is a small-size rendering of the SAME turnip mark (body `#813387`, leaves `#64A64F`); `node branding/tools/make_favicon.mjs` (Playwright Chromium rasteriser) regenerates `favicon.ico` (16/32/48), `favicon-16/32.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `icon-192/512.png`, maskable 512 and `site.webmanifest` (served as `application/manifest+json`); `make_variants.py` no longer writes the favicon. `useStatusFavicon('ok')` leaves the favicon alone. `brand-default.test.jsx` pins sizes, palette, ICO frames and public/ ↔ branding/assets parity.
  - **Live tour:** `e2e/live/site-tour.live.spec.js` visits every tab at 1280 + 390 against the real backend (no 5xx, no raw keys, localized title/description, no overflow, heading visible) + the 404 contract. Run it after cross-cutting UI changes.
- **Error contract (2026-10-08, user request: "hata mesajları çok açıklayıcı olsun").**
  - **Backend:** every error body is `{success:false, code, error (Msg.t, what · why · next step), request_id}`; `CorrelationIdFilter` honours a safe incoming `X-Request-Id` (≤ 40 chars) or generates one, returns it in the header and logs `[istek=<id>]` on 500s. `GlobalExceptionHandler` never leaks exception text (unexpected 500 → generic friendly text + log); stable codes for validation (`VALIDATION_FAILED` + `fields`), DB constraint classes (`DUPLICATE_RECORD`, `REFERENCED_RECORD`, `MISSING_REQUIRED_FIELD`, `VALUE_TOO_LONG`), `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (+ `max_mb`), `CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION`, `DB_UNAVAILABLE`, etc. A pessimistic lock failure (`PessimisticLockingFailureException`: PostgreSQL deadlock 40P01, serialization 40001, lock wait 55P03) is 503 `LOCK_CONFLICT` + `Retry-After: 2` (body `retry_after_seconds: 2`) "the action may not have completed — try again in a few seconds", never `DB_UNAVAILABLE` (the database is reachable, two transactions collided) and never a 409 / `CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION`: client screens read those as "someone else saved this record" (report autosave stops with the conflict banner, the inventory form writes it under the domain field, code login opens the "session elsewhere" step), while a 503 keeps autosave backing off and retrying; the text never says "nothing was saved" (multi-step requests such as the approval decision or the send claim may have partly run). The client shows the server text (`friendlyServerMessage` keeps it for any status); `api/client.js` still retries only network rejections of GET / HEAD (review 2026-10-18; gates `GlobalExceptionHandlerTest.pessimisticLockIsRetryableLockConflict503`, `errorMessages.test.js`, `TechErrorReportAutosave.test.jsx`). Existing domain codes (DOMAIN_EXISTS, MAINTENANCE, ACCOUNT_INACTIVE, MANUAL_CERT, …) are unchanged. `util/ErrorTexts.safeMessage` for controller catch blocks — never return `e.getMessage()` to a client.
