The tail is now `AuditLogRepository.findTopBySeqIsNotNullOrderBySeqDesc` — NO `, id DESC` tie-break (review F1: `idx_audit_seq(seq)` cannot serve it; with the degenerate statistics PostgreSQL sorted every chained row on every audit write while holding the cluster-wide chain lock, ~190 ms at 1 M rows; without it a 1-row backward index scan; gate `AuditChainPostgresTest.tailQueryPlan_degenerateStatistics_backwardIndexScanNoSort` EXPLAINs it under `n_distinct = 1` statistics, `AuditChainNullsFirstH2Test.tailQuery_hasNoTieBreak` pins the generated SQL). `AuditChainRestartJob` (10 min after `ApplicationReadyEvent` — `site.monitor.audit.chain-restart.initial-delay-ms` — then hourly until done on this pod; `scheduler_lock` `audit-chain-restart`; marker `2026-10-23-audit-chain-restart` re-read INSIDE the lock; ≤ 24 failed attempts per pod; unreadable dialect / marker = retry later): when the chain is degenerate (> 1 row with `seq = 1`, `FETCH FIRST 2 ROWS`) and no restart row exists, `AuditService.restartChainIfDegenerate` writes ONE `AUDIT_CHAIN_RESTART` row (category SYSTEM) — the statistics scan runs BEFORE the lock (F5), and only the re-check, the tail read and the INSERT run under the SAME chain lock as the writers (PostgreSQL advisory xact lock, else the JVM lock): `seq = tail + 1`, `prev_hash = GENESIS`, `seq_locked = TRUE`, actor SYSTEM, detail `{reason, rows_before, max_seq_before, seq1_rows, period_from, period_to}` (no user data; counts taken just before the lock). The marker is written ONLY when the restart row was written or already exists — a "not degenerate" probe finishes this pod only and the next boot probes again (F3: an earlier "healthy" marker would have blocked the repair forever if old pods degenerated the chain later). No hashed column of an existing row is ever rewritten. On PostgreSQL the job stops at once when `ux_audit_seq` is already valid (F8); otherwise one conditional UPDATE clears `seq_locked` (NOT hashed — `canonical(...)` excludes it) on locked rows whose seq is DUPLICATED among locked rows below the restart seq (the verified segment is never touched; the seq criterion also covers seq-1 rows an old 20.168.0 pod writes AFTER the restart), then starts a builder for `ux_audit_seq` ONLY (`DeferredIndexBuilder.only(jdbc, name)` — F6; does not touch `lastRun()`), unless `DeferredIndexBuilder.SQL_AUDIT_SEQ_OLD_WRITER_RECENT` counts a row written after the oldest restart row but below its seq in the last 10 min (an old pod still alive → retry next hour; the index would turn its second seq-1 insert into a fallback-file write); ≤ 3 kicks per pod. The SAME query guards the boot-time deferred build (`DeferredIndexBuilder.BUILD_GUARDS`, `Session.guardCount`; F7): while an old writer was active in the last 10 min the boot run SKIPS `ux_audit_seq` (an INVALID leftover is dropped) and builds the rest. **Verify anchor (F4):** `verifyChain` starts at the OLDEST remaining `AUDIT_CHAIN_RESTART` row with `GENESIS` (`seq >=` its seq; only one is ever written legitimately, so a forged later restart row with `GENESIS` + a correct hash is a visible break, not a way to hide earlier rows); with no restart row it starts at the LOWEST remaining chained row and uses ITS stored `prev_hash` as the anchor — an un-purged healthy chain starts at `GENESIS` exactly as before (`verifiedFrom` null), a chain whose oldest rows were deleted by audit retention no longer reads as broken (`verifiedFrom.kind = retention`). `ChainVerification` carries `verifiedFrom` (`VerifiedFrom(id, seq, eventTime, kind)`, kind `restart` | `retention`, null when verified from GENESIS) and `unverified` (chained rows below a restart row; 0 for the retention anchor; seq-less legacy rows are not counted); the 4-arg constructor stays. `GET /api/admin/audit/integrity` adds `unverified` (always) and `verified_from {id, seq, event_time, kind}`. The audit viewer draws the "verified from" strip ONLY when the verification is ok (F2 — a failed verification shows only the failure): restart → "Zincir <tarih> tarihinden itibaren doğrulandı — öncesindeki N kayıt onarımdan önce yazıldı, doğrulanamaz" (`.one` for 1), retention → "Zincir <tarih> tarihli kayıttan itibaren doğrulandı; daha eski kayıtlar saklama süresi dolduğu için silindi" (`data-slot="audit-integrity-verified-from"`, `data-kind`; old server = today's text). Known limit: in an environment where an old pod wrote seq-1 rows AFTER the restart, those rows outlive the restart row by minutes; for the ≤ 1 day between the night retention deletes the restart row and the night it deletes them, the lowest-row anchor sees them and reports a break. Rolling deploy: 20.168.0 pods keep writing `seq = 1 / GENESIS` during the rollout — outside the verified segment, counted in `unverified`; until the job runs (≥ 10 min after the first new pod's boot) the integrity check still reports the break. Rollback past this release: the restart row stays and old code's `verifyChain` reports a break (as before); once `ux_audit_seq` exists, 20.168.0's NULLS-FIRST writer collides on its SECOND seq-1 insert and every later audit write lands in the fallback file — drop the index first (`DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF EXISTS ux_audit_seq;`); after re-upgrading, the job clears those duplicates and rebuilds it. Gates: `AuditChainNullsFirstH2Test` (H2 with `DEFAULT_NULL_ORDERING=HIGH` = PostgreSQL ordering: legacy NULL rows → increasing seq; tail SQL without tie-break; restart row + verified segment + tamper detection; idempotent; marker re-read inside the lock; healthy chain → no marker; later degeneration repaired next boot; retention purge with / without a restart row; forged second restart row = break; valid index skips cleanup; old-writer query on real rows; duplicate-lock cleanup only below the restart; index kick rules; 24-attempt cap; lock elsewhere), `AuditServiceTest.restart_statsScanBeforeAdvisoryLock`, `DeferredIndexBuilderTest.run_auditSeqGuard_*|only_buildsSingleIndex_doesNotRecordLastRun`, `AuditEventCatalogTest.auditChainRestartIsSystem`, `AuditChainPostgresTest.legacyNullRows_twoPods_increasingUniqueSeq|restartJob_degenerateChain_verifiedFromRestart_uniqueIndexBuildable|tailQueryPlan_degenerateStatistics_backwardIndexScanNoSort` (postgres-it), `AuditControllerTest.integrity_verifiedFromRestart`, `AuditLogViewer.test.jsx` (strip only when ok, `.one`, retention text, old server), `e2e/audit-integrity.spec.js` (390 / 768 / 1280). **Failed-login anomaly scan on several pods (2026-10-21, R1-C2, MP-10):** `FailedLoginAnomalyIncidentService.scan()` skips when the shared `login_anomaly_state.last_scan_at` is newer than `now − site.monitor.failed-login.scan-ms + 5 s` (read inside the `failed-login-anomaly` lock — another pod already scanned this interval). Every INITIAL / ESCALATION / REALERT stamps `login_anomaly_incident.last_alert_attempt_at` before the async dispatch, and a REALERT also needs the last attempt to be ≥ 10 min old (an in-flight / just-failed first mail is not followed by an immediate REALERT; a failed mail is still retried on the next scan). `SecurityMailDispatcher` writes `last_alert_at` with a targeted UPDATE (`LoginAnomalyIncidentRepository.stampLastAlertAt`) — never a whole-entity save that could undo a concurrent `resolved=true` — and the entity maps `last_alert_at` `updatable=false`. Gates: `FailedLoginAnomalyMultiPodTest`, `SecurityMailDispatcherTest`.
- **`MonitoringOutageService`** — confirmation state machine for the uptime/port/DNS sweeps (alarm types ACCESSIBILITY / PORT_DOWN / DNS_FAILURE / DNS_CHANGED). Requires N consecutive confirmations before alerting to damp flapping; type-isolation keeps one signal type from masking another. Monitoring-type suppression rows (`network_outage_events.source = <alert type>`) are single per source (2026-10-21, R1-B, MP-16): `noteSuppression` inserts only if no ONGOING row of the source exists (a unique violation from the PostgreSQL partial index `ux_noe_one_ongoing` = "already ongoing"), `clearSuppression` resolves EVERY ONGOING row of the source (bounded loop, 20) — a handover used to leave a second row "ongoing" forever. Gate: `SuppressionRowsMultiPodTest`.
- **LDAP/AD** (`LdapSettingsService` / `LdapDirectoryService` / `LdapProvisioningService`, UI under `/api/admin/ldap`) — DB-persisted config with AES-GCM bind password and live reload (no restart); config, test-bind, attribute viewer, login (`AuthController.tryLdapLogin`) and provisioning (`LdapProvisioningService`) are live; group→role mappings are applied since 2026-10-11 only when `ldap_settings.role_mapping_enabled` is on (default off; `service/ldap/LdapRoleMapping`, locked role / last active ADMIN / bootstrap admin never changed); the "Kim ne olacak?" preview resolves CN rules at the naming-context root (the `DC=` suffix of the base DN, which is usually the USER search base) because login matches a `memberOf` CN anywhere, not only under the group search base. No JNDI dependency was added. **Per-field locks** (`LdapFieldLocks`, column `app_users.locked_fields`, wire `locked_field_keys`): an admin edit that REALLY changes an AD-sourced field of an LDAP user locks that field so provisioning/resync never overwrites it (blank ↔ null is not a change); only a global admin releases it via `POST /api/admin/users/{id}/field-unlock` (audit `USER_FIELD_UNLOCK`, scoped manager 403), and `LdapMembershipService.check()` lists every field as `fields[{key, ad, local, locked, differs}]` for the "Compare with AD" tab.
  **2026-10-12:** a settings save is applied to the CURRENT DB row (never the up-to-10-s-stale pod cache; first save starts from the form defaults) and the form sends only changed fields — the three role-mapping fields always together, so a rule set is enabled exactly as previewed. The manager path (provisioning a subordinate's manager from AD) never provisions, modifies or links a same-named LOCAL account (bootstrap admin included). Gates: `LdapSettingsServiceTest.saveAppliesToCurrentRowNotStaleCache`, `LdapProvisioningServiceTest.managerPathNeverTouchesLocalAccount`, `LdapRoleMappingSection.test.jsx`.
- **Weekly reports** (`WeeklyReportService`, `WeeklyReportReminderService`, `/api/weekly-reports`) — per-team DRAFT→PENDING→APPROVED state machine on ISO weeks; Friday 09:00 Europe/Istanbul reminder cron. Public **email-token approval links** intentionally bypass login. **Transitions are claimed, then mailed (2026-10-21, multi-pod MP-39):** submit / approve / reject / resend / reopen and the e-mail link (`approveViaToken` / `rejectViaToken`, no longer `@Transactional`) take the state with ONE conditional UPDATE first, each in its own transaction (`WeeklyReportRepository.claimSubmit|claimApprove|claimApproveByToken|claimReject|claimRejectByToken|claimReopen`; `claimResend` = status APPROVED + the read `version`). Only the winner (rowcount 1) writes the system comment and sends the PO / manager / team mail and the approval push. The loser (double click, two tabs, two pods, link ∥ in-app, approve ∥ reject) gets 409 `WEEKLY_REPORT_STATE_CHANGED` + `current_status` (`WeeklyReportService.StateChangedException`, a `CodedConflictException`; TR/EN text from `lostMessage`) and sends nothing; on the e-mail link pages it is a neutral page without a security event (`WeeklyReportController.decisionAlreadyTaken`). A link whose token was replaced by a re-submit cannot decide (the claim also matches the token). Manager recipients are resolved BEFORE the claim (no manager → 409 `MANAGER_CONTACT_MISSING`, status unchanged). A mail / build failure AFTER the claim never rolls the transition back: the `weekly_report_mails` attempt row says `FAILED: <exception class>`, the push says the e-mail failed, "Tekrar gönder" repeats it. `sent_at` is stamped by `markSent` only for the same approval cycle (`approved_at`; legacy rows with null `approved_at` match "-"). No writer saves the whole entity over a transition: content save = `updateContent` conditional on the read `version` (a stale autosave cannot turn a submitted report back into DRAFT or clear the PO's token → 409 VERSION_CONFLICT), lock heartbeat / release write only the editing columns (`setEditingLock`, `touchEditingLock`, `releaseEditingLock`), and `WeeklyReport` is `@DynamicUpdate`. Known, unchanged: an approval through the e-mail link sends no team / manager push (only the in-app approval does). Mock tests stub the claims with `WeeklyReportClaimStubs.allWin`. Gates: `WeeklyReportTransitionRaceTest`, `WeeklyClaimsPostgresTest` (a lock-waiting conditional UPDATE re-evaluates and returns 0 rows, not an error), `WeeklyReportControllerTest.approveLink_lostRace_neutralPageNoSecurityEvent|approve_lostRace_409WithCurrentStatus`. **Approval token never stored or returned (2026-10-22, WR-1):** the PO mail carries `…/approve-link?token=…`; `recordMail` stores the body with the token masked (`WeeklyReportService.redactApprovalTokens`, `approve|reject-link[/confirm|/reject]?token=` → `•••`) and `GET /{id}/mails` masks stored rows too (rows written before the fix) — the mail history is readable by every reader of the report (USER, AUDIT), the token is an unauthenticated approval. `mails()` returns copies (the stored entity is never touched). The link pages show the service's own text for IllegalArgument/IllegalState, a generic `Msg.t` text + `log.warn` for anything else, and the mail result only as delivered / not delivered (never the raw `mail_status`). `create()` with a foreign `team_id` from a non-global actor → 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` field `team_id` (K8, WR-2 default decision: no silent swap to the actor's team; only a global admin picks another team). Gates: `WeeklyReportTokenRedactionTest`, `WeeklyReportControllerTest.approveLink_unexpectedError_genericText|approveLink_mailStatus_notLeaked`. "Onaya Gönder" waits for an in-flight (auto)save before saving the rest and submitting (`savePromiseRef`, FE-8: the confirm dialog could be open while the autosave started, and the submit stopped silently); if the content still cannot be saved it says so (`wr.submitNotSaved`), save errors keep their own message. **Friday reminder (multi-pod R2a-D1, MP-42):** `WeeklyReportReminderService` isolates each team (one team's template / mail failure no longer stops the remaining teams) and writes one `notification_logs` row per decision of a team whose module is on (trigger `WEEKLY_REPORT_REMINDER`, alertEventId 0: the mail status, `FAILED: <class>`, `SKIPPED: takım e-postası yok`, `SKIPPED: rapor zaten onaya gönderilmiş`, `SKIPPED: takımın hatırlatması kapalı`; the sent body is in `message` — resendable from the SMTP log, the 421 write-back matches subject + recipient; label in `admin/logTriggers.js`). The manual trigger still reminds every team (product decision C-R7 open). Old pods write no log rows. Gate: `WeeklyReportReminderServiceTest.teamTwoThrows_teamsOneAndThreeSentAndLogged|traceFailureDoesNotStopReminders`.
- **Monthly certificate inventory report status (2026-10-22, UIA-C2 / K9):** `CertificateInventoryReportService.historyStatus` maps the mail result for the history row: `SENT` → SENT, `QUEUED…` → QUEUED, `SKIPPED…` → SKIPPED, else FAILED (`NO_RECIPIENT` kept). Before, everything not FAILED was archived as SENT and shown as "last run". The same-day guard ignores undelivered attempts (FAILED / SKIPPED / NO_RECIPIENT rows don't block the scheduled run; SENT / QUEUED / legacy null do). Send-test (inventory + weekly availability) is `success` only for SENT / QUEUED (`delivered`); "Run now" returns `outcome` (`SENT | QUEUED | SKIPPED | FAILED | NO_RECIPIENT`), which `CertInventoryReportSettings` / `WeeklyAvailabilitySettings` classify (`reportSendStatus.js`; absent → derived from `status`). The month's single history row that already says SENT is never overwritten by a later attempt that did not deliver (QUEUED / SKIPPED / FAILED / NO_RECIPIENT — usually a manual "Run now"); the attempt stays in `notification_logs` (archive row) and the app log. A new SENT updates the row. Gate `CertificateInventoryReportServiceTest.sentRowIsNeverOverwrittenByUndeliveredAttempt`.
- **Weekly availability mail = at most once per team × ISO week (2026-10-21, multi-pod MP-40).** `WeeklyAvailabilityReportService.sendWeeklyReports` is no longer ONE `@Transactional` over all teams (one failure or a pod stop used to roll back the SENT rows of teams already mailed, and the next run mailed them again). Per team: the `weekly_availability_log` row is claimed as `SENDING` BEFORE the report is built (no row → INSERT, `uk_wal_team_week` stops a second pod / run; existing row → `claimExisting` CAS on the read status + `sent_at`; while SENDING, `sent_at` holds the claim stamp) → build + send → `finishClaim` (only this claim) to `SENT` (SENT / QUEUED_RETRY) / `FAILED` / `SKIPPED` (mail channel off — was recorded SENT and never retried) / `NO_RECIPIENT`. One team's exception makes only that team FAILED; the others are sent and recorded. Every decision on a claimed team writes a `notification_logs` row (trigger `WEEKLY_AVAILABILITY`; `SKIPPED: alıcı yok`, `FAILED: rapor hazırlanamadı/gönderilemedi (<class>)`, and `FAILED: envanter okunamadı (<class>)` before any claim). A catch-up tick on the trigger day mails teams that have NO row for the week yet (missed main run — see "Cron rounds" / "Weekly availability catch-up" under the cluster-schedule bullet). `SENT` is skipped by EVERY run: the manual `POST /api/admin/system/weekly-availability/run` (`sendWeeklyReports(true)`, formerly "force = send to every team again") now sends only teams not yet sent this week — a deliberate re-send to one team goes through the Settings test send to the team address; FAILED / NO_RECIPIENT / SKIPPED are retried by any run. A `SENDING` row older than `STALE_SENDING_MINUTES` (30) = interrupted (pod died mid-send; the mail may have gone): scheduled runs never take it over (WARN + `interrupted` counter), only the manual run does (at-most-once rule); the status card shows it as `INTERRUPTED` and the System Health weekly card counts it as failed. `SendResult` gained `failed`, `skippedMailOff`, `skippedInProgress`, `interrupted` (6-arg constructor kept). Known limit: if the final `finishClaim` write fails after the mail went, the row stays SENDING → shown INTERRUPTED; a manual run would mail that team again. Rollout / rollback: a pod older than this change treats `SENDING` as "not sent" (and its manual run is still "force"). Gates: `WeeklyAvailabilityClaimTest`, `WeeklyClaimsPostgresTest`, `WeeklyAvailabilityReportServiceTest.send_idempotent` (re-baselined: the manual run no longer re-sends a SENT team).
- **Page usage and other boot / admin jobs on several pods (multi-pod Sürüm 2a, 2026-10-23, MP-46).** `PageUsageService.flush` writes each (day, user, tab) key with a race-safe upsert (PostgreSQL `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, `last_seen = GREATEST`; H2: UPDATE → INSERT → on duplicate UPDATE) and decrements the in-memory buffer right after that key's write (a failing write stops the round; written keys are never counted twice). `MonitoringGroupBackfill` fills null monitor teams with a conditional `UPDATE … WHERE id = ? AND team_id IS NULL` under the `monitoring-group-team-backfill` lock, once (marker `2026-10-23-monitoring-group-team-backfill-v2`, re-read inside the lock, written only after full success); the registry seed still runs on every boot. `POST /api/admin/retention/run` runs under the `nightly-cleanup` lock: a valid lock row held by someone → 409 `RETENTION_RUNNING` (TR/EN text); the lock not taken and no valid row / row unreadable (transient DB error) → 503 `DB_UNAVAILABLE`; nothing deleted, not audited either way; the dry run stays unlocked. A manual run that overlaps the nightly job's start (03:30 Istanbul) delays that night's `cleanupOldLogs`: since multi-pod Sürüm 2b it retries the lock every 30 s for ≤ 15 min and then runs (roll-ups + purge + prune); only after 15 min does it skip the night (WARN). Rolling deploy: old pods keep the abort-and-recount flush (possible over-count from those pods only) and an unlocked manual retention run. Gates: `PageUsageMultiPodTest`, `PageUsagePostgresTest`, `MonitoringGroupBackfillMultiPodTest`, `RetentionAdminControllerTest.runNow_whileNightlyHoldsLock_409|runNow_lockCallFailedWithoutHolder_503`.
- **Incidents** (`IncidentService`, `/api/incidents`) — SRE incident ledger with image attachments, trend rollups, and configurable status/priority/category options.
- **Postmortem template + action tracking (2026-10-11, user request).** `service/incident/*`, `IncidentPostmortemController`, `components/incidenthistory/postmortem/*`. The incident record itself is unchanged.
  - **Data:** `incident_postmortems` (one per incident, `@Version` → stale save 409 `CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION`; status DRAFT → IN_REVIEW → PUBLISHED, `published_at/by` stamped on publish, cleared when unpublished) with ten blameless sections (summary, impact, root_cause, trigger (`trigger_text` column), detection, resolution, went_well, went_wrong, lucky, lessons; ≤ 20 000 chars each). `incident_action_items` (≤ 50 per incident; kind PREVENT/DETECT/MITIGATE/PROCESS/OTHER, priority, status OPEN/IN_PROGRESS/DONE/CANCELLED, owner = an ACTIVE user (`owner_user_id`, in `UserRefWire.KEYS`; `owner_name` snapshot), due date `yyyy-MM-dd` Istanbul, `completed_at`, `reminded_on`). With no report the GET returns a template prefilled from the incident's description / business impact / RCA / resolution — nothing is written until Save. Section/status/kind lists are mirrored in `postmortemModel.js` (test reads the Java source).
  - **Gates (same as incident records):** read = `incidents.view` + `IncidentController.canReadIncident`; write = `incidents.manage/edit` + `inOwnIncidentScope` (the 7/24 read widening never writes). Exception: the action's OWNER may change ONLY its status (any other field → 403, never a silent partial save). The cross-incident list `GET /api/incidents/actions` (status open|done|all, mine, overdue, q) = read scope OR owner. Rows carry `mine` (computed server-side — the client never compares opaque ids). Audit `INCIDENT_POSTMORTEM_SAVE`, `INCIDENT_ACTION_ADD|UPDATE|DELETE`.
  - **Timeline:** built on read from existing rows only — incident stamps, the linked alarm (opened / first SENT notification + count / acknowledged / resolved / comments ≤ 50) ONLY with `alerts.read` and when the alarm's team is visible (teamless alarm = linked by the incident → visible), action added / done / cancelled, postmortem published. `deployment_history` is SiteMonitor's own rollouts, so it is deliberately NOT in the timeline.
  - **List badges:** `/api/incidents` rows gain `postmortem_status`, `actions_open|overdue|done|total` from TWO grouped queries per page (`IncidentPostmortemService.summaries`). Deleting an incident deletes its postmortem + actions (retention orphan rules `incident-postmortems-orphan` / `incident-action-items-orphan` back it up).
  - **Reminder:** `IncidentActionReminderService` daily 09:00 Istanbul (`site.monitor.incident.action-reminder-cron`, `…-enabled` true) under `scheduler_lock` `incident-action-reminder` + (since multi-pod Sürüm 3-1) the per-trigger cron round `round:incident-action-reminder` claimed inside the lock (item-claim protected — a round read error still runs it; a muted system-maintenance day writes ONE `SKIPPED` row per owner; the manual run claims no round): one mail per owner for actions due tomorrow, today, on the first overdue day, then weekly. Each item is claimed BEFORE sending (conditional UPDATE on `reminded_on`) → at most once across pods. Every decision is a `notification_logs` row (trigger `INCIDENT_ACTION`, alertEventId 0): SENT / FAILED / no address / passive user / system maintenance (muted → NO claim, retried next day). `reminded_on` is `updatable=false` (2026-10-22, IN-1): only the claim UPDATEs and `resetReminder` (new due date / new owner, targeted UPDATE) write it — an entity save racing the 09:00 claim can no longer write the old value back. Gate `IncidentPostmortemQueriesTest.staleSaveKeepsReminderClaim`.
  - **UI:** detail sheet card "Postmortem ve aksiyonlar" → `PostmortemModal` (tabs Report / Action items / Timeline, forceMounted; textarea editing, rendered markdown read-only, conflict banner + "load latest", Markdown download via `utils/csvExport.downloadText`); `ih_view=actions` switch = `ActionItemsView` (KPI tiles open / overdue / mine, filters `ih_astatus|ih_amine|ih_aover|ih_aq`, owner status change, "Open incident"). Adding a section / status / kind: Java list + `postmortemModel.js` + TR/EN `pm.*`.
  - **Gates:** `IncidentPostmortemQueriesTest` (H2: JPQL, claims, delete), `IncidentPostmortemServiceTest`, `IncidentActionReminderServiceTest`, `IncidentPostmortemControllerTest`, `postmortemModel.test.js`, `PostmortemModal.test.jsx`, `IncidentPostmortemFlow.test.jsx`, `e2e/postmortem.spec.js` (390/768/1280).
  - **2026-10-12:** `GET /api/incidents/{id}` carries the same summaries as the list; rows whose summaries were read carry `pm_summary: true` (without it the card says "open to see", never "none yet"). The owner picker lists ACTIVE users only (`/api/users/directory` rows carry an additive `active`), a stored inactive owner stays selectable as "(pasif)". The actions view reloads page 0 + totals after a status change and de-duplicates appended pages.
- **Channel technical error reports (2026-10-13, user request: "her ay sorumluluğumuzdaki kanallar için önceki ayın teknik hata raporu; rapor generic olsun, bir takımda birden fazla kanal olabilir; her ekip kendi şablonunu oluşturabilmeli; X kanalı şablonunu aç dediğinde onu açmalı")** — a SEPARATE module (weekly reports / executive summary untouched). `service/techerror/*`, `controller/TechErrorReportController` (`/api/tech-error-reports`), PDF `service/report/TechErrorReportPdfWriter`, mail `service/mail/TechErrorReportMail`, UI `components/techerror/*` at `?tab=channelreports` (the tab id must not contain "error" — `errorMessageQuality.test.js` reads every key with "error" as an error message).
  - **Model:** `tech_error_channels` (team × name unique, `name_key` = Turkish lower-case; ≤ 50 per team; own `template_json` + `template_version` — the template is PER CHANNEL), `tech_error_reports` (channel × year × month unique; the channel template is COPIED into `template_json` at creation — a later template change never alters a report; a draft takes it only via `refresh-template`, which keeps values / images of matching ids and deletes the rest after a confirm that shows the counts), `tech_error_report_images` (one per slot, BYTEA — `@Column(length)` only for H2's VARBINARY(255) default; PG stays `bytea`, `TechErrorReportPostgresTest`). No column on existing tables.
  - **Template (`TechErrorTemplate`):** sections `metrics` (number / percent / yesno / text / textarea), `issues` (repeated anomaly topics, title pattern `{konu}`), `text`, `images` (slots `half` = one column, `full` = two). A field may carry `skip_if_no` = a yes/no field of the same template (2026-10-14, user request: "kullanıcı kesinti yok işareti koyarsa bu alan muaf olsun"): when that question is explicitly "No" the field is EXEMPT — hidden in the form (with a note under the question), its value dropped on save (`TechErrorValues`), left out of the PDF and the mail (`TechErrorTemplate.exempt`); blank or "Yes" keeps it required. Server rule (`resolveSkipRefs`): target is a yesno field, not itself, and not itself linked (no chains) — strict 400, stored templates drop a bad link; the editor's `sanitizeSkipRefs` clears links that became invalid. Default template: `had-outage` ("Dönemde kesinti oldu mu?") controls `error-rate-excl`, `outage-errors`, `outage-share`; reports opened before keep their copied template (no question → unchanged). Placeholders `{kanal}` `{ay}` `{yil}` (EN `{channel}` `{month}` `{year}`), month names in the TEMPLATE language. Client templates are validated STRICTLY (400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` field `template`), stored ones LENIENTLY (`fromStored` never throws). Limits: 20 sections, 30 fields, 40 slots, 64 KB; values 256 KB, 30 topics. `techErrorModel.test.js` reads the Java limits and month names — change both sides together.
  - **Values:** user-entered, EXCEPT fields with an automatic calculation (`calc`, 2026-10-16 — see "Automatic rates" below). Number ≥ 0, percent may be negative, unknown ids dropped. Save = `@Version` (stale → 409 `CONCURRENT_MODIFICATION`); image upload / send claim / send result / `touchContent` are bulk JPQL and do NOT bump the version. `@DynamicUpdate` + `updatable=false` on every send column (and on the channel's template columns): an entity save never rewinds a send result or a template written meanwhile. "Changed after sending" = `content_rev > sent_rev` — every content write (values, image, template refresh) bumps `content_rev` ONLY through `touchContent` (atomic `+1`) and the sender stores the revision of the row it rendered (`sent_rev`); a timestamp comparison missed a save whose "now" was taken before the claim but committed after the PDF read. Rows sent before the counter (`sent_rev` null) fall back to `content_changed_at > last_sent_at`. Applying the latest template carries a value only for the SAME id AND type (`carry`). Template ids from the UI are `prefix-<time><random>` — never a deleted field's id (a reused id attached old values / images / last-month hints to a new field). Placeholders are capped like the server: titles / labels 300, text-section bodies 12 000 (`resolveTextPlaceholders` — the title cap once cut a default body in the editor and the next keystroke saved the cut text).
  - **Images (`TechErrorImageValidator`):** type from MAGIC BYTES (PNG / JPEG only), ≤ 5 MB, ≤ 6000 px side and ≤ 12 MP read from the header WITHOUT decoding (`MemoryCacheImageInputStream` — no temp file on the read-only root FS); ≤ 40 MB per report. PDF generation ≤ 2 per pod (`renderPdf(Supplier)` takes the permit BEFORE the images are loaded into memory, 15 s → 429 `PdfBusyException`; a render failure is 500, not 409). An image PDFBox can't draw becomes a "couldn't be drawn" box sized for its row (`PdfCanvas.tryImage`), never a silent gap; an over-long text ends with a "shortened" note, never a silent cut.
  - **Sending (`TechErrorReportSender`):** recipients = the team's `email` (comma / semicolon, deliverable only); none → 409 `TEAM_EMAIL_MISSING`, nothing written. Claim (`claimSend`, at most once; a SENDING claim older than 10 min can be retaken — manual path only) → the report is RE-READ after the claim (the PDF is that row; its `content_rev` becomes `sent_rev`) → PDF (> `site.monitor.tech-error-report.max-mail-mb`, 15 → 409 `PDF_TOO_LARGE`) → `sendHtml` with the PDF → result in `finally`, only for this claim: SENT (`finishSent`, `last_sent_at` = claim time, `last_sent_to` without passive-only addresses) / QUEUED (`finishQueued` — the mail service retries, delivery is NOT known yet: the `notification_logs` row is written FIRST and its id + the would-be `last_sent_*` / `sent_rev` go to `send_log_id` / `queued_*`; the mail service's retry write-back turns that row SENT / FAILED and every read path (`overview`, `listReports`, `getReport`) calls `reconcileQueued`, which conditionally finishes the report — SENT from the pending fields, FAILED without touching the last send; status `queued` until then, and a member cannot delete a queued report) / SKIPPED / FAILED; PDF queue full → `releaseClaim` (state derived in SQL from the row's own last result, never from the caller's stale read) + a SKIPPED trace. ONE `notification_logs` row per decision (trigger `TECH_ERROR_REPORT`, alertEventId 0). Subject = the resolved report title (no `[Site Monitor]` prefix — the team forwards it). Controller: SKIPPED → 409 `MAIL_DISABLED`, FAILED → 502 `MAIL_FAILED` (SMTP text never returned). No scheduled send, no system-maintenance mute (manual, like other reports). **Periodic reconciler (multi-pod R2a-D1, MP-41):** `MailRetryOrphanSweeper` (5 min, one pod, `mail-retry-orphans` lock) calls `TechErrorReportService.reconcileAllQueued()` after writing the log orphans — the queue result, SEND_RESULT event and result pushes are written even when nobody opens the channel (≤ 200 channels per round, `queuedChannelIds`).
  - **Access:** permissions `tech_error_reports.read` (view) / `.crud` (edit) — TEAM_ADMIN + USER both, AUDIT read. Scope is a TEAM document (no 7/24 widening): read = global viewer or view / manage / member scope **or a team access grant (VIEW+, release 9, 2026-10-17, user request: "raporlar paylaşıma açılabilsin")**; report CONTENT write (`canWrite`: values, images, open a report (`contentWritableChannel` — `writableChannel` is the channel's OWN scope; review 2026-10-17), apply the latest template) = global admin, `canManage`, MEMBER **or grant EDIT+** (`SessionScope.grantedEdit`); everything that is the team's own business stays own-scope (`canWriteOwn`: create / edit / delete a channel, save the template, executive recipients, approval request, send (`ownWritableReport`), delete a report) — a grantee can edit but never send; channel delete and deleting a SENT report = `canManage`. Rows: channel `can_edit` (channel / template, own), `can_report` (content, grants too), `shared_access` (`viewableOnlyByGrant`); report `can_edit` (content), `can_send` (own), `can_delete` (own), `shared_access`; `approval_detail.can_request|can_send_executive` own. **People never shown through a grant (review 2026-10-17):** a grant-only reader gets `executive_emails: null` (count kept), `last_sent_to: null` + `last_sent_count` (`maskSharedPeople` on every status row), nameless approver rows + `approver_count`; document authorship (`updated_by_name`, history actors, requester / decider names) stays. `TechErrorVisibilityService.sessionTeamIds` counts granted teams (menu and 403 gate agree with `visibleTeamIds`); the Settings overview uses `ownVisibleTeamIds` (admin surfaces never widen through a grant). Rolling deploy: a grantee saving on an OLD pod gets 403 → autosave `autoBlocked`, the draft stays. UI: `canReport(ch)` (falls back to `can_edit` on an old server) drives "raporunu hazırla" / "Yeni rapor"; `ReportEditor` hides send and the team-e-mail banner when `can_send:false`; a "Paylaşımla erişim" badge (`te-shared`) on shared rows / the channel header; the template opens read-only. Module visibility (S6) applies to granted teams too. Gates: `TechErrorReportControllerTest.teamAccessGrant_*`, `TechErrorSharedAccess.test.jsx`. Deletes are decided IN the database (`deleteIfAllowed`: a member only deletes a never-sent draft, nobody deletes during a fresh send claim → 409 `REPORT_SENT_OR_SENDING`; a channel with a report being sent is not deleted either). Concurrent "open report" for the same month → 409 `REPORT_EXISTS` with the winner's `report_id` (re-read after the rolled-back transaction — reading inside it failed on PostgreSQL). Another team's channel / report / image → 404. Rows carry `can_edit` / `can_delete`; the tab is shown to everyone (server answers 403 → "no access" screen).
  - **UI:** channels (team filter, period KPIs, cards with "open/prepare <month> report", "Open template"), channel view (Tabs reports | template — `te_tab=template`), report editor (last month's value + change hint under each figure, topic → auto title, 2-column chart grid like the PDF, drag & drop, local draft backup `sm.te.draft.<id>`, conflict banner keeps the draft as a backup, PDF / send / apply-template / delete; secondary actions in a labelled menu). Editor rules (review 2026-10-13): edits typed during a save are kept (the response replaces only the draft that was sent); a reload while a dirty draft exists never advances the base version (conflict banner instead — a later save must 409, not overwrite); server field errors clear when the field is edited and never block Save, client errors are keyed by issue id + field; PDF / send / apply-template stop on invalid fields; apply-template re-reads the preview AFTER saving; a language switch does not reload; percent inputs are shown without grouping (`%1000`, a single separator is a decimal in a percent field); an unfinished number (`-`, `1.250.0`) shows its error on blur. Drafts (`sm.te.draft.<id>`, `sm.te.tpl.<channel>`) are personal storage (`utils/personalStorage` — cleared at logout / user change), the last second is flushed on leave, and a draft that turns clean drops the backup it wrote. URL `te_view|te_ch|te_tab|te_rep|te_team` (prefix `te_` in `PAGE_STATE_PREFIXES`); an unknown / invisible `te_team` clears itself. List titles come from the report's own copied title (`title_pattern` / `template_lang`), not the channel's current template. Copy icons (2026-10-16, user request: copy section titles, field and image names while writing a report): `TechErrorParts.CopyTextButton` sits right AFTER the report title, every section title, metric and issue field labels, image slot names, the trend section title and each chart caption — a SIBLING of the inline h2 / h3 / label / caption span inside a `data-slot="te-copy-row"` wrapper (never inside: the heading / label / figure names stay clean; the figure uses `aria-labelledby`), copies the RESOLVED text, full `muted-foreground` (no opacity — 3:1 in the light theme), 40 px on touch without negative margin; table headers have none (the table width budget). The e2e `clipped()` helper measures `te-copy-row` wrappers; report `getByLabel` lookups use `exact: true` (the button name "Kopyala: <label>" contains the label). Gate `TechErrorCopyLabels.test.jsx`. Audit `TECH_ERROR_CHANNEL_CREATE|UPDATE|DELETE`, `TECH_ERROR_TEMPLATE_SAVE`, `TECH_ERROR_REPORT_CREATE|UPDATE|DELETE|SEND` (REPORT category). Retention: orphan rules only (team → channel → report → image); reports are never aged out. Analysis doc for the team: Claude Docs "Kanal Teknik Hata Raporu — Analiz".
  - **Executive structure (2026-10-14, user request: "raporun başında teknik hata oranı, map yapılmamış ve kota adetleri, SLA taahhüdü; konular free format, madde / alt madde / kalın; SLA erişilebilirlik tablosu; executive kişiye gidecek"):**
    - **`summary` section** (`lines`, ≤ 20 × 500 chars): sentence patterns with `{f:<field>}` (formatted value; number / percent printed **bold**) and `{f:<yes/no field>?yes text|no text}`. `TechErrorSummary` (JS mirror `techErrorModel.summaryAutoText`) DROPS a sentence whose value is empty, exempt (`skip_if_no` answered No), unknown or type-mismatched — a half sentence never reaches management. Plain sentences flow into one paragraph; bullet lines stay separate. A report may override the text (`values.texts[<summary id>]`); a BLANK override means automatic (not stored, `effective` ignores a stored blank — review 2026-10-14: "edit by hand" on an empty summary locked it blank). A `textarea` field reference drops its sentence (multi-line note; 100 refs × 10 000 chars), the item / paragraph decision is made on the PATTERN (a value or channel name starting "1. " does not break the flow) and a paragraph-start sentence that would parse as an item gets U+00A0 after the marker (`keepParagraph`; the PDF fallback font maps it to a space); an item pattern resolving to an empty body is dropped; a reference body containing `{` is not a reference (strict save rejects it as malformed; yes/no clauses are never cut), a text value's run of 2+ `*` becomes one `*` (`neutral`), and generation stops past 12 000 chars. Strict template validation rejects only MALFORMED references (`TechErrorSummary.malformed`); a sentence tied to a removed field is accepted (removing a field must not lock the template) and the editor warns under it (`summaryLineIssues`) and confirms field removal (`summaryLinesUsing`). Default template (TR + EN) starts with an executive summary. Its SLA availability table is NOT typed in (user decision 2026-10-15): section `sla-shots` is an `images` section with two FULL-width slots for screenshots of the reporting tool's table (one or two, e.g. one per sub-channel); the charts sit in TWO `images` sections under "Grafikler hakkında" (user decision 2026-10-16, replacing the 2026-10-15 four-section flow of 22 slots): `charts` "12 Aylık Özetler - Yıllık Göstergeler" (8 slots, all "… (Son 12 Ay)": annual summary half, total operations half, technical/business rate trend full, quota & unmapped full, technical errors ÷ all operations full, department / directorate summary table full, technical and business errors by channel half + half) and `charts-daily` "{ay} {yil} Özetler - Günlük Göstergeler" (7 slots, all starting "{ay} {yil} ": month summary, daily total operations, technical / business by directorate (halves), daily rate trend, daily quota & unmapped, technical ratio (full)) — names, order and widths as the user gave them (`TechErrorTemplate.defaultCharts`, `TechErrorTemplateTest`). A slot keeps its old id where the meaning stayed (`annual-summary`, `monthly-tx`, `annual-tech-rate`, `quota-unmapped-monthly`, `annual-table`, `daily-tx`, `month-tech-counts`, `month-business-counts`, `tech-ratio` — `carry` moves the image); new meaning → new id (`tech-ratio-monthly`, `channel-tech-errors`, `channel-business-errors`, `month-summary`, `daily-rate-trend`, `daily-quota-unmapped`). Image wording in the editor / history / versions / server messages is "görsel" / "image" (SLA screenshots are not charts). The report total + upload hint show only under the FIRST images section; the PDF keeps an images section's title with its first image row (`rowHeight` before `sectionTitle`, `imageSectionTitleNeverOrphaned`); the version diff reports a field / slot that moved between sections as ONE `move` row plus its own rename / retype / link / resize rows (labelled with the destination section; a retype drops the value in `carry`), and as a plain `add` when its source section was removed or retyped (ids are template-wide, `carry` keeps values / images of the same id + type). The `table` kind stays for custom templates; existing channels keep their copied template (a team takes the new default with "reset to default"). An empty image section prints "Bu bölüme görsel yüklenmedi.".
    - **`table` section** (≤ 10 columns `number|percent|text`, ≤ 40 rows, `group` row = bold + shaded): values `values.tables[sid][row][col]` (key written only when non-empty — table-less reports keep the old JSON), error key `tables.<sid>.<row>.<col>`, cells ≤ 200 chars. UI: real table when the container ≥ `tableMinWidth` + 2 px border (176-px row header `w-44` + 112 per number / percent column + 160 per text column, ≥ 640 px — the 6-column default fits a 1280 px laptop with the sidebar open; compact 13 px cell inputs; back to the table only 24 px above it — no flicker), read-only viewers see cell values as text (not dimmed inputs), headers carry the TEMPLATE language, cards below (2 columns, text column full row); a layout switch clears the table's client errors (a half-typed value is gone with the remounted input) and refocuses the focused cell. Rows are indented only AFTER a group row (all three renderers). PDF: header row repeated on every page, numbers right-aligned and NEVER split mid-digits (`PdfCanvas.numberCell`: shrink to ≥ 5.5 pt, then break only after the language's THOUSANDS separator — never inside the decimals), text columns get twice a number column's width, a cut header / row label / text cell adds a "shortened" note under the table, the title never stays alone at a page bottom; a table with no values is not printed.
    - **Rich text** (`TechErrorRichText`, JS `parseRichText` — same truth table both sides): `- ` / `* ` / `• ` bullet, 2 spaces per level (≤ 3), `1. ` / `1) ` numbered, `**bold**` (EXACTLY two asterisks paired by the CommonMark flanking rule — no space after the opener / before the closer, and an opener followed by punctuation needs whitespace / punctuation / line start before it, the closer mirrored; any other run such as a masked card number `4543 **** 1234` or mail `ab**@x.com` stays literal; review 2026-10-14: the old rule deleted those asterisks in text saved by the previous release), blank line = paragraph gap; no HTML. Whitespace is the same set on both sides (`TechErrorRichText.ws` = `isRichSpace`: Java `isWhitespace` + NBSP U+00A0 / U+2007 / U+202F + U+FEFF), list numbers are ASCII digits only, a leading tab is two spaces (one sub-level) and an inner tab one space — in `multiline` (storage) AND in both parsers (`tabs` / `richTabs`) — and the first line's indentation is kept. The "Bold" button keeps edge whitespace outside the markers and wraps each line of a multi-line selection. Applies to textarea fields, issue descriptions, text sections and the summary — UI `RichText` / `RichTextarea` (toolbar bold / bullet / indent / outdent, Ctrl+B, preview), PDF `PdfCanvas.wrapSpans`, mail `TechErrorReportMail.richHtml` (escaped; the mail opens with the summary).
    - Template editor: section cards are keyed by a generation counter bumped on every draft replacement (load, discard, save response, reset, backup restore) — sentence ids, carets and the yes/no choice panel never carry over; sentences are single-line (Enter blocked, pasted newlines → space).
    - **Rollback note:** a pre-20.134 pod does not know `summary` / `table`; a report saved through such a pod (rolling deploy window, rollback) loses its table cells and summary override. Avoid editing channel reports during the rollout; never roll back past 20.134 without telling the teams.
    - `carry()` (template refresh) keeps summary overrides and table cells whose row + column ids survive with the SAME column type. Gates: `TechErrorExecutiveTest`, `techErrorExecutiveModel.test.js` (reads the Java limits / kinds), `TechErrorExecutive.test.jsx`, `TechErrorTableLayout.test.jsx` (layout switch), PDF / mail cases in `TechErrorReportPdfWriterTest` (number cell, wide table note, title never orphaned), `TechErrorExecutiveTest.BOLD_TABLE` (read by the JS test — one truth table), `e2e/channel-reports.spec.js` (summary, table ↔ cards, toolbar at 390/768/1280).
  - **Change history + template versions (2026-10-14, user request: "kim, ne zaman, hangi alanı neyden neye değiştirdi; şablonun sürümleri"):**
    - **Report events:** `tech_error_report_events` (`TechErrorReportEvent`; kind CREATE | VALUES | IMAGE_ADD | IMAGE_REPLACE | IMAGE_DELETE | TEMPLATE_REFRESH | SEND | SEND_RESULT; `actor_id` `@JsonIgnore` — the API gives only `actor_name`, the name AT THE TIME, and `system` for a null actor; `detail_json`). Written by `TechErrorHistoryService` INSIDE the transaction of the change (a rolled-back save leaves no event). SEND is written by `TechErrorReportSender` in its `finally` for EVERY attempt after the claim — PDF busy / too large / unexpected errors included — with a `reason` code (`sendReason`: MAIL_OFF, INACTIVE_RECIPIENTS, PDF_BUSY, PDF_TOO_LARGE, NO_RECIPIENT); it runs in its own transaction and swallows its failure, so it never changes a send result. SEND_RESULT is written by `reconcileQueued` in ONE `REQUIRES_NEW` transaction with the conditional finish (an event write failure rolls the finish back, the next read retries; one row across pods); its time is when the result was READ (the UI says so). A send that claims over a QUEUED row writes SEND_RESULT `SUPERSEDED` (the first send's outcome is never read) — decided in the `finally` from the POST-claim read (`last_send_status` still `QUEUED_RETRY…`) and never when the claim was released (PDF busy: the queued send lives on and its own result follows). **A queue is never sent over while its result can still land (user decision 2026-10-22, multi-pod R2a-D1, MP-41 — reverses the 2026-10-14 "resend over a queued send" rule):** while the report is `QUEUED` and its claim is younger than `QUEUED_RESULT_WINDOW` (20 min) a send is refused with 409 `SEND_QUEUED` ("the previous send is waiting in the mail server's retry queue; check its result in a few minutes") — decided both on the pre-claim read and inside the claim UPDATE (`TechErrorReportRepository.claimSendUnlessQueued`), so two tabs / pods cannot race past it; nothing is claimed, sent, logged or audited as a send (the controller skips the `TECH_ERROR_REPORT_SEND` audit for this code). The old queue would otherwise deliver on retry and the team / executives got two copies with different content. After the window the old behaviour stays (finish + SUPERSEDED). The approval auto-send gets the same code (`approvalOutcome` / push `approvedParts("SEND_QUEUED")`: "earlier send still queued"; the approval card says it in its own sentence). Client (review REP-2): `sendModel.sendFailureKind` → `'queued'`: the send dialog stays open with an info band (`te.send.queuedTitle` + `te.send.queuedWait` "the first send is waiting for the mail server's result — the result will appear here"; never "press Send again"), the typed extra CC is kept and "Gönder" / Ctrl+Enter are LOCKED; the editor polls the EXISTING queue (`pollBaselineForQueuedRefusal`: the row read right after the refusal if queued, else the pre-click row — `own: true`, the queue's own result) and unlocks only on a re-read result: delivered → dialog closes + `te.send.pollSent` (no second send), failed → the "not sent — you can send again" band with the CC, superseded → closes, 10 min without a result → a `retry` band `te.send.queuedTimeout` (the server still refuses while the queue lives). Closing and reopening the dialog while waiting shows the same locked band + CC. Gates: `TechErrorSendDialog.test.jsx` ("409 SEND_QUEUED" describe), `techErrorSendModel.test.js`, `e2e/channel-reports.spec.js` ("SEND_QUEUED", 390/768/1280). Rolling deploy: an old pod still allows a resend over a live queue (no 409) and does not reconcile periodically. `send()` first runs `reconcileQueued` for the channel (a delivered queue gets its real SEND_RESULT, not "unknown"); an attempt over a queued send that delivered and queued NOTHING (never reached the mail service — PDF too large, unexpected error — or the service RETURNED SKIPPED / FAILED) RELEASES the claim instead of `finishNotSent`, so the queued send keeps being tracked (the service's retry writes back to the OLD log row) — but only while its result can still land (`QUEUED_RESULT_WINDOW` 20 min since the queued claim; the 421 retries live ~10.5 min in ONE pod's memory and die with it), otherwise a report would stay "queued" forever; an older queue, or an exception thrown by the mail service itself (delivery unknown), finishes it and writes SUPERSEDED. "Was queued" = the POST-claim read (`QUEUED_RETRY…` last status), the pre-claim state only when that read failed (a stale SENDING takeover over a queue); `releaseClaim(id, claimedAt, previous)` restores the previous `send_claimed_at` (a later `confirmQueued` stamps the real send time). `recordSend` never self-invokes the `@Transactional` `record` (`TransactionalSelfInvocationGuardTest`) — both use the private `insert`. A values save that changes nothing writes no event. VALUES detail = `TechErrorChangeDiff` per field (section, resolved label, type, op set / clear / change, topics add / remove, summary manual / auto; before / after formatted as the PDF shows them, long text as a plain 300-char preview plus its full length; numeric-aware `same`; ≤ 200 changes per event, the rest counted in `more`); a diff failure still saves the values with `unavailable: true`. Issue ORDER is a change (`reorder` row); a text section's unstored default is compared RESOLVED (placeholders); `*_length` only when the preview was cut (plain length, markup not counted); previews never split a surrogate pair. ≤ 500 events per report (`MAX_EVENTS_PER_REPORT`, pruned once 50 over), detail ≤ `MAX_DETAIL_CHARS` 64 000 (the rest only counted in `more`), pages ≤ 50; recipients ≤ 20 per event.
    - **Template versions:** `tech_error_template_versions` (UNIQUE channel × version; full template JSON + counts + `saved_by_name`; ≤ 100 per channel). Channel create records v1; `saveTemplate` records the new version AFTER its conditional UPDATE won (a losing writer gets 409 and writes nothing) and, for a channel saved before this feature, the replaced version as a baseline. A channel never saved since has no rows — the list returns the current version from the channel itself with `recorded: false`.
    - **API (read gate = the report / channel read scope, another team → 404):** `GET /reports/{id}/history?before=<event id>&limit=` (newest first, keyset, `has_more`, `recorded_since`, `capped` on the last page when the cap was reached — with no CREATE event the UI then says "only the newest N are kept; older ones and changes before this feature aren't shown" (the versions dialog says "older ones aren't listed" only when the list is full AND version 1 is not in it) — `max_events`, `report` created / updated names), `GET /channels/{id}/template/versions`, `GET /channels/{id}/template/versions/{v}` (stored templates read with `fromStored`). `GET /reports/{id}` carries `neighbors {prev, next}` = the nearest earlier / later report of the SAME channel (gaps allowed). Deletes of a report / channel delete their history in the same transaction; retention orphan rules `tech-error-report-events-orphan`, `tech-error-template-versions-orphan`.
    - **UI:** report editor nav row (`te-nav-prev|next`, through the unsaved-changes guard — also when a field holds invalid typed text; locked while an action runs; focus moves to the new report's title) + "Change history" Sheet (`ReportHistory.jsx`; before value struck through, load more (never while reloading; the busy flag resets on reopen / retry), baseline or "capped" note — never "no changes" from an unreadable answer; unknown kinds / states / reason codes never show raw keys; unknown removal counts are "unknown", never 0); Template tab "Version history" Dialog (`TemplateVersions.jsx`; diff vs the previous version via `templateDiff` — `null` (unknown) when a version can't be read, `reorder` rows for fields / slots / columns / rows; outline; "load into the editor" = a dirty draft on the CURRENT base version, so a concurrent save still 409s; `recorded:false` says "not recorded yet", a full list says "older ones were removed"; phone list → detail moves focus to the sticky back button and back to the item); both return focus to their opener on close (`returnFocusRef` → `onCloseAutoFocus`); day / year groups are headings, not `region` landmarks; channel page reports grouped by year (`groupByYear`).
    - **Gates:** `TechErrorChangeDiffTest`, `TechErrorReportServiceTest` (history cases, `valuesDetailIsBounded`, `queuedResultAndEventAreAtomic`), `TechErrorReportSenderTest` (`exceptionalAttemptsAreInHistory`, `resendOverQueuedRecordsSuperseded`, `sendReasonCodes`), `TechErrorReportControllerTest` (history / versions / neighbors), `TechErrorReportPostgresTest.historyAndTemplateVersions`, `techErrorHistoryModel.test.js`, `TechErrorHistory.test.jsx`, `e2e/channel-reports.spec.js` ("Faz B", 390/768/1280).
  - **Image preparation + automatic trend (2026-10-15, Faz C, user request: "görseli yuvaya göre küçült, net hata söyle; geçmiş aylardan otomatik trend — varsayılana ekle"):**
    - **Images are prepared in the BROWSER** (`components/techerror/imagePrep.js`, `prepareImage(file, deps)` — no slot argument since 2026-10-16): type from MAGIC BYTES (`sniffKind`: BMP needs a known DIB size, AVIF is found among the ftyp COMPATIBLE brands before HEIF, SVG after a UTF-8 BOM / XML declaration / comment), PNG / JPEG / WebP / GIF / BMP / AVIF decode (`createImageBitmap`, EXIF orientation applied), PDF / SVG / TIFF / HEIC / unknown → `ImagePrepError(key, params)` with a what-to-do message (`te.img.err*`; byte params are formatted, never raw). Pixel size is read from the HEADER BEFORE decoding (`headerSize`: PNG IHDR, GIF, BMP incl. the 16-bit OS/2 core header, WebP VP8 / VP8L / VP8X with their signature checked, JPEG SOF in the first 256 KB) — over `MAX_DECODE` (60 MP or a 30 000 px side) → `errHuge`, the tab never decodes a decompression bomb; a PNG / JPEG whose header size is within the server caps (upright, ≤ 5 MB) is still DECODED as a check — a half-downloaded / damaged file is caught here (`errDecode`), the server reads only the header — and is uploaded as is without decoding ONLY when the browser cannot decode at all (`ReferenceError` / `TypeError`). `decode` retries without the `imageOrientation` option on a TypeError (iOS 15, Chrome < 112). A file that can't be read (changed after picking, revoked content URI) → `errRead`. **Resolution is kept (2026-10-16, user request: "çözünürlüğe etki etmeyelim, rakamlar / yazılar okunur kalsın")** — the old slot-based downscale (PDF box × 3: a half slot became 760 px and blurred once the screen made images fill their slot) and the PNG → JPEG conversion of large screenshots are GONE. `targetSize(natW, natH)` = the SERVER caps only; a PNG / JPEG within them is uploaded as the same file. Resizing (caps exceeded, or bytes > 5 MB) uses `areaResample` (exact fractional area averaging, integer weights, separable, streamed in 64-row strips, premultiplied alpha — no aliasing, no Lanczos halo; each shrink step resamples the ORIGINAL bitmap, ≤ `MAX_SHRINK_STEPS` 5, every canvas released); PNG / GIF / BMP sources are NEVER JPEG (bytes over the cap → smaller dimensions), JPEG sources stay JPEG at `JPEG_QUALITY` 0.95, WebP / AVIF try JPEG 0.95 only when their PNG is over 5 MB. The PDF embeds at most `PdfCanvas.EMBED_SCALE` 6 × the drawn size (≈ 432 dpi): a larger image is reduced with the Java twin `ImageAreaAverage` (same vectors as the JS test; not AWT `getScaledInstance`), lossless for PNG, JPEG 0.95 for JPEG, read through `MemoryCacheImageInputStream` (read-only root FS); within the cap it is embedded as is. A GRAY source (gray PNG / JPEG, 8 / 16 bit, gray + alpha) is read from its RASTER samples (`ImageAreaAverage.Rows` / `toSrgb`) — `getRGB`, `drawImage` and PDFBox all treat gray as linear and turned 128 into 188 (faded grid lines / digits; review 2026-10-16); a gray PNG (IHDR colour type 0 / 4, `PdfCanvas.isGrayPng`) takes this path even when it fits, so it prints the tone the screen shows. `renderPdf` (download AND send — the same file) renders at `EMBED_SCALES` 6 → 4 → 3 → 2 until the PDF fits `max-mail-mb` (stored images are full resolution, so a report full of retina screenshots could pass 15 MB); `PDF_TOO_LARGE` now only means "doesn't fit even at 2×". `areaResample` is async: the browser yields on every 64-row strip through a `MessageChannel` task (`yieldToUi` — `setTimeout` is throttled to seconds / minutes in a hidden tab, and users switch tabs to take the next screenshot; `willReadFrequently` strip context), so a 48 MP photo never freezes the page; a failed scale releases the destination canvas too. Gates also: `ImageAreaAverageTest.grayPngKeepsLevels`, `TechErrorPdfSizeLadderTest`. Gates: `imagePrep.test.js` (areaResample vectors, caps-only target, never-JPEG, shrink steps), `ImageAreaAverageTest`, `TechErrorReportPdfWriterTest.imagesFillSlotWidth` (embedded sizes), `e2e/channel-reports.spec.js` (3000 × 1500 as is, 7200 × 1800 → 6000 × 1500 PNG on a real canvas — its PIXELS are decoded (`decodePng`): block colours exact across strip borders, the border pixel the exact area average), live spec step 2. Formerly: downscaled to the slot's PDF box × `PRINT_SCALE` 3 (`PDF_LAYOUT` mirrors `TechErrorReportPdfWriter`: slot width × `IMAGE_MAX_H`; `RESIZE_SLACK` 1.1 — a slightly larger image is kept) and to the SERVER caps (`SERVER_IMAGE` = `TechErrorImageValidator` 5 MB / 6000 px / 12 MP / min 16 px — `imagePrep.test.js` reads the Java constants); the short side never goes below 16 px (a 1920 × 40 strip was resized to 760 × 15 and refused) — and when that floor pushes the factor back within `RESIZE_SLACK` the image is kept, not resampled (2000 × 17) — and an image that can only meet the max caps below it is refused (`errThin`). Scaling happens ONCE (`deps.scale`, canvas step-down halving, intermediate canvases released) and is shared by every encode (`deps.encode`; non-PNG/JPEG → PNG, a JPEG stays JPEG, a PNG over 1.5 MB becomes JPEG only when that is ≥ 30 % smaller, white background), then `deps.release`; a null 2D context / null blob (browser out of canvas memory, iOS) → `errBrowser`, and an unexpected exception in the slot also says `errBrowser`, never "file damaged". A PNG / JPEG that already fits is uploaded AS IS (same File) — except a JPEG whose EXIF orientation ≠ 1 (`jpegOrientation`), which is re-encoded upright (the server and the PDF ignore EXIF; `info.rotated`). The report total (40 MB) is read at check time (`otherBytes()` through a ref — a slot finishing during the prepare phase counts) with the slot's old image subtracted. The slot shows "Hazırlanıyor…" then "Yükleniyor…"; the resize / convert / rotate note is under the image (bound to the uploaded image id — another user's replacement loaded later never shows it) AND in the success toast (8 s; the note appears with the image, so it is not a live region). The server validator is unchanged (it still decides). Inputs over 40 MB are refused before reading.
    - **Trend section kind `trend`** (`TechErrorTemplate`, `series` = field ids, ≤ `MAX_TREND_SERIES` 8; strict save: non-empty, id pattern, deduped; lenient read drops bad ids). References are NOT type-checked on save (same rule as the summary: removing / retyping a field must not lock the template) — the editor (`TrendEditor`) lists `missing` / `notNumeric` refs (plain warnings, not live regions) with a remove button and the report / PDF skip them. Only `number` / `percent` fields chart (`trendable`); a field EXEMPT in the current report (`skip_if_no` answered No) is not charted in the report screen or the PDF (the exempt rule). The default template has NO trend section since 2026-10-16 (user decision: "bu grafikleri görsel olarak ben sağlayacağım"); a team can still add one, and `TechErrorCalcMigration` removed the default `auto-trend` section from existing channels (a team's own trend section stays). Trend tests use `TechErrorTestTemplates.withTrend`. Removing a charted field (or a section holding one) asks first and `pruneRemovedTrendRefs` drops ONLY ids this change removed (a moved field keeps its id; an already-dangling ref is left for the editor) — but never the LAST id of a section (an empty series is refused on save, so the ref stays dangling and the editor warns).
    - **Earlier months are read LIVE:** the trend is not snapshotted at send time — correcting an earlier month's figure changes the trend of every later report (screen, PDF download, re-send) while their "changed after sending" state (`content_rev > sent_rev`, this report only) stays as it was. This is deliberate (the corrected figure is the right one); the help says so.
    - **Rollback note:** a pre-20.136 pod does not know `trend`: a channel template saved or a report OPENED through such a pod during the rollout loses the trend section (lenient read drops it, the copied template is permanent); a rollback past 20.136 does the same. Avoid editing channel templates / opening reports during the rollout.
    - **Data (`TechErrorTrend.build`):** 12 months ending with the report's month (`MONTHS` 12, `MIN_POINTS` 2), values = what was ENTERED in the channel's reports of those months (never calculated) — only for the SAME field id AND type in that report's OWN copied template; an exempt field (`skip_if_no` = No) or a missing month is null (never 0). `TechErrorReportService.trend` runs no query when the template has no trend refs and THROWS on a read failure; `GET /reports/{id}` adds `trend` {months, values, min_points} only when refs exist and `{unavailable: true}` when the read failed (UI "couldn't be read" — never "need two months"). The PDF reads the same data (`TechErrorPdfInput.trend`) and a read failure fails the PDF like other base loads.
    - **Rendering:** web `ReportTrend.jsx` (recharts; the report month's value comes from the editor DRAFT so a typed figure shows at once, blank → empty; the current bar is primary, others muted; grid columns by CONTAINER width `@2xl` — the viewport breakpoint squeezed the bars on a 768 px tablet with the sidebar; bar labels `trendLabelMode` (all, or only the current month when they would collide), month ticks `trendTicks` (skip step so names never touch, the current month always shown, the year under the first shown month and where it changes) — both decide on the REAL text width (canvas `measureText` with the page font, re-measured after `document.fonts.ready`) plus a 4 px gap; the per-character estimate is only the fallback without canvas and is deliberately wide (6 / 7 px — a Windows-tuned 5 px let labels touch on CI Linux); an sr-only month list carries every value). PDF `trendCell`: caption "<label> (Son 12 Ay)" — the time base never drops (a label too long for two lines is shortened with "…" and the suffix gets its own line; the row's caption height is the taller cell's), compact labels `TechErrorFormat.compact` (Qlik k / M / G / T in both languages since 2026-10-18 — before TR B / Mn / Mr, EN K / M / B; HALF_UP, 1 decimal under 100, a value that rounds to 1000 moves up a unit — "1Mn", never "1.000B"; percent ≤ 2 decimals, below 0.1 enough for two significant digits up to 6 (the storage precision) — %0,042, %0,00004, never a fake "%0"; JS `compactNumber` = same truth table, `techErrorTrendModel.test.js` reads `TechErrorTrendTest`), a label that does not fit even at 4.2 pt (7 integer steps of 0.2 from 5.6 — a float loop took an extra step to 4.0) is not drawn (never over its neighbour), negative values reserve label room under the deepest bar (above the month names), note rows when < 2 points, title kept with the first row. The report screen reads the channel's report rows ONCE for the neighbours and the trend (`trend(r, t, v, rows)`).
    - **Typing performance (2026-10-15 live measurement, 4× CPU throttle, 10 keystrokes):** the editor re-renders on every keystroke, and the 5 recharts charts made it 3.3 s of long tasks (single task 740 ms) vs 0.7 s without trend. Now each `TrendChart` is `memo` with a by-value point comparison (`samePoints`), the current-month value reaches the charts only after typing settles (`useSettled`, `TREND_DRAFT_DELAY_MS` 250 — `useDeferredValue` still redrew the typed field's chart on every key), and `ImagesBlock` is `memo` with a STABLE `onImagesChange` (`useCallback` in `ReportEditor`) so the 22 slots never re-render while typing → ≈ 0.7 s / 0.3 s. Never pass an inline callback to `ImagesBlock` / `TrendChart`. Live gate: `e2e/live/channel-reports.live.spec.js` (real backend: trend contract, real-canvas upload through the real validator, PDF timing, template series, history / prev-month navigation, SLA slots, ≤ 2 long tasks while typing; opt-in `E2E_LIVE=1`).
    - **Gates:** `TechErrorTrendTest`, `TechErrorTemplateTest` (trend kind), `TechErrorReportPdfWriterTest.trendCharts|trendReviewRules|trendNegativeLabelClearsMonthNames` (text positions), `TechErrorReportControllerTest.readReportTrend`, `TechErrorReportServiceTest.trendReadsChannelReports`, `imagePrep.test.js`, `techErrorTrendModel.test.js`, `TechErrorTrend.test.jsx`, `TechErrorImagePrep.test.jsx`, `e2e/channel-reports.spec.js` ("Faz C", 390/768/1280: labels / ticks never collide, real-canvas 3000 × 1500 → slot size upload, PDF refused).
  - **Automatic rates (2026-10-16, user request: "kullanıcıdan adetleri al, yüzdeleri sen hesapla; üstte adetler, altta oranlar; kota / map yapılmamış hata adedi girişinde yüzde de görünsün"):**
    - **Model:** a metrics field may carry `calc` (`TechErrorTemplate.Calc`): `ratio` on a percent field = (`num` − optional `minus`) ÷ `den` × 100; `change` on a percent field = |this month − last month| ÷ last month × 100 of field `of`, unsigned; `increase` on a yes/no field = this month's `of` > last month's. Refs must be number fields WITHOUT a calc (no chains). Strict save checks only the SHAPE (kind, field type, required refs, id pattern, no self-ref); a ref that is missing / not a number / computed is NOT rejected (removing a field must not lock the template — same rule as summary / trend): the field then becomes manual and the editor warns (`calcRefIssues`). Lenient read drops an invalid calc (field kept).
    - **Result (`TechErrorCalc`, JS mirror `techErrorModel.calcResult` — one truth table `TechErrorCalcTest.TABLE`, the JS test reads the Java source):** `OK` (value), `MISSING` / `ZERO` / `NEGATIVE` / `EQUAL` / `RANGE` (formula usable, no result → value REMOVED, never a stale or typed value), `MANUAL` (no report for last month or no value there — change / increase only) / `BAD_REF` → the field is entered by hand and the typed value is kept. Exempt inputs (`skip_if_no` answered No) count as missing; an exempt calc field is not computed.
    - **Where:** the SERVER computes — `saveValues` (client-sent rates ignored), template refresh (`refreshTemplate` / `refreshPreview` after `carry`) and `pdfInput` (PDF + mail, LIVE: a corrected last-month figure changes this month's change rate at once; the stored value refreshes on the next save). "Last month" = `previousReport` (exact previous calendar month), same id AND type (`TechErrorReportService.sameTypeFields`, also used by the `previous` DTO). Rounded to 2 decimals HALF_UP on the decimal form (`TechErrorCalc.round2`, user decision 2026-10-16; typed percents keep their own precision). The editor computes the same LIVE for display only (`calcResults` / `applyCalc` in `ReportEditor` → `<output>` labelled by the field label + "Otomatik" badge + formula / reason note, summary preview, trend's current month); the draft never holds computed values (no false "unsaved changes"), the save payload is the draft. Under a count input every ratio that uses it as the NUMERATOR shows its live value (`derivedRates`, `te-calc-derived`). Values are formatted in the UI language (`formatPercent`, ≤ 4 decimals).
    - **Default template:** section `summary` "{kanal} Kanalı — {ay} {yil} Verileri" (entered — since 2026-10-18 not printed in the PDF / mail when every value is in the executive summary, see "No repeated figures": total-tx, error-count, unmapped-count, quota-count, had-outage, outage-errors, sla-rate (manual percent), sla-above) then `rates` "… Oranları (otomatik)": error-rate = error-count ÷ total-tx, error-rate-excl = (error-count − outage-errors) ÷ total-tx, unmapped-rate / quota-rate = count ÷ error-count (user choice: share of TECHNICAL errors), outage-share = outage-errors ÷ error-count, tx-increase / tx-increase-pct vs last month's total-tx. Ids unchanged (summary, history, last-month hints keep working); the `unmapped` / `quota` sections keep only their notes.
    - **Existing channels — `TechErrorCalcMigration` (one-time, marker `2026-10-16-tech-error-calc-and-charts` (renamed before release to cover the chart sections too; after release NEVER rename it), lock `tech-error-calc-migration`, 2 min after boot then hourly, ≤ 24 attempts per pod, marker re-read inside the lock and written only after ALL channels):** default-derived fields get their calc only when the field exists with the expected type, has no calc and all refs are inputs; unmapped-rate / quota-rate are added to error-rate's section when absent and their inputs exist (a full section gets none — decided BEFORE the summary is processed); in changed sections entered fields go first, computed last; ONLY old-default hints / labels / summary sentences are replaced; the default `auto-trend` section is removed; chart sections that are EXACTLY an old default (the first release's single 14-slot "Grafikler" or the 2026-10-15 four sections — id, kind, title and slots compared; `OLD_CHARTS`, copied verbatim from git history, TR + EN) are replaced by the two new sections at the position of the first one — a changed section, an id collision or the 20-section / 40-slot limit leaves the channel's charts alone, the SLA screenshot section always stays. Each channel goes through `saveTemplate(…, null, "SiteMonitor")` (conditional update, version +1, history name "SiteMonitor", ≤ 3 retries on a concurrent team save). Opened / sent reports keep their copied template (a draft takes it with "apply latest template").
    - **Template editor:** "Otomatik hesap" per percent / yes-no field (`CalcEditor`: kind, part / subtracted / base or compared count pickers from the template's number inputs), shape errors under the field (`calcShapeError` → `te.err.calc*`), ref warnings, a type change drops an incompatible calc, removing a field used in a formula asks first (`calcFieldsUsing`); the version diff shows `field.calc` rows.
    - **Review rules (same day):** a computed yes/no field is never a `skip_if_no` target (server `resolveSkipRefs` → 400 / dropped, `sanitizeSkipRefs`, the "Hayır ise sorma" picker; the migration does not convert a yes/no that is already a target) — exemption is decided from the stored answer before the calculation. "Apply latest template": `lost_values` counts only what `carry` drops (newly computed rates must not hide a loss) and `recalculated` (typed rate values replaced or emptied by the calculation) is added to the confirm (`te.applyTemplateRecalc`). The client rounds the same way (`roundCalc`: 2 decimals HALF_UP on the decimal form; truth-table rows `2.675/100 → 2.68`, `8529/1234567 → 0.69`) — double rounding showed %0,6908 on screen and %0,6909 in the PDF. A ratio whose formula can't be used (MANUAL / BAD_REF) is not shown under its count input. The migration adds as many new rates as fit and logs a template that would exceed 64 KB.
    - **Template editor UI (same day, user requests):** under every image-slot name the name with `{kanal}` / `{ay}` / `{yil}` filled in (`te-tpl-slot-preview`, same "Örnek:" as the title); field / slot / summary-line / column / row items are distinct cards (`TPL_ITEM`: stronger border + shadow, a header strip with the number and the move / delete buttons, 12 px between items).
    - **Rounding (same day, user decisions):** a computed rate is rounded to 2 decimals HALF_UP; a non-zero rate below 0.01 keeps 2 SIGNIFICANT digits (≤ 6 decimals, the storage precision: 0.0028, 0.0041 — never a fake %0 while errors exist; below 0.0000005 it is 0). `TechErrorCalc.roundRate` rounds the digits of `BigDecimal.valueOf` (shortest repr); JS `roundCalc` / `roundHalfUp` round the SAME digit string (`decimalParts`) — never `Math.round(x * 100)`: 59 / 4000 × 100 = 1.4749999999999999 gave 1.48 on screen and 1.47 in the PDF (review 2026-10-16; TABLE rows). Percent DISPLAY follows the same small-value rule on both sides (`TechErrorFormat.percent` / `formatPercent`: ≤ 4 decimals, below 0.01 two significant digits ≤ 6 — typed values too). Typed percents keep their own precision.
    - **Notes and mail (same day):** when at least one RATIO was really computed in this report (`TechErrorCalc.anyRatioComputed`: not exempt, formula usable, result OK — change / increase may be typed in a first report) the PDF closing note and the mail note say the counts were entered and the rates calculated from them; otherwise the old "the system does not calculate" note. The mail's stat tiles = the first metrics section + the directly following ALL-computed sections (`MAX_STATS` 16) — moving the rates to their own section had dropped the technical error rate from the mail.
    - **Images fill their slot (same day, user request: "hepsi yüklendiği alanın boyutunu alsın"):** screen `img` = `block w-full h-auto mx-auto` with `width` / `height` attributes (space reserved, no jump on load) and `maxWidth` = `imagePrep.displayWidthPct` (the PDF rule: only an image taller than `IMAGE_MAX_H` narrows) — the old `max-h-80 object-contain` narrowed a tall image and left grey margins; PDF `fit()` = slot width, aspect kept (small images enlarged, large ones reduced), only an image taller than `IMAGE_MAX_H` 640 pt is reduced to it (section title + caption + image fit one page). Gates: `TechErrorReportPdfWriterTest.imagesFillSlotWidth|computedRatesNoteAndMailStats`, `e2e/channel-reports.spec.js` (image width = slot width).
    - **Abbreviated numbers + image zoom (2026-10-16, user decisions):** every number input (`ReportSections.NumberInput` — figures, issue counts, table number cells) takes Qlik / Turkish / English abbreviations (`techErrorModel.NUMBER_ABBREVIATIONS`: k / bin / thousand = 10³, M / Mn / mio / milyon / million = 10⁶, G / Mr / Mlr / milyar / bn / billion = 10⁹, T / Tn / trilyon / trillion = 10¹²; case- and Turkish-İ-insensitive). The mantissa is SHIFTED as a decimal string (`shiftDecimal` — 7,42 × 10⁹ is exactly 7.420.000.000), a single separator before a suffix is always the decimal ("1.250M" = 1,25 million); "B" alone is refused (`isAmbiguousB` → `te.err.abbrevB`; Turkish bin vs English billion), a half-typed suffix ("7,42mi") is incomplete, not an error; above `LIMITS.maxNumber` (= `TechErrorValues.MAX_NUMBER` 1e15) → `te.err.tooLarge`; percent fields take none (`te.err.notNumber`, number fields `te.err.notNumberAbbrev`). The typed text stays, the number it was read as is shown underneath (`te-number-expanded`, in `aria-describedby`); the number is saved, and since 2026-10-18 the abbreviation too (`values.units`, next bullet). Number inputs use `inputMode="text"` (letters needed). Clicking an uploaded image opens `ImageLightbox` (fit / 100 % / 200 %, the image itself toggles fit ↔ 100 %, scroll to pan, × or Esc, phone full screen, 40 px targets). Gates: `techErrorAbbrev.test.js`, `TechErrorAbbrevInput.test.jsx`, `TechErrorImageLightbox.test.jsx`, `e2e/channel-reports.spec.js` ("1,25M", lightbox fits at 390/768/1280).
    - **Abbreviated values stay abbreviated + zero tick (2026-10-18, user requests: "4.4G gibi bir giriş raporda da aynı şekilde gözüksün, çok uzun sayılar yazılmasın"; "0 adet gibi olursa 0 yanına yeşil yuvarlak tik"):**
      - **Storage:** additive `values.units` = key → exponent 3 / 6 / 9 / 12 for every number typed WITH a suffix (a number typed in full stays full, user decision). Keys: a metrics field id (NUMBER type, no calc — percent / yes-no / text never), `issues.<section>.<issue id>.customers|errors` (`TechErrorValues.issueUnitKey`), `tables.<section>.<row>.<col>` (number columns, `tableUnitKey`). `TechErrorValues.units` walks the TEMPLATE (cost bounded by the template, not the client map) and drops silently — strict save included, never 400 (the number is the truth): unknown key, exponent not exactly 3/6/9/12, text / boolean, no numeric value at that place, exempt field (dropped with its value), a value that needs more than 6 decimals after the shift (`TechErrorFormat.abbreviable`, `ABBREV_MAX_DECIMALS` 6 — "4,123456789G" would print a ROUNDED number and a later edit would save it; review 2026-10-18), a ZERO ("0G" / "0k": `abbreviable` is false for 0 on both sides — the report printed "0G" next to the green tick; a zero has no magnitude, it prints "0" everywhere, the client never sends its unit and "0" → "0k" on a stored 0 is no change; `ABBREV_TABLE` / `UNIT_SUMMARY_TABLE` / `UNIT_TILES_TABLE` rows, `TechErrorValuesTest.unitsDroppedForZero`). Stored only when non-empty (reports without it keep byte-identical JSON); lenient read applies the same filter; `Values.withFields` drops a field's unit when the field lost its value. `carry()` (apply latest template) keeps a unit only where the value is kept (same id + type). Calc math, CSV / Excel export (`TechErrorExportService`) and the cross-channel analysis keep FULL numbers (BI / comparison data) — deliberately not applied there.
