A folded `ref` is rejected with 400 on create / batch / renew. Never reintroduce one record per chain member.
  - **Pipeline:** `loadDomainsFromInventory` drops manual rows (network sweep, outage arithmetic and metrics unchanged). `SchedulerService.runManualCertificateStep` runs after the hourly / stale / full runs under its own `scheduler_lock` key `manual-cert-sweep`; `ManualCertificateEvaluationService` builds the SAME result map offline (`via=upload`, leaf-only ⇒ `chain_status=UNKNOWN`, trust only for a complete chain, revocation cached 6 h) → `CertificateService.saveResult` → `processResults`. Same thresholds / tiers / routing / re-alerts; HOSTNAME_MISMATCH is never raised for manual rows. Upload, renew, `/evaluate`, `/check/{domain}` and `/health/refresh` are manual triggers: recovery-only (`resolveVerifiedStaleCertAlerts`), never a new alarm.
  - **Skipped for manual rows:** uptime sweep/overview, derived Port/DNS provisioning + sweep gates, `DomainExpiryRefreshService`, weekly availability, `/check-preview` and `/admin/diagnostics/*` (409 `MANUAL_CERT`), network-only health rows (NA, `notApplicableManual`), deployment/auto-pin SAN gates, storm denominators (`countNetworkActive*`, `SQL_ACTIVE_BY_TEAM`). Purge paths delete versions.
  - **API:** `/api/manual-certs` (`analyze`, create, `batch` ≤ 20, `{id}/versions` renew with `OLDER_THAN_CURRENT` / `SAME_CERTIFICATE` (`allow_same=true` stores an identical certificate as v+1 — "Yine de yükle", never bypasses OLDER for a different cert), `DELETE {id}/versions/{vid}` permanent delete of a NON-current version (409 `CURRENT_VERSION`, numbers kept, audit `CERT_MANUAL_VERSION_DELETE`), list, detail, PEM download, `evaluate`); `GET /check-preview/{domain}` on a manual row = offline map of the current version (no row, no alarm)); write = `inventory.crud` + team scope (create reuses `AdminController.createInventoryRecord`); audit `CERT_MANUAL_UPLOAD` / `CERT_MANUAL_RENEW`. DTO/list rows carry `cert_source`, `manual_version`, `manual_uploaded_at` only for manual rows (omitted otherwise). The alarm mail adds a "Sertifika Kaynağı" line for manual rows only.
  - **UI:** `?tab=manualcerts` (`components/manualcert/*`: guidance card — COLLAPSED by default, also on an empty list (user decision 2026-10-07), open state remembered in `sm.mcert.guide` — list, `UploadWizard` file → review → track/renew → result), "Dosyadan sertifika ekle" in the Dashboard/Inventory "Domain Ekle" split button, `ManualCertBadge` on card/table/inventory/modal, CertificateModal manual mode (SSL tab = offline preview with the SAME verdict / cert+trust groups / `SslChainView` as network rows, no connection group or hostname row; no diagnose; no Grafik tab; "Yeniden değerlendir"; Versions tab with "Sil" on old versions and "Görüntüle"). **Browser-style hierarchy viewer (2026-10-08):** `GET /api/manual-certs/{id}/versions/{vid}/chain` (read gate as detail; offline from `chain_pem`, no writes) returns nodes ROOT FIRST (`ManualCertificateHierarchy`: role root|intermediate|leaf, `issuer_missing` when the top is not self-signed, per-node fields + single-cert `pem`); `certmodal/CertHierarchyView` (tree role, keyboard, details pane, per-node PEM download) behind the SSL tab's "Zincir | Hiyerarşi (tarayıcı gibi)" switch (chain view stays default; network rows since 2026-10-12, see "Network certificate hierarchy") and the Versions "Görüntüle" dialog; i18n `chier.*`, `InventoryFormModal` manual mode (network fields hidden; the tracking key is EDITABLE since 2026-10-07 — `trackingKeyError` client rule, rename confirm, server `manualKeyForUpdate` + rename of latest/checks/alerts/notes; versions are keyed by inventory id).
  - **Adding a warning code:** TR+EN `mcert.warn.<CODE>` (`ManualCertWarningI18nGateTest`). Gates: `CertificateFileParserTest`, `ManualCertificateSchedulerSplitTest`, `ManualCertificateEvaluationServiceTest`, `ManualCertificateControllerTest`, `CertificateServiceManualSourceTest`, `InventoryNetworkCountQueriesTest`, `e2e/manual-cert.spec.js`.
- **Network certificate hierarchy (2026-10-12, user request: "otomatik çekilen sertifikalarda da tarayıcı gibi hiyerarşi; Root / Intermediate / Leaf net, her birinin bitiş tarihi ayrı ayrı; mevcut işlevler bozulmasın").**
  - **Why live:** network rows store only chain METADATA (`latest_checks.chain_details`, wiped to `[]` on an error check); no PEM, no root. The hierarchy therefore reads the chain on demand.
  - **Endpoint:** `GET /api/certificates/{domain}/hierarchy` (`CertificateHierarchyController`). Gate = `inventory.list/view` + an INVENTORY row (ad-hoc host → 404) + the SAME read rule as the SSL tab preview (`canViewMonitoring` on SY or UG, 7/24 operator, org-wide read; else 403). A manual row → 409 `MANUAL_CERT` (it has its own view). 4 in flight (checked FIRST, so a busy 429 does not spend the quota), then 6 per minute per user × domain → 429 `RATE_LIMITED`; connection / handshake failure (IOException) → 502 `HIERARCHY_UNREACHABLE`, unresolvable name → 502 `DNS_UNRESOLVABLE` (not a security block), SSRF block → 400 `BLOCKED`, any other exception → 500 `HIERARCHY_FAILED` + ERROR log (exception text never leaves the server). Optional `tls_mode` (browser|default) and `via` (proxy|direct) = the route the SSL tab's preview just succeeded on (`tls_mode_used` / `via`), so hosts that pass only on the check's fallback work too; other values are ignored and the proxy is still used only when configured and not NO_PROXY. Read-only: no row, cache, alarm or audit.
  - **Capture:** `CertificateCheckerService.capturePresentedChain` = the check's own route (SSRF guard, `use_proxy ∧ proxy ∧ ¬NO_PROXY`, multi-A, SNI, browser TLS mode, record timeout), trust-all read, one attempt, no OCSP / CRL / DNS / HSTS. Total time (connect + handshake) ≤ `CAPTURE_TOTAL_SEC` 75 s — under the client's 90 s GET timeout; the deadline scheduler removes cancelled tasks at once. `via=direct` is honoured only when the check could really fall back (`retryFallbackEnabled` = retry-fallback ∧ transient retry ∧ ≥ 2 attempts).
  - **Build (browser / PKIX-like):** `service/certhierarchy/NetworkCertificateHierarchy` (pure). From the leaf up, each step tries (1) a SELF-SIGNED trust anchor (`TrustEvaluator.trustAnchors()`: system store, then the corporate CA bundle) that signs the current cert — the path ENDS there (cross-signed roots: the store root is shown, the server's cross-sign becomes an extra; the server's identical copy is used when it sent one), (2) an issuer among the sent certs (name + SIGNATURE, order-independent), (3) a non-self-signed trust-store intermediate (the walk continues). Several candidates → currently valid first, then latest `notAfter`. A self-signed STORE anchor is accepted without CA extensions (PKIX does not check the anchor — old corporate roots); store intermediates and server-sent issuers must be CAs (`canIssue`). A signature algorithm this JVM can't verify counts as "signed" when the names match (same rule as `ManualCertificateChains.isSelfSigned`) — for the PATH only: such a link sets `signature_unverified: true` and `root_trusted: false`, and the UI shows the root as "unknown" (`chier.net.why.signatureUnverified`), never green (review round 6). Depth ≤ 10, input ≤ 20, cycle-safe. Response = the manual `ManualCertificateHierarchy` node format (+ per node `source` server|trust_store, `trust_store` system|corporate) + `root_source` (`trust_store|server|self_signed`, null when the top is not self-signed), `root_trusted` (the root IS in a store — a server-sent root that no store holds is NOT trusted), `missing_kind` (`intermediate` = the server sent only the leaf, `anchor` = the chain ends at a store intermediate, `root`), `order_ok`, `presented_count`, `extra_certificates` (≤ 10, deduped), `via`, `port`, `checked_at`. Negative `days_remaining` is kept.
  - **UI:** the SSL tab's "Zincir | Hiyerarşi (tarayıcı gibi)" switch now also shows on network rows when `CertificateModal` passes `networkHierarchyDomain` (not in preview mode, not manual); "Zincir" (SslChainView) stays the default and is unchanged. `certmodal/NetworkCertHierarchy` (lazy) = summary card (root source badge + why, sent count, wrong-order warning, proxy, read time) + `CertHierarchyView` + collapsible extra certificates. The result is cached per preview response in a MODULE-level WeakMap keyed by the `data` object (the in-flight request is shared, errors are not cached), so toggling views or switching window tabs never reconnects and "Yeniden kontrol et" refreshes it. `networkHierarchyModel`: `issuer_missing: true` always means "missing" (a store intermediate is never shown as a root); an absent field is unknown; the root badge is green ONLY for a store root or a server root with `root_trusted: true`; `missing_kind` picks the badge / why / placeholder text, and the placeholder shows the expected issuer (`missingIssuerLabel`).
  - **Tree (both manual and network):** every row shows a role badge (`chier.role.*`: root primary, intermediate secondary, leaf outline), the EXPIRY DATE (`chier.expiresOn`) and the days-left badge; names wrap (no `truncate`); a trust-store root carries a "Güven deposundan" badge and a "Kaynak" row in the details. Network wording via `missingRootText` / `emptyText` props.
  - **Gates:** `NetworkCertificateHierarchyTest` (cross-sign, store intermediate, untrusted server root, missing intermediate, valid-anchor preference), `CertificateHierarchyControllerTest`, `NetworkCertHierarchy.test.jsx`, `networkHierarchyModel.test.js`, `CertHierarchyView.test.jsx`, `CertHierarchyEntryPoints.test.jsx` (no prop → no switch), `e2e/cert-hierarchy-network.spec.js` (390/768/1280: default chain, roles + expiry per row, no clipping / overflow, one request across toggles, ≥ 40 px rows).
  - **Review round 4:** only a CA (basicConstraints, keyCertSign when keyUsage is present; a v1 cert only when self-signed) can be an issuer (`canIssue`). A sent certificate named like the top's issuer that cannot be used → `missing_kind=wrong_intermediate`; a lone CA-flagged server cert with no issuer → `intermediate`. `trust_store_readable:false` (system store unreadable) makes the root badge "unknown". `via=direct` is honoured only if the record is not proxied or the check's retry fallback is on; `via=proxy` needs the record's proxy or (fallback ∧ auto proxy fallback). Per-user in-flight cap 2 (`MAX_PER_USER_CONCURRENT`) before the pod-wide cap; the capture uses the SSRF-validated addresses and a 2× timeout socket deadline.
- **Check engine / certificate data fixes (2026-10-22, bug hunt):**
  - **Keyword snippet (CHECKS-3):** the snippet position found in `body.toLowerCase(ROOT)` is mapped back to the original body (`KeywordCheckerService.snippet` / `toBodyIndex`; "İ" lowercases to 2 chars). The snippet can never fail a check.
  - **k6 anomaly guard (CHECKS-4):** auto-disable is a targeted conditional UPDATE (`ScriptedMonitorRepository.disableIfActive`, `WHERE id=? AND active=true`); 0 rows (user paused / deleted it meanwhile) = nothing disabled, no notification. The sweep's stale entity is never `save`d (it reverted the user's script / env / name). Alarm close uses the monitor's owner ctx (L-M3). Gate `ScriptedAnomalyGuardStaleEntityTest` (H2, NOT_SUPPORTED).
  - **Card sparkline / SLA use the verdict (DATA-1):** `MonitorSparklineService.KINDS` fail = `ok = FALSE` (http, keyword, page, scripted; legacy `error` rule only when `ok IS NULL`), `open = FALSE` (port), DNS = lookup `error` only (a record change is not downtime), pagespeed = `ok = FALSE` ∨ error ∨ threshold breach.
  - **Key size rule (DATA-13):** `CertificateHealthRules.sizedKeyFamily` — EC first (name starts with "EC"), EdDSA / ML-DSA / SLH-DSA / FN-DSA never RSA/DSA, size ≤ 0 = unknown (never "short"). `LatestCheckRepository.findWeakAlgorithmCandidates` / `CertificateCheckRepository.weakObservationsSince` mirror it in SQL (`NON_SIZED_KEY_TOKENS`; gate `WeakKeySqlParityTest`).
  - **Column fits (DATA-14):** `HttpCheck.setError` clips to 255 ("…"); `ChainValidationService.extractCrlUrl` stores the first CDP URL that fits 255 (long AD CS LDAP CDP first → the HTTP one), else the first clipped.
  - **Mixed content from the right page monitor (DATA-9):** `PageMonitorRepository.findActiveForHost` / `existsActiveForHost` — LIKE prefilter + `URI.getHost()` exact match; the probe value (`CertificateAppLayerProbe`) and the health list's "page monitor exists" flag (`CertificateController.certificateHealth`) both use it.
- **Keyword failure diagnosis + end-to-end diagnosis (2026-10-04, user request).**
  - **Every check stores response meta:** `final_url` (masked), `redirect_count`, `content_type`, `body_bytes`, `body_truncated`, `charset`, `via`.
  - **Failed checks also store:**
    - `failure_reason` (`KeywordFailureClassifier.CODES`; network errors via `HttpFailureDiagnostics.classify`);
    - `failure_detail` (Turkish, ≤ 500 chars);
    - `hints` (`KeywordBodyAnalyzer.HINT_CODES`, JSON in the column, a list in the API);
    - a masked `excerpt` (≤ 600 visible chars).
    - `error` is clipped to 255 chars — a longer error used to fail the row insert.
  - **One mapper:** scheduled and manual checks map through `KeywordResultMapper` only (expectation-aware `KeywordCheckerService.check(…, Expectation)`). `ok` / `found` / alarm decisions are unchanged, and manual checks still never alarm.
  - **Diagnosis endpoint:** `POST /api/monitoring/keyword/{id}/diagnose` (+ history) mirrors HTTP diagnosis:
    - `RawHttpProbe` with the keyword User-Agent and `captureBody`;
    - own route + other route;
    - real-checker `client_check`;
    - a keyword analysis block.
  - **Access and limits:** gate `diagnostics.run/execute` + `canOperateTeam` (`can_diagnose`); 6/min per user and per monitor, 4 in flight (its own limiter, separate from HTTP).
  - **History:** `diagnostic_runs` `KEYWORD_DIAG` / `keyword-monitor:<id>`; no body previews or contexts stored.
  - **UI:** `keyword/KeywordCheckFailure.jsx` (history rows), `keyword/diagnose/*` (variant of `HttpDiagnoseDialog`; without a variant the HTTP output is identical), deep link `kdx`.
  - **Adding a code:** TR+EN `kwfail.<C>.short|why|effect|fix`, `kwhint.<C>.title|cause|effect|fix` or `kwdx.finding.<C>.title|body` (`KeywordDiagFindingsI18nGateTest`).
  - **Form "Test" (`POST /keyword/test`):** calls the expectation-aware check and returns the same diagnosis keys (only when the condition is NOT met); the form renders the history `KeywordFailurePanel` (no diagnose button — unsaved monitor).
- **Failure diagnosis + end-to-end diagnosis for the other types (2026-10-05, user request).**
  - **Stored reason:** `service/failure/` — `CheckFailureReason` (code catalogue with a Phase), `CheckFailure` (masked detail map, compact JSON ≤ 4000 chars), `CheckFailureClassifier` (`forException` reuses `HttpFailureDiagnostics.classify`, plus DNS rcode / ping / HTTP status / page resources / domain).
    - Columns `failure_reason` + `failure_detail` on ping/port/dns_records/page/pagespeed/uptime/domain checks, plus `dns_records.error`, `certificate_checks.error_stage|resolved_ips` (literal `patch()` lines).
    - Classification is METADATA only: it never changes ok/up/open/status, error text, alarms, notifications or DNS change detection (`DnsCheckerService.applyFailure` runs only when `!success` and never touches value/changed/manual). It never throws and adds no query.
    - Every save site copies it (sweep in `SchedulerService`, manual checks in `MonitoringController`, `DomainCheckerService`, `CertificateService`) — `CheckFailurePersistenceWiringTest`.
  - **History UI:** `components/checks/` (`checkFailureModel.failureOf(type,row)` — server code first, legacy rows inferred from the card classifiers with `legacy:true`; `CheckFailurePanel` / `CheckFailureCell`). DNS history: failed lookups have `status=fail` + `counts.errors` (opt-in `errorTile` on `CheckHistoryTab`).
  - **Diagnose endpoints:** `POST /api/monitoring/{ping|port|dns}/{id}/diagnose` (`NetworkMonitorDiagnosticsController`, `service/diagnose/*`, step contract via `NetDiagSupport.envelope`) and `/{page|pagespeed}/{id}/diagnose` (`PageDiagnosticsController`, `service/page/diagnose/*`, `RawHttpProbe` with a `Profile` = the real checker's headers). Each has `…/diagnose/history[/{runId}]`.
    - Same rules as HTTP/keyword: `diagnostics.run/execute` + `canOperateTeam` on the effective team (list rows carry `can_diagnose` via `MonitoringController.withDiagnoseFlag`), 6/min per user and per monitor, 4 in flight, side-effect free (no check rows, no alarm evaluation).
    - History: `diagnostic_runs` `PING|PORT|DNS|PAGE|PAGESPEED_DIAG`, key `<type>-monitor:<id>`. `/api/admin/diagnostics/history/{id}` 404s every monitor run type (`NetDiagnosticsHistory.MONITOR_RUN_TYPES`).
    - Audit `*_DIAGNOSTICS_RUN` (MONITOR).
  - **UI:** `components/diagnose/NetDiagnoseDialog` (ping/port/dns; deep links `pgdx`/`ptdx`/`dndx`) and `HttpDiagnoseDialog` variants in `components/page|pagespeed/diagnose/` (`pidx`/`psdx`).
  - **Access fixes:** Domain "Sorun Tanıla", Uptime and the cert modal's diagnose button follow `diagnostics.run` + team scope, not the role. `domain-expiry` also accepts a standalone domain monitor the user can operate. The Domain FORM's "Sorun Tanıla" shows for a global admin, or on a saved monitor's edit form whose domain is unchanged and whose row says `can_diagnose` (FE-5; an unsaved / changed domain stays global-admin only, server `requireAdminOrMonitoredDomain`).
  - **Adding a code:** TR+EN `chkfail.<C>.short|why|effect|fix` (`CheckFailureI18nGateTest`), `ndx.finding.<C>.title|body` (`NetDiagFindingsI18nGateTest`), `pgdx.finding.*` / `psdx.finding.*` (`PageDiagFindingsI18nGateTest`).
- **Team data quality score (2026-10-10, user request: "sahipsiz kayıt, eksik iletişim, yanlış katman, 7/24'e bildirilmeyen kritik izleme → tek skor + düzeltme listesi").**
  - **Rules:** `service/quality/DataQualityRule` is the single catalogue (code, scope, severity; weight = HIGH 3 / MEDIUM 2 / LOW 1). Ownership rules (`INV_NO_TEAM`, `MON_NO_TEAM`: no team, deleted team or PASSIVE team) never enter a team score — only the org score and the "Ownerless" bucket. Hygiene rules reuse `InventoryHygieneService` codes (`no_contacts`, `never_checked`, `stale`, `error`; failing = current error AND ≥ 3 errors in 7 days). `NOC_CRITICAL_UNCOVERED` reuses `NocCoverageService.reason` for tier 1–2 inventory and applies only while 7/24 is usable (active addressed group + SSL type on), otherwise an org note. Tier heuristics (`TierHeuristics`) flag only strong contradictions (EV on tier 3–4, whole-word test token on tier 1–2, internal cert on tier 1). Team address/escalation rules apply only to teams with ≥ 1 active monitored item.
  - **Score:** per rule health = 1 − failing/eligible; score = round(100 × Σ w·health / Σ w) over applicable rules (`DataQualityScore`, size-independent; no applicable rule → null/`NO_DATA`). Bands ≥ 90 / ≥ 75 / ≥ 50 / below.
  - **Cost:** `DataQualitySource` loads everything org-wide in a FIXED number of queries (12 JDBC + 4 JPA + hygiene + NOC config; `DataQualityQueriesTest.fixedQueryCount`), memoised 60 s (`site.monitor.data-quality.cache-ms`), `?fresh=1` at most every 5 s. Never add a per-team or per-row query.
  - **API/scope:** `GET /api/data-quality` + `/teams/{id|unassigned}`, gate `data_quality.view/view` (PermissionCatalog, all roles by default). Read scope = monitoring read (`canViewMonitoring`; 7/24 operators see all); Ownerless bucket only for `seesAllMonitoring`; row `can_edit` uses each record's OWN write gate (`inventoryWriteTest` / `canOperateTeam` / `canManage`). No user ids in the payload (item details are under `facts`, not `detail`). `DataQualityService.digest()` is the reusable unfiltered summary for other reports.
  - **Trend:** `data_quality_daily` (day × bucket, team_key 0 = org, −1 = ownerless) written by `DataQualitySnapshotJob` (hourly tick, `scheduler_lock` `data-quality-snapshot`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, self-prunes > 120 days; RetentionCatalog BOUNDED).
  - **Partial data (2026-10-12):** required inputs (teams, members, escalation, notification groups, inventory, monitors, pause history) THROW (`DataQualitySource.required`) — no score and no daily snapshot from partial data; optional inputs (hygiene, scan errors, 7/24 config) degrade to "unknown". The snapshot of day D is written at 00:25 on D, i.e. it reflects the END of D-1: a month end is the snapshot of the 1st of the next month (`monthEnds`, labels shifted back one day).
  - **UI:** `?tab=dataquality` (Reports group; `components/dataquality/*`, URL prefix `dq_`; redesign 2026-10-10): org card (`DataQualityOverview.OrgHero`: ring + band + 7-day delta + counts | wide 30-day trend, full-width band distribution), costliest rules + team comparison (top / priority teams, `TeamLeaders`, hidden with < 2 scored teams), ranking card (band tiles, search, sort; table ↔ cards by container width), fix list modal grouped by rule ordered by points lost with a team-vs-org benchmark and rule counts on the severity filter; deep links via `linkFor` (inventory drawer / cert window `open=noc|cert` / monitor detail / Admin teams or contacts). Adding a rule: enum + `dataQualityCodes.js` (same order) + TR/EN `dq.rule.<CODE>.title|why|fix` (`DataQualityRuleI18nGateTest`) + `linkFor` if it needs a new target.
- **TLS configuration grade A+–F (2026-10-10, user request: "protokol, şifre takımları, OCSP stapling, HSTS ve anahtar boyu tek notta; kartta rozet; not düşünce uyarı").**
  - **Rubric (single source `service/tlsgrade/TlsGradeRules`):** caps, no invented score. Starts at A+, each finding caps the grade, result = lowest cap; every grade carries reason codes. F: expired / hostname mismatch / untrusted / revoked / broken chain / weak key / SHA-1 / NULL-anon-EXPORT cipher. D: RC4/3DES negotiated or ≥ 2 C-level reasons (`MULTIPLE_SERIOUS`). C: no TLS 1.2, weak cipher accepted. B: TLS 1.0/1.1 on, no PFS, CBC chosen. A (no A+): no TLS 1.3, HSTS missing/short/unchecked, no OCSP stapling, profile pending/failed/partial. Unknown never lowers the grade but A+ needs proof. Reuses `CertificateHealthRules` + the HSTS 180-day threshold. Manual (uploaded) certs are NOT graded (`not_applicable`).
  - **TLS profile probe (`TlsHelloProbe` + `TlsProfileProbeService`):** raw ClientHello per version (1.0–1.3), TLS 1.2 `status_request` (stapling = CertificateStatus seen), weak-cipher-only hello; never completes a handshake, never touches JVM security properties; bounded (5 s per probe, 30 s per endpoint, 64 KB / 64 records). SSRF-validated; proxy decision identical to the cert check (`use_proxy` ∧ proxy ∧ ¬NO_PROXY). Unknown stays UNKNOWN. Table `tls_profiles` (domain PK).
  - **Job (`TlsProfileJobService`):** any pod since multi-pod Sürüm 3-1 (two-phase round `round:tls-profile` (`TlsProfileJobService.ROUND_NAME`, permanent), period `site.monitor.tls-profile.tick-ms`, + `scheduler_lock` `tls-profile`; the sweep leader yields it to a live pod that COMPLETED the round within 2 periods (`round:tls-profile:done`); the body runs on the side-job runner as a fixed-delay job — the next tick starts at the earliest `tick-ms` after this pod's last finish; an interrupted / failed / shutdown-stopped tick is not completed — due again after its run lease; the tick stops within ~200 ms of `ContextClosedEvent` (finished probes saved, no reconcile / drop notice); before 3-1: sweep leader only); tick 10 min, ≤ 40 due endpoints (24 h interval, failed retried after 6 h), own 4-thread pool, 8 min tick budget. Never on the hourly sweep, never from "Check now". Keys `site.monitor.tls-profile.*` (`enabled` default true), `site.monitor.tls-grade.drop-notice-days` (7).
  - **Drops:** `tls_grade_status` (last grade per inventory id) + `tls_grade_changes` (log, series-certificate retention); the job tick reconciles grades from the cached `getAllLatest` rows, writes only on change, activity `TLS_GRADE_DROPPED`. Still NOT an alarm (no alert type; a real alarm would need a new type wired everywhere `HTTP_SLOW` is).
  - **Drop notification (2026-10-11, user request: "TLS notu düştüğünde yalnız uygulama içinde görünüyor"):** a DROP row is born `notify_state=PENDING` (REFINE/RISE never). After reconcile, every job tick runs `TlsGradeDropNotifier.notifyPending()` under the `tls-profile` lock: conditional claim PENDING→SENDING (`transitionNotifyState`, at-most-once; SENDING never resent), then per owning team (SY and UG separately, each sees only its own rows) ONE summary mail (`TlsGradeDropMail`, deep link to the cert window) to `teamEmailsForMonitor` + a team push (`enqueueTeamNoticeLocalized`, trigger `TLS_GRADE_DROP`), one `notification_logs` row per team (alertEventId 0). Final states `SENT` / `SKIPPED: bildirim kapalı | eşik üstü not | sistem bakımı | takım yok | alıcı yok` (+ `notified_at`). Keys `site.monitor.tls-grade.drop-notify` (true) and `…drop-notify-min-grade` (A: new grade ≤ this is notified), group `tls-grade`. Off → pending rows are marked off, never sent later; system-maintenance mute applies. Pre-feature rows (null state) are never sent. A team / recipient READ failure is not final (review round 5): the rows go back to PENDING and are retried on the next ticks (≤ `READ_RETRY_MAX` 6 per pod), then `FAILED`; every decision — including an unexpected send error — writes a `notification_logs` row (`FAILED: alıcılar okunamadı`). The SENDING→PENDING retry transition runs AFTER the round's results are written, so a failing transition never turns delivered rows into "interrupted"; retry counters are dropped for every row that ended (review round 6). **Pod death mid-batch (multi-pod R2a-D1, MP-43):** the claim (`claimNotify`) stamps `notified_at` with the claim time (a stale SENDING row is aged by it, not by `changed_at`; an old pod's unstamped claim still falls back to `changed_at`); a row is finished as soon as ALL its owner teams are done (it used to wait for every team — a pod dying mid-loop lost the teams not yet attempted as "interrupted"); a stale SENDING row with no `TLS_GRADE_DROP` trace for any owner team within `TRACE_WINDOW` (10 min) of its claim goes back to PENDING (nobody got it; at least once only for the team in flight), a traced one becomes `FAILED: yarıda kaldı` (never resent); after an exception, rows of teams never attempted go back to PENDING. The read-retry counter is `tls_grade_changes.notify_attempts` (DB, `ReportIntegritySchemaPatch`, not pod memory — a restart / leadership change no longer resets the cap). Gates: `TlsGradeDropNotifierTest.podDiesAfterFirstTeam_secondTeamNotifiedNextTick|staleSendingWithTrace_isInterruptedNotResent|staleAgedByClaimStampNotChangedAt|retryCapSurvivesNewInstance|failureMidRunMarksInterrupted`. `/api/tls-grade/drops` rows carry `notify` (UI code via `TlsGradeDropNotifier.uiCode`) + `notified_at`; the "Recent drops" dialog shows it as a badge. Gates: `TlsGradeDropNotifierTest`, `TlsGradeServiceTest.dropsCarryNotifyCode`, `TlsProfileJobServiceTest.tickNotifiesPendingDropsAfterReconcile`, `TlsGradeOverview.test.jsx`, `e2e/tls-grade.spec.js`.
  - **2026-10-12:** an exception after the claim, or a SENDING row older than 1 h, becomes `FAILED: yarıda kaldı` (UI `interrupted`, never resent). A manual rescan only saves the profile and evicts caches — the next tick reconciles under the `tls-profile` lock from a fresh list (`cert-latest` is evicted before every reconcile; an interrupted tick skips reconcile and notify). A grade change explained only by HSTS becoming unmeasured (`HSTS_NOT_CHECKED` added) is REFINE, not DROP (`onlyMeasurementGapAdded`). `/api/tls-grade/drops` filters the viewer's teams in SQL (`findRecentForTeams`).
  - **API:** list rows `tls_grade`, `tls_grade_reasons` (codes), `tls_grade_drop` (NON_NULL/NON_EMPTY — absent when not graded); `/certificates/list` `filter_grade`, facet `grades`, sort `tls_grade`, CSV `tls_grade(_reasons)`; `GET /api/certificates/{d}/tls-grade` (health read gate), `POST …/tls-grade/rescan` (`diagnostics.run` + `canOperateTeam`, 3/min per user and domain, 2 in flight, audit `CERT_TLS_PROFILE_RESCAN`, 409 `MANUAL_CERT`), `GET /api/tls-grade/drops` (monitoring scope + profile coverage).
  - **UI (`components/tlsgrade/*`):** `TlsGradeBadge` (card status row + All Certificates column; tap → "Neden B?"), `TlsGradeSection` (top of the Health tab), `TlsGradeOverview` (All Certificates distribution chips + "Recent drops" dialog). The UI never computes a grade; `tlsGradeCodes.js` mirrors codes + caps.
  - **Adding a reason code:** enum in `TlsGradeRules.Reason`, `tlsGradeCodes.js` (same order + cap), TR+EN `tlsg.reason.<C>.title|why|fix` (`TlsGradeI18nGateTest`, `tlsGradeModel.test.js`). Gates: `TlsGradeRulesTest` (truth table), `TlsHelloProbeTest` (fake server), `TlsProfileProbeServiceTest`, `TlsProfileJobServiceTest`, `TlsGradeServiceTest`, `TlsGradeControllerTest`, `CertificateServiceTlsGradeTest`, `e2e/tls-grade.spec.js` (390/768/1280).

### Deployment specifics
- **K8s manifests** (`k8s/`): **LEGACY, unmaintained, reference only** — `helm/site-monitor/` is the single source of truth for every environment (CI lints only the chart). The manifests have drifted (no `SITE_MONITOR_SECRET_KEY`, dead env keys, `certmonitor`↔`sitemonitor` DB names, registry-less image, no `/var/log` volume); `kubectl apply` as-is will not start a working app. Known incompatibilities: `k8s/README.md`. Prod deploy command: `docs/PROD_DEPLOY_CHECKLIST.md`.
- **Helm chart** (`helm/site-monitor/`) is the authoritative deploy path (`k8s/` is legacy, see `k8s/README.md`). The bundled Bitnami PostgreSQL subchart was removed — prod uses an external/managed DB. Prod deploy command and checklist: `docs/PROD_DEPLOY_CHECKLIST.md` (never `--reuse-values`; `SITE_MONITOR_SECRET_KEY` must be carried over UNCHANGED or every encrypted secret becomes unreadable). Values split per environment under `environments/` (`develop.yaml` / `release.yaml` / `master.yaml`). `develop.yaml` runs 1 replica, email OFF, 2h scan, HPA OFF — useful diff to copy from when setting up a new lower env. `postgresql.primary.extendedConfiguration` preloads `shared_preload_libraries = 'pg_stat_statements'` so the DB-wide query analytics light up (needs a `helm upgrade` + DB restart in prod; the app falls back gracefully until then).
  - **Multi-pod (2026-10-21, R1-D):** `helm template` FAILS when more than one pod can run (`replicaCount > 1` or `autoscaling.enabled`) and `config.sessionStoreType` (trimmed, case-insensitive — the app's own reading) ≠ `jdbc` (in-memory sessions would log users out at random and every pod start would clear every user's single-session marker). Shutdown values `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` (120) / `preStopSleepSeconds` (10) — see "Shutdown budget + Helm". Placement values, all empty = not rendered: `nodeSelector`, `affinity`, `tolerations` (pin pods to nodes with known egress), `topologySpreadConstraints.whenUnsatisfiable` (default `DoNotSchedule`) and `.matchLabelKeys` (e.g. `["pod-template-hash"]`, K8s ≥ 1.27; enable spread only with ≥ replicas + 1 schedulable nodes). `NOTES.txt` reads EVERY pod's log and, with > 1 pod, says how to verify the single leader.
  - **Environments:** staging (`release.yaml`) runs 2 FIXED replicas with the HPA OFF (the JVM memory metric pinned it at max and scale-in could remove the leader); prod (`master.yaml`) stays at `replicaCount: 1` in the R1 release. Going 1 → 2 is its OWN commit + `helm upgrade` with the SAME image (`replicaCount: 2`, PDB on, `dbPoolMax` from `max_connections`, `--timeout 15m`) after the prerequisites P1–P5 (`docs/PROD_DEPLOY_CHECKLIST.md` "Çok pod (1 → 2 replika)"; P1 = `scripts/multipod-egress-check.sh`); never together with a version change (two new-version pods would start at once). Back to 1: `replicaCount: 1` AND PDB off. The HPA stays off.
  - **DB connections:** `max_connections ≥ (replicas + 1) × dbPoolMax + 25`; `dbPoolMax = min(30, floor((max_connections − 25) / (N + 1)))`; PgBouncer only in `session` mode (advisory locks + `SET lock_timeout … RESET` need a session). `docs/db-scaling.md` §3.
  - **Chart hardening (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b, R2b-E, MP-48–50):** HPA (stays OFF): no `replicas:` in the Deployment while it is on (the first upgrade that turns it on drops the field — Kubernetes falls back to 1 replica until the HPA's first sync), each metric only when its target is set, memory target empty by default (`autoscaling.targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: ""`), `autoscaling.behavior` (down 600 s window, 1 pod / 300 s; up 1 pod / 120 s), no target / min > max fails. PDB: exactly one of `minAvailable` / `maxUnavailable`; integer `minAvailable ≥ replicaCount` (HPA: `≥ minReplicas`), `100%`, or `maxUnavailable` 0 fails `helm template`. `config.dbPoolMin` → `DB_POOL_MIN` (5; 0 allowed; > `dbPoolMax` fails; the app default without Helm stays 10). `config.extraEnv` → ConfigMap for non-secret keys the chart has no value for (e.g. `TOMCAT_MAX_THREADS`); a ConfigMap / container-env / Secret / agent-env name, an invalid name or a map / list value fails. `priorityClassName`, optional `topologySpreadConstraints.zone` (ScheduleAnyway), staging spread `matchLabelKeys: ["pod-template-hash"]`. `resources.*.ephemeral-storage` 1Gi / 4Gi and `storage.varLogSizeLimit` 2Gi (`/var/log` emptyDir; `storage.tmpSizeLimit` empty) — past them the kubelet evicts the pod instead of filling the node. The agent env is unchanged from 20.166.0 (8 variables; the @Scheduled instrumentation stays on — see "OpenTelemetry Java agent").
  - **Logs of ALL pods:** `kubectl logs "$DEPLOY"` / `deploy/<name>` reads ONE pod — every doc / canary uses `kubectl logs -n "$NS" -l app.kubernetes.io/instance="$REL" --prefix --tail=-1`.
- **Dockerfile**: 3 build stages — `node:24-alpine` (frontend) → `maven:3.9` (backend) → `eclipse-temurin:25-jre-alpine` (runtime), every base image pinned to an exact tag + digest (refresh instructions at the top of the file) — plus `previous-release` / `asset-carry` (previous release's own page files, build arg `PREVIOUS_IMAGE`, see "Rolling update without a blank page") and the CI-only `asset-carry-check`. Container-aware JVM (`-XX:+UseContainerSupport`, heap ≈ 75%), in-pod debug tools (`curl bash dig`), healthcheck via `wget /health`. Final image runs as `appuser:1000`.
- **k6 smoke** (`perf/k6-smoke.js`): 50 VU × 30s against `/health` + `/api/system/network-status`. SLA: error rate < 1%, p95 < 500 ms, 99% checks pass. Auth-gated endpoints returning `401` are treated as healthy (gate working).
- **Local DB**: `data/` holds a SQLite file + WAL/SHM for dev runs (gitignored). Prod uses PostgreSQL exclusively.

### Timezone
Backend stores timestamps as UTC (audit, alerts, notifications). Log timestamps are localized via `LOG_TIMEZONE` (default `Europe/Istanbul`). Frontend formats with `date-fns` against Europe/Istanbul (UTC+3, no DST). When doing date arithmetic, use `setUTC*` to avoid the 3-hour drift trap.

## Before a feature counts as done (2026-10-12, user decision: "hata yapmayalım")

Two review rounds after 20.123.0 found 26 + 17 real bugs. None was on the happy path, every one passed the existing tests, and they came from the same few blind spots. Every new feature AND every fix goes through this section BEFORE it is pushed. For each checklist item that applies, write the code that handles it AND a test that proves it.

**Process**
- Small batches: at most one or two features per release — several finished features may ship in ONE release when the user agrees (2026-10-18).
- Adversarial review BEFORE the first push: fresh eyes per area asking "how does this break?", verify each finding in the code, fix, then release. Fixes get the same review — 6 of the 17 second-round bugs were inside first-round fixes. Since 2026-10-18 (user decision): ONE final independent review per release, not after every small fix.
- Gates (2026-10-18, user decision — replaces the 2026-10-16 local-verify rule):
  - **Full suites ONLY on the PR's CI:** full backend `mvn -B clean verify`, full vitest and full Playwright run on CI, never skipped and never run locally: commit on a `feat/…` / `fix/…` branch, `gh pr create --base main` (CI runs on `pull_request` to main, 4 shards, ~15 min), and only when the PR's CI is green bring the SAME commit to main (`git push origin HEAD:main`, fast-forward — the conventional subject drives the release bump). main never goes red; Release waits for main's own CI run of that commit.
  - **Locally, targeted only:** the backend test classes and the vitest files of the touched areas, the gates (`i18n-parity`, `i18n-used-keys`, `errorMessageQuality`, `shadcnOnly`), `npm run build` and the area's e2e spec. ONE heavy test run on the machine at a time (never two Maven / vitest / Playwright runs in parallel — other worktrees included).
  - New requests that arrive while a release is in its gates go to the queue — the release's scope is frozen once its gates start.

**Failure-mode checklist** — ask every question; one test per item that applies:
1. **Several pods.** Does the job run once per cluster (`scheduler_lock`, cluster schedule round, sweep-leader fence)? Does manual triggering use the same lock? Is in-memory per-pod state still valid after another pod writes the shared row (prune / merge by timestamp)? Since 2026-10-21: long jobs hold a RENEWED lock (`ClusterLockService` via the `SchedulerService` helpers) and check their hold before writing results; cluster gates fail CLOSED on transient DB errors (only a missing table keeps the legacy fallback); per-pod alarm memory never decides across a leadership change (it is reset) and never "first sight → due"; edge-triggered signals seen once (DNS / domain change) are made durable in a row before the in-memory decision; a manual action served by a follower is forwarded to the leader; a new periodic round uses `claimRound` + `completeRound`; nothing new starts after `ContextClosedEvent`; docs, runbook steps and canaries read ALL pods' logs; a Helm value that only matters with > 1 pod gets a `helm-template-check.sh` assertion. A new in-memory cache / memo / snapshot that another pod could serve is either a Spring cache (on the cache bus automatically) or registers a local evictor with `CacheBus` and bumps next to its local clear (section "Cross-pod cache invalidation"); a write endpoint never relies on a local eviction alone. A security / abuse limit (rate limit, lockout, quota on a public or secret-revealing endpoint) is counted in the DB — `RateLimitCounterService` or a count of the rows the action already writes (`PersistedRowLimits`) — never only in pod memory; pure load limits (diagnose, PDF permits) may stay per pod. Since multi-pod Sürüm 3-1: a new long-running `@Scheduled` job goes through `SchedulerService.runSideJob` / `offloadSideJob` (per-pod side-job runner, leader-yield / cron deferral) instead of holding the shared 8-thread scheduling pool, and keeps its own lock / round / item claims (deferral re-runs the same guarded body); a new piece of MOS evaluation memory gets an enqueue-only `EvalStateSink` hook + `EvalStateSource` value + VERIFY kind (section "Durable evaluation state"); a DB-free leadership hint (`sweepLeaderHint()`) may place work, never decide an alarm.
2. **Interrupted halfway.** Pod dies or an exception happens after a claim: can a row stay in an intermediate state (SENDING, PENDING, CLAIMED) forever? Claim → try/catch → final state; only manual paths may take over a stale claim. Shared senders follow claim → send → final state, and a final state must exist for a dead pod too: a stale SENDING claim becomes UNKNOWN (no resend) or is re-run only when the work is idempotent (`resolution_notify`, `MailRetryOrphanSweeper`, system-maintenance `reclaimCatchUp`, weekly availability `INTERRUPTED`).
3. **Restart / shutdown.** Background work stops before processing results when shutdown starts. Slow work is off the startup path. One-time data fixes use a `schema_patch_markers` marker written only on success — never gate on "rows changed in this boot" (an earlier release may already have changed them).
4. **Existing data.** What does the new code do with rows written by the previous release: null new columns, data already repaired, old snapshots (`summary_json`), old hint/verdict parameter counts, alarms opened before the deploy?
5. **Load fails / partial data.** The UI never derives a success / green / "complete" / "all clear" state from null or partial data. Unknown ≠ OK ≠ missing.
6. **Scoped viewer.** What does a scoped admin, AUDIT, USER, TEAM_ADMIN and 7/24 operator see? Lists are scope-filtered (e.g. `/admin/users`), so an id missing from a scoped list is UNKNOWN, not "absent". A button shows only when the write endpoint would accept it (`can_edit` = permission AND scope).
7. **Time.** Europe/Istanbul day and month boundaries, the current unfinished month, handovers (on-call), and "now" vs "at the time it happened" (who received the opening, which rule was in force for a stored month).
8. **Notifications.** No duplicate (same person via two paths, manual resend), none lost (recovery after an outage), passive users dropped on every channel, recipient language, SKIPPED is not FAILED, every decision leaves a `notification_logs` row, and logged values fit their columns (`recipient_email` / `email_status` are VARCHAR(255) — clip).
9. **Concurrent edits.** Edit forms send only changed fields (never overwrite a concurrent status change); unchanged values are not re-validated; an empty change sends no request.
10. **Project rules.** Names and long text never clipped (wrapping pills need `rounded-md overflow-visible`), 390 / 768 px layouts, opaque user ids, TR + EN keys. Add EN text with an apostrophe through the Edit tool or a Write-tool script, never a heredoc, then check `node -e "import('./src/i18n/en.js')"`.
11. **Gates that read source.** No `);` inside comments in `PermissionCatalog` lists; the audit field is named `auditService`; busy flags cleared in `finally`; `docs/RETENTION_POLITIKASI.md` regenerated after a catalog change; a whitepaper source change (`frontend/src/assets/whitepaper*.md`) needs BOTH `npm run gen:guide-pdf` (PDF + manifest) and, from the repo root, `node scripts/sync-whitepaper.mjs` (root `WHITEPAPER*.md` copies — `whitepaper-sync.test.jsx`); Jacoco class floors (measure with `jacoco:report` when touching a floored class).
12. **Grafana dashboard.** Did the change add / rename / remove a `sitemonitor_*` meter, a monitor type, an alert family or anything a team would want to see on the dashboard? Then update `GrafanaDashboardGenerator`, bump `DASHBOARD_REVISION`, APPEND the printed line to `dashboard-content.sha256` (never edit an existing line) and regenerate the e2e fixture (section "Grafana dashboard"). A new business meter goes into `MonitoringMetricNames` (served only on the token-protected `/metrics/sitemonitor`), never onto the global registry / `/metrics`. The gates fail when a meter or a type is missing or the content changed without a revision bump — never silence them with the allow-list unless the meter truly has no dashboard value.

## When adding tests

- **Backend**: mirror the package layout under `src/test/java/`. Prefer `@ExtendWith(MockitoExtension.class)` for unit isolation; reach for `@SpringBootTest` only when you need full context (currently exactly one: `SqlSamplesIntegrationTest`). H2 is on the test classpath.
- **Frontend**: place `.test.jsx` next to the component file under `src/test/`. Use `test-utils.jsx#render()`. Add new i18n keys to **both** `TR` and `EN` in the same change or the parity test will fail.
- **Live e2e (2026-10-07):** `frontend/e2e/*.spec.js` mock every `/api` route, so they cannot see frontend↔backend contract bugs (the 20.110 renew-wizard reset was only caught live). `frontend/playwright.live.config.js` + `e2e/live/` drive the REAL UI against the local backend on :8080 (login → manual-certificate journey with cleanup, check-history failure panels, diagnose dialogs). Opt-in only (`E2E_LIVE=1`; CI skips): `bash e2e/live/make-cert-fixtures.sh "$DIR"`, export `E2E_USER` / `E2E_PASS` from `.env` without printing them, then `E2E_LIVE=1 E2E_CERT_DIR="$DIR" npx playwright test -c playwright.live.config.js`. It logs the user's other session out (single-session rule), never triggers notifications, purges every `e2e-live-*` record, and waits if the hourly sweep is < 12 min away. Run it after features that span both sides.
- **Two-pod tests (2026-10-21):** `src/test/java/com/sitemonitor/it/MultiPodTestSupport` — fresh H2 (PostgreSQL mode) with `scheduler_lock` + `ClusterScheduleService.DDL`, a shared `AtomicLong` clock (`advance`), `owner(n)`, `pod(n)` (= `SchedulerService` built from its constructor with SHARED Mockito dependencies `mock(Type)`, own `ClusterScheduleService` and own `RecordingEventPublisher`), `newPod(owner)`, `legacyPod()`, `lease` / `leaseOwner` / `schedulerLockHolder`, `race(...)` (barrier start, 30 s cap). Limit: `scheduler_lock` uses the JVM-wide `SchedulerService.instanceId()` and wall-clock TTLs, so two pods in one JVM share that owner (a test that needs two lock owners builds its own `ClusterLockService` with an owner + clock). Monitoring alarm state: `src/test/java/com/sitemonitor/service/MonitoringMultiPodKit` — `podWithMos(mp, n)` gives each pod its OWN `MonitoringOutageService` (fence = the pod's leadership, `SweepLeadershipChangedEvent` → reset, as in production), `immediate()` / `Manual` (queued, step-by-step) executors for confirmation and recovery chains. PostgreSQL-only claim SQL (`ON CONFLICT`, partial / expression indexes, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, `RETURNING`) also gets a `@PostgresIntegration` test.

## CI gates (`.github/workflows/`)

- `ci.yml` — Java 25 + Node 24. Backend runs `mvn -B clean verify` (uploads Surefire + Jacoco artifacts). Frontend runs `npm ci` → `lint --if-present` → `build` → `npm audit --audit-level=high --omit=dev` (non-blocking). Helm-lint runs against all three env values files.
  - **Sharded since 2026-10-08.** The private repo's standard runner has 2 vCPU, so vitest (CPU − 1) and Playwright (CPU / 2) each ran ONE worker and CI took 29–50 min.
    - `frontend-unit` runs the vitest suite in 4 shards (`--shard=i/4 --maxWorkers=2 --reporter=blob --coverage`).
    - `VITEST_COVERAGE_SHARD=1` turns off thresholds and reports inside a shard (`vite.config.js`).
    - `frontend-coverage` downloads the blobs, runs `vitest --merge-reports --coverage` (the global threshold gate), then `coverage:floor`.
    - `frontend-e2e` runs Playwright in 4 shards.
    - Locally `npm run test:coverage` is unchanged.
  - **Helm template checks (2026-10-21):** the `helm-lint` job also runs `scripts/helm-template-check.sh` (Helm 3.14 in CI, Helm 4 locally): all three environments + bare values render; grace − preStop ≥ the app's shutdown budget; every multi-pod render uses the jdbc session store; the memory-store guard rejects 2 replicas / HPA; preStop 0 → no hook, preStop ≥ grace → rejected; staging 2 fixed replicas without HPA; placement hooks render only when set. Multi-pod Sürüm 2b (2026-10-23): HPA on → no Deployment replicas, CPU-only metric by default, behavior; PDB 1/1, both keys, `maxUnavailable` 0 and `100%` fail, `maxUnavailable` renders; `priorityClassName` / zone spread only when set, staging `pod-template-hash`; agent env unchanged (8 variables, no scheduling switch; agent.extraEnv still adds variables); `ephemeral-storage` request + limit and the `/var/log` sizeLimit in every environment; `DB_POOL_MIN` 5, min > max fails, `config.extraEnv` renders and every container-env / Secret name is refused.
  - **Image assets (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b):** job `image-assets` (PR) — carry-script tests (`node --test scripts/__tests__/carry-previous-assets.test.mjs`), Dockerfile `asset-carry-check` (exports `/out` + the previous image's `/previous` inputs) without a previous image (nothing carried) and with `:latest` (digest-pinned), each checked by `scripts/check-carry-output.mjs`: the expected set is recomputed independently (previous history's first K entries ∩ files present − this build) and must equal the carried set; nothing to carry because the frontend did not change = `::notice::`, never an error (a backend-only PR must not turn main red). Main's image is built only by release.yml after the merge, so this is the Dockerfile's only PR check.
- `docker-build.yml` — multi-arch image build + Trivy scan (HIGH/CRITICAL, currently report-only).
- `release.yml` — runs on `main`; detects bump from conventional commit prefix (`feat:` → minor, `fix:` → patch, `BREAKING CHANGE` → major), writes `VERSION`, publishes Helm chart. Does **not** re-run tests; trusts `ci.yml`.
- `ci.yml` job `backend-postgres-it` (2026-10-02, gating): `postgres:16` service + `mvn -B -Ppostgres-it test` with `IT_DB_URL/USER/PASSWORD` and `IT_DB_REQUIRED=true`. The `@PostgresIntegration` suite (tag `postgres`, excluded from the default surefire run) refuses a non-empty database, boots the context twice and asserts 0 failed schema patches, real indexes, PostgreSQL-only SQL (ON CONFLICT, LATERAL, claims, rollup upserts) and the polled-screen queries. Locally: point `IT_DB_*` at an EMPTY throwaway database (the dev DB user has no CREATEDB).
- Image boot test (non-gating, reusable `image-boot-test.yml`, called by `release.yml` after the release image is pushed — main's image is built ONLY there — and by `docker-build.yml` on develop/release branches): pulls the pushed image, runs it like Helm (prod profile, read-only root FS, 1 GiB) against a postgres service and runs `scripts/image-boot-check.sh` (health, 401 gate, schema-patch gauges, no ERROR / BAŞARISIZ in the log; with the image's `own-assets.txt` / `carried-assets.txt` / `carry-report.json` (step 7) an own and (when anything was carried) a carried previous-release asset are served 200 + immutable — carried 0 is a notice, a missing one plain-text 404 + no-store, `/boot-guard.js` no-store).

## Things that bite
- **No unbounded loop, regex, retry or wait on untrusted data (2026-10-09, user request: "sonsuz döngüye girmeyecek şekilde geliştirelim").** Every loop driven by network, file, user or DB data needs a cap or a deadline, and the cap must hold even when a timeout cannot interrupt the work.
  - **Regex:** `java.util.regex` ignores `Future.cancel` / interrupts, so a time box never stops a regex. Check length BEFORE any regex on request input. Never put two unbounded quantifiers over overlapping classes around a literal (`[^x]*KEY[^x]*=`), and never use a lazy body plus a backreference (`BEGIN (L)(.*?)END \1`) on uploads. Scan linearly instead: `SecretMask.maskUrlQuery`, `CertificateFileParser.scanPem` / `extract/asn1.js`, `PageCheckerService.globMatches`. In the SPA, `x+$` trailing-punctuation regexes on long text become backward scans. When you rewrite a regex, keep the old one in the test as an oracle (`SecretMaskTest`, `CertificateFileParserPemScanTest`, `linearTextScans.test.js`).
  - **Lists from the outside world** (certificate extensions, ZIP directories, keystore counts, 1xx responses, headers) are deduplicated and capped: `ChainValidationService.MAX_CRL_DOWNLOADS` plus a shared deadline, `RawHttpProbe.MAX_INTERIM_RESPONSES` / `MAX_HEADER_LINES`, `ExtractedUpload.MAX_CERTS` on BOTH upload paths, `ScriptedSafetyRules.MAX_SCRIPT_CHARS` / `MAX_LOOP_HEADERS`, and the browser extractor's PKCS#12 / JCEKS / ZIP caps.
  - **Graphs and chains:** use a visited set plus a depth cap. Exception cause walks use `CauseChain.MAX_DEPTH`; the `CauseChainGuardTest` source gate fails on an unguarded `while/for … getCause()`.
  - **Retry / re-send loops:** claim BEFORE sending (conditional UPDATE), or back off with an attempt cap, and record the give-up in `notification_logs`. Examples: the NOC storm tick (`tickRetryHeld`), the storm daily re-alert (`claimDailyRealert`), the push outbox (`writeRejected`, `sentUnrecorded`). Since multi-pod R1 also: cert / monitoring re-alerts (`EscalationService.claimReAlert`), resolution mails (`resolution_notify` PENDING → SENDING → DONE, stale SENDING → UNKNOWN without resend), the system-maintenance side jobs (released claim + `MAX_JOB_ATTEMPTS`), SMTP 421 orphans (`MailRetryOrphanSweeper`, give-up written to `notification_logs`) and the change-type `mon-alert` lock retry (6 × 5 s, then a `notification_logs` trace).
  - **SPA polling must not overlap itself:** `useVisibleInterval` skips while the previous promise is pending, and one-off polls chain `setTimeout` after the await. Self-reloading paths need a count guard, not only a time guard (`utils/chunkReloadGuard.js`, `utils/sessionExpiry.js`). A boot-time import needs a timeout (`LANG_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS`).
  - **CI:** every job has `timeout-minutes`, Surefire has `forkedProcessTimeoutInSeconds`, and tests wait with a timeout (`await(n, unit)`, `get(n, unit)`, `assertTimeoutPreemptively`).
- **Spring keeps firing `@Scheduled` ticks during shutdown (≈ 40 s with `await-termination`, 2026-10-21).** Never start new work after `ContextClosedEvent`: take locks / rounds / leadership only through `SchedulerService` (it refuses once `shutdownStarted`); a job with its own executor must check the same flag. A new `ContextClosedEvent` listener that waits must NOT outrank `SchedulerService.onContextClosed` (`@Order(HIGHEST_PRECEDENCE)` — the lease is released first). A long job moved to the side-job runner (multi-pod Sürüm 3-1) keeps its lock until `onShutdown` stops the runner (shared deadline, then interrupt) — `stopSideJobs()` must stay before `releaseAllOwn()` (`ShutdownBudgetGateTest.sideJobsStopBeforeOwnLocksAreReleased`).
- **One shutdown budget for destroy-time waits (2026-10-21, review D-1).** Every destroy-time wait reads `config/ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(ownCap)`: a shared 10 s budget (`DESTROY_BUDGET_MS`), started at the FIRST destroy-time wait after `ContextClosedEvent` and active only while the JVM is really shutting down (tests and hand-built objects keep their own caps). `notifyExecutor` / `loginIssueMailExecutor` are `WebConfig.DeadlineAwareTaskExecutor` (await time read at `shutdown()`); `EmailNotificationService`, `LoginOtpDeliveryService`, `UserPushService`, `IncidentNotificationService` and the scheduler's manual-run pool use `waitMs` in their `awaitTermination`; the cache bus's final flush joins its helper thread for `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(2000)` (skipped when the table is missing or the last DB call failed; `ShutdownBudgetGateTest.cacheBusFinalFlushUsesTheSharedDeadline`), and the heartbeat shutdown marker (multi-pod Sürüm 2b) is written on a daemon thread awaited at most `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(2 s)`; since multi-pod Sürüm 3-1 the side-job runner's `stopAndWait` (`ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(5000)`, interrupt, `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(1000)` unwind) and the evaluation-state writer's join (`ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(1000)`) draw from it too. The incident-record mail pool and the new-device check queue drain through `service/DroppableTask` (multi-pod Sürüm 2a, 2026-10-23): tasks are `execute`d as `DroppableTask`, `drain(exec, cap, 200, onMore)` = shutdown → `awaitTermination(ShutdownDeadline.waitMs(cap))` → `shutdownNow()` and one `notification_logs` row for each of the first 200 tasks that never started, ONE summary row for the rest (`INCIDENT_NOTIFY` `FAILED: sonuç yok — pod kapanırken olay kaydı postası kuyrukta kaldı, gönderilmedi`; `NEW_DEVICE` `SKIPPED: yeni cihaz denetimi yapılamadı …`, only while the feature is on; labels in `admin/logTriggers.js`); a task refused by a closed / full queue writes its row at once; a task that started and was cut writes none (it may have mailed). `NewDeviceNotifier` no longer uses `@Async("certCheckExecutor")` (that pool does not wait at shutdown) — it has its own 1-thread queue (1 000). Gate: `DroppedMailTraceTest`. The `NotifyExecutorDrain` (20 s, at `ContextClosedEvent`, beans alive — it also stops and drains the push worker inside the same deadline, see "Push outbox is claimed") and the `@Scheduled` pool's await (20 s) are separate and counted in the Helm grace budget. A new pool / `awaitTermination` must use `ShutdownDeadline.waitMs` — `ShutdownBudgetGateTest.everyDestroyWaitUsesTheSharedDeadline` fails otherwise. Gates: `ShutdownDeadlineTest`, `ShutdownBudgetGateTest`.
- **One pod's log is not the cluster's log (2026-10-21).** With more than one pod `kubectl logs deploy/<name>` (or `"$DEPLOY"`) prints a single, arbitrary pod — a `decrypt failed` or ERROR on the other pod is invisible. Read `-l app.kubernetes.io/instance=$REL --prefix --tail=-1`. Monitoring diagnostics (`kubectl exec … nslookup / curl`) must run in the LEADER pod (System Health `sweep_leader.owner` minus the `-xxxxxxxx` suffix), because only the leader runs the sweeps.
- **Never `save` a read `AlertEvent` (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b, R2b-F).** `AlertEvent` has no `@Version` / `@DynamicUpdate`: a whole-entity save writes every column from the stale read — a close that happened meanwhile (often during the SMTP send) is undone (`resolved = false`, a ghost alarm), an ack / stamp / storm unlink is lost, and a "re-close" rewrites `resolved_by` and retries the RESOLVE push. Close through `SilentAlarmCloser` (`closeCopyIfOpen` / `closeIfOpen`) or a CAS (`markResolvedIfOpenNotify`); write anything else with `AlertEventFieldWriter` (only the columns the path changes; `onlyOpen` when the intent is "open alarms"); push from the returned copy; never mutate the read entity inside a transaction (dirty checking writes the whole row at commit). Only a NEW event is `save`d.
- **PostgreSQL sorts NULLs FIRST in `ORDER BY … DESC`; H2 sorts them LAST (2026-10-23, audit chain).** A "newest / highest row" lookup over a nullable column (`findTopByOrderByXDesc`, `ORDER BY x DESC LIMIT 1`) returns a NULL row on PostgreSQL as soon as one exists — the audit chain tail did that for three months and every H2 test stayed green. Filter it (`…XIsNotNull…` / `WHERE x IS NOT NULL`) or say `NULLS LAST`. An H2 test that must see PostgreSQL's ordering sets `DEFAULT_NULL_ORDERING=HIGH` in its own datasource URL (`AuditChainNullsFirstH2Test`, `MonitoringMetricsSqlTest`); the postgres-it suite is the real gate. And keep a hot "top 1" query to exactly the ORDER BY its index carries: a tie-break column the index lacks (`ORDER BY seq DESC, id DESC` on an index of `seq`) makes PostgreSQL read and sort every row of the first group — with skewed statistics that is the whole table (review F1; EXPLAIN gate `AuditChainPostgresTest.tailQueryPlan_*`).
- **A local cache eviction is not a cluster eviction (2026-10-22).** Since multi-pod Sürüm 2a the cache bus carries it — but only for Spring caches and registered snapshots. A new `volatile Snapshot` / `TtlMemo` that a write invalidates must register on `CacheBus`, and its remote evictor must never publish (two pods would ping-pong forever), must be cheap (it runs on the bus thread — mark stale, never read the database; keep the previous data so a rebuild that fails on a DB error does not leave every pod with an empty snapshot) and should bump a generation so a load racing it does not store the old value; an evictor that throws keeps its name un-applied and is retried every poll.
- **Session attribute values are JDK types only (2026-10-23).** A JDBC session written by a new pod is read by an old pod mid-rollout (and back): an app class (record, enum, DTO) whose serialized form changed throws on the other pod and the user is logged out at random. Store ids / names / lists of `Long` instead (`SessionAttributeTypesGateTest`).
- **The boot guard is not part of the bundle (2026-10-23).** `frontend/public/boot-guard.js` is copied verbatim (no transpile, no hash, `no-store`) and runs before React; keep it dependency-free, CSP-safe (external file, no inline script / `on*=`), and keep its reload rule identical to `utils/chunkReloadGuard.js` (`bootGuard.test.js`).
- **Names and long text are never clipped (2026-10-10, user report: "takımı yöneten müdürler kısmındaki kayma" — "bu sorunları bir daha yapmayalım").** A person / team name in a 14rem `Badge` with `truncate` lost its START and end (the shadcn Badge centres and hides overflow; `truncate`'s ellipsis does not work on inline-flex). Rules: the Badge base is `justify-center-safe`; no `<Badge>` carries `truncate` (gate `badgeNoTruncate.test.js`, multi-line tags included); show people as plain wrapping text (name · e-mail, `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]`) and list ALL of them (no silent "+N" for recipients); test with real-length AD display names ("Ad Soyad (Bölüm / Unvan)", 60+ chars) and add a Playwright `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` check for every new name/address list. Also (2026-10-22): the executive KPI caption (`ex-kpi-caption`), command-palette rows (`PaletteRow` label / sub), the login-methods user table (name, team, failure reason) and the system-maintenance history (window, who) wrap with `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]` instead of `truncate` (a `TableCell` needs `whitespace-normal` too — shadcn's cell is `whitespace-nowrap`).
  - A multi-line name inside a pill (`rounded-full` + `overflow-hidden`) is clipped at the line ends by the rounded corners even when `scrollWidth` fits (2026-10-11, Teams phone card: "ijital Kanallaı"): `TeamBadge wrap` switches to `rounded-md overflow-visible`; the Teams e2e also flags a pill container taller than one line.
- **Admin → Teams (2026-10-11, user request: "eksik olanların filtrelenebileceği bir süzgeç yok … tablo araları çok açık, menüler net belli değil, sayfaya sığmıyor").**
  - **Missing-information model:** `admin/teams/teamGaps.js` is the single catalogue (email, leader, active members, escalation contact, manager, inactive members, notification group, description, monitored assets; severity high/medium/low; "Teams with gaps" KPI = high + medium). Derived from `/admin/teams` + `/admin/teams/stats` + the user list only — no backend change. A rule whose input has not loaded (stats / users) is UNKNOWN, never counted as missing.
  - **UI:** KPI strip (`MonitorStatsBar dense`), `TeamGapsPanel` (chips with counts computed from the search + panel filters but NOT the gap filter itself; any/all), per-row `TeamGapBadge` popover with fix shortcuts that follow the row's own write gate (edit → team form, members → member manager, contacts / groups → `AdminPanel.jump` with `g_team`, assets → inventory). URL: `g_miss` (CSV), `g_mmode=all`, `g_sort` (default = server name order, not re-sorted).
  - **Layout:** container width decides (`TEAM_TABLE_MIN_PX` 900): `table-fixed` table (owners stacked, actions = `KebabMenu variant="labeled"`) or cards (2 columns ≥ 640 px). Gates: `teamGaps.test.js`, `TeamManagerGaps.test.jsx`, `e2e/teams-admin.spec.js` (390 / 768 with real touch emulation, 1280; never call `page.emulateMedia` there — it resets `pointer: coarse` in Chromium). Adding a gap: catalogue + TR/EN `team.gap.<key>.label|why|fix` + an action in `gapActionsFor`.
  - **Unknown vs missing (2026-10-12):** leader / manager / member checks use the raw id set INCLUDING passive users; the user list is scope-filtered for scoped admins, so an id not in it is UNKNOWN (no gap counted, the badge says why: `pending` / `scope`, `team.leaderNotVisible`), never "missing"; AUDIT and global admins see the full list. While the user list loads the owner cells say "loading" (not "no leader"); if it can't be read they say "unknown — the user list could not be read", the badge reason is `failed` (`team.gap.loadFailed`, also for unreadable stats), the "no leader" filter lists only teams with NO assigned leader, and the CSV marks every team as not fully checked. Gate `TeamManagerGaps.test.jsx`.
  - **Delete impact lists team DOCUMENTS (2026-10-22, ADMIN-1 / ADMIN-5).** `TeamAdminService.impact` counts tech-error channels (with report count), the team template library and unfinished maintenance windows (live / upcoming / paused / recurring) — each makes `empty=false`, so delete returns 409 "move first" (the 409 text names them; retention orphan rules would otherwise delete the channels' reports, images and history for good). Executive summary team settings / reports are informational only (`executive_summary`, a muted "deleted with the team" line in `TeamDeleteImpactModal`). `moveAll` moves channels (appended to the target's order; the denormalized `team_id` of `tech_error_reports`, `_report_images`, `_report_events`, `_template_versions` too), the template library (the moved default is dropped when the target already has one — `uq_tett_one_default`) and every maintenance window; a channel / template name clash or a full target (50 channels / 20 templates) → 409 `TEAM_MOVE_*` and nothing moves (checked first, team rows locked `FOR UPDATE`, smaller id first); the `TEAM_MOVE_ASSETS` audit carries the document counts. Maintenance window OWNER = the single owning team of its targets (`MaintenanceController.ownerTeamFor`); several → the creator's first manage-scope team that owns a target (global admin: primary if among them, else the smallest id); "all monitors" by a global admin → null; recomputed on update only when targets / all-monitors change. Gates: `TeamAdminDocumentsTest`, `AdminControllerTest.teamDocuments_deleteTextAndMoveAudit`, `MaintenanceControllerTest.create_ownerIsTargetsTeam|scopedAdminWithoutPrimaryTeam_canEditOwnWindow|owner_severalTeams_andGlobalAll`, `TeamAssets.test.jsx`.
  - **Team form sends only changed fields (2026-10-22, UIA-A11):** `changedFields(teamPayload(form), teamPayload(teamToForm(team)))`; an untouched form sends no request; a cleared leader sends `leader_id: ''` (UIA-A10 / ADMIN-11). "Üyeleri yönet" lists passive members last with the destructive "Pasif" badge (UIA-A7). Gates `TeamManagerQuietHours.test.jsx`, `TeamManager.test.jsx`, `TeamMembersManager.test.jsx`.
  - **Members and moves (2026-10-22, A3 / FE-8):** "Üyeleri yönet" follows `requireCanAdministerTarget`: a non-global admin sees no ADMIN / AUDIT candidates and a lock + `team.memberAdminLocked` instead of "Çıkar" on such members (`TeamMembersManager globalAdmin`, K1 fallback `scope.global_admin`). The move toast lists every non-zero moved kind (`movedSummary`, `team.mv.<key>`; "taşınacak kayıt yoktu" when none), never "0 / 0 / 0".
- **Offset paging needs a unique tie-breaker (2026-10-22, DATA-7).** `created_at` / `event_time` are second-precision strings; on PostgreSQL a top-N sort with different LIMIT / OFFSET orders ties differently per page (rows repeat or vanish). Every paged sort ends with `id DESC`: alarm list / CSV (`AdminController.AlertSort`), weekly outage report `collectPages` (`WeeklyOutageReportService.STABLE_ORDER` — the query had no ORDER BY at all), audit lists (`AuditLogRepository`), incident records, incidents (alarm) page (`IncidentsController.sortFor`, which also sorts severity by `AlertSort.LEVEL_RANK`, DATA-8). Check History overlap (`findOverlappingForHistory`) = `createdAt <= to AND (open OR resolvedAt >= from)` (DATA-4: outages that started before the range were missing). Gates: `AlertEventReadPathQueriesTest`, postgres-it `ReadPathPagingPostgresTest` (1 200 same-second alarms, pages of 500).
- **Downloads only through `utils/csvExport.downloadBlob` (2026-10-22, UIM-27).** `utils/exportInventory.triggerDownload` (inventory CSV, Domain CSV, shared-certificate Excel, crypto-inventory Excel) delegates to it and throws a `DOWNLOAD_FAILED`-coded error when the browser can't start the download (no success toast); the executive-summary and weekly-outage PDF helpers in `api/client.js` return `{ success: false, code: 'DOWNLOAD_FAILED' }` and run the session path on 401. Never write a detached anchor + immediate `revokeObjectURL` again (Safari / iPad start nothing).
- **Alarm detail opened from a heat-map / team-cell slot uses a PARTIAL row:** always read the full row (`api.admin.getAlert`) before rendering actions / why-chips (`AlertHistory.openFullAlert`, 2026-10-22, UIM-31). A heat-map / team-distribution cell opens the alert through a sequence-guarded single read (`AlertHistory.openFullAlert`); opening or closing any other detail (or logging a call) bumps the same sequence, so a late reply never replaces or reopens a window (FE-8, `AlertHistoryLateSlotOpen.test.jsx`).
- **Never mark an `/api/**` response `Cache-Control: public`.** `/api/branding` and `/api/public-stats` were `public, max-age=60`; the NetScaler in front of test/prod cached them with its own TTL, so after a deploy the login page showed the previous version number for minutes and a browser hard refresh could not fix it (2026-09-10). All `/api` responses are `no-store` now (`WebConfigTest.publicEndpoints_areNoStore`); if a public endpoint needs load relief, memoize server-side (see `PublicStatsController.cacheMs`), which also resets on pod restart.

- **CSP has no `'unsafe-inline'` in `script-src` (2026-10-02).** `index.html` loads only the external module and no server HTML has inline scripts; style-src keeps `'unsafe-inline'` (shadcn Chart injects `<style>`, mail previews). Never add an inline `<script>`, an `on*=` attribute or a `javascript:` URL (`WebConfigTest.csp_scriptSrc_hasNoUnsafeInline`).
- **k6 scripts can read pod files — policy gate (2026-10-02, proposal 1).** `ScriptedCheckerService.scanFileReads` flags `open()`, `k6/fs`, local imports and system paths (comment- and string-aware). `site.monitor.scripted.file-read-policy` REPORT (default: save warning + startup summary + `GET /api/admin/scripted/file-read-report`, behaviour unchanged) | BLOCK (save rejected, run returns SKIPPED). Heuristic only — the real fix (k6 in a secret-free sidecar) is still open.
- After any backend change, the user expects: `mvn verify` → if green, `npm run build` (so the frontend is bundled) → restart backend → smoke. Don't skip the restart, login regressions show as 500.
- `start-local.ps1` launches the **packaged jar** (`backend/target/*.jar`), not `spring-boot:run`. `mvn test` does **not** repackage — after a backend code change run `mvn package -DskipTests` (or `verify`) *before* restarting, or the running app serves stale code (e.g. a new response field comes back empty).
- Don't hand-edit `VERSION` / `Chart.yaml` in a release commit — `release.yml` auto-bumps both after every push to `main` (a `chore(release): bump version … [skip ci]` commit). A manual bump makes the next `git pull --rebase` conflict on those files. Let CI own the version; just commit code with a conventional prefix (`feat:` → minor, `fix:` → patch) to drive the bump.
- Frontend always runs on port **5173**. Kill stale processes on 5173/5179 before `npm run dev`.
- Don't put `*` directly in i18n strings for required-field markers — use `<span className="req-star">*</span>` as a separate JSX node. Double-star bug has happened more than once.
- When removing a `useState` or handler, grep for **all** JSX references too — guards in render branches will throw at runtime, not at build time.
- Email credentials in `.env` are committed-locally-only. The Gmail app password in chat history must be rotated before any production deploy (see memory).
- **`.properties` files are read as ISO-8859-1**, not UTF-8. A raw Turkish character in a property *value* gets double-encoded at runtime (`Site Monitör` → UI shows `Site MonitÃ¶r`; a stray default like this even shadows a correct code-side default because Spring property beats the Java fallback arg). Write non-ASCII values as `\uXXXX` escapes (`Site Monit\u00f6r`). `PropertiesEncodingTest` fails the build on raw non-ASCII bytes in non-comment lines. Related trap: javac processes `\u` escapes even inside comments — an illustrative `\uXXXX` in Javadoc is a compile error; write `\\uXXXX`.
- **Renaming a settings-key prefix (e.g. `cert.monitor.*` → `site.monitor.*`) must touch the frontend too.** `AppSettingsCatalog` keys are hardcoded in admin components (`BrandingSettings.jsx` `K()`, `GeneralSettings.jsx` base-url lookup, `HttpMetricsExplorer.jsx` `RETENTION_KEY`) and — easy to miss — i18n labels are built dynamically as `t('general.lbl.' + it.key)`, so the `general.lbl.<full-key>` dict entries never show up when you grep for the construction site. A key mismatch fails *silently*: screens render with empty values / raw-key labels, and unit tests stay green because they mock the API with the same (wrong) keys. `scripts/check-brand.(sh|ps1)` now scans the dotted `cert.monitor` pattern as well — run it after any brand/key rename.
- **The turnip logo (`branding/assets/`) is the permanent default brand mark** (product decision 2026-08-06; brand name is written "SiteMonitor", one word). It ships on every surface — UI (`BrandLogo`), favicon, emails (CID via `BrandMailAssets`), PDFs (`pdfBrand.js`), docs — and only an explicit white-label `branding.logo-data` override may replace it at runtime. **In-app the logo stays neutral green (`ok`) at all times** — same-day user decision: navbar/favicon never turn red with fleet status (status lives in badges/counters; emails DO use severity variants). The status-aware plumbing (`deriveGlobalStatus`, `useStatusFavicon`) is kept ready but unwired. Guards pin this: `frontend/src/test/brand-default.test.jsx` (assets + default renders) and `BrandMailAssetsTest` (classpath assets). If one goes red, restore the default — never loosen the guard. Rules live in `branding/BRAND.md`; regenerate assets only via `branding/tools/make_variants.py`.
- **Helm release rename loses every `--set`-supplied value.** The certmonitor→sitemonitor fresh install silently dropped `HTTP_PROXY_HOST/PORT` (they lived only in the old release's values, not in the repo) — domain registration (RDAP) died with connect timeouts for days. Operational values that prod depends on belong in `environments/master.yaml`, and `docs/DEPLOY_GECIS_PLANI.md` §2.1's checklist must be walked on any release migration. Related trap: the scheduled domain sweep's WHOIS gate must use `whois.anySourceEnabled()` (socket OR `.tr` web-whois) — gating on `enabled()` alone turns off `.tr` web-whois too, while "Sorun Tanıla" (gate-free) keeps working, which hides the bug.
- **Every `*/response-series` endpoint MUST return through `buildResponseSeries(...)`** — never the raw repo `Object[]` rows (Jackson serializes them as arrays, `s.ts` comes back undefined, and the chart screen crashes to the ErrorBoundary; happened 2026-08 with the scripted type). The contract test `MonitoringControllerTest.responseSeries_contract_allEndpoints` counts the mappings reflectively — adding an 8th monitor type without registering it there fails the build by design.
- **Chart components must drop malformed records, not crash**: `ResponseTimeChart` filters entries without a string `ts` before mapping. Keep that defensive filter when refactoring; `ResponseTimeChart.test.jsx` pins it.
- **A derived `deleteBy…` repository method without `@Transactional` fails on every call.** Spring Data only wraps `SimpleJpaRepository`'s own methods (`save`/`delete`/`deleteAll`/`deleteById`); query-derived deletes and `@Modifying` queries get no transaction, and with `open-in-view=false` plus non-transactional controllers the call throws `TransactionRequiredException: No EntityManager with actual transaction available`. Shipped 2026-08-21 as `ScriptedDraftRepository.deleteByOwnerAndMonitorKey`: the controller swallowed it at `log.debug` and the UI announced "draft discarded" without reading the response, so the "unfinished draft" banner came back forever with **zero** log lines. Mock-based service tests can't see this (mocks have no transaction) and `@DataJpaTest` *hides* it (it wraps every test in a transaction) — pin such paths with `@Transactional(propagation = Propagation.NOT_SUPPORTED)`, as `ScriptedRepositoriesTest` now does. `RepositoryWriteTransactionGuardTest` scans every repository interface and fails the build on any writing method that declares neither `@Transactional` nor an entry in its `CALLER_MANAGED_TX` allow-list; adding a line there is a promise that a `@Transactional` caller owns the transaction — verify the caller, don't just silence the test. Return `int`, not `void`, so callers can refuse to report success on 0 rows, and never announce success in the UI without checking `res?.success`.
- **The IDE's Java extension (Eclipse compiler) can rewrite `backend/target/classes`, and its semantics differ from javac.** On 2026-10-02, `x instanceof Number n ? n.longValue() : parseLong(s)` (a `long`/`Long` conditional) passed under javac but threw NPE on a null `Long` when ECJ had recompiled the class, so two NOC tests "failed" with no source change. Write such conditionals with BOTH branches as references (`Long.valueOf(n.longValue())`). Before trusting a backend test failure on unchanged code, re-run after `mvn clean`.
- When output contains mojibake (`Ã¶`, `ÃÂ¶`…), don't dismiss it as a console/terminal display artifact — verify the raw bytes end-to-end (`curl … | od -c`, check DB with `octet_length`, check the compiled class) before concluding. The 2026-08 branding bug shipped because the double-encoding was visible in a smoke output and got waved off as console encoding.

## Skill routing

When the user's request matches an available skill, invoke it via the Skill tool. When in doubt, invoke the skill.

Key routing rules:
- Product ideas/brainstorming → invoke /office-hours
- Strategy/scope → invoke /plan-ceo-review
- Architecture → invoke /plan-eng-review
- Design system/plan review → invoke /design-consultation or /plan-design-review
- Full review pipeline → invoke /autoplan
- Bugs/errors → invoke /investigate
- QA/testing site behavior → invoke /qa or /qa-only
- Code review/diff check → invoke /review
- Visual polish → invoke /design-review
- Ship/deploy/PR → invoke /ship or /land-and-deploy
- Save progress → invoke /context-save
- Resume context → invoke /context-restore
- Author a backlog-ready spec/issue → invoke /spec

## Sürüm & Dağıtım geçmişi (2026-09-11)
- Yayın dizini `docs/releases/index.json` CI'da `scripts/gen-release-index.mjs --append` ile büyür (yerelde `--check`); imajda `/app/releases.json`, `ReleaseIndexService` okur. Çalışma anında GitHub'a ÇIKILMAZ.
- `deployment_history` asla silinmez (RetentionCatalog BOUNDED); yalnız MANUAL satır silinir. Tür (UPGRADE/RESTART/ROLLBACK) `deriveKinds` ile türetilir, yazılmaz.
- İzin anahtarları TAM ad: `canView('release_history.read')`, `canEdit('release_history.edit')` — `canView('release_history')` sessizce false döner.
- `schema_table_registry` (SQL Playground "oluşturma ≈ ilk görülme / son veri değişimi"): heartbeat'te pg_stat sayaç farkı; katalogsuz ortamda yalnız first_seen.
- CI `release.yml`: elle major ya da anlamlı commit yokken bump → `confirm_major=yes`; `BUILD_DATE` gerçek build anı.
- **Deploy notice once per cluster, restarts per pod (2026-10-23, multi-pod Sürüm 2b, R2b-C2, MP-37e).** `DeploymentHistoryService.deriveKinds` is multi-pod aware: a row is NOT a transition (kind RESTART, `prev` does not advance) when at its start another live pod already ran the same version AND (the environment is already on that version OR the row's Helm revision is not higher than every live pod's) — an extra replica / 2-replica rollout / scale-out (a row whose version differs from the environment's and whose Helm revision is higher than every live pod's IS a transition even while an old pod of that version still lives: v2 never became ready → `--atomic` / `helm rollback` starts v1 with a new revision while the old v1 lives → ROLLBACK + notice, environment v1; rows without a Helm revision keep the old rule, different version = transition; review W-1), the same pod key (pod name, else hostname) last ran the same version (container restart — an OLD pod restarting mid-rollout no longer yields ROLLBACK + UPGRADE), or a live pod of a higher Helm revision ran another version (a straggler of the old ReplicaSet). "Live at start" = an earlier STARTUP row whose `ended_at` (exact) or `last_seen_at` + 2 min (`ALIVE_SLACK_MS`) is not before the start; rows without liveness data (BACKFILL / MANUAL) keep the old rule. `Derived.countedRestart` (`replica()` = RESTART not counted; `toMap` adds `"replica": true`): a RESTART counts only for a container restart or when no other pod of that version was alive (single-pod pattern) — `current().restartsSince`, `timeline.restartCount`, `summary.restartsLast7d` and the weekly availability report (`WeeklyAvailabilityReportService.collectDeployments`: `case RESTART -> if (d.countedRestart()) restarts++`, gate `WeeklyAvailabilityReportServiceTest.deployments_replicaRestartNotCounted`) use it; `current()` takes the version from the last TRANSITION row. The release-history UI shows a `replica: true` row as "Ek pod" / "Extra pod" (neutral `KindBadge replica`, `data-replica`, table + card; absent = "Yeniden başlatma"; counts come from the server, replicas excluded; gate `DeployReplicaBadge.test.jsx`). Publishing: an UPGRADE / ROLLBACK / CHANGED start first inserts `deploy-notice:<env>:<version>:<helmRev|->` into `schema_patch_markers` (`claimNotice`; ≤ 120 chars, longer keys → SHA-256) — only the inserter publishes `TransitionEvent`; a duplicate key = already announced; any other DB error publishes (old behaviour). Known limits: without Helm revisions (local) a second v1→v2 to the same version is not re-announced; a straggler with a NEW pod name and no Helm revision still derives ROLLBACK; a pod dying between the marker insert and the publish loses that notice; `kubectl rollout undo` restores the OLD Helm revision number, so its pods count as stragglers of the old ReplicaSet (no ROLLBACK row / notice, the environment shows the newer version until the next Helm release) — `helm rollback` is the documented path. History is re-derived on read, so past multi-pod rollouts are corrected retroactively (extra replicas no longer counted as restarts; a same-version single-replica `rollout restart` with surge is a replica now, not a restart). Gates: `DeploymentNoticeMultiPodTest` (two `DeploymentHistoryService` on one H2: 20 concurrent rounds → one UPGRADE each, marker stops a second UPGRADE, old pod restart mid-rollout → no event, restarts per pod, real Helm rollback still announced), `DeploymentNoticeMultiPodTest.atomicRollbackWhileOldPodAlive` (and `twoNewPodsStartTogether_oneNotice` forces both pods to derive UPGRADE — only the marker keeps it to one event), `DeploymentTransitionTest.stragglerOfOlderRevisionIsNotARollback|sameHostnameAcrossVersions_realTransitionsKept|replicaVersusRestart|rollbackWhileOldVersionPodAlive`, `WeeklyAvailabilityReportServiceTest.deployments_replicaRestartNotCounted`, `DeployReplicaBadge.test.jsx`.
